Normative draft of the R standard library

|===
|Field |Value

== Preface

This document defines the mandatory R 0.1 standard-library surface independently of any
implementation. The public interfaces are safe R abstractions over C17 facilities or
over target-native asynchronous services. They specify signatures, ownership, errors,
observable semantics, portability requirements, and implementation boundaries rather
than library source code.

Normative provisions have stable identifiers. The words *shall*, *shall not*, *should*
and *may* have the meanings established by the R Core Language Specification. This
English file is normative. A Russian file with the same revision is its translation; if
the two files conflict, this English file governs.

[[scope]]
== 1. Scope

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0001]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0001* — This document specifies the standard-library modules required by R
0.1 profiles, their exact public operation families, ownership and failure contracts,
C17 or target-native mappings, and library conformance requirements.

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0002]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0002* — A conforming implementation shall expose only interfaces whose
observable behavior satisfies this document. Internal C functions, operating-system
handles, executor objects and adapter entry points are implementation details unless a
rule explicitly exposes an R value representing them.


The following standard nominal types are explicitly classified as errors under
R-TYPE-0012 and have the semantics of an `+error+` declaration: `+core::recursion_error+`,
`+core::utf8_error+`, `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`, `+std.async::start_error+`, `+std.bits::read_error+`,
`+std.bytes::bytes_error+`, `+std.c::runtime_error+`, `+std.c::string_error+`,
`+std.convert::parse_error+`, `+std.convert::range_error+`, `+std.env::env_error+`,
`+std.error::error+`, `+std.format::format_error+`, `+std.fs::fs_error+`, `+std.fs::path_error+`,
`+std.io::io_error+`, `+std.math::math_error+`, `+std.net::address_error+`, `+std.net::net_error+`,
`+std.process::process_error+`, `+std.string::boundary_error+`, `+std.string::string_error+`,
`+std.sync::barrier_error+`, `+std.thread::thread_error+`, `+std.time::duration_error+`,
`+std.time::time_error+`.
The closed error families are `+std.alloc::new_error<T>+`, `+std.array::push_error<T>+`,
`+std.dict::insert_error<K,V>+`, `+std.list::push_error<T>+`.
This classification does not depend on a name suffix and does not include code enums
such as `+std.fs::error_code+`. Existing names, variants, fields, representation and
Copy/Move contracts are preserved. In particular, allocation and container errors
continue to return unaccepted owners; no POD restriction is imposed.

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0009]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0009* — The explicit standard POD confirmations for R-TYPE-0033 are:
`+core::memory_order+`, `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`, `+std.async::start_error+`,
`+std.hash::md5_digest+`, `+std.hash::sha1_digest+`, `+std.hash::sha256_digest+`,
`+std.hash::sha512_digest+`, `+std.time::duration+`, `+std.time::duration_error+`,
`+std.time::instant+`, `+std.time::system_time+`, `+std.time::utc_datetime+`.
Other opaque standard types do not obtain POD from their name, Copy property or
C representation. These confirmations are recorded in the implementation inventory
and do not change any representation or error-payload contract.

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0008]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0008* — The complete R 0.1 networking layer consists of IP address
conversion, DNS resolution, TCP and UDP specified here. HTTP, TLS and other protocol
stacks belong to separately versioned libraries outside this standard. The complete
standard I/O surface for potentially blocking operations uses the asynchronous
signatures in sections 12 and 14..18 with task-owned `+array<u8>+` or
`+arc (array<u8>)+` storage, or with a task-group loan of caller storage through the
scoped forms of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012; synchronous I/O aliases and other suspension-live
borrowed buffers are not public standard-library items.

[[normative-references]]
== 2. Normative references

[[R-SLIB-REF-0001]]
*R-SLIB-REF-0001* — xref:R_LANGUAGE_SPECIFICATION_0_1.en.adoc[R Core Language Specification 0.1]
defines R syntax, types,
ownership, borrowing, errors, concurrency, C17 translation, diagnostics and safety
contracts used by this document. An unqualified `+R-...+` reference whose identifier is
not defined in this document denotes a rule in the Core Specification; locally anchored
R-LIB and R-SLIB identifiers resolve in this document. Within the preserved R-LIB rules,
“section 21”, “Annex A” and
“Annex E” denote section 21 and Annexes A and E of that specification.

[[R-SLIB-REF-0002]]
*R-SLIB-REF-0002* — ISO/IEC 9899:2018, _Information technology — Programming languages —
C_, defines the C17 facilities named by the mapping clauses. R semantics remain
controlling at every safe interface.

[[R-SLIB-REF-0003]]
*R-SLIB-REF-0003* — RFC 3629 defines the UTF-8 well-formedness required by `+str+`,
`+constexpr str+`, `+std.string::string+`, environment text and UTF-8 paths.

[[R-SLIB-REF-0004]]
*R-SLIB-REF-0004* — NIST FIPS PUB 180-4, _Secure Hash Standard_, defines the SHA-1,
SHA-256 and SHA-512 algorithms and digest byte order used by `+std.hash+`.

[[R-SLIB-REF-0005]]
*R-SLIB-REF-0005* — IETF RFC 1321, _The MD5 Message-Digest Algorithm_, defines the
legacy MD5 algorithm and digest byte order used by `+std.hash+`.

[[terms]]
== 3. Terms and conventions

[[R-SLIB-TERM-0001]]
*R-SLIB-TERM-0001* — A *logical result* T of an asynchronous operation is the T produced
when its `+task<T throws E...>+` reaches terminal completion. A declared E is a checked
completion effect carried by that task. Starting the operation produces the task or
immediately throws `+std.async::start_error+`.

[[R-SLIB-TERM-0002]]
*R-SLIB-TERM-0002* — A *native asynchronous operation* is submitted to a target facility
that can await readiness or completion without executing a blocking C operation on an
executor worker. A short non-blocking C call used to prepare or complete submission
remains part of the adapter boundary.

[[R-SLIB-TERM-0003]]
*R-SLIB-TERM-0003* — A *staged value* is a by-value argument prepared for an operation
before the operation commits to consuming it. A failure outcome that carries the staged
value returns the same ownership obligation; it does not clone or reconstruct the
resource.

[[R-SLIB-TERM-0004]]
*R-SLIB-TERM-0004* — Signature notation
`+async module::operation(P...) -> T throws E...+` in this
document inventories an R async declaration whose logical return type is T. Its source
declaration uses the Core order `+async T operation(P...) throws E...+`, and its call has
the immediate start effect fixed by R-SLIB-ASYNC-0002. Arrow notation is specification metanotation rather than
additional source grammar.

[[profiles]]
== 4. Profiles and conformance

[[R-SLIB-PROFILE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-PROFILE-0001* — Library availability is cumulative. `+freestanding+` provides
R-LIB-0001..R-LIB-0003 and R-LIB-0011. `+allocation+` additionally provides R-LIB-0004, R-LIB-0012 and
R-LIB-0018..R-LIB-0023 plus `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`. `+hosted+` additionally provides
the R-SLIB-ALLOC-0001..R-SLIB-ALLOC-0002 `+std.alloc+` operations,
`+std.error+`, `+std.bytes+`, `+std.hash+`, `+std.utf8+`, `+std.bits+`, `+std.string+`, `+std.convert+`, `+std.format+`,
`+std.math+`, synchronous `+std.time+`, `+std.env+`, `+std.c+`, `+std.secret+`,
`+std.random+`, `+std.test+` and R-SLIB-PROC-0007. `+hosted-thread+` additionally
provides R-LIB-0005..R-LIB-0018 as applicable. `+hosted-native-async+` includes hosted
threading and every `+std.async+`, `+std.io+`, `+std.fs+`, `+std.net+`, `+std.signal+`,
async `+std.time+` requirement, and R-SLIB-PROC-0001..R-SLIB-PROC-0008 in this document. The
standard modules written in R join this ladder at the profile their library map entry names
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0002).

[[R-SLIB-PROFILE-0002]]
*R-SLIB-PROFILE-0002* — Profile selection for `+hosted-native-async+` shall fail before
program translation when the selected target lacks a conforming native backend for any
mandatory facility. The implementation shall not silently substitute a blocking worker
pool or remove an operation from the profile. The permitted blocking adapters are the
closed filesystem adapter lane of Core R-TERM-0014/R-CMAP-0039 and R-SLIB-ASYNC-0011, the
blocking call pool of Core R-TERM-0015 and R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017 and the file payload adapter
of Core R-TERM-0016 and R-SLIB-ASYNC-0019; none is an executor worker pool.

[[R-SLIB-PROFILE-0003]]
*R-SLIB-PROFILE-0003* — The target manifest shall name the native backend and record the
cancellation, acknowledgement and monotonic-deadline mode for file, console, DNS, TCP, UDP
and child-process operations. Event-driven facilities shall record `+native+`. An
operation admitted to the filesystem adapter lane shall instead record
`+queued_or_acknowledged_after_return+` and its exact non-cancellable external commit
point. An operation whose own rule defines a native creation or atomic commit interval
shall record `+before_commit_only+` and that exact commit point. Every mandatory family
shall identify one of those closed modes; any other mode causes profile selection to fail.
The blocking call pool of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017 shall record its thread count, its bound of
waiting calls and the mode `+queued_removed_or_acknowledged_after_return+`. Regular-file
payload transfer on the file payload adapter of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0019 shall record
`+queued_or_acknowledged_after_return+`, the bound of concurrently entered transfers and
the admission policy.

[[R-SLIB-PROFILE-0004]]
*R-SLIB-PROFILE-0004* — A library implementation shall publish, for every public
operation, the exact source signature and the ownership, allocation, panic, Send/Sync,
cancellation, deadline, error and C17/native mapping properties required by the
normative inventory of this document. Implementation-private helpers do not extend the
public R interface.

[[preserved-library]]
== 5. Core, ownership, threads and containers

The following rules are preserved from the R Core Language Specification. Their stable
identifiers and anchors remain authoritative in this document.

=== 5.1 Freestanding core

[[R-LIB-0001]]
*R-LIB-0001* — Every implementation shall provide `+core+` with the exact
`+core::utf8_error+` and `+core::validate_utf8+` public items of Core R-ARRAY-0007, the
exact `+core::recursion_error+` of Core R-FUNC-0026 and the
representation-transmute form and compiler-recognized unsafe `+core+` intrinsic families
of Core R-UNSAFE-0007..0008, plus the compiler-recognized atomic families and memory-
order enum of R-LIB-0011. The allocation
profile additionally provides the exact closed `+core::hash+` and `+core::key_equal+`
families through R-LIB-0020. For each literal suffix in the closed set
`+i8,u8,i16,u16,i32,u32,i64,u64,isize,usize+`, `+core+` also provides public Copy
constants `+core::min_SUFFIX+` and `+core::max_SUFFIX+`, each of type `+SUFFIX+`, and the
exact operations:

* `+core::checked_add_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> o<SUFFIX>+`,
  `+core::checked_sub_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> o<SUFFIX>+` and
  `+core::checked_mul_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> o<SUFFIX>+`;
* `+core::wrapping_add_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> SUFFIX+`,
  `+core::wrapping_sub_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> SUFFIX+` and
  `+core::wrapping_mul_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> SUFFIX+`;
* `+core::saturating_add_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> SUFFIX+`,
  `+core::saturating_sub_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> SUFFIX+` and
  `+core::saturating_mul_SUFFIX(SUFFIX left, SUFFIX right) -> SUFFIX+`.

The constants are the mathematical bounds of their type. A checked operation returns
`+o::some+` of the exact mathematical result when representable and `+o::none+`
otherwise. A wrapping operation returns the unique value in the type range congruent
to the mathematical result modulo the number of distinct values of that type. A
saturating operation clamps that result to the nearest type bound. These items allocate
nothing, do not panic and have no side effect. The suffix is literal API spelling and
does not create user generics or overload resolution. Construction of `+o+`,
ordinary slice semantics and panic categories remain Core language facilities governed
by the Core Specification, not additional callable standard-library items.

[[R-LIB-0002]]
*R-LIB-0002* — A safe library function shall express ownership in its source signature,
shall expose compiler-verifiable borrow-origin relations in interface metadata and
shall not rely on an undocumented unsafe precondition.

[[R-LIB-0003]]
*R-LIB-0003* — `+core+` shall not allocate implicitly, perform I/O, read environment, spawn
threads or depend on locale. Safe non-atomic operation that does not observe memory
shall produce the same result for the same explicit values. Atomic, volatile and unsafe
raw-memory operations may additionally depend only on memory, device and scheduling
state permitted by their documented contracts.

[[R-LIB-0026]]
*R-LIB-0026* — The following closed standard operations return supplementary information
about the mutation the caller requested and carry the discardable-result contract of Core
R-FUNC-0021: a direct call in statement position destroys the result as `+as void+` would,
without further spelling. They are `+core::replace+`, `+core::atomic_exchange+`,
`+core::atomic_fetch_add+`, `+core::atomic_fetch_sub+`, `+core::atomic_fetch_and+`,
`+core::atomic_fetch_or+` and `+core::atomic_fetch_xor+` (the previous value);
`+std.array::pop+`, `+std.array::remove+`, `+std.list::pop_front+`, `+std.list::pop_back+`
and `+std.list::remove+` (the removed element, destroyed when discarded); `+std.dict::insert+`
and `+std.dict::remove+` (the replaced or removed value); `+std.fs::seek+` (the new position);
and `+std.sync::barrier_wait+` (the generation role). The library inventory records
`+discardable_result=true+` on exactly these operation records, and the compiler registry is
generated from it. Among the R-source modules, the `+insert+` and `+remove+` methods of
`+std.set::set<T>+`, `+std.sorted::set<K>+` and `+std.sorted::map<K, V>+`, `+pop_front+` and
`+pop_back+` of `+std.deque::deque<T>+` and `+pop+` of `+std.heap::heap<T>+` declare
`+@discardable+` in their source. No other standard operation is discardable: a result that
reports progress, failure, ownership transfer, a borrowed position or a value that exists only
to be consumed (for example `+core::take+`, `+core::atomic_compare_exchange+`,
`+std.list::push_back+`, `+std.io::write+`, `+std.sync::send+`) requires use or an explicit
`+as void+`. Discarding never resolves a checked error or a task.

[[R-LIB-0025]]
*R-LIB-0025* — Every profile provides the compiler intrinsics
`+core::replace(T* destination, T replacement) -> T+` and
`+core::take(T* destination) -> T+` under Core R-OWN-0019. Availability of T retains
its profile requirements. They return the previous value without dropping it and keep
the borrowed destination initialized. They have no checked errors, allocate nothing
and add no panic after valid operand preparation. Existing operand evaluation, bounds
checks, default initialization and cleanup retain their contracts. No runtime library,
C ABI layout or callback entry is added. Sync and async lowering use the same concrete
copy/move representation and initialization tracking.

Every profile also provides `+core::swap(T* first, T* second) -> void+` under Core
R-OWN-0019, with the same guarantees, and every profile with allocation provides
`+core::clone(const T* value) -> T+` under Core R-OWN-0020. A clone of a value that is not
Copy throws exactly `+std.alloc::alloc_error+` and calls only the generated clone glue,
the runtime container and string operations it names, and clone hooks; a Copy clone is a
read.

[[R-LIB-0024]]
*R-LIB-0024* — Every profile provides these exact compiler-recognized static reflection
intrinsics of Core R-REFL-0001..R-REFL-0005 as closed families over the type or value they
name:

* `+core::enum_count::<T>() -> usize+`, `+core::enum_min::<T>() -> T+`, `+core::enum_max::<T>() -> T+`
  and `+core::enum_variants::<T>() -> T[N]+` for a fieldless enumeration `+T+` with `+N+`
  variants;
* `+core::enum_name(T value) -> constexpr str+`, `+core::enum_ordinal(T value) -> usize+`,
  `+core::enum_at::<T>(usize index) -> o<T>+` and
  `+core::enum_from_name::<T>(str name) -> o<T>+`;
* `+core::variant_count::<T>() -> usize+` and `+core::variant_name(const T* value) -> constexpr str+`
  for a tagged union `+T+`;
* `+core::type_name::<T>() -> constexpr str+`, `+core::field_count::<T>() -> usize+` and
  `+core::field_name::<T>(usize index) -> constexpr str+`;
* `+core::target_name() -> constexpr str+`, `+core::profile_name() -> constexpr str+` and
  `+core::location() -> constexpr str+`;
* `+core::type_attribute::<A, T>() -> o<A>+`,
  `+core::field_attribute::<A, T>(usize index) -> o<A>+` and
  `+core::variant_attribute::<A, T>(T value) -> o<A>+` for an attribute type `+A+` (Core
  R-AGG-0013).

Every constant form folds at translation time and every selection is lowered to direct
comparisons against translation-time tables; the items allocate nothing, do not panic
beyond the Core checks of their operands, have no side effect, introduce no library symbol
and are available in the freestanding profile. The type operand and the family suffix are
API spelling and create no user generics or overload resolution. A fieldless enum of this
Library, including one implemented natively such as `+std.process::termination_kind+`, is a
fieldless enumeration for these forms, for its variant constants and for Core R-STMT-0006
switches; its variants are ordered as this specification lists them.

=== 5.2 Allocation-profile and hosted modules

[[R-LIB-0004]]
*R-LIB-0004* — Every profile that supports allocation shall provide the
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+` type and the `+std.arc+`, `+std.rc+`, `+std.array+`,
`+std.list+` and `+std.dict+` modules. A hosted implementation additionally shall provide `+std.alloc+`
with owned byte buffers and typed single-object allocation, `+std.bytes+`, `+std.hash+`, `+std.utf8+`,
`+std.bits+`, `+std.string+`
with owned UTF-8 string, `+std.env+` process arguments and environment snapshots,
`+std.process+` current-process terminal operations, `+std.c+` ABI conversions and
`+std.secret+` erased secret buffers.
A hosted-native-async implementation additionally shall provide
`+std.async+`, `+std.io+`, `+std.fs+`, `+std.net+`, `+std.signal+`, async `+std.time+` and
async child-process execution together with the complete `+std.process+` operation families
in this document.
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+out_of_memory+`, `+size_overflow+`, `+unsupported_alignment+` and `+budget_exhausted+` and
owns no rejected value.
Every standard allocation first performs checked size, count, capacity and object-bound
arithmetic; an unrepresentable request is `+size_overflow+`. It next validates a
requested alignment; a valid numeric alignment unsupported by the selected allocator is
`+unsupported_alignment+`. Only then may it call the allocator, whose refusal of the
otherwise conforming request is `+budget_exhausted+` when a budget of the executing task
refuses it (Core R-STMT-0020) and `+out_of_memory+` otherwise. This precedence applies through every
typed wrapper carrying an `+alloc_error+`; no failure exposes a partial allocation or
constructed object.
Hosted-thread profile additionally shall provide
`+std.thread+` and `+std.sync+` with all operations in R-LIB-0010..R-LIB-0018. Portable
inter-thread communication uses ownership transfer, `+arc+`, standard synchronization,
channels, atomics and the edges fixed by section 21.

Standard operations with a natural receiver also expose the explicit method mappings
in `library/standard_methods.json`. The receiver access and position are fixed by that
catalogue; factories and unrelated free functions do not acquire methods. Canonical
aliases share one method namespace. Methods preserve the operation's runtime entry point,
checked errors, borrowing, consumption, profile and two-phase async startup. Endian names,
validated UTF-8 conversion, partial/all writes and destination-specific conversion names
remain distinct. Qualified operation spellings remain available.

[[R-LIB-0005]]
*R-LIB-0005* — Every successfully spawned safe thread shall return one Move-only join
handle owning the right to observe its completion. Entry parameters receive the
explicit arguments transferred at thread-creation commit. Multiple transferred values
are written as multiple parameters or one payload struct. Entry may return a Send value
or `+void+`. Spawn uses two-phase commit: named Move operands are staged without ending
their source state. Each staging establishes the Core exclusive overlap reservation of
R-MEM-0013 until failure or commit; every later overlapping access, borrow, move or
restaging is rejected by that Core rule. A named Move source receives this transactional
treatment only when the complete argument expression is a direct `+move place+`;
nesting a named Move in an aggregate, constructor or other argument expression requires
`+R-DIAG-MOVE-003+`. The caller may first construct a named payload and then pass that
payload by direct move. Failed creation throws `+std.thread::thread_error+`, creates no thread, releases all
provisional runtime state and leaves each named source initialized with its original
ownership obligation. Successful creation atomically commits and consumes the staged
operands before the entry observes them. A staged temporary is destroyed exactly once
when failed creation ends its full expression.
If evaluation of a later argument panics, unwind releases every provisional reservation
and destroys initialized staged temporaries according to Core; abort performs no
additional R drops under R-ERR-0005.

[[R-LIB-0006]]
*R-LIB-0006* — Hosted-thread `+std.sync+` shall provide mutex, rw_lock, condvar, barrier,
once, once_lock and MPSC channel families. Each protected T is reachable only through a
guard or other operation specified here; guard destruction is deterministic and no
manual unlock is required for scope exit. A project-specific safe synchronization
abstraction may encapsulate unsafe operations only under R-UNSAFE-0006 and shall state
edges at least as precisely as section 21. Destruction of an unlocked mutex or rw_lock
destroys its protected T exactly once and releases its runtime state. Destruction of an
Initialized once_lock destroys its stored T exactly once and releases its runtime state;
an Uninitialized once_lock destroys no T. Destruction of once, condvar or barrier releases
its runtime state. On unwind strategy, a first panic from T destruction still performs
the remaining resource-state cleanup; T destruction is never repeated, and a second
panic aborts under R-ERR-0008. On abort strategy, a first panic performs no further drops
under R-ERR-0005.

[[R-LIB-0007]]
*R-LIB-0007* — Library allocation and I/O failures shall be declared as checked
`+throws+` effects or represented by a closed
standardized ownership-preserving outcome schema explicitly fixed by this document. The
language `+new+` convenience is the sole allocation operation specified to panic instead.
Thread creation failure throws `+std.thread::thread_error+`. No library call shall terminate process for an
ordinary recoverable error. For any closed standard operation whose exact signature has
no failure alternative, absence of such an alternative is a normative guarantee that
recoverable allocation or OS-resource acquisition cannot fail at that call, apart from
an explicitly specified panic or contract violation. The runtime shall use inline,
preallocated or fallible-free fallback state as needed; a target unable to provide that
guarantee shall reject the requiring profile at profile selection rather than add an
undocumented result or panic.

[[R-LIB-0008]]
*R-LIB-0008* — Standard library version shall match language 0.1 major/minor and publish
normative mapping from each public API to ownership, panic, thread-safety and C ABI
contracts.

[[R-LIB-0009]]
*R-LIB-0009* — Hosted `+std.c+` shall provide checked scalar conversions, borrowed and owned
NUL-terminated byte buffers, bounded C-string validation, raw-handle adoption/release
helpers, C-thread attachment/detachment support and target-manifest queries. It shall
not turn arbitrary raw pointer or unresolved symbol into safe value.

[[R-LIB-0010]]
*R-LIB-0010* — Hosted-thread profile provides compiler-recognized forms
`+std.thread::spawn(entry, arguments...)+`, `+std.thread::spawn_scoped(entry,
arguments...)+`, `+std.thread::join(move handle)+` and `+std.thread::detach(move handle)+`.
The first operand of either spawn form is the direct safe function designator described
by R-MEM-0013 and is accepted exactly in that operand position. If its signature is
`+R entry(P...) throws E...+`, spawn returns
`+std.thread::join_handle<R throws E...>+` and scoped spawn returns
`+std.thread::scoped_join_handle<R throws E...>+`; either operation itself throws
`+std.thread::thread_error+` on creation failure. The scoped handle directly carries and
transfers the borrow-origin identity, and its
creation-failure path ends staged exclusive-borrow suspension, exactly as R-MEM-0015.
Both spawn forms apply the R-LIB-0005 two-phase commit to every argument; failure does not
consume a named Move operand, while successful creation consumes it.
Only a complete direct `+move place+` argument denotes such a provisional named operand;
a nested named Move requires `+R-DIAG-MOVE-003+` under R-MEM-0013.
Join consumes either handle, returns `+std.thread::join_result<R>+` on normal or panicked
completion, and declares the exact E... set carried by the handle. Detach accepts any
unscoped handle and returns `+void+`. Dropping an effect-free unscoped
handle detaches, while dropping a scoped handle transfers its observation right to the
scope supervisor for automatic join. An unscoped handle carrying any E is must-resolve
under Core R-MEM-0017 and shall be explicitly joined or detached; normal implicit drop
is invalid. A scoped handle carrying any E shall be explicitly joined under R-MEM-0016.
Detached cleanup acquires completion and destroys any unobserved checked-error payload
exactly once.

[[R-LIB-0011]]
*R-LIB-0011* — `+core+` provides compiler-recognized atomic families for every permitted T
with these schematic signatures: `+atomic_load(obj, order) -> T+`, `+atomic_store(obj, desired, order) -> void+`, `+atomic_exchange(obj, desired, order) -> T+`, `+atomic_compare_exchange(obj, expected, desired, success_order, failure_order) -> core::atomic_compare_exchange_result<T>+`, integer-only
`+atomic_fetch_add/sub/and/or/xor(obj, operand, order) -> T+`, and `+atomic_is_lock_free(obj) -> bool+`. In every signature `+obj+` has type `+const (atomic T)*+`; mutation through that
shared borrow is a closed standardized interior-mutation exception and grants no
ordinary mutable access. `+order+` values are `+core::memory_order::{relaxed,acquire,release,acq_rel,seq_cst}+` as applicable by R-MEM-0007;
compare-exchange has exactly the two independently checked ordering arguments shown.
Load/exchange/fetch return previous T; `+core::atomic_compare_exchange_result<T>+` is a
closed ordinary outcome with exactly `+exchanged(T observed)+` and `+unchanged(T observed)+`.
Compare-exchange returns `+exchanged(observed)+` after writing desired iff observed equals
expected, otherwise `+unchanged(observed)+` without modification. Each
object expression is evaluated once. Fetch arithmetic/bitwise result follows R integer
semantics; its final conversion back to T is the intrinsic fixed-integer conversion of
R-EXPR-0013 and requires no `+as+`. Every overflow, ordering, arithmetic and conversion
check completes before the atomic modification, possibly by a CAS loop. This schematic T is standard intrinsic metanotation and does not add user-defined
generics.

[[R-LIB-0012]]
*R-LIB-0012* — This rule is required in every allocation-supporting profile, including
freestanding. `+std.arc+` and `+std.rc+` provide the same closed operation family for their
respective owner kind. Exact schematic signatures are `+Owner<T> clone(const Owner<T>*
source)+`, `+Weak<T> clone_weak(const Weak<T>* source)+`, `+Weak<T> downgrade(const Owner<T>*
source)+`, `+o<Owner<T>> upgrade(const Weak<T>* source)+`, `+T*? get_mut(Owner<T>*
owner)+`, and `+TryUnwrap<T> try_unwrap(Owner<T> owner)+`, where `+TryUnwrap<T>+` is
`+std.arc::try_unwrap_result<T>+` or `+std.rc::try_unwrap_result<T>+` according to the
module. Each is a closed ordinary outcome with exactly `+unwrapped(T value)+` and
`+shared(Owner<T> owner)+`. The optional exclusive
borrow returned by `+get_mut+` has the same inferred origin as the borrow of `+owner+`.
Count and identity
signatures are `+usize strong_count(const Owner<T>* source)+`, `+usize weak_count(const
Owner<T>* source)+` and `+bool ptr_eq(const Owner<T>* left, const Owner<T>* right)+`; R 0.1
defines no corresponding weak-handle observations. Token conversions have exact
signatures `+raw const (T)* into_raw(Owner<T> owner)+` and `+unsafe Owner<T> from_raw(raw const
(T)* pointer)+`. The owner argument to `+try_unwrap+` or `+into_raw+` is consumed and requires
`+move+` when named; every other owner operand shown is an explicit borrow. In `+std.arc+`,
`+Owner<T>+`/`+Weak<T>+` mean `+arc T+`/`+weak arc T+`; in `+std.rc+`, they mean `+rc T+`/`+weak rc T+`.
The module selects the family and the raw pointee statically selects T; target-type
inference and overload resolution are not used. Token operations follow
R-SAFETY-SHARED-INTO-RAW/R-SAFETY-SHARED-FROM-RAW.

[[R-LIB-0013]]
*R-LIB-0013* — Standard thread schemas are `+std.thread::join_handle<R throws E...>+`,
`+std.thread::scoped_join_handle<R throws E...>+`, `+std.thread::join_result<R>+` and
`+std.thread::thread+`. join_result has exactly the alternatives `+completed+` when R
is `+void+`, `+returned(R)+` when R is non-void, and
`+panicked(std.thread::panic_report)+` under unwind strategy. `+panic_report+` is an
owned, Move-only, Send+Sync, non-throwing description containing at least the stable
category; its text is diagnostic-only. Its destruction releases all owned report storage
exactly once and cannot panic. Exact observation operations are
`+std.thread::panic_category(const std.thread::panic_report* report) -> constexpr str+`
and `+std.thread::panic_text(const std.thread::panic_report* report) -> str+`.
The first runtime-selects the exact stable lowercase program string from R-ERR-0004 and
remains valid independently of the report lifetime; the second returns implementation
diagnostic text whose borrow is bounded by the report borrow. Neither operation performs
allocation. `+std.thread::thread_error+` describes creation failure and
retains no argument. `+std.thread::thread+` is a Move-only identity handle. Its additional operations
are exactly `+std.thread::current() -> std.thread::thread+`,
`+std.thread::clone_thread(const std.thread::thread*) ->
std.thread::thread+`, `+std.thread::unpark(const std.thread::thread*) -> void+`,
`+std.thread::park() -> void+`, `+std.thread::yield_now() -> void+` and
`+std.thread::sleep_nanoseconds(u64) -> void+`. They have R-MEM-0026 semantics; sleep may
return later than requested. `+thread_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+unavailable+`,
`+resource_exhausted+` and `+permission_denied+`. Its creation-failure classification is
exhaustive: an explicit native security or execution-policy denial is
`+permission_denied+`; otherwise an R/runtime allocation failure or a native thread,
descriptor, address-space or process-limit exhaustion is `+resource_exhausted+`;
every remaining native condition in which the thread provider cannot presently create
the requested thread is `+unavailable+`. That order controls a native status with
overlapping descriptions. Argument and capability violations are compile-time
diagnostics rather than thread_error values. Dropping a
`+std.thread::thread+` identity
handle never joins, detaches or cancels its target; descriptor storage remains valid
through the last identity/runtime reference and is released exactly once after that last
reference ends. The total number of live identity and runtime references to one descriptor
shall not exceed half the maximum value representable by `+usize+`, rounded down.
Successful thread creation establishes
its fixed runtime references within that bound. `+current+` or `+clone_thread+` that would
exceed it causes `+reference_count_overflow+` before changing the count or producing a
handle. Reference destruction never underflows; underflow can arise only from an unsafe
or runtime-contract violation and is UB. After target exit, `+unpark+` is a no-op.

[[R-LIB-0014]]
*R-LIB-0014* — Standard lock schemas in `+std.sync+` are `+mutex<T>+`,
`+mutex_guard<T>+`, `+rw_lock<T>+`, `+rw_read_guard<T>+` and `+rw_write_guard<T>+`.
Result schemas name the protected value type directly: mutex operations use
`+lock_result<T>+`/`+try_lock_result<T>+`, read operations use
`+read_lock_result<T>+`/`+try_read_lock_result<T>+`, and write operations use
`+write_lock_result<T>+`/`+try_write_lock_result<T>+`. Exact operations are
`+std.sync::mutex_new(T value) -> std.sync::mutex<T>+`,
`+std.sync::lock(const std.sync::mutex<T>* mutex) -> std.sync::lock_result<T>+`,
`+std.sync::try_lock(const std.sync::mutex<T>* mutex) -> std.sync::try_lock_result<T>+`,
`+std.sync::rwlock_new(T value) -> std.sync::rw_lock<T>+`,
`+std.sync::read(const std.sync::rw_lock<T>* lock) -> std.sync::read_lock_result<T>+`,
`+std.sync::try_read(const std.sync::rw_lock<T>* lock) -> std.sync::try_read_lock_result<T>+`,
`+std.sync::write(const std.sync::rw_lock<T>* lock) -> std.sync::write_lock_result<T>+`, and
`+std.sync::try_write(const std.sync::rw_lock<T>* lock) -> std.sync::try_write_lock_result<T>+`.
Each constructor value parameter follows R-FUNC-0002: a named Move T requires `+move+`,
whereas a named Copy T is copied.

Each blocking result has `+locked(guard)+`, `+poisoned(guard)+` and guardless
`+would_deadlock+`; its matching `+try_*+` result additionally has guardless
`+would_block+`. The guard payload is respectively `+mutex_guard<T>+`,
`+rw_read_guard<T>+` or `+rw_write_guard<T>+`. A same-thread conflict is tested first and
returns `+would_deadlock+`. Otherwise an immediately compatible acquisition returns
`+locked+` or, when advisory poison is set, `+poisoned+`. A `+try_*+` operation facing
incompatible live holders returns `+would_block+` without a guard or synchronizes-with
edge; the matching blocking operation waits and then returns an acquired guard.
`+std.sync::unlock(G guard) -> void+` consumes any of the three guard types and is
equivalent to guard destruction.

Exact guard-access operations are
`+std.sync::mutex_guard_ref(const std.sync::mutex_guard<T>* guard) -> const T*+`,
`+std.sync::mutex_guard_mut(std.sync::mutex_guard<T>* guard) -> T*+`,
`+std.sync::rw_read_guard_ref(const std.sync::rw_read_guard<T>* guard) -> const T*+`,
`+std.sync::rw_write_guard_ref(const std.sync::rw_write_guard<T>* guard) -> const T*+`
and `+std.sync::rw_write_guard_mut(std.sync::rw_write_guard<T>* guard) -> T*+`.
Each guard carries an unforgeable lock identity and a hidden region tied to the creating
lock borrow. A returned borrow is bounded by both the guard borrow and that hidden lock
region; no guard permits T or a subobject to outlive the guard or lock.

[[R-LIB-0015]]
*R-LIB-0015* — `+std.sync+` additionally provides `+std.sync::condvar+`,
`+std.sync::barrier+`, `+std.sync::once+` and `+std.sync::once_lock<T>+` as Move-only
synchronized resources. Exact constructors are `+std.sync::condvar_new() ->
std.sync::condvar+`, `+std.sync::once_new() -> std.sync::once+` and
compiler-recognized `+std.sync::once_lock::<T>() -> std.sync::once_lock<T>+`.
`+std.sync::barrier_new(usize count) -> std.sync::barrier throws
std.sync::barrier_error+`. `+barrier_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly the alternative `+zero_participants+`,
returned exactly when count is zero. For nonzero count, construction succeeds; the hosted-thread
profile guarantees that recoverable allocation or operating-system resource failure cannot occur.
`+std.sync::wait(const std.sync::condvar* condition,
std.sync::mutex_guard<T> guard) -> std.sync::lock_result<T>+` consumes the guard and returns a new
guard after reacquisition. It returns only `+locked+` or `+poisoned+`, never
`+would_deadlock+`; the supplied guard was released before reacquisition and no second
same-mutex guard can be live in safe code. `+std.sync::notify_one(const
std.sync::condvar*) ->
void+` and `+std.sync::notify_all(const std.sync::condvar*) -> void+` take a shared
borrow. `+std.sync::barrier_wait(const
std.sync::barrier*) -> std.sync::barrier_wait_result+`; `+barrier_wait_result+` is a Copy,
Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+leader+` or `+follower+`; the last arrival is the sole Leader in each generation.
`+std.sync::call_once(const std.sync::once* once, initializer) -> void throws E...+`
accepts a direct zero-argument safe function designator `+void initializer() throws E...+`
and exposes exactly its checked-error set. On Completed it returns
without invocation; on Poisoned it causes `+once_poisoned+` before invocation, and a
concurrent Running attempt is awaited.
`+std.sync::call_once_force(const std.sync::once* once, initializer) -> void throws E...+`
accepts the same direct designator and checked-error set, also returns without invocation on Completed, and a winning caller after
Poisoned shall perform the serial retry and clear poison only on successful completion.
A checked initializer error restores retryable Uninitialized, publishes no completion,
sets no poison and propagates exact E; poison remains panic-only. The complete transition
and synchronization rules are R-MEM-0023.
`+std.sync::get_or_init(const std.sync::once_lock<T>* lock, initializer) -> const
T* throws E...+` accepts a direct zero-argument safe function designator
`+T initializer() throws E...+` and exposes exactly its checked-error set.
`+std.sync::get(const std.sync::once_lock<T>* lock) -> o<const T*>+` and
`+std.sync::set(const std.sync::once_lock<T>* lock, T value) ->
std.sync::set_result<T>+` complete the API; set_result has exactly `+stored+` and
`+occupied(T)+`. The `+set+` value parameter follows R-FUNC-0002: a named Move T requires
`+move+`, whereas a named Copy T is copied; `+occupied(T)+` returns that staged value.
`+get+` is non-blocking; `+get_or_init+` never invokes its designator after publication;
`+set+` and `+get_or_init+` wait for a competing Running attempt and then apply the exact
state/outcome mapping of R-MEM-0023. A checked initializer error restores retryable
Uninitialized, publishes no value, sets no poison and propagates the exact E; poison is
panic-only. Recursive initialization of the same once_lock causes
`+contract_violation+`; an initializer panic leaves it uninitialized.

[[R-LIB-0016]]
*R-LIB-0016* — Compiler-recognized `+std.sync::channel::<T>()+` and
`+std.sync::sync_channel::<T>(capacity)+` return, respectively,
`+std.sync::channel<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` and
`+std.sync::sync_channel<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`; capacity has type
`+usize+`. A successful result owns a Move-only endpoint factory. Exact extraction and
clone signatures are `+std.sync::sender(const (std.sync::channel<T>)* factory) ->
std.sync::sender<T>+`, `+std.sync::sync_sender(const (std.sync::sync_channel<T>)* factory)
-> std.sync::sync_sender<T>+`, `+std.sync::receiver(std.sync::channel<T> factory) ->
std.sync::receiver<T>+`, `+std.sync::sync_receiver(std.sync::sync_channel<T> factory) ->
std.sync::receiver<T>+`, `+std.sync::clone_sender(const (std.sync::sender<T>)* source) ->
std.sync::sender<T>+` and `+std.sync::clone_sync_sender(const
(std.sync::sync_sender<T>)* source) -> std.sync::sync_sender<T>+`. A named factory
argument requires `+move+`. The number of live sender endpoints shall not exceed
half the maximum value representable by `+usize+`, rounded down; creation or cloning
that would exceed it causes
`+reference_count_overflow+` before change. The factory owns the sole receiver-creation
right. Sending before receiver creation has ordinary queue/blocking semantics.
Consuming the factory transfers that right; dropping it instead disconnects the
receiver side, wakes blocked senders and synchronously executes one drop duty for each
queued value before that factory destruction returns or propagates its first panic,
subject to R-MEM-0025 and R-ERR-0005/R-ERR-0008. A receiver created after zero sender
endpoints first drains any values already queued and observes disconnection when the
queue becomes empty; it observes immediate disconnection only when that queue is already
empty.

Exact data-operation signatures are `+std.sync::send(const (std.sync::sender<T>)*
endpoint, T value) -> std.sync::send_result<T>+`, `+std.sync::sync_send(const
(std.sync::sync_sender<T>)* endpoint, T value) -> std.sync::send_result<T>+`,
`+std.sync::try_send(const (std.sync::sync_sender<T>)* endpoint, T value) ->
std.sync::try_send_result<T>+`, `+std.sync::recv(const (std.sync::receiver<T>)* endpoint) ->
std.sync::recv_result<T>+` and `+std.sync::try_recv(const (std.sync::receiver<T>)*
endpoint) -> std.sync::try_recv_result<T>+`. send_result has exactly `+sent+`,
`+disconnected(T)+` and `+allocation_failed(T)+`; bounded sync_send never returns
`+allocation_failed+`. try_send_result has exactly `+sent+`, `+full(T)+` and `+disconnected(T)+`;
recv_result has `+received(T)+` or `+disconnected+`; try_recv_result has `+received(T)+`, `+empty+`
or `+disconnected+`. Each send value parameter follows R-FUNC-0002: a named Move T
requires `+move+`, whereas a named Copy T is copied. Every failure payload is the staged
unsent T; for Move T it is the same transferred owner and for Copy T the source remains
unchanged. The complete endpoint schemas in `+std.sync+` are
`+channel<T>+`, `+sync_channel<T>+`, `+sender<T>+`, `+sync_sender<T>+`, `+receiver<T>+`,
`+send_result<T>+`, `+try_send_result<T>+`, `+recv_result<T>+`, `+try_recv_result<T>+`,
`+permit<T>+`, `+reserve_result<T>+` and `+try_reserve_result<T>+`; their queue and disconnect
semantics are fixed by R-MEM-0024..R-MEM-0025 and by the reservation paragraph below.

In the hosted-native-async profile,
`+async std.sync::receive(const (std.sync::receiver<T>)* endpoint) -> o<T>+`, also spelled
`+endpoint.receive()+`, receives asynchronously; T shall be Send and contain no ordinary
borrow, slice or runtime `+str+` (Core R-TYPE-0029), otherwise `+R-DIAG-ASYNC-001+` is
required, and the start error set is `+std.async::start_error+` (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0002). The
call borrows the receiver only for the start; the task retains the channel until it is
terminal. It completes with `+o::some+` of the oldest queued value or of the next value a
sender sends while it waits, and with `+o::none+` once no sender endpoint remains and the
queue is empty, or once the receiver has been destroyed. Pending receives of one channel
are served in start order and take values before they are queued. Cancellation removes a
pending receive, whose value stays queued; a receive selected before its cancellation has
already moved its value into its result, which is destroyed with that result when it stays
unobserved (Core R-STMT-0018). The asynchronous
for of Core R-STMT-0021 over a borrowed receiver starts this receive at each iteration and
ends at its `+o::none+`. The operation
has no deadline parameter; like `+await+`, it is bounded by a select with an until clause
or a bounded group wait rather than by a deadline block (Core R-STMT-0018, R-STMT-0019).

In the hosted-native-async profile,
`+async std.sync::reserve(const (std.sync::sync_sender<T>)* endpoint) -> std.sync::reserve_result<T>+`,
also spelled
`+endpoint.reserve()+`, reserves one slot of a bounded channel, and
`+std.sync::try_reserve(const (std.sync::sync_sender<T>)* endpoint) -> std.sync::try_reserve_result<T>+`,
also spelled `+endpoint.try_reserve()+`, reserves one
without waiting; T follows the receive requirement above. `+std.sync::reserve_result<T>+`
has exactly `+reserved(std.sync::permit<T>)+` and `+disconnected+`, and
`+std.sync::try_reserve_result<T>+` has exactly `+reserved(std.sync::permit<T>)+`,
`+full+` and `+disconnected+`. `+std.sync::permit<T>+` is a Move-only owner of one slot,
Send and Sync iff T is Send, and
`+std.sync::send_permit(std.sync::permit<T> permit, T value) -> void+`,
also spelled `+permit.send(value)+`, consumes the permit and gives the
value to the oldest pending receive or to the slot; after the receiver is destroyed the
value is destroyed, so the send neither waits nor fails. Destroying an unused permit
returns its slot. A slot is free when it holds neither a queued value nor a permit, so
`+sync_send+` and `+try_send+` count permits as occupied slots; reservations are served in
start order, and `+try_reserve+` returns `+full+` while one waits. A permit counts as a
sender endpoint until it sends or is destroyed. A rendezvous channel has no slot to
reserve, so `+reserve+` and `+try_reserve+` on a channel of capacity zero cause
`+contract_violation+`. Cancellation removes a waiting reservation, and a slot selected
before cancellation returns with the destroyed permit, so no slot is lost.

[[R-LIB-0017]]
*R-LIB-0017* — Capability table for standardized resources: `+mutex<T>+` is Send iff T is
Send and Sync iff T is Send; `+rw_lock<T>+` is Send iff T is Send and Sync iff T is
Send+Sync; `+once_lock<T>+` is Send iff T is Send and Sync iff T is Send+Sync. condvar,
barrier, once and `+std.thread::thread+` are unconditionally Send+Sync. channel
factories are neither Send nor Sync. sender endpoints are Send+Sync iff T is Send.
`+receiver<T>+` is Send iff T is Send and is never Sync.
`+join_handle<R throws E...>+` is Send+Sync iff R and every E are Send, or R is
`+void+`; a scoped handle has the same capability within its region. Every lock
guard is not Send and is Sync iff T is Sync. `+join_result+`, `+lock_result+`,
`+try_lock_result+`, `+read_lock_result+`, `+try_read_lock_result+`,
`+write_lock_result+`, `+try_write_lock_result+`, `+set_result+`, `+send_result+`,
`+try_send_result+`, `+recv_result+`, `+try_recv_result+`,
`+atomic_compare_exchange_result+`, `+try_unwrap_result+` and `+o+` derive
capabilities structurally from every possible payload.

[[R-LIB-0018]]
*R-LIB-0018* — The standard-library signatures in this section are normative schemas: T,
R, G, K and V are compiler metanotation, not source identifiers or user type parameters.
The only calls with type operands are, written with the explicit generic argument list of
Core R-TYPE-0036 and never among the value arguments, `+std.sync::channel::<T>()+`,
`+std.sync::sync_channel::<T>(capacity)+` and `+std.sync::once_lock::<T>()+`,
`+std.array::create::<T>()+`, `+std.array::with_capacity::<T>(capacity)+`,
`+std.list::create::<T>()+`, `+std.dict::create::<K, V>()+`,
`+std.dict::with_capacity::<K, V>(capacity)+` and `+std.async::broadcast::<T>(capacity)+`,
admitted by Annex A `+standard-type-call-expression+`; Core R-TYPE-0036 lets a call omit
the list where the expected type of its result supplies the operands. All ordinary value
arguments remain subject to R-EXPR-0020 and all returned call results shall be named
before use as an argument or return value. The five direct-function designator positions
enumerated by R-FUNC-0007 accept exactly the direct safe function designators and
signatures fixed by that rule.

[[R-LIB-0019]]
*R-LIB-0019* — `+array<T>+` is a Move-only owner of a contiguous, growable sequence of
live T objects. `+std.array::create::<T>() -> array<T>+` creates an empty value without
allocation. `+std.array::with_capacity::<T>(usize capacity) ->
array<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` creates an empty value able to receive at least
capacity elements without reallocation; zero capacity performs no allocation.
`+std.array::filled(usize length, T value) -> array<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
creates a value of length elements, each a copy of value, with capacity at least length in
one allocation; zero length performs no allocation, and size overflow or allocation failure
throws without a partial value. T is the element type of an expected `+array<T>+` result
and otherwise the type of value; T shall be Copy and hold no views, otherwise
`+R-DIAG-TYPE-001+` is required. The exact remaining operations are:

* `+std.array::capacity(const array<T>* source) -> usize+`;
* `+std.array::reserve(array<T>* target, usize additional) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.array::push(array<T>* target, T value) ->
  void throws std.array::push_error<T>+`;
* `+std.array::pop(array<T>* target) -> o<T>+` and
  `+std.array::remove(array<T>* target, usize index) -> o<T>+`;
* `+std.array::get(const array<T>* source, usize index) -> o<const T*>+` and
  `+std.array::get_mut(array<T>* source, usize index) -> o<T*>+`;
* `+std.array::as_slice(const array<T>* source) -> const T[]+` and
  `+std.array::as_slice_mut(array<T>* source) -> T[]+`;
* `+std.array::clear(array<T>* target) -> void+`.

Capacity is always at least `+len(target)+`. `+reserve+` guarantees capacity for
`+len(target) + additional+` without shrinking or changing elements and throws without
changing target on size overflow or allocation failure. `+push+`
appends at index old length. Its `+push_error<T>+` has exactly one variant
`+allocation_failed { std.alloc::alloc_error reason; T value; }+`; on failure target is
unchanged and that checked-error payload carries the staged value. A named Move T argument
therefore requires `+move+` and is never silently destroyed merely because growth failed.
`+pop+` removes the last element, while `+remove+` removes index and moves every following
element one position toward zero; either returns `+o::none+` when no requested element
exists. `+get+` and `+get_mut+` likewise return `+o::none+` rather than panic. Direct
subscript semantics remain those of R-EXPR-0021.

The address of element zero is suitably aligned for T and live elements occupy exactly
the contiguous order `+0..len-1+`. Successful growth may change every element address.
Capacity growth shall be geometric when repeated single-element pushes fit the target
address space, giving amortized constant-time push; exact capacity is implementation-defined
and observable through `+capacity+`. `+reserve+`, `+push+`, `+pop+`, `+remove+` and `+clear+` are
structural mutations. The last use of every element or slice borrow from that array
shall precede any such mutation. `+clear+` and array destruction drop live elements in reverse index order;
the backing allocation is then released exactly once.

[[R-LIB-0020]]
*R-LIB-0020* — `+dict<K,V>+` is a Move-only owner of insertion-ordered key/value entries.
K and V shall satisfy R-TYPE-0012, and K shall have a dictionary key contract. A complete
nominal type with the associated pair from R-FUNC-0009 uses that custom contract.
Otherwise the compiler provides a predefined contract for booleans, characters, R and
available C ABI numeric types, fieldless enums, strings and safe or raw pointer
categories. Integer, Boolean,
character and enum contracts use value equality; string contracts use exact UTF-8 byte
equality; pointer contracts use preserved allocation identity and offset. Floating key
equality treats both signed zeros as equal and all NaN values as one equal key class;
its hash canonicalizes those classes. The dictionary owns each stored K object. A key containing a borrow,
slice or ordinary `+str+` carries its inferred source regions, while no operation exposes
mutable access to a stored key.

Compiler-recognized `+core::hash(const T* value) -> u64+` and
`+core::key_equal(const T* left, const T* right) -> bool+` invoke the exact predefined
or associated contract for T. T is selected structurally from the borrow operands; this
closed intrinsic family is not function overloading or a user generic. It permits an
associated operation to compose the contracts of its fields without reimplementing
standard string, floating or pointer rules.

For one dictionary the implementation chooses a seed and computes an effective hash by
mixing that seed with the key contract's `+u64+` hash; the seed remains fixed until the
dictionary is destroyed. Seeding does not alter key equality or insertion order.

`+std.dict::create::<K, V>() -> dict<K,V>+` creates an empty dictionary without allocation.
`+std.dict::with_capacity::<K, V>(usize capacity) ->
dict<K,V> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` creates an empty dictionary able to receive at
least capacity distinct keys without rehashing; zero capacity performs no allocation.
The exact remaining operations are:

* `+std.dict::reserve(dict<K,V>* target, usize additional) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.dict::insert(dict<K,V>* target, K key, V value) ->
  o<V> throws std.dict::insert_error<K,V>+`;
* `+std.dict::contains(const dict<K,V>* source, const K* key) -> bool+`;
* `+std.dict::get(const dict<K,V>* source, const K* key) -> o<const V*>+` and
  `+std.dict::get_mut(dict<K,V>* source, const K* key) -> o<V*>+`;
* `+std.dict::remove(dict<K,V>* target, const K* key) -> o<V>+`;
* `+std.dict::clear(dict<K,V>* target) -> void+`;
* `+std.dict::iter(const dict<K,V>* source) -> std.dict::iter<K,V>+` and
  `+std.dict::next(std.dict::iter<K,V>* iterator) ->
  o<std.dict::entry_ref<K,V>>+`.

`+reserve+` guarantees room for at least `+len(target) + additional+` distinct live
entries without rehashing and leaves target unchanged on error. `+insert+` replaces an
equal existing value and returns it as `+o::some(old)+`, retaining the original
stored key and its insertion position; a new key is appended and returns
`+o::none+`. Its `+insert_error<K,V>+` has exactly one variant
`+allocation_failed { std.alloc::alloc_error reason; K key; V value; }+`; an allocation
or size failure leaves target unchanged and throws that payload carrying both staged inputs. A named Move K
or V therefore requires `+move+`. On successful replacement the incoming K is consumed
after the old V has been staged. `+contains+` is true exactly when one live stored key is
equal to the supplied key under the same key contract used by lookup, and is false
otherwise. `+get+`, `+get_mut+` and `+remove+` return `+o::none+` for
an absent key. Removal drops the stored K and transfers V to the result. Reinserting a
removed key appends it at the end. `+clear+` and dictionary destruction drop live entries
in reverse insertion order, value before key, and release all allocations exactly once.

[[R-LIB-0021]]
*R-LIB-0021* — The required `+dict<K,V>+` representation model has a contiguous
insertion-order entry sequence and a separate sparse index table. The index table has a
power-of-two slot count, at least eight after its first allocation. A slot is empty, a
tombstone, or a live entry index. Lookup starts at `+hash & (slot_count - 1)+` and visits
successive slots by `+(slot + 1) & (slot_count - 1)+` until it finds an equal key or an
empty slot. Insertion remembers the first tombstone but shall continue lookup through
it, so an existing equal key is replaced rather than duplicated. Before insertion would
make live plus tombstone slots exceed two thirds of slot count, the dictionary shall
rehash into a table satisfying that limit; it may compact deleted entry records or grow
geometrically while preserving the order of every live entry. Rehash failure is reported
through R-LIB-0020 without observable mutation. Collision resolution uses this exact
open-addressed linear probe sequence.

`+std.dict::entry_ref<K,V>+` is a Copy value with public fields `+const K* key+` and
`+const V* value+`. `+std.dict::iter<K,V>+` is a Move-only cursor carrying a hidden shared
borrow of its dictionary. Successive `+next+` calls return live entries in insertion
order, skipping deleted records, then permanently return `+o::none+`. Iterator creation
and advancement allocate nothing. Every borrow region belonging to an iterator, entry
reference, value borrow or key-derived borrow shall end before `+reserve+`, `+insert+`,
`+remove+` or `+clear+` begins on that dictionary. `+array<T>+` is Send iff T
is Send and Sync iff T is Sync; `+dict<K,V>+` derives those capabilities from K and V.
An iterator or entry reference is Send+Sync iff K and V are Sync.
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+` is Send+Sync; `+std.array::push_error<T>+` and
`+std.dict::insert_error<K,V>+` derive Send/Sync structurally from their returned value
payloads.

[[R-LIB-0022]]
*R-LIB-0022* — `+list<T>+` is a Move-only owner of an ordered doubly linked sequence of
stable nodes. Each live node owns exactly one T and has an object identity distinct from
the list descriptor and every other node. `+std.list::create::<T>() -> list<T>+` creates an
empty list without allocation. The exact remaining operations are:

* `+std.list::push_front(list<T>* target, T value) ->
  T* throws std.list::push_error<T>+` and `+std.list::push_back(list<T>* target, T value) ->
  T* throws std.list::push_error<T>+`;
* `+std.list::insert_before(list<T>* target, const T* position, T value) ->
  T* throws std.list::push_error<T>+` and
  `+std.list::insert_after(list<T>* target, const T* position, T value) ->
  T* throws std.list::push_error<T>+`;
* `+std.list::front(const list<T>* source) -> o<const T*>+` and
  `+std.list::back(const list<T>* source) -> o<const T*>+`;
* `+std.list::front_mut(list<T>* source) -> o<T*>+` and
  `+std.list::back_mut(list<T>* source) -> o<T*>+`;
* `+std.list::get(const list<T>* source, usize index) -> o<const T*>+` and
  `+std.list::get_mut(list<T>* source, usize index) -> o<T*>+`;
* `+std.list::remove(list<T>* target, T* element) -> T+`;
* `+std.list::pop_front(list<T>* target) -> o<T>+` and
  `+std.list::pop_back(list<T>* target) -> o<T>+`;
* `+std.list::clear(list<T>* target) -> void+`;
* `+std.list::iter(const list<T>* source) -> std.list::iter<T>+` and
  `+std.list::next(std.list::iter<T>* iterator) -> o<const T*>+`.

Every successful insertion allocates one node, links it at the requested position and
returns an exclusive borrow to its T with the list origin and a fresh node identity.
`+push_error<T>+` has exactly one variant
`+allocation_failed { std.alloc::alloc_error reason; T value; }+`; failure throws that
payload carrying the staged value and leaves target unchanged. A named Move T requires `+move+`. A position or
element operand shall carry the same hidden list origin as target. `+remove+` consumes an
exclusive borrow to exactly that node, unlinks it in constant time, transfers its T, and
releases its node storage. `+get+` and `+get_mut+` traverse from the nearer end and return
`+o::none+` outside `+0..len-1+`. Front/back insertion and removal are constant-time;
indexed lookup is linear-time.

[[R-LIB-0023]]
*R-LIB-0023* — Linking or unlinking a `+list<T>+` node never relocates any other live T.
An insertion preserves every existing element borrow and its address. Removal preserves
every borrow whose node identity differs from the consumed element identity. The borrow
rules admit the structural list access required by those operations because link and
descriptor storage does not overlap T storage; ordinary shared/exclusive rules continue
to govern each individual T. `+remove+` consumes the node's sole exclusive borrow, and
alias rules end every borrow of that node before its storage is released. Every borrow
region that might designate an endpoint node shall end before the matching `+pop_front+`
or `+pop_back+`; every borrow region that might designate any node shall end before
`+clear+` or list destruction. Every node-borrow region is bounded by the unchanged list
owner lifetime.

`+std.list::iter<T>+` is a Move-only forward cursor carrying a hidden shared borrow of
the list. It yields nodes in current front-to-back order and then permanently returns
`+o::none+`. Structural mutation while that cursor is live conflicts with its borrow.
List destruction drops remaining values from back to front and releases every node
exactly once. `+list<T>+` is Send iff T is Send and Sync iff T is Sync; its iterator is
Send+Sync iff T is Sync. `+std.list::push_error<T>+` derives Send/Sync structurally from T.

[[common-contracts]]
== 6. Common library contracts

=== 6.1 Naming, ownership and failure

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0003]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0003* — Every public module, type, operation, field and variant introduced by
this document has the lowercase spelling shown here. Schematic T, K, V, H and S denote
compiler metanotation under R-LIB-0018. A signature containing such a metavariable
denotes only the closed family explicitly bounded by its rule.

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0004]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0004* — A by-value parameter follows R-FUNC-0002. A named Move value is
transferred with `+move+`; a temporary transfers implicitly; a Copy value is copied.
Every borrow in a synchronous operation is bounded by that call unless the result
explicitly carries its inferred origin. An asynchronous operation completes every
retain, clone or owned copy needed by its task before its successful start commit is
returned, so no call-bounded borrow enters the asynchronous frame, except that a scoped
operation (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012) holds its view as a task-group loan instead of copying it.

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0005]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0005* — A recoverable failure is a checked `+throws+` effect. An explicitly
listed closed outcome instead represents ordinary non-error alternatives. When retry is
meaningful after a by-value Move input has been staged, the checked error payload carries
that input. Such a payload carries the original ownership obligation and has structural
Send/Sync properties. Every
resource is released exactly once on success, error, cancellation, timeout, panic and
start failure.

Allocation needed for any asynchronous frame occurs before start commit and maps to
`+std.async::start_error::allocation_failed+`, or to `+budget_exhausted+` when a budget
refuses it (Core R-STMT-0020); the same applies to native submission state
or a required call-bounded-input copy of a standard asynchronous operation. After
successful start, only a standard asynchronous operation whose module declares
`+resource_exhausted+` uses that code for an implementation allocation or size failure
while materializing a data-dependent logical output, and only when no operation-specific
code applies. It reports the failure through its closed ownership-preserving outcome and
cleanup. Its `+native_code+` is zero for an R allocator failure or the immediately
captured native code for a native resource failure. This mapping applies to dynamic
filesystem outputs, DNS results and every analogous standard-module operation. Failures
inside a user async body remain determined by the operations that body calls.

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0006]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0006* — Standard safe operations panic only for a Core-language condition
that already panics, for a documented invariant violation proving a compiler or unsafe
boundary defect, or for destruction panic under the selected Core strategy. Size,
encoding, allocation, operating-system, network and process failures use their stated
recoverable outcomes.

[[R-SLIB-GEN-0007]]
*R-SLIB-GEN-0007* — Each module captures a native failure code before another foreign
call can replace it. Conversion to a portable error preserves that code in `+i64+` and
restores any C floating environment, locale view or runtime-global state changed by the
adapter before returning to safe R.

=== 6.2 Common portable error

[[R-SLIB-ERR-0001]]
*R-SLIB-ERR-0001* — `+std.error::domain+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+allocation+`, `+async_runtime+`, `+bytes+`, `+string+`, `+conversion+`, `+format+`, `+math+`,
`+time+`, `+environment+`, `+io+`, `+filesystem+`, `+network+`, `+process+`,
`+threading+` and `+c_abi+`.
`+std.error::error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields:

* `+std.error::domain domain+`;
* `+u32 code+`, the stable module-defined portable numeric code;
* `+i64 native_code+`, zero when no native code applies.

[[R-SLIB-ERR-0002]]
*R-SLIB-ERR-0002* — Exact operations are
`+std.error::name(std.error::error value) -> constexpr str+` and
`+std.error::diagnostic(std.error::error value) ->
std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`. Exact typed conversions are
`+std.error::from_alloc(std.alloc::alloc_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_async(std.async::start_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_bytes(std.bytes::bytes_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_string(std.string::string_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_boundary(std.string::boundary_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_parse(std.convert::parse_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_range(std.convert::range_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_format(std.format::format_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_duration(std.time::duration_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_path(std.fs::path_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_address(std.net::address_error value) -> std.error::error+`,
`+std.error::from_thread(std.thread::thread_error value) -> std.error::error+` and
`+std.error::from_barrier(std.sync::barrier_error value) -> std.error::error+`.
Each conversion uses its assigned domain and code and sets native code to zero.
`+from_async+`, `+from_path+` and `+from_address+` are supplied by the
hosted-native-async profile; `+from_thread+` and `+from_barrier+` are supplied by the
hosted-thread profile; the other operations are hosted.

Every typed source of these conversions is Copy. Conversion therefore leaves the typed
source available for inspection but deliberately does not carry an index or embedded
Copy `+std.alloc::alloc_error reason+` into the common error. In particular, an
ownership-producing operation's typed error retains that reason before and after an
explicit conversion; no generic ownership-bearing outcome or returned owner is itself
converted or consumed. Every conversion is non-allocating and cannot panic.

`+name+` returns, without allocation, the exact lowercase declared variant identifier
selected by domain and code; equal variant identifiers in different families may have
the same name. `+diagnostic+` creates an owned human-readable diagnostic identifying
the domain, family, variant name, numeric code and native code. Its language,
wording and inclusion of additional native text are diagnostic-only and shall not affect
program decisions.
For any code value not assigned within its domain, `+name+` returns the fixed
program-lifetime string `+"unknown"+`; `+diagnostic+` remains defined and identifies the
domain, numeric code and native code when allocation succeeds.

The primary family of every domain occupies the reserved range 0x0000..0x00ff;
its code is `+0x0000 + variant declaration index+`. The primary families are exactly
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+`, `+std.async::start_error+`, `+std.bytes::bytes_error+`,
`+std.string::string_error+`, `+std.convert::parse_error_code+`,
`+std.format::format_error+`, `+std.math::error_code+`, `+std.time::error_code+`,
`+std.env::error_code+`, `+std.io::error_code+`, `+std.fs::error_code+`,
`+std.net::error_code+`, `+std.process::error_code+`, `+std.thread::thread_error+` and
`+std.c::string_error+`, in their corresponding domains.

Each secondary pure or validation family occupies the disjoint reserved range
0x0100..0x01ff; its code is `+0x0100 + variant declaration index+`. The secondary families are
exactly `+std.string::boundary_error+`, `+std.convert::range_error+`,
`+std.time::duration_error+`, `+std.fs::path_error+`,
`+std.net::address_error_code+`, `+std.sync::barrier_error+` and
`+std.c::runtime_error+`, in their corresponding domains. These assignments include the
existing c_abi ranges: codes 0x0000..0x0003 are the `+std.c::string_error+` variants and
codes 0x0100..0x0102 are the `+std.c::runtime_error+` variants. Variant order is
declaration order, and no implementation may renumber an assigned family.

At the generated main boundary (R-FUNC-0008), `+core::utf8_error+` maps to
`+string+`, code zero (`+invalid_utf8+`), native code zero. `+std.bits::read_error+`
maps to `+bytes+`, code `+0x0100 + variant declaration index+`, native code zero;
`+name+` returns `+unexpected_end+` or `+invalid_width+` for those two assigned codes.
Only scalar diagnostic indexes are omitted. These mappings allocate nothing and add no
owner-bearing family to the implicit main set.

[[R-SLIB-ERR-0003]]
*R-SLIB-ERR-0003* — Each native-facing error struct explicitly declared with a typed
`+code+` and `+i64 native_code+` in this document is Copy and Send+Sync and has an exact
`+as_error+` operation returning the matching `+std.error::error+`. It uses the primary
0x0000 range and copies `+native_code+` exactly. The numeric value for each fieldless
code variant is stable for R 0.1 and is its zero-based declaration order added to that
base. A native indication that matches no more specific semantic classification maps to
the module's `+other+` variant while remaining available through `+native_code+`, when
that code enum declares `+other+`; this includes both documented known statuses with no
narrower meaning and unknown target codes. A code family
without `+other+` is produced only by its exhaustively specified semantic conditions and
does not turn an otherwise unclassified host indication into an error. Every other exact
conversion sets `+native_code+` to zero and discards only typed diagnostic fields as
specified by R-SLIB-ERR-0002. A program builds such a struct, `+std.error::error+` and
`+std.bits::read_error+` by braced initialization of their public fields like a struct of
its own (Core R-INIT-0004, R-AGG-0007), for example to report a failure of a stream it
implements (R-SLIB-STREAM-0001).

[[R-SLIB-ERR-0004]]
*R-SLIB-ERR-0004* — `+std.error::fault+` is the common root of the standard errors (Core
R-AGG-0011). It is an error without fields that is never constructed or thrown itself; its
direct descendants are exactly the checked errors of the implicit set of the generated
main boundary (Core R-FUNC-0008), and the hosted profile supplies it. A value of type
`+std.error::fault+` holds one of those errors: it is Copy and Send+Sync,
`+throws std.error::fault+` names all of them, `+catch (std.error::fault failure)+`
receives any of them and a rethrow keeps the held error (Core R-ERR-0002).
`+std.error::from_fault(std.error::fault value) -> std.error::error+` returns the portable
error of the held error with the domain, code and native code that the generated main
boundary reports for it (R-SLIB-ERR-0002); it allocates nothing and cannot panic.

=== 6.3 Allocation

[[R-SLIB-ALLOC-0001]]
*R-SLIB-ALLOC-0001* — `+std.alloc::new_error<T>+` is a Move-only checked-error payload with
public fields `+std.alloc::alloc_error reason+` and `+T value+`. Exact typed allocation
operations are `+std.alloc::try_new(T value) -> own T* throws std.alloc::new_error<T>+` and
`+std.alloc::into_value(own T* object) -> T+`. Successful `+try_new+` allocates suitably
aligned storage, constructs exactly one T and transfers its unique owner. Failure
constructs no T in allocated storage and throws `+new_error<T>+` carrying the staged value. `+into_value+` moves
T out and releases its allocation without invoking T drop there.
The compiler-recognized closed family selects T from the value or owning-pointer operand
without user generics or overload search; `+new_error<T>+` derives Send/Sync structurally
from T.

[[R-SLIB-ALLOC-0002]]
*R-SLIB-ALLOC-0002* — Exact owned-byte operation
`+std.alloc::bytes(usize length, u8 fill) ->
array<u8> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` returns an array of exactly length initialized
bytes, each equal to fill. Zero length performs no allocation. Size overflow and target
allocation failure throw the exact R-LIB-0004 error variant and expose no partial
array.

[[R-SLIB-ALLOC-0003]]
*R-SLIB-ALLOC-0003* — The R part of `+std.alloc+`, loaded by `+import std.alloc;+`, declares
the limits of a budget block (Core R-STMT-0020): `+std.alloc::limits+` is a struct with the
fields `+o<usize> bytes = o::none+`, the most bytes that the allocations charged to the
budget hold at once, and `+o<usize> tasks = o::none+`, the most tasks that count under it at
once, where none sets no limit of that kind. A budget charges the size that an allocation
requests, and an allocation of zero bytes charges nothing.

[[R-SLIB-ALLOC-0004]]
*R-SLIB-ALLOC-0004* — In the `+hosted-native-async+` profile the R part of `+std.alloc+` reports
the budget of the calling task: `+std.alloc::usage+` is a Copy struct with the public fields
`+usize bytes+` and `+usize tasks+`, the bytes and tasks charged to the innermost budget now,
`+o<usize> byte_limit+` and `+o<usize> task_limit+`, its limits, and
`+o<usize> bytes_available+`, the bytes that it and every enclosing budget still admit, none
where no limit applies. `+std.alloc::budget_usage() -> o<std.alloc::usage>+` returns the usage of the
budget under which the calling code runs, or none outside every budget block. It reads the
counters of the runtime through the native provider `+std.alloc.native+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0003)
and allocates nothing.

[[bytes]]
== 7. Bytes, hashing, UTF-8 and bit streams

=== 7.1 Owned bytes and byte operations

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0001]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0001* — `+std.bytes::bytes_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+out_of_bounds+` and `+range_overflow+`. The exact operations are:

* `+std.bytes::copy(u8[] destination, const u8[] source) ->
  usize throws std.bytes::bytes_error+`;
* `+std.bytes::copy_within(u8[] buffer, usize destination, usize source,
  usize length) -> usize throws std.bytes::bytes_error+`;
* `+std.bytes::fill(u8[] destination, u8 value) -> void+`;
* `+std.bytes::compare(const u8[] left, const u8[] right) -> i32+`;
* `+std.bytes::equal(const u8[] left, const u8[] right) -> bool+`;
* `+std.bytes::find(const u8[] source, u8 value) -> o<usize>+`;
* `+std.bytes::find_slice(const u8[] source, const u8[] pattern) -> o<usize>+`;
* `+std.bytes::starts_with(const u8[] source, const u8[] prefix) -> bool+`;
* `+std.bytes::ends_with(const u8[] source, const u8[] suffix) -> bool+`.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0002]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0002* — `+copy+` copies exactly `+len(source)+` bytes between the distinct
borrow regions admitted by Core and returns that length. `+copy_within+` copies length
bytes between the two ranges of one buffer and defines every overlap as if all source
bytes were preserved before a destination byte replaced them. `+copy+` reports
`+out_of_bounds+` exactly when destination is shorter than source. `+copy_within+`
first checks `+destination + length+` for overflow, then `+source + length+`; the first
failed checked addition reports `+range_overflow+`. Only after both additions succeed it
checks the destination range against buffer length and then the source range; the first
range exceeding the buffer reports `+out_of_bounds+`. Thus destination has priority
over source within each phase. Every complete validation precedes writing, and an error
performs no write. `+compare+`
returns exactly -1, 0 or 1 according to unsigned lexicographic byte order. An empty
pattern is found at index zero. `+equal+` is true exactly when the lengths and every
corresponding byte are equal. `+find+` and `+find_slice+` return `+o::some+` of the
lowest matching byte index and return `+o::none+` when no match exists. `+fill+`
replaces every destination element with value, including none when the destination is
empty. `+starts_with+` is true exactly when prefix length does not exceed source length
and every prefix byte equals the corresponding initial source byte; `+ends_with+` uses
the corresponding final bytes. An empty prefix and an empty suffix therefore each
produce true. These operations allocate nothing and do not panic.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0003]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0003* — The exact owned-byte construction and append operations are:

* `+std.bytes::with_capacity(usize capacity) ->
  bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.bytes::append(bytes* target, const u8[] source) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.bytes::append_u8(bytes* target, u8 value) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.bytes::append_u16_le(bytes* target, u16 value) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.bytes::append_u32_le(bytes* target, u32 value) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.bytes::append_u64_le(bytes* target, u64 value) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

The `+bytes+` spelling is the exact transparent `+array<u8>+` alias defined by Core
R-TYPE-0030, not a second owner type. There is no callable empty constructor in this
module: `+bytes value = {};+` is the canonical allocation-free empty construction.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0004]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0004* — `+with_capacity+` constructs an empty value with capacity at
least the requested value; zero capacity performs no allocation. `+append+` preserves
the existing prefix and appends exactly the source bytes in order. `+append_u8+`
appends one byte. Each `+append_uN_le+` appends exactly N/8 bytes, least-significant
byte first, including leading zero bytes. An empty append succeeds without allocation
or mutation.

Every required length and capacity calculation is checked under R-LIB-0004 before
allocation. On any `+size_overflow+`, `+unsupported_alignment+` or `+out_of_memory+`
result, the target retains its exact length, capacity, allocation identity and byte
contents. On success, growth may relocate its storage, so all prior borrows follow the
ordinary `+array<u8>+` structural-mutation rules. The operations have no failure other
than the stated `+alloc_error+`, do not panic, and use the capacity policy selected for
`+std.array+` by R-SLIB-IDB-0015. Because the alias is transparent, the complete
`+std.array+` observation and mutation API for `+array<u8>+` also applies directly to a
`+bytes+` value without conversion.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0009]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0009* — `+import std.bytes;+` loads the R part of `+std.bytes+`
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0001) in every profile that provides the module. `+std.bytes::cursor+` is a Copy struct with the
public field `+usize position+`; `+std.bytes::cursor {}+` is the cursor at position zero.
Its methods `+read_u8+`, `+read_u16_le+`, `+read_u16_be+`, `+read_u32_le+`,
`+read_u32_be+`, `+read_u64_le+` and `+read_u64_be+`, each
`+(cursor* this, const u8[] source) -> uN throws std.bytes::bytes_error+`, return the
unsigned integer whose bytes follow the position in source in little-endian (`+_le+`) or
big-endian (`+_be+`) order, and `+write_u8+`, `+write_u16_le+`, `+write_u16_be+`,
`+write_u32_le+`, `+write_u32_be+`, `+write_u64_le+` and `+write_u64_be+`, each
`+(cursor* this, u8[] target, uN value) -> void throws std.bytes::bytes_error+`, store its
bytes there in that order; each then moves the position past those bytes. When fewer bytes
follow the position, the operation reports `+out_of_bounds+` and changes neither the
position nor a byte. None allocates or panics.

=== 7.2 `std.hash`

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0005]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0005* — The independent `+std.hash+` module exports this exact legacy digest type and operation:

[source,r]
----
struct md5_digest {
    u8[16] bytes;
};
----

* `+std.hash::md5(const u8[] source) -> std.hash::md5_digest+`.

The digest structure and its fixed byte field are Copy and Send+Sync, have no user drop,
