and are returned by value. The field holds exactly the 16 digest bytes, is not a string
and has no terminator.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0006]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0006* — The module additionally exports these exact digest types and
operations:

[source,r]
----
struct sha1_digest {
    u8[20] bytes;
};

struct sha256_digest {
    u8[32] bytes;
};

struct sha512_digest {
    u8[64] bytes;
};
----

* `+std.hash::crc32(const u8[] source) -> u32+`;
* `+std.hash::sha1(const u8[] source) -> std.hash::sha1_digest+`;
* `+std.hash::sha256(const u8[] source) -> std.hash::sha256_digest+`;
* `+std.hash::sha512(const u8[] source) -> std.hash::sha512_digest+`.

Each digest structure and its fixed byte field are Copy and Send+Sync, have no user
drop, and are returned by value. The field holds exactly the digest bytes; it is not a
string and has no terminator. The field spelling `+bytes+` remains legal because Core
classifies that spelling as a predeclared type only in type position.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0007]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0007* — `+crc32+` implements CRC-32/ISO-HDLC with width 32, polynomial
`+0x04c11db7+`, reflected polynomial `+0xedb88320+`, initial register
`+0xffffffff+`, reflected input and output, and final XOR `+0xffffffff+`. The empty
input produces zero and the ASCII bytes `+"123456789"+` produce `+0xcbf43926+`.

`+md5+` implements exactly MD5 as defined by RFC 1321. Its digest field stores the
four result words in RFC display order, least-significant byte first within each word.
The empty input produces the bytes represented by hexadecimal
`+d41d8cd98f00b204e9800998ecf8427e+`, and the ASCII bytes `+"abc"+` produce
`+900150983cd24fb0d6963f7d28e17f72+`.

`+sha1+`, `+sha256+` and `+sha512+` implement exactly SHA-1, SHA-256 and SHA-512,
respectively, as defined by FIPS PUB 180-4. A digest field stores the standard result in
display order: the most-significant byte of the first result word is at index zero, with
the remaining words following in their specified order. These five operations process
every source byte including embedded zero bytes, retain no source borrow after return,
allocate nothing, mutate no state, do not panic and are safe for concurrent calls.

The `+const u8[]+` parameter is a non-owning view. The contextual conversions of
Core R-EXPR-0027 accept slices, fixed arrays, `+array<u8>+`/`+bytes+` owners and
strings without copying or moving source data. `+std.hash+` has no dependency on
`+std.bytes+` or its allocator API.

Every profile providing these operations shall ensure that the maximum length of a live
byte slice accepted by `+md5+`, `+sha1+` or `+sha256+` is at most
2305843009213693951 bytes, so
its bit length is strictly less than 2^64 as required by RFC 1321 and FIPS PUB 180-4. That limit
shall be reflected in the applicable R-IDB-010 and R-IDB-012 choices; it is not a
runtime failure of any operation. The SHA-512 2^128-bit message-length domain is
strictly larger than any slice representable by an R 0.1 `+usize+`.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0008]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0008* — CRC-32 detects accidental corruption only. It provides no
collision resistance, preimage resistance, authentication or protection from an
adversary. `+md5+` and `+sha1+` are supplied solely for compatibility with legacy
formats and protocols. New security-sensitive code shall use neither: practical
collision attacks invalidate both algorithms for collision-resistant purposes, and MD5
also lacks adequate preimage-security margin for new designs. SHA-256 and SHA-512 are
cryptographic hash functions, but an unkeyed digest alone does not authenticate data.
Authentication requires a protocol-defined MAC or signature facility outside this API.
None of these operations claims constant-time behavior with respect to input length,
and none handles secret keys or erases caller-owned input.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0010]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0010* — `+import std.hash;+` loads the R part of `+std.hash+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)
in every profile that provides the module. `+std.hash::sha256_state+` and
`+std.hash::sha512_state+` are Copy, Send+Sync structs with protected state that compute
SHA-256 and SHA-512 over data given in pieces: `+sha256_state::create() ->
std.hash::sha256_state+` starts one, `+update(sha256_state* this, const u8[] data) -> void+`
appends data, and `+finish(sha256_state this) -> std.hash::sha256_digest+` returns the digest
of everything appended, equal to `+std.hash::sha256+` of the concatenation; `+sha512_state+`
has the same three operations with `+std.hash::sha512_digest+`. A copy of a state continues
on its own, so a common prefix is hashed once. `+std.hash::sha384_state+` is the same for
SHA-384 (FIPS PUB 180-4 section 6.5): SHA-512 from the initial words of SHA-384 whose
`+finish(sha384_state this) -> u8[48]+` returns the first 48 bytes of its output, and
`+std.hash::sha384(const u8[] data) -> u8[48]+` hashes one piece.

[[R-SLIB-BYTES-0011]]
*R-SLIB-BYTES-0011* — `+std.hash::hmac_sha256(const u8[] key, const u8[] message) ->
std.hash::sha256_digest+` and `+std.hash::hmac_sha512(const u8[] key, const u8[] message) ->
std.hash::sha512_digest+` compute HMAC of RFC 2104 with SHA-256 and SHA-512, and
`+std.hash::hmac_sha384(const u8[] key, const u8[] message) -> u8[48]+` with SHA-384: a key longer
than the block of 64 or 128 bytes is hashed first, and the key padded with zeros to the block is
combined with the byte 0x36 for the inner hash and with 0x5c for the outer one. The padded
keys are erased with `+std.secret::zeroize+` before the result returns; a caller compares a
received code with `+std.secret::constant_time_equal+`. Neither operation allocates, fails or
panics.

=== 7.3 UTF-8 validation

[[R-SLIB-UTF8-0001]]
*R-SLIB-UTF8-0001* — The `+std.utf8+` module exports exactly these operations:

* `+std.utf8::is_valid(const u8[] source) -> bool+`;
* `+std.utf8::validate(const u8[] source) -> str throws core::utf8_error+`.

These are single exact signatures over Core's canonical read-only byte view. They are
not overload families and introduce no string-view or byte-view wrapper type. At a
direct call, every source admitted by Core R-EXPR-0027 is converted contextually to that
one parameter type without allocation or copying.

[[R-SLIB-UTF8-0002]]
*R-SLIB-UTF8-0002* — `+validate+` is an exact public forwarding operation to
`+core::validate_utf8+`: it has the same RFC 3629 validation, first-invalid-byte index,
returned `+str+` origin, borrow duration and source-freezing behavior required by Core
R-ARRAY-0007. When its argument view was formed contextually, the successful result
retains the provenance and inferred borrow origin of the original string, owner or fixed
array rather than the temporary slice descriptor. `+is_valid+` is true exactly when
that validation returns and false exactly when it would throw; it creates no `+str+`
whose borrow escapes the call. Both operations
allocate nothing, mutate nothing, retain no hidden source reference and do not panic.

=== 7.4 LSB-first bit reading

[[R-SLIB-BITS-0001]]
*R-SLIB-BITS-0001* — The `+std.bits+` module exports these exact Copy, Send+Sync types:

[source,r]
----
enum read_error_code {
    unexpected_end,
    invalid_width,
};

error read_error {
    read_error_code code;
    usize byte_index;
};

struct lsb_reader {
    usize byte_index;
    u64 hold;
    u8 bit_count;
};
----

The numeric value of each `+std.bits::read_error_code+` variant is its zero-based
declaration index. `+std.bits::read_error.byte_index+` identifies the source-byte
position specified by R-SLIB-BITS-0003. `+std.bits::read_error_code+`,
`+std.bits::read_error+` and `+std.bits::lsb_reader+` have no user drop. The
allocation-free zero initializer
`+std.bits::lsb_reader reader = {};+` is the initial state; the module defines no
callable constructor.

[[R-SLIB-BITS-0002]]
*R-SLIB-BITS-0002* — The exact operations are:

* `+std.bits::read(const u8[] source, std.bits::lsb_reader* reader, u8 width) ->
  u64 throws std.bits::read_error+`;
* `+std.bits::align_byte(std.bits::lsb_reader* reader) -> void+`.

For a reader whose `+bit_count+` is at most 64, the low `+bit_count+` bits of `+hold+`
are the buffered bits in next-to-read order, least-significant bit first; higher bits of
`+hold+` have no meaning. `+byte_index+` is the index of the next source byte after all
buffered bytes. The zero state therefore starts at source byte zero with no buffered
bits. A reader is not bound to an owner or slice and retains no source borrow between
calls; the caller shall pass the same byte sequence, or intentionally accepts that the
state indexes the newly supplied sequence.

[[R-SLIB-BITS-0003]]
*R-SLIB-BITS-0003* — `+read+` accepts width 0 through 64 inclusive. Conceptually it
forms a bit stream from the buffered low bits followed by each source byte beginning at
`+byte_index+`, taking bit 0 through bit 7 of every byte. On success, result bit i equals
stream bit i for every `+i < width+`; all higher result bits are zero. It consumes
exactly width stream bits and updates the three reader fields to the remaining buffered
bits and the first not-yet-buffered source byte. When the incoming bit count is valid,
width zero returns zero and leaves the reader unchanged. No generated or library C
operation shall shift a C integer by its width while implementing the 64-bit case.

`+invalid_width+` is selected first when width exceeds 64 or the incoming
`+reader.bit_count+` exceeds 64; its `+byte_index+` is the incoming
`+reader.byte_index+`. Otherwise `+unexpected_end+` is selected when the buffered bits
plus the available source bytes cannot supply width bits. Its `+byte_index+` is the
first byte index that the operation required but the slice did not contain. Every error
leaves `+byte_index+`, `+hold+` and `+bit_count+` bit-for-bit unchanged.

[[R-SLIB-BITS-0004]]
*R-SLIB-BITS-0004* — For an incoming bit count at most 64, `+align_byte+` discards
exactly `+reader.bit_count % 8+` low buffered bits and preserves any remaining complete
buffered bytes in their next-to-read order. For every u8 bit count, it shifts `+hold+`
right by that modulo-8 discard, subtracts the discard from `+bit_count+` and does not
change `+byte_index+`; those operations are always defined. Thus alignment may reduce
values 65 through 71 to 64, and `+read+` rejects exactly any state whose resulting count
still exceeds 64. Both operations allocate nothing, retain nothing, do not panic and
perform no access outside the supplied slice. Their only recoverable
failures and failure priority are those stated in R-SLIB-BITS-0003.

[[secret]]
=== 7.5 Secret byte buffers

[[R-SLIB-SECRET-0001]]
*R-SLIB-SECRET-0001* — `+std.secret::buffer+` is a Move-only, Send and Sync owner of one
contiguous byte allocation whose contents are erased before the storage is released. It has
no clone or copy operation and no interior mutability: shared access only reads it, so a key
that holds one may live in state that tasks and threads share. The exact operations are:

* `+std.secret::with_length(usize length) ->
  std.secret::buffer throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.secret::from_bytes(bytes source) -> std.secret::buffer+`;
* `+std.secret::len(const std.secret::buffer* source) -> usize+`;
* `+std.secret::as_slice(const std.secret::buffer* source) -> const u8[]+`;
* `+std.secret::as_slice_mut(std.secret::buffer* source) -> u8[]+`;
* `+std.secret::zeroize(u8[] target) -> void+`;
* `+std.secret::constant_time_equal(const u8[] left, const u8[] right) -> bool+`.

[[R-SLIB-SECRET-0002]]
*R-SLIB-SECRET-0002* — `+with_length+` constructs a buffer of exactly length zero-valued
bytes; zero length performs no allocation. The required size calculation is checked under
R-LIB-0004 before allocation. Its only failure is the stated `+alloc_error+`, after which no
allocation is retained. `+from_bytes+` adopts the allocation, length and capacity of source
without copying or reallocating and leaves no second owner; the consumed source is not
erased separately because its storage becomes the buffer storage. `+len+` returns the exact
byte length. `+as_slice+` and `+as_slice_mut+` return call-bounded views of exactly `+len+`
bytes carrying the buffer borrow origin. Neither view allocates, and no operation of this
module retains a borrow beyond its call or panics.

[[R-SLIB-SECRET-0003]]
*R-SLIB-SECRET-0003* — Dropping a buffer first overwrites every byte of its allocation,
including the bytes beyond the length up to the capacity, with zero through volatile stores
that the implementation shall not elide or reorder past the release, and then releases the
storage. `+zeroize+` overwrites every target byte with zero through the same volatile
stores, including none for an empty target, allocates nothing and does not panic. A buffer
whose storage has been moved out by Core move semantics owns nothing, and its drop erases
nothing.

[[R-SLIB-SECRET-0004]]
*R-SLIB-SECRET-0004* — `+constant_time_equal+` returns true exactly when the lengths and
every corresponding byte are equal. When the lengths are equal it examines every byte of
both slices and accumulates the comparison without a data-dependent branch or early return,
so the executed instruction sequence does not depend on the byte contents. When the lengths
differ it returns false after comparing the lengths only. It allocates nothing, retains
nothing and does not panic.

=== 7.6 Randomness: `+std.random+`

[[R-SLIB-RANDOM-0001]]
*R-SLIB-RANDOM-0001* — `+std.random+` is a `+hosted+` module.
`+std.random::fill(u8[] target) -> void+` fills target with bytes of the cryptographically
secure generator of the operating system, so that they serve as keys, nonces and identifiers.
It allocates nothing, cannot fail, does not panic, does not wait for entropy and may run on
any thread and inside an async function; an empty target stays unchanged. `+import
std.random;+` also loads the R part of the module (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001), whose functions draw
from `+fill+`:

* `+std.random::next_u64() -> u64+` and `+std.random::next_u32() -> u32+`, the
  little-endian value of eight or four bytes;
* `+std.random::below(u64 bound) -> u64+`, uniform in `+[0, bound)+`: it draws values x
  until x is at least (2^64 − bound) mod bound and returns x mod bound, so that no remainder
  is favoured;
* `+std.random::range(u64 low, u64 high) -> u64+`, low plus `+below(high - low)+`, uniform
  in `+[low, high)+`.

A zero bound and an empty range panic through the Core arithmetic of the computation.

[[R-SLIB-RANDOM-0002]]
*R-SLIB-RANDOM-0002* — `+std.random::generator+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with protected
state that produces the reproducible pseudo-random sequence of xoshiro256** 1.0 (Blackman and
Vigna) over four `+u64+` words s0..s3; a copy continues the same sequence on its own.
`+std.random::generator::seeded(u64 seed) -> std.random::generator+` takes the words from
four consecutive outputs of SplitMix64 started at seed: each output adds
`+0x9e3779b97f4a7c15+` to the running value x and returns z ^ (z >> 31), where
y = (x ^ (x >> 30)) · `+0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9+` and z = (y ^ (y >> 27)) ·
`+0x94d049bb133111eb+` in arithmetic modulo 2^64.
`+std.random::generator::from_entropy() -> std.random::generator+` takes the words from
`+fill+` and draws again while all four are zero. The methods are:

* `+next_u64(generator* this) -> u64+`, the value rotl(s1 · 5, 7) · 9, after which
  t = s1 << 17, s2 ^= s0, s3 ^= s1, s1 ^= s2, s0 ^= s3, s2 ^= t and s3 = rotl(s3, 45);
* `+next_u32(generator* this) -> u32+`, the upper 32 bits of `+next_u64+`;
* `+below(generator* this, u64 bound) -> u64+` and
  `+range(generator* this, u64 low, u64 high) -> u64+`, as in R-SLIB-RANDOM-0001 over
  `+next_u64+`;
* `+fill(generator* this, u8[] target) -> void+`, the little-endian bytes of successive
  `+next_u64+` values, the last one cut to the remaining length;
* `+shuffle(generator* this, T[] items) -> void+` for each element type that
  `+std.slice::swap+` accepts: for each index i from the last down to 1 it exchanges
  items[i] with items[`+below(i + 1)+`].

The values depend only on the seed and the calls, so every implementation produces the same
sequence. A generator is predictable: it serves tests, simulations and sampling, never keys.

[[strings]]
== 8. Owned UTF-8 strings

[[R-SLIB-STRING-0001]]
*R-SLIB-STRING-0001* — `+std.string::string+` is a Move-only owner of a contiguous
well-formed UTF-8 byte sequence. It is Send+Sync, contains no interior NUL restriction,
and has the same scalar-boundary interpretation as Core `+str+`. Its exact constructors
are:

* `+std.string::create() -> std.string::string+`;
* `+std.string::with_capacity(usize capacity) ->
  std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.string::from_str(str source) ->
  std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.string::from_utf8(const u8[] source) ->
  std.string::string throws std.string::string_error+`;
* `+std.string::from_bytes(array<u8> source) ->
  std.string::from_bytes_result+`.

`+string_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync tagged type with exactly
`+invalid_utf8 { usize index; }+` and
`+allocation_failed { std.alloc::alloc_error reason; }+`. `+from_bytes_result+` is Move-only,
Send+Sync and has exactly
`+valid(std.string::string)+` and `+invalid { array<u8> bytes; usize index; }+`.
`+from_utf8+` validates the complete source before allocation: it reports
`+invalid_utf8+` at the first invalid byte, so allocation pressure cannot mask invalid
input. `+from_bytes+` validates without allocation. Its `+valid+` outcome transfers the
same allocation and byte sequence into the string; its `+invalid+` outcome returns the
same array owner with byte-identical contents and the first invalid-byte index. It has no
recoverable allocation failure and does not panic.
`+create+` produces length and capacity zero. `+with_capacity+` produces an empty string
with capacity at least the requested value. `+from_str+` and a successful `+from_utf8+`
produce an owned byte-for-byte copy of the complete source, with the same length; an
empty source requires no allocation.

[[R-SLIB-STRING-0002]]
*R-SLIB-STRING-0002* — Exact observation and mutation operations are:

* `+std.string::len(const std.string::string* source) -> usize+` and
  `+std.string::capacity(const std.string::string* source) -> usize+`;
* `+std.string::as_str(const std.string::string* source) -> str+` and
  `+std.string::as_bytes(const std.string::string* source) -> const u8[]+`;
* `+std.string::reserve(std.string::string* target, usize additional) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.string::append_str(std.string::string* target, str suffix) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.string::append_utf8(std.string::string* target, const u8[] suffix) ->
  void throws std.string::string_error+`;
* `+std.string::push_scalar(std.string::string* target, char value) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.string::truncate(std.string::string* target, usize byte_length) ->
  void throws std.string::boundary_error+`;
* `+std.string::clear(std.string::string* target) -> void+`;
* `+std.string::into_bytes(std.string::string source) -> array<u8>+`.

`+boundary_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+out_of_bounds+` and `+not_scalar_boundary+`.

[[R-SLIB-STRING-0003]]
*R-SLIB-STRING-0003* — Empty construction performs no allocation. Capacity is at least
length. `+len+` is the current byte length. `+as_str+` and `+as_bytes+` expose the exact
complete current byte sequence with a borrow origin in the string. `+reserve+` checks
`+len(target) + additional+`, reports `+size_overflow+` if that addition or capacity is
unrepresentable, and on success guarantees at least that capacity without changing any
byte. `+append_str+` and successful `+append_utf8+` append exactly the suffix bytes after
the prior bytes; `+push_scalar+` appends exactly the canonical UTF-8 encoding of value.
Reserve and append operations are transactional: failure leaves target unchanged.
`+append_utf8+` validates its entire suffix
before allocation or mutation and reports the first invalid byte, so only a valid suffix
can produce `+allocation_failed+`. `+truncate+` first reports `+out_of_bounds+` when
byte_length exceeds current length; otherwise it reports `+not_scalar_boundary+` when
that index is not a UTF-8 scalar boundary. Either error preserves bytes and capacity.
Success retains exactly the prefix of byte_length bytes and the existing capacity.
Every operation that can relocate storage requires all earlier borrows of
that string to end first. `+clear+` sets length to zero, leaves the valid empty UTF-8
sequence, and retains the current storage and capacity; it allocates nothing and cannot
fail. `+into_bytes+` transfers the existing allocation and exact byte sequence without
allocation.

[[R-SLIB-STRING-0004]]
*R-SLIB-STRING-0004* — `+import std.string;+` loads the R part of `+std.string+`
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0001):

* `+std.string::replace_range(std.string::string* target, usize start, usize end, str text) -> void throws std.string::boundary_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
  replaces the bytes from start to end with text;
* `+std.string::insert_str(std.string::string* target, usize index, str text) -> void throws std.string::boundary_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
  inserts text at index.

An end beyond the length or a start after the end reports `+out_of_bounds+`, and an index
inside a scalar reports `+not_scalar_boundary+`; these checks precede allocation, and every
failure leaves target unchanged. Success may relocate the storage (R-SLIB-STRING-0003).

[[conversion]]
== 9. Numeric conversion and parsing

[[R-SLIB-CONV-0001]]
*R-SLIB-CONV-0001* — `+std.convert::parse_error_code+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum
with exactly `+empty+`, `+invalid_radix+`, `+invalid_digit+`, `+trailing_character+`,
`+below_minimum+` and `+above_maximum+`.
`+std.convert::parse_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.convert::parse_error_code code+` and `+usize index+`. A program builds one by braced
initialization of these fields, as the structs of R-SLIB-ERR-0003, for example to report a
failure of a parser of its own (R-SLIB-ENCODING-0002).

[[R-SLIB-CONV-0002]]
*R-SLIB-CONV-0002* — For each suffix in
`+i8,u8,i16,u16,i32,u32,i64,u64,isize,usize+` and each available suffix among
`+c_char,c_schar,c_uchar,c_short,c_ushort,c_int,c_uint,c_long,c_ulong,c_llong,c_ullong,c_bool,c_wchar,c_wint,c_int8,c_uint8,c_int16,c_uint16,c_int32,c_uint32,c_int64,c_uint64,c_intptr,c_uintptr,c_intmax,c_uintmax,c_size,c_ptrdiff+`,
`+std.convert+` provides the exact operation
`+parse_SUFFIX(str source, u32 radix) -> SUFFIX throws std.convert::parse_error+`.
Radix is 0 or 2 through 36. Radix zero recognizes `+0b+`, `+0o+`, `+0x+` and otherwise
uses decimal. Parsing consumes the entire source. One leading ASCII U+002B PLUS SIGN is
permitted for every listed destination; one leading ASCII U+002D HYPHEN-MINUS is
permitted exactly when the destination range contains negative values, so `+c_char+`
and `+c_wchar+` follow their manifest signedness. A disallowed sign is
`+invalid_digit+` at its sign byte. Parsing reports the byte index of the first byte
establishing failure. Digits are ASCII `+0..9+` and case-insensitive `+a..z+` up to the radix; input
contains at least one digit and contains no whitespace, separator, suffix or trailing
byte. It uses no locale and allocates nothing.
Error selection is deterministic. Radix is validated first; `+invalid_radix+` has index
zero. An empty source then yields `+empty+` at index zero. A permitted sign or recognized
base prefix not followed by a digit
yields `+invalid_digit+` at the first offending byte or at `+len(source)+` when the digit
is missing at end. While consuming the token, an ASCII alphanumeric digit whose value is
not below radix yields `+invalid_digit+` at that byte; any other byte after at least one
valid digit yields `+trailing_character+` there, and before the first valid digit yields
`+invalid_digit+`. Only after complete lexical success is range tested; `+below_minimum+`
or `+above_maximum+` then has index `+len(source)+`.

[[R-SLIB-CONV-0003]]
*R-SLIB-CONV-0003* — Exact floating parsers are
`+std.convert::parse_f32(str source) -> f32 throws std.convert::parse_error+` and
`+std.convert::parse_f64(str source) -> f64 throws std.convert::parse_error+`; hosted targets
additionally provide `+std.convert::parse_c_float(str source) ->
c_float throws std.convert::parse_error+`, `+std.convert::parse_c_double(str source) ->
c_double throws std.convert::parse_error+` and, when available,
`+std.convert::parse_c_long_double(str source) ->
c_long_double throws std.convert::parse_error+`. They accept the
ASCII decimal form with optional leading sign, optional point and optional signed
decimal exponent, plus lowercase `+inf+`, `+-inf+` and `+nan+`; at least one mantissa
digit is required and no whitespace, separator, suffix or trailing byte is accepted.
They consume the entire source, round according to the destination format, allocate
nothing, and do not depend on the active C locale. A result outside the finite destination range reports
`+below_minimum+` or `+above_maximum+`; an accepted explicit infinity or NaN succeeds.
For these parsers, empty source yields `+empty+` at index zero. Before a complete
mantissa or special token, the first grammar violation or missing required byte yields
`+invalid_digit+` at the offending byte or `+len(source)+`; after a complete token, the
first extra byte yields `+trailing_character+`. Range is tested only after lexical
success and reports index `+len(source)+`.

[[R-SLIB-CONV-0004]]
*R-SLIB-CONV-0004* — `+std.convert::range_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+below_minimum+`, `+above_maximum+`, `+not_finite+`, `+fractional+` and
`+inexact+`. For every
numeric source type S and every numeric suffix D listed by R-SLIB-CONV-0002 or
R-SLIB-CONV-0003, the compiler-recognized closed family provides
`+std.convert::checked_D(S value) -> D throws std.convert::range_error+`. It succeeds exactly
when the mathematical value is exactly representable by D. For an integer destination,
the fixed precedence is nonfinite `+not_finite+`, below/above range, then
`+fractional+`. For a floating destination, a supported infinity or NaN class succeeds;
a finite value outside its finite range reports below/above range, and an in-range value
that would require rounding reports `+inexact+`. Thus integer-to-float and float narrowing
also require exact representability. This family is not user overloading and allocates
nothing.

[[formatting]]
== 10. Typed formatting

[[R-SLIB-FMT-0001]]
*R-SLIB-FMT-0001* — `+std.format::builder+` is a Move-only, Send+Sync owner of a valid
UTF-8 output sequence. Exact lifetime operations are
`+std.format::create() -> std.format::builder+`,
`+std.format::with_capacity(usize capacity) ->
std.format::builder throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`,
`+std.format::as_str(const std.format::builder* source) -> str+`,
`+std.format::clear(std.format::builder* target) -> void+` and
`+std.format::finish(std.format::builder source) -> std.string::string+`.

[[R-SLIB-FMT-0002]]
*R-SLIB-FMT-0002* — Exact append operations are:

* `+std.format::append_str(std.format::builder* target, str value) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.format::append_char(std.format::builder* target, char value) ->
  void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* for every integer suffix of R-SLIB-CONV-0002,
  `+std.format::append_SUFFIX(std.format::builder* target, SUFFIX value, u32 radix) ->
  void throws std.format::format_error+`;
* `+std.format::append_f32(std.format::builder* target, f32 value) ->
  void throws std.format::format_error+`;
* `+std.format::append_f64(std.format::builder* target, f64 value) ->
  void throws std.format::format_error+`;
* `+std.format::append_c_float(std.format::builder* target, c_float value) ->
  void throws std.format::format_error+`;
* `+std.format::append_c_double(std.format::builder* target, c_double value) ->
  void throws std.format::format_error+`;
* when `+c_long_double+` is available,
  `+std.format::append_c_long_double(std.format::builder* target, c_long_double value) ->
  void throws std.format::format_error+`.

`+std.format::format_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync tagged type with exactly
`+invalid_radix+` and `+allocation_failed { std.alloc::alloc_error reason; }+`.

The append signatures above additionally form `+std.format::append+` overloads selected
by R-FUNC-0004. Their receiver method is `+builder.append(value)+`, or
`+builder.append(value, radix)+` for integers. `+std.string::append+` similarly selects
string-view append or scalar append. Numeric format overloads retain format_error;
text and character overloads retain alloc_error. Owning strings use their explicit
borrowed view when passed as value arguments. The real and complex typed math operations
also expose the name without its numeric suffix, selected by the same rule. Core checked,
wrapping and saturating arithmetic expose their names without the integer suffix.
Existing suffixed spellings remain compatible and use the same runtime operations.

The same receiver-based dispatch exposes `+std.arc::clone+` and `+std.rc::clone+`
for strong and weak owners, `+std.io::close+`, `+std.fs::close+`, and `+std.net::close+`
for their existing handle kinds, and `+std.time::add+`, `+std.time::sub+`,
`+std.time::multiply+`, and `+std.time::compare+` where receiver types distinguish
existing operations. `+std.bytes::append+` accepts a byte or byte view, and
`+std.bytes::find+` accepts a byte or pattern view. These aliases retain each selected
operation's access, explicit consumption, and error contract.

[[R-SLIB-FMT-0003]]
*R-SLIB-FMT-0003* — `+create+` produces empty output without allocation;
`+with_capacity+` produces empty output able to receive at least the requested number of
bytes without allocation; and `+as_str+` exposes the exact complete current output with
a borrow origin in the builder. Every successful append adds exactly its declared value
representation after all existing bytes: `+append_str+` adds the source bytes and
`+append_char+` adds the canonical UTF-8 encoding. Integer radix is 2 through 36 and output uses lowercase ASCII digits,
a leading minus only for negative values, and no prefix. Finite nonzero floating output
is the unique canonical round-trip candidate selected as follows. A fixed candidate has
an optional minus, a nonempty integer part with no leading zero except `+0+`, and an
optional decimal point followed by a nonempty fractional part whose last digit is not
zero. A scientific candidate has an optional minus, one nonzero digit before an optional
decimal point and fractional part ending in a nonzero digit, lowercase `+e+`, and a
base-10 exponent with a minus only when negative and no leading zero except exponent
zero. Every candidate shall parse to the identical source floating value. Selection
first minimizes total UTF-8 byte length, then minimizes exact decimal distance from the
source mathematical binary value, then prefers an even last significant decimal digit,
then prefers fixed notation, and finally selects lexicographically smaller ASCII bytes.
These rules produce one locale-independent spelling. Floating output preserves signed
zero, spelling negative zero `+-0+` and positive zero `+0+`. It spells
exceptional values `+inf+`, `+-inf+` and `+nan+`; the NaN spelling is independent of
sign and payload. Each append is transactional and preserves target on error.
An integer append validates radix before sizing, allocation or mutation, so
`+invalid_radix+` has priority over `+allocation_failed+`.
`+clear+` sets output length to zero, leaves valid empty UTF-8, and retains the current
storage and capacity without allocation or failure. `+finish+` transfers storage
without allocation and produces a string with exactly the complete builder bytes.

[[R-SLIB-FMT-0004]]
*R-SLIB-FMT-0004* — `+std.format::format+` is the compiler-defined local template family
of Core R-EXPR-0028. Each literal has a concrete capture schema and typed rendering
helpers; no uniform runtime representation, dynamic slot parsing, type erasure,
or general variadic function ABI is implied. The type is Move-only and its snapshots
own runtime text. Creation and each `.format` expression may raise checked
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+`; formatting returns `+std.string::string+` without consuming
the template. No template is exported through interface v4 or a C boundary.

The builder is also the output of the Core trait `+core::Format+` (R-TYPE-0046):
`+value.format(&builder)+` appends the text of a value whose type satisfies it, and a
formatted literal inserts that text into its slot. The texts of scalars and strings are
those of R-SLIB-FMT-0003 in radix 10; `+std.net::ip_address+` and
`+std.net::socket_address+` are written as R-SLIB-NET-0001 states. A slot width pads any
such text with spaces to its number of Unicode scalar values.

[source,r]
----
constexpr str name = "Ada";
std.format::format f0 = f"{name} {1} {2} {1}";
std.string::string first = f0.format(1, 2);
std.string::string second = f0.format("A", "B");
std.string::string direct = f"hello {name}";
----

[[mathematics]]
== 11. Mathematics

[[R-SLIB-MATH-0001]]
*R-SLIB-MATH-0001* — `+std.math::error_code+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+domain+`, `+pole+`, `+overflow+` and `+underflow+`.
`+std.math::math_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.math::error_code code+` and `+i64 native_code+`. Exact conversion is
`+std.math::as_error(std.math::math_error value) -> std.error::error+`.

[[R-SLIB-MATH-0002]]
*R-SLIB-MATH-0002* — The scalar suffix set F is exactly
`+f32,f64,c_float,c_double+` plus `+c_long_double+` when that C ABI type is available.
For each S in F, `+std.math+` provides these exact non-failing operations:

* `+abs_S(S value) -> S+`, `+floor_S(S value) -> S+`, `+ceil_S(S value) -> S+`,
  `+trunc_S(S value) -> S+` and `+round_S(S value) -> S+`;
* `+copy_sign_S(S magnitude, S sign) -> S+`, `+min_S(S left, S right) -> S+`,
  `+max_S(S left, S right) -> S+` and `+next_after_S(S from, S toward) -> S+`;
* `+is_finite_S(S value) -> bool+`, `+is_infinite_S(S value) -> bool+`,
  `+is_nan_S(S value) -> bool+`, `+is_normal_S(S value) -> bool+` and
  `+sign_bit_S(S value) -> bool+`.

The suffix is literal API spelling, for example `+std.math::sqrt_f64+`; this closed
schema introduces no overload selection. Each operation has the value semantics of its
corresponding C17 real-floating function for S. In particular, `+round_S+` rounds a
halfway value away from zero. `+min_S+` and `+max_S+` return the non-NaN operand when
exactly one operand is NaN; with two NaNs they return NaN; for opposite signed zeros,
minimum returns negative zero and maximum returns positive zero. `+next_after_S+` returns
`+toward+` when the operands compare equal and otherwise the adjacent S value in that
direction; stepping beyond the finite range returns the correctly signed IEEE infinity
and remains non-failing.

[[R-SLIB-MATH-0003]]
*R-SLIB-MATH-0003* — For each S in F, exact fallible unary operations
`+OP_S(S value) -> S throws std.math::math_error+` exist for OP in
`+sin,cos,tan,asin,acos,atan,sinh,cosh,tanh,asinh,acosh,atanh,exp,exp2,expm1,log,log2,log10,log1p,sqrt,cbrt,erf,erfc,gamma,log_gamma+`.
Exact fallible binary operations `+OP_S(S left, S right) ->
S throws std.math::math_error+` exist for OP in
`+atan2,pow,hypot,remainder+`. Conditions have precedence over success in this order:
domain, pole, overflow, underflow. Underflow occurs exactly when the nonzero exact
mathematical result rounds to zero, or when its rounded result is subnormal and inexact,
under round-to-nearest-ties-to-even. An exactly representable nonzero subnormal result
succeeds; every other finite representable result succeeds. Each listed condition
uses its corresponding error.
An input NaN produces a successful NaN except where the named mathematical operation
requires another listed condition first.

Operation-specific classification is independent of host errno/fenv and is exhaustive:

* `+log_S+`, `+log2_S+` and `+log10_S+` report domain for a negative input and pole
  for either signed zero; `+log1p_S+` reports domain below -1 and pole at -1;
* `+sqrt_S+` reports domain for a negative input other than negative zero;
  `+asin_S+` and `+acos_S+` report domain when absolute input exceeds 1;
  `+acosh_S+` reports domain below 1; `+atanh_S+` reports domain outside [-1,1] and pole
  at either endpoint;
* `+sin_S+`, `+cos_S+` and `+tan_S+` report domain for infinite input;
* `+remainder_S+` reports domain for any zero divisor or any infinite dividend;
  a finite dividend and infinite divisor succeeds with the dividend;
  signed-zero cases of `+atan2_S+`, including two zeros, follow C17 and succeed;
* `+pow_S+` first succeeds with 1 for every zero exponent, including a NaN or zero base,
  and for base 1 with every exponent, including NaN. It otherwise reports domain for a
  finite negative base and finite non-integral exponent,
  and pole for either signed-zero base with negative exponent; all remaining NaN and infinity cases have the
  corresponding C17 `+pow+` value;
* `+gamma_S+` is the C17 `+tgamma+` family and `+log_gamma_S+` is the C17 `+lgamma+`
  family; either reports pole for zero or a negative integer, `+gamma_S+` reports domain
  for negative infinity, and `+log_gamma_S+` succeeds with positive infinity for
  negative infinity;
* the remaining listed cases have no additional domain or pole condition. In particular,
  non-trigonometric infinity inputs take their named C17 infinity result unless an
  earlier row assigns a condition.

After this table, overflow/underflow use the general exact-result rule above. C17
infinity and NaN results not assigned an earlier condition are successful.

[[R-SLIB-MATH-0004]]
*R-SLIB-MATH-0004* — For each S in F, public Copy structs
`+std.math::fraction_parts_S+` and `+std.math::binary_parts_S+` have, respectively,
fields `+S whole+`, `+S fraction+` and fields `+S fraction+`, `+i32 exponent+`.
Exact operations are `+std.math::split_fraction_S(S value) ->
std.math::fraction_parts_S+`, `+std.math::split_binary_S(S value) ->
std.math::binary_parts_S+` and `+std.math::compose_binary_S(S fraction, i32 exponent) ->
S throws std.math::math_error+`. Split operations allocate nothing and exactly reconstruct
every finite input under the corresponding compose or addition operation when the
reconstruction is representable. `+split_fraction_S+` has C17 `+modf+` semantics: whole
is truncation toward zero, fraction is value minus whole, both zero results preserve the
input sign, infinity produces the same infinity and a same-sign zero fraction, and NaN
produces NaN in both fields. `+split_binary_S+` has C17 `+frexp+` semantics for finite
nonzero input; signed zero returns the same zero with exponent zero, infinity returns the
same infinity with exponent zero, and NaN returns NaN with exponent zero.
`+compose_binary_S+` has C17 `+scalbn+`/`+ldexp+` value semantics: NaN, signed infinity
and signed zero return unchanged successfully for every exponent; a finite nonzero result
uses the R-SLIB-MATH-0003 overflow and underflow classification.

[[R-SLIB-MATH-0005]]
*R-SLIB-MATH-0005* — `+std.math::complex_f32+` and
`+std.math::complex_f64+` are Copy, Send+Sync structs with public `+real+` and `+imag+`
fields of the named component type. For C in those two types, exact non-failing
operations are `+add_C(C,C)->C+`, `+sub_C(C,C)->C+`, `+mul_C(C,C)->C+`,
`+conjugate_C(C)->C+` and `+phase_C(C)->COMPONENT+`. Exact fallible magnitude is
`+magnitude_C(C)->COMPONENT throws std.math::math_error+` and uses the R-SLIB-MATH-0003
classification of the corresponding real hypot result.
Exact fallible operations `+OP_C(C value) -> C throws std.math::math_error+` exist for OP
`+exp,log,sqrt,sin,cos,tan,sinh,cosh,tanh+`; exact binary operations are
`+div_C(C,C)->C throws std.math::math_error+` and
`+pow_C(C,C)->C throws std.math::math_error+`. These names use the literal suffix `+complex_f32+` or
`+complex_f64+`. Their values follow the corresponding C17 `<complex.h>` operations,
including principal values, branch cuts and signed-zero side selection; add, subtract,
multiply and divide use the C17 complex operators. Conditions precede success in the
order domain, pole, overflow, underflow. Zero divided by zero is domain; a nonzero value
divided by zero, complex log of zero and any other pole of the named C17 function are
pole. Overflow occurs when either finite exact component exceeds its component range.
Underflow occurs when, with no earlier condition, either nonzero exact component rounds
to zero or its rounded component is subnormal and inexact. A condition in either
component classifies the whole fallible operation; otherwise C17 NaN propagation and
the resulting complex value are successful. Non-failing operations return their C17
IEEE component values without converting floating overflow or underflow into an error.

[[R-SLIB-MATH-0006]]
*R-SLIB-MATH-0006* — Each math operation evaluates operands once, uses the R
round-to-nearest-ties-to-even canonical environment, captures C `+errno+` and floating
exceptions needed to classify its result, and restores the calling thread's prior C
floating environment before return. An implementation may use an equivalent intrinsic
or correctly rounded routine. It shall preserve the R result and error classification
defined here and normalize host `+errno+`/floating-exception indications to the
R-SLIB-MATH-0003 and R-SLIB-MATH-0005 definitions. A host indication that does not
establish one of those four semantic conditions neither creates a math error nor changes
the R value. Canonical-environment setup governs evaluation and error
capture; it does not change the named value semantics of `+floor_S+`, `+ceil_S+`,
`+trunc_S+`, `+round_S+`, `+min_S+`, `+max_S+` or `+next_after_S+`. The implementation
shall not expose process locale or persistent floating-environment
mutation.

[[time]]
== 12. Time

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0001]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0001* — `+std.time::duration+` is an opaque Copy, Send+Sync value with
canonical components `+i64 seconds+` and `+u32 nanoseconds+`, where nanoseconds is less
than 1,000,000,000 and the represented value is
`+seconds + nanoseconds / 1,000,000,000+`. Exact operations are:

* `+std.time::duration_from_seconds(i64 seconds) -> std.time::duration+`;
* `+std.time::duration_from_parts(i64 seconds, u32 nanoseconds) ->
  std.time::duration throws std.time::duration_error+`;
* `+std.time::duration_seconds(std.time::duration value) -> i64+`;
* `+std.time::duration_nanoseconds(std.time::duration value) -> u32+`;
* `+std.time::duration_add(std.time::duration left, std.time::duration right) ->
  std.time::duration throws std.time::duration_error+`;
* `+std.time::duration_sub(std.time::duration left, std.time::duration right) ->
  std.time::duration throws std.time::duration_error+`;
* `+std.time::duration_multiply(std.time::duration value, i64 factor) ->
  std.time::duration throws std.time::duration_error+`;
* `+std.time::duration_compare(std.time::duration left, std.time::duration right) -> i32+`.

`+duration_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+invalid_nanoseconds+` and `+overflow+`. Construction and arithmetic produce only the
canonical representation. `+duration_from_seconds(s)+` has components `+s+` and zero;
`+duration_from_parts(s,n)+` has exactly those components when n is below 1,000,000,000
and otherwise returns `+invalid_nanoseconds+`. Add, subtract and multiply return the
canonical duration of the exact mathematical operation, or `+overflow+` when its
normalized seconds are outside i64. The two accessors return the exact components.
Compare returns exactly -1, 0 or 1 according to the represented mathematical durations.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0002]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0002* — `+std.time::instant+` is an opaque Copy, Send+Sync monotonic-clock
value meaningful only on the creating target execution. `+std.time::system_time+` is a
Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields `+i64 unix_seconds+` and `+u32 nanoseconds+`,
with the same nanosecond bound as duration. Exact clock operations are:

* `+std.time::monotonic_now() -> std.time::instant throws std.time::time_error+`;
* `+std.time::system_now() -> std.time::system_time throws std.time::time_error+`;
* `+std.time::instant_add(std.time::instant base, std.time::duration delta) ->
  std.time::instant throws std.time::time_error+`;
* `+std.time::instant_duration(std.time::instant later, std.time::instant earlier) ->
  std.time::duration throws std.time::time_error+`;
* `+std.time::system_add(std.time::system_time base, std.time::duration delta) ->
  std.time::system_time throws std.time::time_error+`.

`+instant_duration+` returns `+invalid_value+` when later precedes earlier. Arithmetic
outside the target instant or i64 system-time range returns `+overflow+`; an input
system_time with an out-of-range nanosecond field returns `+invalid_value+`.
`+monotonic_now+` observes the current point of the selected monotonic clock and
successful calls do not regress within one execution. `+system_now+` observes the
current selected system clock as normalized Unix seconds and nanoseconds.
`+instant_add+` returns the exact monotonic point displaced by delta;
`+instant_duration+` returns the exact nonnegative elapsed duration; and `+system_add+`
returns the exact normalized sum of the represented Unix time and delta, subject only
to the stated validation and overflow outcomes.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0003]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0003* — `+std.time::utc_datetime+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public
fields `+i32 year+`, `+u8 month+`, `+u8 day+`, `+u8 hour+`, `+u8 minute+`, `+u8 second+`
and `+u32 nanosecond+`. Exact conversions are
`+std.time::to_utc(std.time::system_time value) ->
std.time::utc_datetime throws std.time::time_error+` and
`+std.time::from_utc(std.time::utc_datetime value) ->
std.time::system_time throws std.time::time_error+`. Month is 1..12, day is valid for the
proleptic Gregorian month, hour is 0..23, minute is 0..59, second is 0..59, and
nanosecond has the duration bound. UTC conversion uses Unix days of exactly 86,400
seconds; leap seconds are not represented independently. The two conversions are
inverse for every valid representable value. `+to_utc+` returns
`+std.time::error_code::invalid_value+` when the input nanoseconds field is at least
1,000,000,000. `+from_utc+` returns that same code for any violation of the stated field
or calendar constraints, including second 60. Either conversion returns `+overflow+` when its
otherwise valid result is not representable. The conversions use UTC and allocate
nothing.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0004]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0004* — `+std.time::error_code+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+invalid_value+`, `+overflow+`, `+unavailable+`, `+cancelled+` and `+other+`.
`+std.time::time_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.time::error_code code+` and `+i64 native_code+`. Exact conversion is
`+std.time::as_error(std.time::time_error value) -> std.error::error+`.

The classification is exhaustive. `+invalid_value+` denotes exactly an invalid public
field, calendar value, operand order or other invariant identified by R-SLIB-TIME-0001..0003.
`+overflow+` denotes exactly a valid arithmetic or conversion result outside the
documented representable range. `+unavailable+` denotes a recognized native status that
the selected clock source or timer service is temporarily unable to provide the
requested reading or timer, rather than absence of the mandatory profile facility.
`+cancelled+` denotes a cancellation request winning the terminal race of a timer.
`+other+` denotes an otherwise unclassified native clock or timer failure status,
whether that status is known or unknown to the target adapter.
Task-local validation precedes checked arithmetic and any native access; timer
cancellation races native completion as specified by R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007. Validation,
arithmetic and task-selected cancellation errors have native code zero. A native failure
preserves its immediately captured status. A status denoting the stated temporary
inability uses `+unavailable+`; every other native failure uses `+other+` after the
specific classifications have been tested.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0005]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0005* — Hosted-native-async provides exact asynchronous operations
`+async std.time::sleep_until(std.time::instant when) ->
void throws std.time::time_error+` and
`+async std.time::sleep_for(std.time::duration duration) ->
void throws std.time::time_error+`. A non-positive duration completes successfully after
at least one executor scheduling opportunity. A positive `+sleep_for+` obtains the
current monotonic instant and performs the same checked addition as `+instant_add+`;
a target-horizon overflow returns `+overflow+` before timer submission. Every
`+sleep_until+` instant constructible by safe R is valid for the creating target
execution; a recognized inability of the native timer backend to represent or schedule
it returns `+unavailable+`, not `+invalid_value+`. Cancellation completes with
`+std.time::error_code::cancelled+`; successful completion occurs no earlier than the
requested monotonic instant.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0006]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0006* — `+import std.time;+` also loads the R part of the module
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0001) in the `+hosted+` profile and above: the text forms of time of RFC 3339 and
of HTTP (RFC 9110), the interval of R-SLIB-TIME-0009, the time zones of R-SLIB-TIME-0010..0013
and the local times by pattern of R-SLIB-TIME-0014.
`+std.time::format_rfc3339(std.time::system_time value, u32 digits) -> std.string::string throws std.time::time_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
writes the UTC date and time `+YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS+` of value (RFC 3339 section 5.6), then,
when digits is not zero, a full stop and the first digits digits of the nine-digit
nanoseconds, the others dropped, and last `+Z+`. Digits above 9 report `+invalid_value+`, as
does a value that `+std.time::to_utc+` rejects, and a year outside 0000..9999, which the form
cannot write, reports `+overflow+`.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0007]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0007* —
`+std.time::parse_rfc3339(str text) -> std.time::system_time throws std.convert::parse_error+`
reads a date-time of RFC 3339 section 5.6: `+YYYY-MM-DD+`, then `+T+`, `+t+` or a space,
`+HH:MM:SS+`, an optional full stop followed by one or more digits, of which the first nine
give the nanoseconds and the others are dropped, and last `+Z+`, `+z+` or an offset of a plus
or minus sign and `+HH:MM+`, which the result removes. An empty text reports `+empty+` at
index 0. The text is read from its start: the first byte that its position does not admit
reports `+invalid_digit+` at that byte, a text that ends before the date-time is complete
reports `+invalid_digit+` at its length, and bytes after the date-time report
`+trailing_character+` at the first of them. The fields of a complete date-time are then
checked in text order — month 01..12, day 01 up to the last day of the month in the proleptic
Gregorian calendar, hour 00..23, minute and second 00..59, and the hour 00..23 and minute
00..59 of an offset — and the first field out of its range reports `+below_minimum+` or
`+above_maximum+` at its first digit. Second 60 is above the maximum, as leap seconds are not
represented (R-SLIB-TIME-0003).

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0008]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0008* —
`+std.time::format_http_date(std.time::system_time value) -> std.string::string throws std.time::time_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
writes the IMF-fixdate of RFC 9110 section 5.6.7, such as `+Sun, 06 Nov 1994 08:49:37 GMT+`,
dropping the fraction of the second; it reports the errors of `+format_rfc3339+` with zero
digits.
`+std.time::parse_http_date(str text) -> std.time::system_time throws std.convert::parse_error+`
reads the three forms that section requires a recipient to accept, told apart by the byte
after the three-letter day name: a comma for IMF-fixdate, a space for the asctime form
`+Sun Nov  6 08:49:37 1994+`, whose day below 10 follows a space, and a letter for the
obsolete RFC 850 form `+Sunday, 06-Nov-94 08:49:37 GMT+`, whose two-digit year names a year
of 1970..2069. Day and month names are case-sensitive. The text is read and its day, hour,
minute and second are checked as by `+parse_rfc3339+`; after that, a day name that is not the
weekday of the date reports `+invalid_digit+` at index 0.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0009]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0009* — In the `+hosted-native-async+` profile `+std.time::interval+` is a Copy,
Send+Sync struct with protected state that ticks with a fixed period on the monotonic clock.
`+std.time::interval::every(std.time::duration period) -> std.time::interval throws std.time::time_error+`
starts one at the current monotonic instant; a period that is not positive or not below 2^63
nanoseconds reports `+invalid_value+`.
`+tick(interval* this) -> u64 throws std.time::time_error, std.async::start_error+` is a
`+@scoped async+` method (Core R-STMT-0017) that completes one tick and returns its number n,
counted from 1; tick n completes no earlier than the start plus n periods. A call made before
the tick after the previous one is due waits for that tick; a call made when it is due
completes at once the latest due tick, the one of the whole periods passed since the start,
and skips those between. Ticks thus keep to the grid of the start whatever time the program
spends between them, and a delay gives one late tick instead of a burst. A deadline beyond
2^63 nanoseconds after the start reports `+overflow+`. A cancelled tick completes with
`+cancelled+` as `+std.time::sleep_until+` does (R-SLIB-TIME-0005) and leaves the interval
unchanged.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0010]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0010* — The R part of the module also provides time zones.
`+std.time::zone_error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+invalid_name+`,
`+unknown_zone+`, `+malformed+`, `+nonexistent_time+` and `+ambiguous_time+`, formatted by its
variant names, and `+std.time::zone_error+` is an error of the module with the field
`+std.time::zone_error_code code+`. `+std.time::zone+` is the Move, Send and Sync zone of a
name: its transitions with their local time types (an offset east of UTC in seconds, whether it
is daylight saving time, an abbreviation) and the rule of a POSIX TZ string for the times after
its last transition. Zones are made by:

* `+std.time::utc_zone() -> std.time::zone throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.time::fixed_zone(i32 offset, str name) -> std.time::zone throws std.time::zone_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.time::posix_zone(str rule) -> std.time::zone throws std.time::zone_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.time::parse_tzif(str name, const u8[] data) -> std.time::zone throws std.time::zone_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+async std.time::load_zone(std.string::string name) -> std.time::zone throws std.time::zone_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+async std.time::local_zone() -> std.time::zone throws std.time::zone_error, std.error::fault+`.

`+utc_zone+` is UTC with the abbreviation `+UTC+`; `+fixed_zone+` has one offset of at most 25
hours either way and name as its name and abbreviation, any other offset being `+malformed+`.
`+posix_zone+` reads a POSIX TZ string with the extensions of RFC 8536 section 3.3.1:
abbreviations of at least three letters or quoted in angle brackets, offsets written west of UTC
as `+[+-]hh[:mm[:ss]]+`, and rule dates `+Jn+`, `+n+` and `+Mm.w.d+` with an optional `+/time+` of
-167 to 167 hours (2 a.m. by default); daylight time one hour ahead of standard time when no
offset follows its abbreviation, and the rule of the United States when no dates follow; any
other text is `+malformed+`. `+parse_tzif+` reads a TZif file of RFC 8536, versions 1 to 4, in its
64-bit form with the rule of its footer when the version is 2 or later; leap-second records are
ignored, and a file that is not such a TZif is `+malformed+`. In `+hosted-native-async+`,
`+load_zone+` reads the file of an IANA name, such as `+Asia/Shanghai+`, in the directory that the
environment variable `+TZDIR+` names, or else `+/usr/share/zoneinfo+`: a name that is empty,
absolute, holds a component of only dots or a byte other than ASCII letters, digits, `+_+`, `++++`,
`+-+`, `+.+` and `+/+` is `+invalid_name+`, a name without a file is `+unknown_zone+`, and other
failures of the file system are reported. `+local_zone+` returns the zone of the environment
variable `+TZ+` when it is set: UTC when it is empty, else, after an optional colon, the zone
file of that name when there is one and the POSIX TZ string otherwise; without `+TZ+` it reads
the TZif file `+/etc/localtime+` under the name `+localtime+`.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0011]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0011* — `+std.time::local_time+` is a Copy struct with public fields
`+i32 year+`, `+u8 month+`, `+u8 day+`, `+u8 hour+`, `+u8 minute+`, `+u8 second+`,
`+u32 nanosecond+`, `+u8 weekday+` (1 for Monday to 7 for Sunday, ISO 8601), `+u16 year_day+` (1
for January 1), `+i32 offset+` and `+bool dst+`:

* `+std.time::zone::name(const std.time::zone* this) -> str+`;
* `+std.time::zone::offset(const std.time::zone* this, std.time::system_time at) -> i32+`;
* `+std.time::zone::abbreviation(const std.time::zone* this, std.time::system_time at) -> str+`;
* `+std.time::zone::to_local(const std.time::zone* this, std.time::system_time at) -> std.time::local_time throws std.time::time_error+`.

The local time type of an instant is that of the last transition at or before it, the first
type for instants before the first transition, and that of the footer rule after the last
transition when the zone has one; a rule decides daylight time by the start and end of the year
of the instant in standard time, so that a southern zone is in daylight time across the new
year. `+to_local+` returns the proleptic Gregorian date and time of the instant shifted by the
offset, with the offset and flag of the type; a nanoseconds field of 10^9 or more is
`+invalid_value+` and a year outside i32 `+overflow+`.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0012]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0012* — `+std.time::ambiguity+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+earlier+`,
`+later+` and `+reject+`:

* `+std.time::zone::from_local(const std.time::zone* this, std.time::local_time wall, std.time::ambiguity choice) -> std.time::system_time throws std.time::zone_error, std.time::time_error+`.

`+from_local+` reads the date and time fields of wall, which shall be a valid date and time of
the proleptic Gregorian calendar (`+invalid_value+` otherwise), and ignores the others. A local
time that the zone shows once resolves to that instant; one shown twice, in an overlap, resolves
to the earlier or the later instant; one never shown, in a gap, is read with the offset in
effect before the gap (`+earlier+`) or after it (`+later+`), as fold 0 and fold 1 of PEP 495 do.
With `+reject+`, an overlap is `+ambiguous_time+` and a gap `+nonexistent_time+`.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0013]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0013* — Calendar steps in a zone are:

* `+std.time::zone::start_of_day(const std.time::zone* this, std.time::system_time at) -> std.time::system_time throws std.time::zone_error, std.time::time_error+`;
* `+std.time::zone::add_days(const std.time::zone* this, std.time::system_time at, i64 days) -> std.time::system_time throws std.time::zone_error, std.time::time_error+`;
* `+std.time::zone::add_months(const std.time::zone* this, std.time::system_time at, i64 months) -> std.time::system_time throws std.time::zone_error, std.time::time_error+`.

`+start_of_day+` returns the instant of local midnight of the day of at, resolved with
`+earlier+`, which is the first instant of the day also when midnight falls in a gap.
`+add_days+` returns the same local time days days later, or earlier for a negative count;
`+add_months+` the same local time months months later, a day past the end of the target month
becoming its last day. Both resolve the new local time with `+earlier+`, so a day of a change
lasts 23 or 25 hours, and report a year outside i32 as `+overflow+`.

[[R-SLIB-TIME-0014]]
*R-SLIB-TIME-0014* — Local times by pattern:

* `+std.time::format_local(std.time::local_time value, str pattern) -> std.string::string throws std.time::time_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.time::parse_local(str text, str pattern) -> std.time::local_time throws std.convert::parse_error, std.time::time_error+`.

A pattern is a sequence of fields and literal bytes. A run of one ASCII letter is one field:
`+yyyy+` the year in four digits, `+MM+` the month, `+dd+` the day, `+HH+` the hour of 00 to 23,
`+mm+` the minute, `+ss+` the second, and `+S+` repeated one to nine times that many leading
digits of the fraction of the second; any other run of letters is `+invalid_value+`. Every
byte that is not an ASCII letter stands for itself. `+format_local+` writes the fields of value
with exactly their number of digits, the fraction truncated; a value whose date and time fields
are not valid (R-SLIB-TIME-0012), or whose year is outside 0 to 9999 when the pattern writes it,
is `+invalid_value+`. `+parse_local+` reads text against the pattern: each field takes exactly
its number of digits, each literal its byte, and the text ends where the pattern ends; a text
that ends early is `+invalid_digit+` of `+std.convert::parse_error+` at its length and any
other mismatch `+invalid_digit+` at the byte. The month, day, hour, minute and second are then
checked in that order, `+below_minimum+` or `+above_maximum+` at the first digit of the field.
The fields the pattern does not name are those of 1970-01-01 00:00:00; the result has offset 0,
`+dst+` false and the weekday and day of the year of its date.

[[environment]]
== 13. Process environment

[[R-SLIB-ENV-0001]]
*R-SLIB-ENV-0001* — `+std.env::error_code+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+invalid_name+`, `+invalid_value+`, `+allocation_failed+`,
`+permission_denied+`, `+resource_exhausted+` and `+other+`.
`+std.env::env_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.env::error_code code+` and `+i64 native_code+`. Exact conversion is
`+std.env::as_error(std.env::env_error value) -> std.error::error+`.

The portable classification is exhaustive:

* `+invalid_name+` means a caller-supplied name violates R-SLIB-ENV-0003 or a native name
  selected for a returned snapshot is not representable as such an R name;
* `+invalid_value+` means a caller-supplied value violates R-SLIB-ENV-0003 or a native
  value selected for a returned R string is not representable as valid UTF-8 without NUL;
* `+allocation_failed+` means R allocation or checked size arithmetic failed while
  constructing an owned copy or snapshot;
* `+permission_denied+` means a native query or mutation was denied by an access or
  security policy;
* `+resource_exhausted+` means a native query, snapshot or mutation reached a process or
  system resource limit;
* `+other+` means an otherwise unclassified native failure status, whether its native
  value is known or unknown to the target adapter.

Name validation precedes value validation, which precedes allocation and native mutation.
A valid removal of an unbound name succeeds without creating a binding. An R allocator
or size failure always produces `+allocation_failed+` with native code zero; native
resource exhaustion produces `+resource_exhausted+` with the immediately captured native
code. Every failure established without a native failure status has native code zero. A
failure selected from a native status preserves that status. Every status matching a
specific portable meaning above shall use that code; `+other+` is considered only after
every specific classification, including for a documented status with no narrower
portable meaning.

[[R-SLIB-ENV-0002]]
*R-SLIB-ENV-0002* — Exact snapshot operations are:

* `+std.env::arguments() -> array<std.string::string> throws std.env::env_error+`;
* `+std.env::variables() ->
  dict<std.string::string,std.string::string> throws std.env::env_error+`;
* `+std.env::get(str name) -> o<std.string::string> throws std.env::env_error+`;
* `+std.env::set(str name, str value) -> void throws std.env::env_error+`;
* `+std.env::remove(str name) -> void throws std.env::env_error+`.

`+arguments+` returns a fresh owned copy of the exact validated immutable UTF-8 startup
snapshot reserved for the hosted launch and, for the argument-taking root signature,
supplied to `+main+`. The snapshot is nonempty. If the host supplied native argument
zero, its first string is that argument after the Core conversion; otherwise it is the
target-documented synthesized UTF-8 argument-zero spelling. That spelling does not
promise executable identity. Every later string is the exact converted corresponding
native argument.
It cannot return `+invalid_value+` after startup. Its only recoverable failure is
`+allocation_failed+` with native code zero for R allocation or checked-size failure
while copying the already-resident snapshot. Each returned string and container owns an immutable snapshot independent of later
environment changes. `+get+` returns `+o::none+` for an unbound valid name. Names and
values are validated for the R and target-native representations and copied before
mutation; success commits one complete change. Removing a valid unbound name is a
successful no-op.
`+variables+` emits one implementation-canonical spelling for each distinct native
name and contains no two keys equal under the target environment-name equality. Native
enumeration converts every name and value to UTF-8 without embedded NUL as one all-or-
error transaction; it exposes no partial dictionary. Among duplicate native entries
equal under target name equality, the last entry in the target-documented native
enumeration order is the canonical winner. `+get+`, `+variables+` and the inherited
snapshot captured by `+std.process::command_create+` use that same winner and value.

[[R-SLIB-ENV-0003]]
*R-SLIB-ENV-0003* — Environment names contain at least one Unicode scalar and no NUL or
`+=+`; values contain no NUL. Both must be representable by the selected native
environment encoding. Validation of the name, then the value, precedes copying and any
native access. A native snapshot entry with an invalid name or value fails the complete
snapshot with the corresponding typed error; no invalid `+str+` is formed. The
implementation serializes all standard environment
snapshot and mutation operations, captures allocation/native errors, and releases the
serialization guard before returning. `+std.string::string+` has the dictionary key
contract of exact UTF-8 byte equality and the corresponding predefined string hash.

[[asynchronous-runtime]]
== 14. Asynchronous runtime

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0001]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0001* — `+task<T throws E1, ..., En>+` is a compiler-recognized Move-only
owner of one asynchronous observation right and the associated runtime frame. The
effect-free spelling is `+task<T>+`. It is Send iff T and every Ei are Send, and it is
never Sync. Its T and every Ei satisfy Core R-TYPE-0029: T is
outermost-unqualified, complete, sized and inhabited and recursively contains no
ordinary borrow, slice or runtime `+str+`; `+task<void>+` is the sole exception, and
`+constexpr str+` remains permitted as specified by Core. It has no C ABI
representation and is not a permitted field of a `+@repr(C)+` aggregate.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0002]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0002* — `+std.async::start_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+allocation_failed+`, `+runtime_stopping+`, `+scope_full+` and `+budget_exhausted+`
(codes 0, 1, 2 and 3). The third category is produced by the Core bounded task group before
preparing a new child when every entry is reserved or occupied, and the fourth by a budget
whose task limit refuses the start or whose byte limit refuses its allocation (Core
R-STMT-0020). Both leave named Move arguments initialized. Calling an
`+async T operation(...) throws E1, ..., En+` performs a start expression whose value
type is exactly `+task<T throws E1, ..., En>+` and whose immediate checked-error set is
exactly `+std.async::start_error+`. A successful start returns the sole task observation
right. A thrown start error means that no body instruction ran and no task or runtime
operation was published or remains live; all provisional start state has been released.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0003]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0003* — Async start uses two-phase commit. Argument expressions are
evaluated left to right and named Move operands are staged without ending their source
state. Each staging establishes the Core exclusive overlap reservation of R-FUNC-0010
until start failure or commit; every later overlapping access, borrow, move or restaging
is rejected by that Core rule. A named Move source receives rollback only when the
complete argument expression is a direct `+move place+`; nesting it inside an aggregate,
constructor or other argument expression requires `+R-DIAG-MOVE-003+`. The caller may
first construct a named payload and pass that payload by direct move. The runtime allocates the complete frame. A standard asynchronous operation that
submits a native operation also reserves its native submission state, and one whose
public signature accepts a call-bounded borrowed input completes the independent owned
copy or runtime retain required by its specific contract, unless it is a scoped operation,
whose view becomes a task-group loan at commit (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012). Only after all state required
by that operation is ready does one atomic commit transfer every staged Move operand and
publish the task. Any start error releases staging and provisional runtime state and
leaves each named Move source initialized with its original ownership obligation. A
staged temporary is destroyed exactly once when that failed start ends its full
expression. If evaluation of a later argument panics, unwind releases every provisional
reservation and destroys initialized staged temporaries according to Core; abort
performs no additional R drops under R-ERR-0005.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0004]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0004* — A user async frame obeys Core R-BORROW-0024: no ordinary borrow,
slice or runtime `+str+`, recursively, is a captured parameter or remains live across an
await. The frame may own permitted Copy or Move values, `+constexpr str+` and `+arc T+`
owners. A standard asynchronous operation with a borrowed public signature instead
publishes only the independent owned copy or runtime retain completed under
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0003; its runtime frame may own that state and runtime-retained standard
handles. A scoped operation (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012) instead keeps its view alive through the
task-group loan of Core R-STMT-0017; its frame owns no copy of that storage.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0005]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0005* — `+await move task_name+` consumes one named
`+task<T throws E1, ..., En>+` inside an async function and yields its logical T or
throws exactly the stored checked error Ei. It is a compiler-recognized suspension
operation and is admitted only as the complete initializer of a declaration or as a
complete statement when T is `+void+`. Completion publishes the result with release
semantics; return from await observes it with acquire semantics. A task panic is
observed by re-raising the same panic category at the await point under unwind strategy;
abort strategy has already terminated the process.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0006]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0006* — Exact compiler-recognized operations are
`+std.async::cancel(task<T throws E...> operation) -> void+` and
`+std.async::detach(task<T throws E...> operation) -> void+`. Either consumes the
observation right. A task with a nonempty checked-error set is must-resolve: every
reachable path consumes it by await, cancel or detach before its scope ends.
Cancel atomically requests cancellation and then detaches; ordinary task destruction
of an effect-free task has the same effect. Detach permits continued execution without requesting
cancellation. The runtime owns frame, native-retain and buffer storage through terminal
completion, then destroys an unobserved returned value or checked-error payload exactly
once. An unobserved panic is
forwarded once to the runtime panic handler.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007* — Every standard asynchronous operation has one linearized terminal outcome.
Native completion, cancel and deadline races select the first linearized terminal event;
completion that has crossed a documented non-cancellable commit point returns its
committed outcome. Losing events cannot publish a second result or repeat cleanup. A
terminal outcome that returns an owned buffer, iterator, child, command or other
caller-accessible state is not observable by await until the native backend has
acknowledged completion/cancellation and can no longer access that returned state. An
adapter may instead use independent bounce storage, but it shall finish any copy into
the returned owner before publication. Terminal-event selection and safe result
publication are therefore distinct moments when acknowledgement is delayed. A scoped
operation's loaned view is likewise inaccessible to the native backend once its outcome is
published, so the loan of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012 ends at that publication.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008* — Every parameter named `+deadline+` in this document has exact type
`+o<std.time::instant>+` and is the last parameter of its operation; a call may omit that
argument, which then is `+o::none+`. The operation deadline is the earlier of that
argument and the deadline of the task that starts the operation (Core R-STMT-0019);
`+o::none+` together with a task without a deadline selects no operation deadline. A
present deadline uses the monotonic clock. A standard operation first performs its
task-local argument, range, encoding and invariant validation; it next completes any
explicitly specified zero-length or other no-op success; otherwise it tests the deadline
immediately before the first external side effect or native submission. An already reached
deadline then produces the module's `+timed_out+` error before that effect. Thus
validation failure precedes an expired deadline, while a valid specified no-op precedes
it. Every operation-specific I/O, filesystem and network clause is interpreted in this
order. After successful start, task cancellation produces the module's `+cancelled+` error
when it wins the R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007 race. An operation whose purpose is mandatory terminal
cleanup is exempt from omitting that cleanup: deadline or cancellation selects its
reported outcome, but consumption, native cancellation acknowledgement and release still
reach terminal completion.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0009]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0009* — Hosted execution accepts exact root signatures
`+async i32 main()+` and `+async i32 main(const str[] args)+`. The validated immutable
UTF-8 startup-argument snapshot is already reserved before R static initialization for
every hosted launch under the Core startup contract. Before root invocation, the
runtime creates the process executor and root frame. On root completion it
requests cancellation of every other nonterminal task, including detached tasks and
tasks for which cancellation was already requested, drains all runtime-owned frames to
terminal completion, performs required module destruction, and returns the root i32
status to the host environment. Failure to reserve the executor or root frame runs no
main-body instruction, reports the exact
`+allocation_failed+` or `+runtime_stopping+` start category through the runtime panic
handler or its allocation-free emergency path, and abruptly terminates with the
target-documented nonzero root-start status. Because no root task became live, that path
performs no R static or module destruction. Failure to validate or reserve the startup
snapshot is instead the Core pre-main `+argument_encoding_failure+` or
`+allocation_failure+` path and is not an async root-start error. That common pre-main
path orders required dynamic-provider readiness first, validation and complete-size
calculation for every native argument second, and the single snapshot-backing
allocation third; the earliest failing phase determines the Core category and no later
phase runs.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0010]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0010* — The hosted-native-async executor waits through target-native
readiness or completion facilities and runs ready R continuations. Its worker threads
execute bounded runtime submission/completion code and ready R computation. File payload,
console, DNS, socket and child-process waiting is represented by native submissions and
completion records rather than by an executor worker blocked in a C17 I/O function. The
closed exceptions are the filesystem operations admitted by R-SLIB-ASYNC-0011, which run
only on the separate filesystem adapter lane, the regular-file payload transfers of
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0019, which run only on the file payload adapter, and the entries of
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017, which run only on the blocking call pool.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0011]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0011* — The filesystem adapter lane has exactly four runtime-owned threads,
is disjoint from executor workers and accepts only descriptor-relative open/close,
metadata query, shared-position seek, directory enumeration, directory creation, unlink,
atomic no-replace rename, file/directory durability and advisory file locks that never wait. Its exact target-native entry
points are closed by the target manifest. Submission retains all operation state before
commit. Cancellation or deadline expiry removes queued work without entering the native
call. A call already entered is non-preemptible; acknowledgement and publication wait
for its return, and handles, buffers and frames remain live until then. Before an
external commit point, cancellation or deadline may win and the returned native result
is discarded after cleanup. After any documented commit point, native success wins and
is published even when cancellation was requested meanwhile. Lane saturation
shall not consume an executor worker or delay progress of unrelated native facilities.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012* — An operation that this document lists as scoped is a standard
asynchronous operation whose public signature accepts a call-bounded view, `+u8[]+` or
`+const u8[]+`, that the operation neither copies nor retains: the view is a loan of the
enclosing task group under Core R-STMT-0017, and the call is well-formed only while a
task group is active in the calling async function; elsewhere it requires
`+R-DIAG-ASYNC-001+`. The loan begins at successful start, is held by the group exactly
like the loan of a `+@scoped async+` call, and ends only after the native backend has
acknowledged completion or cancellation and can no longer access the viewed storage;
publication of the terminal outcome under R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007 is that acknowledgement, so
the consuming await, the group's all-members barrier or its exit observes it. Start failure holds no loan.
Validation order, zero-length no-op success, deadline testing, cancellation and
positive-progress rules are those of the corresponding owning operation; the scoped form
reports no partial progress on failure, and after a failed or cancelled outcome the bytes
of a mutable view are unspecified beyond any reported count. Results and completion
errors are Copy, Send and unborrowed. Every scoped operation has an owning counterpart
whose name it extends with `+_into+` or `+_from+`; only the buffer parameter and the
result shape differ.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0013]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0013* — In the hosted-native-async profile, `+std.async::mutex<T>+` and
`+std.async::rw_lock<T>+` are Move-only handles of one shared lock that protects one T,
and `+std.async::mutex_guard<T>+`, `+std.async::rw_read_guard<T>+` and
