`+std.async::rw_write_guard<T>+` are Move-only owners of one acquisition. T shall be
complete, sized, inhabited, not `+void+`, and shall contain no ordinary borrow, slice or
runtime `+str+`; otherwise `+R-DIAG-TYPE-001+` is required. Exact operations are
`+std.async::mutex_new(T value) -> std.async::mutex<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`,
`+std.async::clone_mutex(const std.async::mutex<T>* mutex) -> std.async::mutex<T>+`,
`+async std.async::lock(const std.async::mutex<T>* mutex) -> std.async::mutex_guard<T>+`,
`+std.async::try_lock(const std.async::mutex<T>* mutex) -> o<std.async::mutex_guard<T>>+`,
`+std.async::mutex_guard_ref(const std.async::mutex_guard<T>* guard) -> const T*+`,
`+std.async::mutex_guard_mut(std.async::mutex_guard<T>* guard) -> T*+`,
`+std.async::rwlock_new(T value) -> std.async::rw_lock<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`,
`+std.async::clone_rw_lock(const std.async::rw_lock<T>* lock) -> std.async::rw_lock<T>+`,
`+async std.async::read(const std.async::rw_lock<T>* lock) ->
std.async::rw_read_guard<T>+`, `+async std.async::write(const std.async::rw_lock<T>* lock)
-> std.async::rw_write_guard<T>+`, `+std.async::try_read(const std.async::rw_lock<T>*
lock) -> o<std.async::rw_read_guard<T>>+`, `+std.async::try_write(const
std.async::rw_lock<T>* lock) -> o<std.async::rw_write_guard<T>>+`,
`+std.async::rw_read_guard_ref(const std.async::rw_read_guard<T>* guard) -> const T*+`,
`+std.async::rw_write_guard_ref(const std.async::rw_write_guard<T>* guard) -> const T*+`,
`+std.async::rw_write_guard_mut(std.async::rw_write_guard<T>* guard) -> T*+` and
`+std.async::unlock(G guard) -> void+`, which consumes any of the three guards and is
equivalent to its destruction. The methods `+lock+`, `+try_lock+`, `+read+`, `+write+`,
`+try_read+`, `+try_write+` and `+clone+` of a lock and `+get+`, `+get_mut+` and
`+unlock+` of a guard name these operations. Each constructor value parameter follows
R-FUNC-0002: a named Move T requires `+move+`, and a failed constructor destroys the
value.

A handle and its clones share one lock and its value. Every handle, guard and started
acquisition keeps that shared state alive, and the value is destroyed exactly once, when
the last of them ends. An acquisition that cannot proceed waits without blocking an
executor worker. Waiting acquisitions are served in start order: an unlocking guard hands
the lock to the oldest waiting acquisition, so a mutex is never free while an acquisition
waits, and `+try_lock+`, `+try_read+` and `+try_write+` return `+o::none+` while the lock
is incompatible or an acquisition waits. A rw_lock admits the readers at the head of the
queue together once no writer holds it and gives a writer at the head exclusive access
once every holder has left; a reader that starts after a waiting writer waits behind it.
Cancelling a waiting acquisition removes it, and an acquisition selected before its
cancellation completes, so its guard is destroyed with the unobserved result
(R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007) and cancellation never loses the lock. The acquisitions have no
deadline parameter and no checked error besides the start error; like `+await+`, they are
bounded by a select with an until clause or a bounded group wait rather than by a deadline
block (Core R-STMT-0018, R-STMT-0019). A guard owns a reference to its lock instead of a
borrow, so it is unborrowed and may remain live across await (Core R-BORROW-0024), and a
borrow returned by a guard access is bounded by the borrow of the guard. These locks are
not poisoned: a guard destroyed during unwinding releases its lock like any other
destruction.

`+std.async::mutex<T>+` is Send and Sync iff T is Send; `+std.async::rw_lock<T>+` is Send
iff T is Send and Sync iff T is Send and Sync. `+std.async::mutex_guard<T>+` is Send iff T
is Send and Sync iff T is Sync; `+std.async::rw_read_guard<T>+` is Send and Sync iff T is
Sync; `+std.async::rw_write_guard<T>+` is Send iff T is Send and Sync, and Sync iff T is
Sync. The number of live handles, guards and started acquisitions of one lock shall not
exceed half the maximum value representable by `+usize+`; a clone or acquisition that
would exceed it causes `+reference_count_overflow+`.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0014]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0014* — `+std.async::semaphore+` is a Move-only handle of one shared count
of permits and `+std.async::semaphore_permit+` is a Move-only owner of one permit; both
are Send and Sync. Exact operations are `+std.async::semaphore_new(usize permits) ->
std.async::semaphore throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`, `+std.async::clone_semaphore(const
std.async::semaphore* semaphore) -> std.async::semaphore+`, `+async
std.async::acquire(const std.async::semaphore* semaphore) ->
std.async::semaphore_permit+`, `+std.async::try_acquire(const std.async::semaphore*
semaphore) -> o<std.async::semaphore_permit>+`,
`+std.async::release(std.async::semaphore_permit permit) -> void+`,
`+std.async::add_permits(const std.async::semaphore* semaphore, usize count) -> void+` and
`+std.async::available_permits(const std.async::semaphore* semaphore) -> usize+`, named by
the methods `+acquire+`, `+try_acquire+`, `+add_permits+`, `+available_permits+` and
`+clone+` of a semaphore and `+release+` of a permit. Destroying or releasing a permit
returns it. Returned and added permits go to waiting acquisitions in start order, and
`+try_acquire+` returns `+o::none+` when no permit is available or an acquisition waits.
Lifetime, waiting and cancellation follow R-SLIB-ASYNC-0013. The permits of one semaphore,
available and held, shall not exceed half the maximum value representable by `+usize+`; a
constructor or `+add_permits+` that would exceed it causes `+reference_count_overflow+`
before any change.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0015]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0015* — `+std.async::notify+` is a Move-only, Send and Sync handle of one
shared notification point. Exact operations are `+std.async::notify_new() ->
std.async::notify throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`, `+std.async::clone_notify(const
std.async::notify* notify) -> std.async::notify+`, `+std.async::notify_one(const
std.async::notify* notify) -> void+`, `+std.async::notify_all(const std.async::notify*
notify) -> void+` and `+async std.async::notified(const std.async::notify* notify) ->
void+`, named by the methods `+notify_one+`, `+notify_all+`, `+notified+` and `+clone+`.
`+notify_one+` completes the oldest waiting `+notified+`; when none waits it stores one
notification, at most one, which the next `+notified+` consumes at once. `+notify_all+`
completes every waiting `+notified+` and stores nothing. A cancelled waiter is skipped, so
a notification goes to the next waiter or is stored; a waiter completed before its
cancellation has consumed its notification. Lifetime, waiting and cancellation follow
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0013.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0016]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0016* — `+std.async::broadcast<T>+` is a Move-only handle that publishes
values to every subscriber, and `+std.async::broadcast_receiver<T>+` is a Move-only
subscription. T shall be Send and unborrowed and shall be cloned structurally
(R-OWN-0020): a Copy type, a standard owner or container of such types, or an aggregate
whose clone is derived; a type whose clone reaches a clone hook requires
`+R-DIAG-TYPE-001+`, because a receive clones outside the control flow of the program.
Exact operations are the compiler-recognized
`+std.async::broadcast::<T>(usize capacity) -> std.async::broadcast<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`,
`+std.async::clone_broadcast(const std.async::broadcast<T>* sender) -> std.async::broadcast<T>+`,
`+std.async::subscribe(const std.async::broadcast<T>* sender) -> std.async::broadcast_receiver<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`,
`+std.async::publish(const std.async::broadcast<T>* sender, T value) -> usize throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
and
`+async std.async::broadcast_receive(const std.async::broadcast_receiver<T>* receiver) -> std.async::broadcast_result<T>+`,
named by
the methods `+subscribe+`, `+send+` and `+clone+` of a broadcast and `+receive+` of a
subscription. `+std.async::broadcast_result<T>+` has exactly `+received(T)+`,
`+lagged(u64)+` and `+closed+`.

A broadcast keeps its newest `+capacity+` values; a capacity of zero causes
`+contract_violation+`. `+publish+` stores the value once for every subscriber that exists
when it runs and returns their number; with no subscriber it destroys the value and
returns zero, and a failed allocation destroys the value and throws. A subscription starts
after the newest value. A receive completes with a clone of the oldest value that its
subscription has not read; when that value was already overwritten it completes with
`+lagged(n)+`, where n counts the values the subscription missed, and the subscription
continues from the oldest value kept. A receive waits only when its subscription has read
every value, and once every broadcast handle is destroyed and the subscription has read
every value it completes with `+closed+`. A clone that fails to allocate causes
`+allocation_failure+`. A slow subscription therefore holds no value beyond the capacity.
Cancelling a waiting receive removes it without moving its subscription.
`+std.async::broadcast<T>+` is Send and Sync iff T is Send,
`+std.async::broadcast_receiver<T>+` is Send iff T is Send and never Sync, and
`+std.async::broadcast_result<T>+` derives its capabilities from its payloads.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017* — In the hosted-native-async profile the compiler-recognized call
`+std.async::blocking(entry, arguments...) -> task<R throws E...> throws std.async::start_error+`
runs `+entry+` on the blocking call pool of Core R-TERM-0015. Its operands follow Core
R-MEM-0013 for an unscoped spawn: `+entry+` is a direct designator of a module-level safe
R function that is neither `+async+` nor `+extern+`, followed by one exactly typed
argument for each parameter; every by-value argument is Send, every borrow-bearing
component has the program region, and a named Move argument is the direct form
`+move place+`, staged as in Core R-FUNC-0010. R is the return type of the entry and E...
its canonical checked-error set; R and every E shall be Send, or R may be `+void+`. A
violation requires the diagnostic of R-MEM-0013 or R-MEM-0014. The call has the immediate
checked effect `+std.async::start_error+`: `+allocation_failed+` when the pool already
holds the `+maximum_pending_calls+` waiting calls of the target manifest or storage cannot
be allocated, `+runtime_stopping+` when the runtime has begun to stop, and `+scope_full+`
under R-SLIB-ASYNC-0002; a failed start leaves every staged argument with the caller.

A started call owns its arguments. Pool threads take calls in submission order, the pool
never runs more calls at once than the `+thread_count+` of the target manifest, and a call
that waits for a thread occupies no executor worker. The entry runs exactly once as
ordinary R code and may block its pool thread, for example in a C function; its returned R
or thrown E is the terminal outcome of the task, and a panic in it is a panic of the task
under Core R-AM-0014. Cancellation removes a call that no pool thread has taken, destroys
its arguments and completes the task as cancelled. A taken call is not interrupted: the
task is acknowledged only after the entry returns, and a cancellation selected before that
return destroys the returned R or E. The task is Send iff R and every E are Send. Calls
that wait for one another can exhaust the pool, so an entry shall not wait for a call
started after it.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0018]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0018* — `+std.async::task_id() -> u64+` returns the identifier of the task whose
code is running. Every task that the runtime starts has an identifier from one upward, unique
for the life of the process and given in start order: the task of an asynchronous `+main+` has
one, and each asynchronous call (R-EXPR-0020) starts a task with a new identifier, so an
awaited function reports the identifier of its own task. A function that
`+std.async::blocking+` runs on the blocking call pool (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017) reports the
identifier of the task of that call. Outside every task, such as in a synchronous `+main+` or
on a thread of `+std.thread+`, it returns zero. It allocates nothing and cannot fail.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0019]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0019* — In the hosted-native-async profile the file payload adapter of Core
R-TERM-0016 performs the byte transfer of std.fs operations on regular files: reads and
writes of an open file at its shared position or at the explicit offset of a positional
operation, append writes, the reads of a whole-file read and the data write of an atomic
file write. An admitted transfer runs as calls of the native entry points that the target
manifest records, on a thread that is not an executor worker at that time. At most the
`+maximum_entered_transfers+` of the target manifest are admitted at once; a transfer
beyond that bound waits for admission in arrival order and occupies no executor worker.
Submission retains the request, buffer, handle and task frame before task commit.
Cancellation or deadline expiry of a transfer that has not entered its native call
completes it as cancelled or timed out without entering the call. An entered call is not
preempted: a cancellation or deadline observed meanwhile is recorded, no further native
call is made for that transfer, and acknowledgement and publication wait for the return,
with the handle, buffer and frame live until then. The outcome follows R-SLIB-IO-0007 with
the exact progress of the returned calls. The descriptor of a closed or dropped file is
released only after no admitted transfer uses it.

[[R-SLIB-ASYNC-0020]]
*R-SLIB-ASYNC-0020* — `+std.async::join(task<T> operation) -> std.thread::join_result<T>+`
observes the panic of a task without continuing it. It is compiler-recognized and admitted
only as the complete operand of await, `+await std.async::join(move operation)+`, where
`+operation+` names one task without checked errors and is consumed as by the await of
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0005; a task with checked errors, any other operand or any other position
requires `+R-DIAG-ASYNC-001+`. The await suspends, publishes and is cancelled exactly as that
await. A task that completes normally yields `+returned(T)+`, or `+completed+` when T is
`+void+`. A task whose panic reached its root under unwind strategy (Core R-ERR-0009) yields
`+panicked(std.thread::panic_report)+`: the panic ends at the join, the awaiting task
continues normally and the report is not delivered to the panic hook. The report carries the
category of the panic and, for an explicit panic (Core R-ERR-0004), its message, cut to at
most 256 bytes at a scalar boundary, as text; other categories have empty text. A report
whose text cannot be allocated keeps its category and an empty text. Under abort strategy
the panic has already terminated the process.

[[io]]
== 15. Native asynchronous byte streams

[[R-SLIB-IO-0001]]
*R-SLIB-IO-0001* — `+std.io::error_code+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+closed+`, `+broken_pipe+`, `+permission_denied+`, `+resource_exhausted+`,
`+invalid_operation+`, `+cancelled+`, `+timed_out+` and `+other+`.
`+std.io::io_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.io::error_code code+` and `+i64 native_code+`. Exact conversion is
`+std.io::as_error(std.io::io_error value) -> std.error::error+`.

The classification for console, process-pipe and other non-filesystem standard streams
is exhaustive. `+closed+` denotes that the local stream identity is terminally closed;
an input peer that has ended normally instead produces `+end+`. `+broken_pipe+` denotes
that a write cannot continue because the peer no longer accepts bytes.
`+permission_denied+` denotes native access or security-policy denial.
`+resource_exhausted+` denotes R post-start output materialization failure or a native
buffer, descriptor, quota, memory or system-resource limit. `+invalid_operation+`
denotes a task-local invalid range or a requested direction/operation incompatible with
the live stream kind. `+cancelled+` and `+timed_out+` have the terminal-race meanings of
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007..0008. `+other+` denotes an otherwise unclassified native stream
failure, whether its status is known or unknown to the adapter. Task-local validation, specified no-op success, deadline testing and native
submission occur in the R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008 order; thereafter the first linearized native
completion or cancellation event controls. For one native status admitting multiple
descriptions, local terminal closure is classified before peer write closure, then
access denial, resource exhaustion and operation incompatibility in that order.
Pre-native validation, task cancellation and deadline errors have native code zero. A
native failure preserves its immediately captured status and uses the first matching
specific code above; a status with no such meaning uses `+other+`, including a
documented residual status. Files opened by
`+std.fs+` instead use the exhaustive adaptation in R-SLIB-FS-0005.

[[R-SLIB-IO-0002]]
*R-SLIB-IO-0002* — `+std.io::input+` and `+std.io::output+` are Move-only, Send+Sync
runtime-retained stream handles. Exact process-console operations are
`+std.io::stdin() -> std.io::input+`, `+std.io::stdout() -> std.io::output+` and
`+std.io::stderr() -> std.io::output+`. Each returned handle is an independently
retained view; destroying or closing it releases that view exactly once. The process
console remains owned by the runtime until process shutdown. For a non-console stream
identity, ordinary destruction releases its R view exactly once and does not cancel a
successfully started operation whose independent retain remains live. The native
identity closes after its last R view and operation retain drain. Console-view
destruction never closes the runtime-owned process console.

[[R-SLIB-IO-0003]]
*R-SLIB-IO-0003* — `+std.io::read_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly:

* `+read { array<u8> buffer; usize count; }+`;
* `+end(array<u8>)+`;
* `+failed { std.io::io_error error; usize count; array<u8> buffer; }+`.

Exact operation
`+async std.io::read(const std.io::input* stream, array<u8> buffer,
o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.io::read_result+` places at most `+len(buffer)+`
new bytes at indices starting at zero. Count is the number placed and never exceeds
length. `+end+` means a terminal end-of-stream was observed before a byte. An empty
buffer completes as `+read+` with count zero. For a nonempty buffer, `+read+` has count
in `+1..len(buffer)+`; native readiness with neither a byte, end nor an error remains
pending rather than publishing zero progress. Failure returns every byte placed before
the terminal error and the same owned buffer. In `+read+` and `+failed+`, exactly indices
`+0..count+` contain the newly read bytes and indices `+count..len(buffer)+` retain their
pre-call byte values. `+end+` preserves every buffer byte.

[[R-SLIB-IO-0004]]
*R-SLIB-IO-0004* — `+std.io::write_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly
`+written { array<u8> buffer; usize count; }+` and
`+failed { std.io::io_error error; usize written; array<u8> buffer; }+`.
`+std.io::write_all_result+` has exactly `+written(array<u8>)+` and
`+failed { std.io::io_error error; usize written; array<u8> buffer; }+`.
Exact operations are:

* `+async std.io::write(const std.io::output* stream, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.io::write_result+`;
* `+async std.io::write_all(const std.io::output* stream, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.io::write_all_result+`;
* `+async std.io::flush(const std.io::output* stream,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.io::io_error+`.

`+write+` attempts one native write and reports the accepted prefix. `+write_all+`
continues from the accepted prefix until every byte is accepted or a terminal condition
wins. Written is never greater than buffer length; all variants return the staged
buffer. For a nonempty buffer, `+write+` publishes `+written+` only with count in
`+1..len(buffer)+`; native readiness accepting no byte remains pending or publishes the
applicable exact error rather than a zero-progress success. Neither operation mutates any buffer byte; written identifies the accepted
prefix only. Flush orders all earlier writes for the same underlying stream identity
before successful completion.
For a zero-length buffer, `+write+` immediately returns `+written+` with count zero and
`+write_all+` immediately returns `+written+` with the owner; neither submits a native
operation nor causes an external side effect.
Reaching the stream's documented flush boundary is a non-cancellable commit point and
returns success despite a later cancel or deadline event.

[[R-SLIB-IO-0005]]
*R-SLIB-IO-0005* — `+std.io::shared_write_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly
`+written(arc (array<u8>))+` and
`+failed { std.io::io_error error; usize written; arc (array<u8>) buffer; }+`.
Exact operation
`+async std.io::write_shared(const std.io::output* stream, arc (array<u8>) buffer,
usize offset, usize length, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
std.io::shared_write_result+` transfers its strong reference at successful start. The
task validates `+offset..offset+length+` before native submission; range failure returns
`+failed+` with `+invalid_operation+`, zero written and the same owner. A valid range is
written with write-all semantics and every outcome returns that owner. The operation
treats the shared owner's bytes as immutable and never mutates them.
A valid zero-length range immediately returns `+written+` with the owner without native
submission or external side effect.

[[R-SLIB-IO-0006]]
*R-SLIB-IO-0006* — Exact terminal operations are
`+async std.io::close_input(std.io::input stream, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
void throws std.io::io_error+` and
`+async std.io::close_output(std.io::output stream, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
void throws std.io::io_error+`. They consume the handle, cancel its pending operations,
wait for native acknowledgement, release the view exactly once, and then return the
close outcome. A close error does not restore a handle whose view has reached terminal
release. An expired deadline does not skip those duties: after task-local validation the
operation consumes the handle and completes cancellation, acknowledgement and release.
A pre-existing terminal close failure detected before that deadline check is reported;
otherwise an already expired deadline reports `+timed_out+` after release. Filesystem
and network explicit close operations, including process-pipe handles, inherit this
terminal-cleanup rule through R-SLIB-IO-0006.

[[R-SLIB-IO-0007]]
*R-SLIB-IO-0007* — Operations submitted in one direction through the same underlying
stream identity are serviced in submission order. Input and output directions of a
duplex resource may progress concurrently. The stream retains independently acquired at
successful start keep native identity and buffers live through completion. Cancellation
and timeout use `+cancelled+` and `+timed_out+`; a positive partial count remains exact in
the failure payload. These operations allocate only task/runtime submission state and
any native retain; each allocation is completed before start commit.

[[R-SLIB-IO-0008]]
*R-SLIB-IO-0008* — The scoped stream operations (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012) are:

* `+async std.io::read_into(const std.io::input* stream, u8[] target,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.io::io_error+`;
* `+async std.io::write_from(const std.io::output* stream, const u8[] source,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.io::io_error+`;
* `+async std.io::write_all_from(const std.io::output* stream, const u8[] source,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.io::io_error+`.

Each is the scoped form of `+read+`, `+write+` and `+write_all+` of
R-SLIB-IO-0003..R-SLIB-IO-0004. `+read_into+` places at most `+len(target)+` bytes at
the start of `+target+` and returns their count, where zero means end of stream or a
zero-length `+target+`, which is a specified no-op success; `+write_from+` writes a
nonempty prefix of `+source+` and returns its length, and a zero-length `+source+` is a
specified no-op success returning zero; `+write_all_from+` writes every byte of
`+source+`. Positive progress, end-of-stream, submission ordering (R-SLIB-IO-0007),
cancellation and deadline semantics match the owning forms, with `+io_error+` reporting
`+cancelled+` and `+timed_out+`; a failed outcome carries only the error.

[[filesystem]]
== 16. Native asynchronous filesystem

[[R-SLIB-FS-0001]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0001* — `+std.fs::path+` is an opaque immutable Move-only, Send+Sync owner of
one native path. Exact synchronous path operations are:

* `+std.fs::path_from_utf8(str text) ->
  std.fs::path throws std.fs::path_error+`;
* `+std.fs::path_from_utf8_bytes(const u8[] bytes) ->
  std.fs::path throws std.fs::path_error+`;
* `+std.fs::path_clone(const std.fs::path* source) ->
  std.fs::path throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.fs::path_to_utf8(const std.fs::path* source) ->
  std.string::string throws std.fs::path_error+`;
* `+std.fs::path_join(const std.fs::path* base, const std.fs::path* component) ->
  std.fs::path throws std.fs::path_error+`;
* `+std.fs::path_is_absolute(const std.fs::path* source) -> bool+`.

`+std.fs::path_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync tagged type with exactly
`+invalid_utf8 { usize index; }+`, `+embedded_nul { usize index; }+`,
`+not_representable { usize index; }+`, `+absolute_component+` and
`+allocation_failed { std.alloc::alloc_error reason; }+`. Path validation and outcome
selection complete before result allocation. For a UTF-8 byte source, the implementation
determines the first reported index of an invalid UTF-8 sequence and of an embedded NUL
and reports whichever lexical violation has the lower index. A `+str+` source is already
valid UTF-8 and is checked for its first NUL. It then rejects an absolute or rooted
component where an operation requires a component, then reports the first source unit
not representable by the target. Only a lexically and semantically valid representable
input can report `+allocation_failed+`, so allocation pressure cannot mask an input
error. An index is a UTF-8 byte index for a UTF-8 source and a native path-element index
when `+path_to_utf8+` reports native conversion failure. Conversion allocates an owned native representation and
round-trips every path representable by the selected target. `+path_join+` reports
`+absolute_component+` when its component has a native root or prefix; otherwise it
inserts exactly one target separator between nonempty base and component as needed. An
empty base returns an owned copy of component, an empty component returns an owned copy
of base, and two empty operands return an empty path; no separator is inserted in those
three cases. The ordinary-operation empty-path rules of R-SLIB-FS-0002 still apply when
the resulting path is later used for filesystem access. `+path_is_absolute+` is true
exactly when the stored target-native path has a root or prefix that makes it absolute
under the selected target path rules, and false otherwise.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0002]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0002* — `+std.fs::error_code+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+invalid_path+`, `+invalid_relative_path+`, `+invalid_operation+`, `+not_found+`, `+already_exists+`,
`+not_directory+`, `+is_directory+`, `+directory_not_empty+`, `+permission_denied+`, `+read_only+`,
`+name_too_long+`, `+too_many_links+`, `+no_space+`, `+file_too_large+`,
`+resource_exhausted+`, `+cancelled+`, `+timed_out+`, `+unsupported+`, `+other+` and
`+closed+`. `+closed+` is appended after every previously defined enumerator, so its
addition does not change any earlier target ABI value.
`+std.fs::fs_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.fs::error_code code+` and `+i64 native_code+`. Exact conversion is
`+std.fs::as_error(std.fs::fs_error value) -> std.error::error+`.

The portable classification is exhaustive:

* `+invalid_path+` means an ordinary operation received an empty path, a native path
  spelling that is not a permissible pathname for that operation, or a final link that
  its explicit no-follow policy rejects;
* `+invalid_relative_path+` means a `+*_beneath+` path violates the lexical, link or
  confinement requirements of R-SLIB-FS-0012;
* `+invalid_operation+` means the parameters, handle state, access mode, offset or object
  kind are incompatible with the requested filesystem operation;
* `+not_found+` means a required path, component or namespace entry does not exist;
* `+already_exists+` means an operation requiring absence found the destination entry;
* `+not_directory+` means a component or target required to be a directory is not one;
* `+is_directory+` means an operation requiring a non-directory target found a directory;
* `+directory_not_empty+` means removal required an empty directory but found at least
  one entry;
* `+permission_denied+` means credentials, access control or confinement policy denied
  the requested access;
* `+read_only+` means the selected filesystem, volume or object is read-only for the
  requested mutation;
* `+name_too_long+` means a component or complete native path exceeds its target limit;
* `+too_many_links+` means link traversal or link-count growth exceeds the target limit;
* `+no_space+` means persistent storage, quota, inode or equivalent filesystem capacity
  is exhausted;
* `+file_too_large+` means the resulting file, offset or bounded whole-file result exceeds
  its target or operation limit;
* `+resource_exhausted+` means a non-storage process or system resource, or a permitted
  post-start data-dependent R allocation, is exhausted;
* `+cancelled+` means cancellation won the R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007 terminal race;
* `+timed_out+` means the operation deadline won that race or was already reached at the
  R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008 check;
* `+unsupported+` means a semantically valid operation or filesystem facility is not
  supported by the selected backend and target;
* `+closed+` means the operation observed a filesystem handle identity whose terminal
  close had already been selected or completed;
* `+other+` means an otherwise unclassified native failure status, whether its native
  value is known or unknown to the target adapter.

Task-local validation selects `+invalid_relative_path+`, `+invalid_path+` or
`+invalid_operation+` before the no-op/deadline ordering of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008. After
native submission, cancellation, deadline and completion use R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007; a
winning native failure is classified by the narrowest matching semantic code above,
with `+read_only+`, `+file_too_large+` and `+no_space+` taking precedence over the more
general `+permission_denied+` or `+resource_exhausted+`. `+other+` is considered last and
shall not represent any listed semantic condition; it may represent a documented native
status for which none applies. A failure established without a native
failure status has native code zero. A native completion, cancellation acknowledgement or
timeout status preserves the immediately captured native code.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0003]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0003* — `+std.fs::directory+` and `+std.fs::file+` are Move-only, Send+Sync
runtime-retained handles. `+std.fs::access+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+read+`, `+write+` and `+read_write+`; `+std.fs::create_mode+` is a Copy,
Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly `+existing+`, `+open_or_create+` and
`+create_new+`. `+std.fs::open_file_options+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public
fields:

* `+std.fs::access access+`;
* `+std.fs::create_mode create+`;
* `+bool truncate+`;
* `+bool append+`;
* `+bool follow_final_symlink+`.

Truncate requires write access; append requires write access and makes each submitted
write select the then-current end position as one serialized operation. An options
combination violating these constraints completes as `+invalid_operation+` before
native submission. `+access::read+` permits byte reads and rejects byte writes and
flushes, `+access::write+` permits writes and flushes and rejects reads, and
`+access::read_write+` permits all three. `+create_mode::existing+` requires an existing
non-directory file and never creates one. `+create_mode::open_or_create+` opens such a
file or atomically creates a new regular file when absent. `+create_mode::create_new+`
atomically creates a regular file only when absent and returns `+already_exists+` for
any existing final entry. An existing directory returns `+is_directory+`; another
incompatible final kind returns `+invalid_operation+`. After selection, `+truncate+`
sets file length to zero at the open commit point, while `+append+` retains the stated
per-write end selection.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0004]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0004* — Exact open operations are:

* `+async std.fs::open_directory(const std.fs::path* path,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.fs::directory throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::open_directory_beneath(const std.fs::directory* root,
  const std.fs::path* relative, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  std.fs::directory throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::open_file(const std.fs::path* path,
  std.fs::open_file_options options, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  std.fs::file throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::open_file_beneath(const std.fs::directory* root,
  const std.fs::path* relative, std.fs::open_file_options options,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.fs::file throws std.fs::fs_error+`.

Successful start independently retains the root and copies the path and options before
return. Successful completion owns one new handle; any error owns none. Native creation
or truncation of the selected file is the open operation's non-cancellable commit point;
after it commits, later cancellation or deadline observation returns the successful
handle outcome. For unrestricted `+open_file+`, `+follow_final_symlink == true+` follows
a final symbolic link using the target path rules, while false returns `+invalid_path+`
without following a final symbolic link. Intermediate components use the unrestricted
target path semantics documented under R-SLIB-IDB-0005. Beneath behavior, including
rejection of true and of every link component, is fixed separately by R-SLIB-FS-0012.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0005]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0005* — File byte and cursor operations are exactly:

* `+async std.fs::read(const std.fs::file* file, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.io::read_result+`;
* `+async std.fs::write(const std.fs::file* file, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.io::write_result+`;
* `+async std.fs::write_all(const std.fs::file* file, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.io::write_all_result+`;
* `+async std.fs::flush(const std.fs::file* file,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::seek(const std.fs::file* file, std.fs::seek_origin origin,
  i64 offset, o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> u64 throws std.fs::fs_error+`.

`+std.fs::seek_origin+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+start+`, `+current+` and `+end+`. Read, write and seek share one synchronized file
position unless append is active; operations affecting that position follow submission
order. Seek chooses a u64 base of zero for `+start+`, the shared position at its
serialized turn for `+current+`, and the file length observed at that turn for `+end+`.
It adds the signed i64 offset mathematically. A negative result or a result greater than
the maximum value representable by `+u64+` returns `+invalid_operation+` without changing position; otherwise success
atomically sets and returns that exact u64 position. A regular file may be positioned
beyond its current end without extending it; only a later successful write extends the
file. A directory, pipe, device or other non-seekable identity returns
`+invalid_operation+`. In append mode seek still controls the read/shared observation
position, while each write selects end independently as R-SLIB-FS-0003 requires and
does not change that read/shared observation position. Without append, a `+read+`
outcome advances the shared position by `+count+`, and a failed read advances it by the
failure `+count+`; a write outcome advances it by `+count+`, a failed write or
write-all advances it by `+written+`, and successful write-all advances it by the
original buffer length. Each addition is exact because published progress cannot exceed
the supplied buffer length and the file-position range was validated by the adapter.
An `+end+` outcome, an empty-buffer no-op and a failure before positive byte progress do
not change the position. In append mode the same progress extends or changes file bytes
at the selected end but leaves the read/shared observation position unchanged.
A successful flush makes earlier writes visible with the target's documented
durability guarantee. The std.io result error has the same native code as the
corresponding filesystem failure. Its portable classification is exhaustive: a
released or closed file identity maps to `+closed+`; a pipe or device whose peer no
longer accepts data maps to `+broken_pipe+`; access denial or read-only media maps to
`+permission_denied+`; allocator, descriptor, quota, no-space or file-size exhaustion
maps to `+resource_exhausted+`; an incompatible access mode, offset, file kind, device
request or unsupported file operation maps to `+invalid_operation+`; cancellation and
deadline expiration map to `+cancelled+` and `+timed_out+`; any otherwise-unclassified
native status maps to `+other+`. End-of-file is the `+end+` read outcome, not an error. Every
mapping preserves the immediately captured native code. Reaching the
documented flush durability boundary and a successful seek's position change are their
respective non-cancellable commit points; after either commit the corresponding flush or
seek returns success despite a later cancel or deadline event. File read, write and
write-all inherit every ownership, byte-preservation, count, positive-progress,
zero-length, partial-failure and EOF rule of R-SLIB-IO-0003..0004; cancellation or a
deadline after partial byte progress therefore returns the declared failed outcome and
does not become success merely because the shared position advanced.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0006]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0006* — `+std.fs::file_kind+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+regular+`, `+directory+`, `+symlink+`, `+character_device+`, `+block_device+`,
`+fifo+`, `+socket+` and `+other+`. `+std.fs::metadata+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with
public fields `+std.fs::file_kind kind+`, `+u64 size+`,
`+o<std.time::system_time> created+`, `+o<std.time::system_time> modified+` and
`+o<std.time::system_time> accessed+`. Exact operations are:

* `+async std.fs::metadata(const std.fs::path* path,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.fs::metadata throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::metadata_beneath(const std.fs::directory* root,
  const std.fs::path* relative, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  std.fs::metadata throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::file_metadata(const std.fs::file* file,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.fs::metadata throws std.fs::fs_error+`.

A missing or unrepresentable timestamp is `+o::none+`; size is zero for a kind without
a meaningful byte size. Unrestricted `+metadata+` inspects the final namespace entry
without following a final symlink. `+metadata_beneath+` likewise inspects the final
namespace entry, reports `+symlink+` for a final symbolic link and never opens or
resolves that link's target; it otherwise applies R-SLIB-FS-0012. File metadata observes
the object identity retained by the handle.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0007]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0007* — `+std.fs::directory_iter+` is a Move-only, Send+Sync owner of one
directory enumeration. `+std.fs::directory_entry+` is a Move-only struct with public
fields `+std.fs::path name+` and `+std.fs::file_kind kind+`; name is one entry component,
is non-empty, contains no separator, and is neither `+.+` nor `+..+`, so it is admissible
as one relative component under R-SLIB-FS-0012. Exact creation is
`+async std.fs::iterate(const std.fs::directory* directory,
o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.fs::directory_iter throws std.fs::fs_error+`.

`+std.fs::directory_next_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly
`+entry { std.fs::directory_iter iterator; std.fs::directory_entry entry; }+`,
`+end+` and
`+failed { std.fs::fs_error error; std.fs::directory_iter iterator; }+`.
Exact advancement is
`+async std.fs::next(std.fs::directory_iter iterator,
o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.fs::directory_next_result+`.
Entry and failure return the same enumeration ownership for a later call. End closes and
releases it. Each native entry is emitted at most once; ordering is the target directory
enumeration order documented under R-SLIB-IDB-0007. Ordinary destruction of a
`+directory_iter+` closes and releases its enumeration exactly once. A successfully
started `+next+` owns independent enumeration state until its terminal outcome. Only
`+entry+` commits one-entry advancement. `+failed+` preserves the logical next-entry
position; when native enumeration obtained an entry before failure publication, the
runtime caches that entry for the retry. `+end+` is the terminal commit and release.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0008]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0008* — Exact namespace mutation operations are:

* `+async std.fs::create_directory(const std.fs::path* path, bool recursive,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::create_directory_beneath(const std.fs::directory* root,
  const std.fs::path* relative, bool recursive, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  void throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::remove_file_beneath(const std.fs::directory* root,
  const std.fs::path* relative, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  void throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::remove_directory_beneath(const std.fs::directory* root,
  const std.fs::path* relative, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  void throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::rename_beneath(const std.fs::directory* source_root,
  const std.fs::path* source, const std.fs::directory* destination_root,
  const std.fs::path* destination, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  void throws std.fs::fs_error+`.

Recursive creation walks components in order and treats an existing directory
component as success. With recursive false, every parent shall already exist and the
final component shall be absent; an existing final entry returns `+already_exists+`.
With recursive true, an existing final directory is also success, while any required
component that is not a directory returns `+not_directory+`.
`+remove_file_beneath+` requires a non-directory entry and returns `+is_directory+` for
a directory. A final symbolic link is the non-directory entry itself: removing it leaves
the target unchanged. `+remove_directory_beneath+` requires an empty directory, returns
`+not_directory+` for another kind, including a final symbolic link whose target is a
directory, and returns `+directory_not_empty+` for a nonempty directory.
`+rename_beneath+` is atomic no-replace: any existing destination entry, including one
of a different kind, returns `+already_exists+` and leaves both entries unchanged. It
moves either a non-directory entry or a directory with its contents; moving a directory
into its own descendant returns `+invalid_operation+`. A target unable to perform the
requested capability-relative atomic move returns `+unsupported+` before mutation.
When the final source component is a symbolic link, rename moves that link entry and
never opens, resolves or changes its target. Rename publishes one native rename operation
and preserves the source when publication fails before its commit point. Creation of each missing
component is its own non-cancellable native commit; a later failure, cancellation or
deadline may leave already created parent components and reports that later outcome
until the requested final directory exists. Final directory creation, removal of the
requested entry and rename publication are terminal commit points: after one occurs,
the operation returns success despite a later cancel or deadline event.

A target without one native directory-creation entry point that enforces
R-SLIB-FS-0012 may create one private staging directory entry directly beneath the same
retained capability and atomically publish it at the requested missing component through
the no-replace rename operation. The target manifest fixes the implementation-reserved
staging-component prefix, creation mode, collision retry bound and exhaustion mapping.
The same prefix and reserved namespace also serve the private-directory whole-file
publication mapping defined by R-SLIB-FS-0010; they are not exclusive to directory
creation. The reserved staging namespace is owned exclusively by the R runtime. Before native
submission, a safe `+std.fs+` filesystem operation shall reject any path operand that
selects a component in that namespace; `+*_beneath+` reports
`+invalid_relative_path+`, and an ordinary path operation reports `+invalid_path+`.
The staging-entry identity and exactly-once cleanup requirements of this paragraph and
R-SLIB-FS-0010 apply only while no independent native or foreign agent mutates the reserved staging
namespace. Such mutation is outside those identity and cleanup guarantees. It does not
relax capability confinement: even in its presence, the implementation shall never
resolve, move or publish an entry outside the retained capability. Staging never follows
a symbolic link, never leaves the retained capability and never makes the requested
target exist before atomic publication. A collision never overwrites an entry and
consumes one bounded retry. Exhaustion returns `+resource_exhausted+` with the captured
native code. For this directory-creation mapping, every pre-publication error,
cancellation or deadline removes the staging entry exactly once; native acknowledgement
and terminal publication wait until that cleanup is complete. Successful publication
consumes the staging entry exactly once and is the missing component's creation commit.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0009]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0009* — Exact whole-file reads are
`+async std.fs::read_file(const std.fs::path* path, usize limit,
o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> array<u8> throws std.fs::fs_error+` and
`+async std.fs::read_file_beneath(const std.fs::directory* root,
const std.fs::path* relative, usize limit, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
array<u8> throws std.fs::fs_error+`. Success returns exactly the file bytes. Observing a
file larger than limit returns `+file_too_large+`; error releases any partial buffer.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0010]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0010* — `+std.fs::write_file_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly
`+committed(array<u8>)+` and
`+failed { std.fs::fs_error error; array<u8> data; }+`. Exact atomic publications are:

* `+async std.fs::write_file_atomic_no_replace(const std.fs::path* path,
  array<u8> data, o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.fs::write_file_result+`;
* `+async std.fs::write_file_atomic_no_replace_beneath(const std.fs::directory* root,
  const std.fs::path* relative, array<u8> data, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  std.fs::write_file_result+`.

The operation writes and flushes private staging storage, then performs one atomic
no-replace publication. For the ordinary operation, a target selecting private-directory
staging creates a private directory directly beneath the destination parent. For the
beneath operation, it creates that directory directly beneath the independently retained
original root capability; it shall not mutate through a destination-parent descriptor
that a concurrent rename could move outside that capability. The directory has mode 0700,
subject to process umask, and contains exactly one regular payload file named by the target
manifest with mode 0600, subject to process umask. The generated directory component uses
the reserved prefix and collision policy of R-SLIB-FS-0008. The target-native asynchronous
payload backend writes all bytes, and the target's documented pre-publication
file-durability barrier shall succeed, before publication is attempted.

Publication moves only that payload file to the destination through one atomic
no-replace rename. For the beneath operation, that one native rename shall resolve both
the root-child staging payload source path and destination path relative to the same
independently retained original root capability; a descriptor derived for the destination
parent is not the authority for staging or publication. An existing destination returns
`+std.fs::error_code::already_exists+` without following or changing it. Every published
`+failed+` outcome occurs before publication, leaves the pre-existing namespace entry
unchanged, and returns the same data owner with byte-identical contents.

After a staging directory has been created, every pre-publication failure, cancellation
or deadline path and the successful-publication path shall execute exactly one
allocation-free, non-cancellable staging cleanup. Before publication, cleanup removes
the payload file if present and then the private directory. After successful publication,
the payload is the destination entry and cleanup removes the now-empty private directory.
Terminal acknowledgement waits for that cleanup and for release of every staging
descriptor and payload-I/O root.

`+committed+` means the complete bytes became the destination and returns the same data
owner with byte-identical contents. Publication is the non-cancellable commit point.
After it, the implementation performs the target's documented destination-parent
metadata-durability action before terminal acknowledgement. A later cancellation,
deadline, staging cleanup, durability or close outcome shall neither replace nor convert
the already selected `+committed+` outcome into `+failed+`; the target manifest documents
the treatment of a post-commit durability failure.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0011]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0011* — Exact terminal operations are
`+async std.fs::close_file(std.fs::file file, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
void throws std.fs::fs_error+` and
`+async std.fs::close_directory(std.fs::directory directory,
o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.fs::fs_error+`. They consume and release
the handle with the terminal semantics of R-SLIB-IO-0006, including cancellation and
acknowledgement of its pending operations. Ordinary destruction instead releases only
that R view exactly once and does not cancel any successfully started operation; each
such operation's independent retain remains live. The native identity closes only after
the last R view and operation retain drain, and the runtime owns any delayed completion
duty.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0012]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0012* — Every `+*_beneath+` operation resolves from the supplied live
directory capability. Its relative path is non-empty and contains no empty, current
(`+.+`) or parent (`+..+`) component, native root or prefix, and no byte sequence that
the target could interpret as an ambiguous separator. Validation uses every separator
form recognized by the selected target and fails before external mutation. Every path
component, including the final component, is resolved without following a symbolic
link, junction or reparse point. Encountering one in a non-final
component returns `+invalid_relative_path+`. An operation that opens or resolves the
object designated by its final component also rejects a final link with
`+invalid_relative_path+`. Operations whose exact contract instead inspects, removes or
renames the final namespace entry treat a final symbolic link as that entry and never
open or resolve its target: `+metadata_beneath+` reports it as `+symlink+`,
`+remove_file_beneath+` removes it, `+remove_directory_beneath+` returns
`+not_directory+`, and `+rename_beneath+` moves it. Creation and no-replace publication
apply their exact existing-final-entry outcomes without following that entry.
`+open_file_beneath+` with
`+options.follow_final_symlink == true+` completes with `+invalid_relative_path+` before
native submission. Resolution retains the directory identity and uses
component-relative native operations so replacement, mount and TOCTOU races cannot
select an object outside that directory tree. A path failing this guarantee returns
`+invalid_relative_path+` or `+permission_denied+` before external mutation. Each
successful start owns independent retains of every directory and file identity used by
the native submission. A target-private component prefix reserved for the staging
mappings permitted by R-SLIB-FS-0008 and R-SLIB-FS-0010 is also rejected as
`+invalid_relative_path+` before external mutation and is documented under
R-SLIB-IDB-0005.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0013]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0013* — The scoped file operations (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012) are:

* `+async std.fs::read_into(const std.fs::file* file, u8[] target,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.io::io_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::write_from(const std.fs::file* file, const u8[] source,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.io::io_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::write_all_from(const std.fs::file* file, const u8[] source,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.io::io_error+`.

Each is the scoped form of the file `+read+`, `+write+` and `+write_all+` of
R-SLIB-FS-0005 and inherits its shared-position, byte-preservation, count,
positive-progress, zero-length, end-of-file, cancellation and deadline rules through
R-SLIB-IO-0008; a failed outcome carries only the `+std.io::io_error+`.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0014]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0014* — Durability of a file at a chosen level:

* `+async std.fs::sync(const std.fs::file* file, std.fs::sync_level level,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.fs::fs_error+`.

`+std.fs::sync_level+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly `+barrier+`,
`+device+` and `+media+`. Sync takes its turn in the order of the shared position of the file
like flush (R-SLIB-FS-0005), so it follows every earlier write of the file, and then waits until
those writes reach the guarantee of its level: `+barrier+` orders them, so that every later write
of the file reaches the storage medium after them, without waiting for the medium; `+device+`
hands their data and the metadata of the file to the storage device, which may still hold them
in a volatile cache; `+media+` makes them persistent on the medium, which is the guarantee of
flush. A target documents the guarantee it reaches for each level (R-SLIB-IDB-0006); a target
without an ordering barrier reaches `+barrier+` as `+device+`. A file opened without write
access returns `+invalid_operation+` without a native call, as flush does. Reaching the
guarantee of the level is the non-cancellable commit point; after it sync returns success
despite a later cancel or deadline event.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0015]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0015* — Positional file operations read and write at a given offset:

* `+async std.fs::read_at(const std.fs::file* file, u64 offset, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.io::read_result+`;
* `+async std.fs::write_all_at(const std.fs::file* file, u64 offset, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.io::write_all_result+`;
* `+async std.fs::read_at_into(const std.fs::file* file, u64 offset, u8[] target,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.io::io_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::write_all_at_from(const std.fs::file* file, u64 offset, const u8[] source,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.io::io_error+`.

`+read_at+` and `+write_all_at+` are the file `+read+` and `+write_all+` of R-SLIB-FS-0005 at
`+offset+` instead of the shared position, and `+read_at_into+` and `+write_all_at_from+` are
their scoped forms (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012); each inherits the ownership, byte-preservation, count,
positive-progress, zero-length, end-of-file, cancellation and deadline rules of the operation it
corresponds to. A positional operation takes its turn in the order of the shared position of the
file, but it neither uses nor changes that position. A read at or past the end of the file is
the `+end+` outcome, or count zero for `+read_at_into+`; a write past the end extends the file,
and the bytes between the previous end and `+offset+` read as zero. An offset greater than the
maximum value of `+i64+` and a positional write to a file opened in append mode return
`+invalid_operation+` without a native call; a file that cannot be positioned returns
`+invalid_operation+`.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0016]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0016* — Advisory locks of a file:

* `+async std.fs::try_lock(const std.fs::file* file, std.fs::lock_kind kind, u64 start,
  u64 length, o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> bool throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::lock(const std.fs::file* file, std.fs::lock_kind kind, u64 start, u64 length,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+async std.fs::unlock(const std.fs::file* file, u64 start, u64 length,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.fs::fs_error+`.

`+std.fs::lock_kind+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly `+shared+` and
`+exclusive+`. A lock covers `+length+` bytes from `+start+`, or with zero length every byte from
`+start+` on, including bytes beyond the current end. A lock belongs to the file value that set
it, that is to its open file: a lock of another file value, opened by this or another process,
conflicts with it when the ranges overlap and either lock is exclusive, while a request through
the same file never conflicts with that file's own locks and sets the kind of the range it names,
splitting or merging the ranges it already holds. Locks are advisory: they bound only other lock
requests, never reads or writes. A lock lasts until `+unlock+` removes it or the file is closed.
Lock operations take their turn in the order of the shared position of the file
(R-SLIB-FS-0005), so they follow every earlier write of the file and precede every later one.

`+try_lock+` sets the lock and returns `+true+`, or returns `+false+` when a lock of another file
conflicts with it. `+lock+` sets the lock, attempting again at growing intervals while a
conflicting lock remains, until it succeeds, the deadline passes (`+timed_out+`) or the task is
cancelled (`+cancelled+`); waiting is not queued, so another request may take the range first,
and a deadlock between files is not detected. `+unlock+` removes every lock of the file over the
range; removing a range without a lock succeeds. A shared lock requires read access and an
exclusive lock write access, otherwise `+invalid_operation+`; a range whose end exceeds the
maximum value of `+i64+` also returns `+invalid_operation+`. Setting or removing a lock is the
non-cancellable commit point; after it the operation returns success despite a later cancel or
deadline event.

[[R-SLIB-FS-0017]]
*R-SLIB-FS-0017* — Memory mappings of a range of a file, provided by the R part of
`+std.fs+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001):

* `+unsafe std.fs::map_file(const std.fs::path* path, u64 offset, usize length,
  std.fs::map_access access) -> task<std.fs::mapping throws std.fs::fs_error>
  throws std.fs::path_error, std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.fs::mapping::bytes(const std.fs::mapping* this) -> const u8[]+`;
* `+std.fs::mapping::bytes_mut(std.fs::mapping* this) -> u8[] throws std.fs::fs_error+`;
* `+std.fs::mapping::size(const std.fs::mapping* this) -> usize+`;
* `+std.fs::mapping::sync(const std.fs::mapping* this) -> task<void throws std.fs::fs_error>
  throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+std.fs::map_access+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly `+read_only+` and
`+read_write+`. `+std.fs::mapping+` is a Move, Send+Sync type holding `+length+` bytes of the
file at `+path+` from `+offset+` mapped into memory and shared with the file: the mapping reads
what the file holds, and with `+read_write+` a write through the mapping reaches the file.
`+map_file+` converts the path, then opens the file, maps the range and closes the file again
on the blocking call pool (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017), returning that call. The range shall be
nonempty and lie within a regular file: an empty range or a range past the end of the file is
`+invalid_operation+`, a directory `+is_directory+` and a file of another kind
`+invalid_operation+`; a missing file is `+not_found+`, missing permission, including
`+read_write+` of a file without write access, `+permission_denied+`, and exhausted address
space or descriptors `+resource_exhausted+`.

`+bytes+` returns the mapped range as a view anchored to the mapping (Core R-UNSAFE-0008) and
`+bytes_mut+` as an exclusive one, so the mapping cannot be moved or dropped while a view is
live; `+bytes_mut+` of a `+read_only+` mapping is `+invalid_operation+`. `+size+` returns
`+length+`. `+sync+` writes the changed pages of the mapping to the file and waits for that
write on the blocking call pool; the call keeps the pages mapped until it ends, so the mapping
may be dropped before it completes. The pages are unmapped when the mapping and every call of
its `+sync+` are gone.

`+map_file+` is unsafe: while the mapping lives, no process shall truncate the file below the
end of the range, since touching a page past the end of a file terminates the program, and
while a view of the mapping is live, nothing but that view shall write the bytes it covers,
whether another process, a write of the file by this program or another mapping of the same
range (Core R-SAFETY-SLICE-PARTS).

[[networking]]
== 17. Native asynchronous networking

[[R-SLIB-NET-0001]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0001* — `+std.net::ip_address+` is a Copy, Send+Sync tagged type with exactly
`+v4(u8[4])+` and `+v6(u8[16])+`; bytes are in network order.
`+std.net::socket_address+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.net::ip_address address+`, `+u16 port+` and `+u32 scope_id+`, where port is the
host-order numeric port. Scope_id is zero for v4 and for v6 addresses without a native
interface scope; other values are target interface indices. Every standard operation
accepting a socket_address validates, before native submission, that scope_id is zero
for v4 and is zero or a target-valid interface index for v6. Invalid input completes
through the operation's declared failure outcome with
`+std.net::error_code::invalid_address+`; `+udp_send_to+` also returns its staged buffer.
Every socket_address produced by DNS, accept, receive or an address-observation
operation satisfies the same invariant. Exact text operations are:

* `+std.net::parse_ip(str text) -> std.net::ip_address throws std.net::address_error+`;
* `+std.net::format_ip(std.net::ip_address address) ->
  std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+address_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.net::address_error_code code+` and `+usize index+`; its code is a Copy, Send+Sync
fieldless enum with exactly
`+empty+`, `+invalid_character+`, `+invalid_component+` and `+out_of_range+`. Parsing
accepts canonical or non-canonical IPv4 decimal dotted form and RFC-compatible IPv6
hexadecimal form. Formatting v4 emits exactly four decimal components without leading
zeroes, separated by dots. Formatting v6 emits eight network-order 16-bit fields as
lowercase hexadecimal without leading zeroes, then replaces the longest run of at least
two all-zero fields by one `+::+`; the first run is selected on equal length, and a
single zero field is never compressed. It never emits an embedded dotted-v4 suffix.
Both address types satisfy `+core::Format+` (Core R-TYPE-0046): an address is written as
`+format_ip+` writes it, and a socket address as `+a.b.c.d:port+` for v4, `+[v6]:port+`
for v6, or `+[v6%scope]:port+` with a nonzero `+scope_id+`, the port and scope in decimal.
A parsed IPv4 address has exactly four nonempty decimal components of value
0..255; leading zeroes are accepted and never select octal. IPv6 expands to exactly
eight 16-bit components. Each explicit hexadecimal component is nonempty and has one to
four digits; without compression all eight are explicit, while at most one `+::+` may
replace one or more zero components. An exact IPv4 form may represent only the final
32 bits. Family selection is IPv6 when the source contains a colon and IPv4 otherwise.

Parsing proceeds left to right and never allocates. Empty input reports `+empty+` at
index zero. The first byte outside the selected grammar's digit and separator alphabet
reports `+invalid_character+` at that byte. An empty, missing, supernumerary or misplaced
component reports `+invalid_component+` at the byte that establishes it, or at
`+len(text)+` when a required component is missing only at end. A second `+::+` reports
`+invalid_component+` at its first colon. The fifth hexadecimal digit of an IPv6
component, or the first decimal digit that makes an IPv4 component exceed 255, reports
`+out_of_range+` at that digit. An embedded IPv4 suffix applies the same IPv4 rules with
indices in the complete input. At any byte, invalid character has priority over the
component or range checks that would otherwise consume that byte; errors already
established at an earlier byte retain priority.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0002]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0002* — `+std.net::error_code+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+invalid_address+`, `+invalid_name+`, `+name_not_found+`, `+temporary_failure+`,
`+connection_refused+`, `+connection_reset+`, `+connection_aborted+`,
`+address_in_use+`, `+address_not_available+`, `+network_unreachable+`,
`+host_unreachable+`, `+not_connected+`, `+closed+`, `+permission_denied+`,
`+resource_exhausted+`, `+message_too_large+`, `+cancelled+`, `+timed_out+`,
`+unsupported+` and `+other+`. `+std.net::net_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with
public fields `+std.net::error_code code+` and `+i64 native_code+`. Exact conversion is
`+std.net::as_error(std.net::net_error value) -> std.error::error+`.

The portable classification is exhaustive:

* `+invalid_address+` means a socket address has an invalid family, scope or target
  representation under R-SLIB-NET-0001;
* `+invalid_name+` means the resolver input violates the presentation grammar of
  R-SLIB-NET-0003;
* `+name_not_found+` means the resolver produced a definitive negative name result;
* `+temporary_failure+` means the resolver reported a retryable temporary failure;
* `+connection_refused+` means a TCP peer or target stack actively refused establishment;
* `+connection_reset+` means an established stream was reset by its peer or the network;
* `+connection_aborted+` means an establishment or established connection was aborted by
  the local stack or a distinct native abort condition rather than reset;
* `+address_in_use+` means the requested local endpoint conflicts with an existing binding;
* `+address_not_available+` means the requested local address or interface is unavailable,
  or a Unix-domain path names no socket;
* `+network_unreachable+` means no route to the destination network is available;
* `+host_unreachable+` means a route exists at network scope but the destination host is
  unreachable;
* `+not_connected+` means a live socket operation requires an established association or a
  peer that the socket does not have;
* `+closed+` means the underlying network identity is closing or closed;
* `+permission_denied+` means credentials or network policy denied the requested operation;
* `+resource_exhausted+` means socket, descriptor, buffer, system or permitted
  data-dependent R allocation resources are exhausted;
* `+message_too_large+` means one UDP datagram cannot be submitted within the target
  datagram limit; receive truncation remains successful under R-SLIB-NET-0008;
* `+cancelled+` means cancellation won the R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007 terminal race;
* `+timed_out+` means the operation deadline won that race or was already reached at the
  R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008 check;
* `+unsupported+` means a semantically valid socket family, option or operation is not
  supported by the selected backend and target;
* `+other+` means an otherwise unclassified native failure status, whether its native
  value is known or unknown to the target adapter.

Task-local address/name validation precedes the no-op/deadline ordering of
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008. After submission, R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007 orders cancellation, deadline and
completion. Definitive DNS absence takes `+name_not_found+`, while only a retryable DNS
condition takes `+temporary_failure+`. A refused TCP connect always takes
`+connection_refused+`, never `+connection_aborted+` or `+other+`; peer reset takes
`+connection_reset+`. `+closed+` applies to a closing/closed identity and takes precedence
over `+not_connected+`, which applies only to a live identity. Every native status
matching a listed meaning uses the narrowest listed code; `+other+` is considered last
and may represent a documented status with no narrower portable meaning. Pre-native failure has
native code zero; more generally, any failure without a native failure status has zero.
A native completion, cancellation acknowledgement or timeout status preserves the
immediately captured native code.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0003]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0003* — `+std.net::family+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+any+`, `+v4+` and `+v6+`. Exact DNS operation
`+async std.net::resolve(str host, u16 port, std.net::family family,
o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> array<std.net::socket_address> throws
std.net::net_error+` copies host into task-owned storage before successful start. The
task validates it before native resolver submission; host is an ASCII DNS presentation
name or numeric IP spelling, and invalid input returns `+invalid_name+`. A value
containing `+:+`, or containing at least one dot and otherwise only ASCII decimal digits
and dots, is a numeric candidate: it is accepted exactly when `+std.net::parse_ip+`
accepts it and is not reinterpreted as a DNS name after numeric parsing fails. Every
other value is checked as a DNS name. A DNS name has one or
more labels separated by single dots and may have one final dot; excluding that final
dot it is at most 253 bytes, each label is 1..63 bytes, each byte is ASCII alphanumeric
or hyphen, and the first and last byte of each label are alphanumeric. Embedded NUL,
empty or interior-empty labels and every other byte are invalid. These checks precede
native resolution. `+family::v4+` retains only v4 addresses, `+family::v6+` retains only
v6 addresses, and `+family::any+` retains both. A valid numeric candidate is converted
directly and subjected to the same filter; a v4/v6 mismatch therefore produces
`+name_not_found+` with native code zero and performs no DNS query. Success returns every
accepted address retained by that filter with the requested host-order port in a nonempty array,
contains no duplicates, and preserves the
target resolver order documented under R-SLIB-IDB-0008. Name failure and temporary
resolver failure use their distinct portable codes. Definitive absence, including zero
addresses after requested-family filtering, is `+name_not_found+`; only a resolver status
that explicitly permits retry is `+temporary_failure+`. Success never returns an empty
array.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0004]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0004* — `+std.net::tcp_listener+`, `+std.net::tcp_stream+` and
`+std.net::udp_socket+` are Move-only, Send+Sync runtime-retained handles.
`+std.net::listen_options+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+u32 backlog+`, `+bool reuse_address+` and `+bool v6_only+`.
`+std.net::tcp_connection+` is a Move-only struct with public fields
`+std.net::tcp_stream stream+` and `+std.net::socket_address peer+`.
Ordinary destruction of each network handle releases that R view exactly once.

Exact TCP establishment operations are:

* `+async std.net::tcp_listen(std.net::socket_address local,
  std.net::listen_options options, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  std.net::tcp_listener throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_accept(const std.net::tcp_listener* listener,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::tcp_connection throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_connect(std.net::socket_address remote,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::tcp_stream throws std.net::net_error+`.

Backlog zero selects the target's documented positive default. Successful accept owns a
new stream and its observed peer address. Address validation follows R-SLIB-NET-0001.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0005]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0005* — `+std.net::tcp_read_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly
`+read { array<u8> buffer; usize count; }+`, `+end(array<u8>)+` and
`+failed { std.net::net_error error; usize count; array<u8> buffer; }+`.
`+std.net::tcp_write_result+` has exactly
`+written { array<u8> buffer; usize count; }+` and
`+failed { std.net::net_error error; usize written; array<u8> buffer; }+`.
`+std.net::tcp_write_all_result+` has exactly `+written(array<u8>)+` and
`+failed { std.net::net_error error; usize written; array<u8> buffer; }+`.

Exact TCP data operations are:

* `+async std.net::tcp_read(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::tcp_read_result+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_write(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::tcp_write_result+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_write_all(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::tcp_write_all_result+`.

Buffer, count, partial-progress and EOF semantics match R-SLIB-IO-0003..R-SLIB-IO-0004
with `+net_error+` replacing `+io_error+`.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0006]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0006* — `+std.net::shutdown_direction+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum
with exactly `+read+`, `+write+` and `+both+`. Exact TCP observation and terminal
operations are:

* `+std.net::tcp_local_address(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream) ->
  std.net::socket_address throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+std.net::tcp_peer_address(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream) ->
  std.net::socket_address throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+std.net::tcp_listener_local_address(const std.net::tcp_listener* listener) ->
  std.net::socket_address throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_shutdown(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream,
  std.net::shutdown_direction direction, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  void throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_close(std.net::tcp_stream stream,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_listener_close(std.net::tcp_listener listener,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`.

Address observations are bounded native queries and allocate nothing.
`+tcp_local_address+` and `+tcp_peer_address+` return respectively the exact local and
peer endpoints of the established stream; either remains unchanged by half-close.
`+tcp_listener_local_address+` returns the exact endpoint bound to the listener,
including the actual ephemeral port chosen after binding port zero. A closing or closed
identity returns `+closed+`; a live identity without the required association returns
`+not_connected+`; every other failure follows R-SLIB-NET-0002 and preserves its native
code. Shutdown orders
earlier operations in the selected direction before publishing its half-close. Close
consumes the handle and applies R-SLIB-IO-0006 terminal ownership.
Successful `+read+` shutdown makes every later nonempty TCP read complete as `+end+`
without receiving further peer bytes; an empty read retains the R-SLIB-IO-0003 no-op
outcome with count zero. It does not affect writes. Successful `+write+` shutdown
publishes the native end-of-stream after every earlier submitted write, makes each later
nonempty write or write-all fail with `+not_connected+`, zero progress and its unchanged
owner, and leaves reads usable. An empty write or write-all retains the
R-SLIB-IO-0004 successful no-op outcome. `+both+` applies both effects. A shutdown whose selected
direction or directions are already shut is an idempotent success and does not repeat a
native half-close. Once the peer's end of stream has arrived, the read direction counts as
already shut: a later `+read+` shutdown succeeds without native publication, and `+both+`
publishes only the write-side half-close. Operations submitted earlier in a selected direction reach their
ordinary terminal outcomes before shutdown commits; the opposite direction may progress
concurrently. The peer observes write-side EOF only after the bytes of every earlier
successful write.
Native publication of the requested half-close is the shutdown commit point and yields
success despite a later cancel or deadline event.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0007]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0007* — Exact UDP establishment and observation operations are:

* `+async std.net::udp_bind(std.net::socket_address local, bool reuse_address,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::udp_socket throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+std.net::udp_local_address(const std.net::udp_socket* socket) ->
  std.net::socket_address throws std.net::net_error+`.

`+std.net::udp_send_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly
`+sent(array<u8>)+` and
`+failed { std.net::net_error error; array<u8> buffer; }+`.
`+std.net::udp_receive_result+` has exactly
`+received { array<u8> buffer; usize count; std.net::socket_address peer;
bool truncated; }+` and
`+failed { std.net::net_error error; array<u8> buffer; }+`.
`+udp_local_address+` is a bounded non-allocating query returning the exact endpoint
bound to the socket, including the actual ephemeral port chosen after binding port zero.
A closing or closed identity returns `+closed+`; every other query failure follows
R-SLIB-NET-0002 and preserves its native code.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0008]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0008* — Exact UDP data and terminal operations are:

* `+async std.net::udp_send_to(const std.net::udp_socket* socket,
  std.net::socket_address peer, array<u8> buffer,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::udp_send_result+`;
* `+async std.net::udp_receive_from(const std.net::udp_socket* socket,
  array<u8> buffer, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  std.net::udp_receive_result+`;
* `+async std.net::udp_close(std.net::udp_socket socket,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`.

A successful send accepts one complete datagram. A zero-length send submits and accepts
one actual empty datagram; it is not the stream-write no-op. Receive places one datagram prefix at
buffer index zero; truncated is true exactly when the datagram exceeded buffer length.
On `+received+`, exactly indices `+0..count+` contain the received prefix and indices
`+count..len(buffer)+` retain their pre-call byte values. A failed receive preserves every
buffer byte. Send never mutates any buffer byte. Every outcome returns the staged buffer. Close consumes the socket and applies
R-SLIB-IO-0006 terminal ownership. Native acceptance of a datagram is the send commit
point and returns `+sent+` despite a later cancel or deadline event.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0009]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0009* — For one TCP stream, reads are serialized in read submission order
and writes plus write shutdown are serialized in write submission order; the two
directions progress independently. Accepts for one listener and sends or receives for
one UDP socket are serialized within their respective operation direction. Successful
start acquires a native retain before returning. Cancel, deadline and completion obey
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007, preserve positive partial TCP progress, and publish one terminal
outcome.
Ordinary handle destruction does not cancel an operation whose start already succeeded;
