that operation's independent retain remains live. The native identity closes after the
last R view and operation retain drain. Explicit `+tcp_listener_close+`,
`+tcp_close+` and `+udp_close+` retain their documented cancellation, wait and terminal
release semantics.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0010]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0010* — `+std.net::datagram+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+usize count+`, `+std.net::socket_address peer+` and `+bool truncated+`. The scoped
network operations (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012) are:

* `+async std.net::tcp_read_into(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream, u8[] target,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_write_from(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream, const u8[] source,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::tcp_write_all_from(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream,
  const u8[] source, o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::udp_send_from(const std.net::udp_socket* socket,
  std.net::socket_address peer, const u8[] source, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  void throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::udp_receive_into(const std.net::udp_socket* socket, u8[] target,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::datagram throws std.net::net_error+`.

Each is the scoped form of the operation of R-SLIB-NET-0005 or R-SLIB-NET-0008 whose
name it extends: byte placement, count, end-of-stream, submission ordering
(R-SLIB-NET-0009), zero-length rules, cancellation and deadline semantics are those of
the owning form with `+net_error+` replacing the outcome payload, following the count
and no-op rules of R-SLIB-IO-0008 for TCP. `+udp_receive_into+` fills `+count+`,
`+peer+` and `+truncated+` exactly as `+received+` of R-SLIB-NET-0007 does, and a
zero-length `+udp_send_from+` remains a real native datagram submission.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0011]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0011* — `+import std.net;+` also loads the R part of the module
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0001), which connects by name:

* `+async std.net::tcp_connect_any(array<std.net::socket_address> candidates) -> std.net::tcp_stream throws std.net::net_error, std.async::start_error+`
  connects to the candidates in order with `+std.net::tcp_connect+` and returns the first
  stream; when every attempt fails it reports the error of the last one, an empty array
  reports `+invalid_address+` with native code zero, and an attempt that ends with
  `+cancelled+` or `+timed_out+` ends the connection at once;
* `+@scoped+`
  `+async std.net::tcp_connect_name(str host, u16 port) -> std.net::tcp_stream throws std.net::net_error, std.async::start_error+`
  resolves host for port with `+std.net::resolve+` and family `+any+` and connects to the
  addresses in resolver order with `+tcp_connect_any+`.

Neither takes a deadline argument: the deadline of an enclosing deadline block (Core
R-STMT-0019) bounds the resolution and every attempt.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0012]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0012* — `+std.net::tcp_options+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+bool nodelay+`, `+o<std.time::duration> keepalive+`, `+u32 hop_limit+`,
`+usize receive_buffer+` and `+usize send_buffer+`. `+std.net::udp_options+` is a Copy,
Send+Sync struct with public fields `+u32 hop_limit+`, `+usize receive_buffer+`,
`+usize send_buffer+`, `+bool broadcast+`, `+u32 multicast_hop_limit+` and
`+bool multicast_loop+`. Exact option operations are:

* `+std.net::tcp_get_options(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream) -> std.net::tcp_options throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+std.net::tcp_set_options(const std.net::tcp_stream* stream, std.net::tcp_options options) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+std.net::udp_get_options(const std.net::udp_socket* socket) -> std.net::udp_options throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+std.net::udp_set_options(const std.net::udp_socket* socket, std.net::udp_options options) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`.

They are bounded native calls that allocate nothing. A query returns the current values:
`+nodelay+` turns off the coalescing of small writes of the stream; `+keepalive+` is none while
keepalive probes are off and otherwise the idle time before the first probe in whole seconds;
`+hop_limit+` is the unicast hop limit of the socket's family; the buffer sizes are those the
target reports for the receive and send buffers; `+broadcast+` permits datagrams to a broadcast
address; `+multicast_hop_limit+` and `+multicast_loop+` are the hop limit of multicast
datagrams and whether the host receives its own. A change first validates the record: a hop
limit of 1..255, a multicast hop limit of 0..255, buffer sizes of 1..2^31 − 1 bytes and a
nonnegative keepalive, rounded up to whole seconds and at least one; any other value reports
`+unsupported+` with native code zero and changes nothing. It then applies, in field order,
only the fields that differ from the current values, so a record that was queried, changed in
one field and applied changes that option alone. A value that the target does not accept
reports `+unsupported+` with its native code, and the fields applied before it stay applied. A
closing or closed identity reports `+closed+`.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0013]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0013* —
`+std.net::udp_join_multicast(const std.net::udp_socket* socket, std.net::ip_address group, u32 interface_index) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`
and
`+std.net::udp_leave_multicast(const std.net::udp_socket* socket, std.net::ip_address group, u32 interface_index) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`
add the socket to and remove it from the multicast group on the interface of that index. Zero
lets the target choose the interface by its routes for the group, and a target without such a
route reports `+address_not_available+`. The group is a multicast address, 224.0.0.0/4 for v4
and ff00::/8 for v6, otherwise `+invalid_address+`; a group of the other family than the
socket's reports `+unsupported+`. Joining a group twice reports `+address_in_use+` and leaving a group
that the socket has not joined `+address_not_available+`; every other failure follows
R-SLIB-NET-0002. Both are bounded native calls that allocate nothing.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0014]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0014* — `+std.net::unix_listener+`, `+std.net::unix_stream+` and
`+std.net::unix_datagram+` are Move-only, Send+Sync runtime-retained handles of Unix-domain
sockets, whose addresses are file-system paths. A path is a nonempty `+str+` of at most 103
bytes without a zero byte; any other path reports `+invalid_address+` with native code zero.
Exact Unix-domain establishment operations are:

* `+async std.net::unix_listen(str path, u32 backlog, bool replace,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::unix_listener throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_accept(const std.net::unix_listener* listener,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::unix_stream throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_connect(str path, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  std.net::unix_stream throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_listener_close(std.net::unix_listener listener,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`.

`+unix_listen+` creates a socket file at the path; backlog zero selects the target's
documented positive default. An existing file at the path reports `+address_in_use+`, except
that with `+replace+` an existing socket file is removed first; closing the listener leaves
its file in place. Connecting to a path that names no socket reports
`+address_not_available+`, to a socket without a listener or with a full backlog
`+connection_refused+`, and a denied search or write `+permission_denied+`. Accept, connect,
close, ordering (R-SLIB-NET-0009), cancellation and deadlines follow `+tcp_accept+`,
`+tcp_connect+` and `+tcp_listener_close+`; an accepted stream has no observed peer address.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0015]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0015* — Exact Unix-domain stream data and terminal operations are:

* `+async std.net::unix_read_into(const std.net::unix_stream* stream, u8[] target,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_write_from(const std.net::unix_stream* stream, const u8[] source,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> usize throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_write_all_from(const std.net::unix_stream* stream,
  const u8[] source, o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_shutdown(const std.net::unix_stream* stream,
  std.net::shutdown_direction direction, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  void throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_close(std.net::unix_stream stream,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`.

The first three are scoped operations (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012). Byte placement, counts, end of
stream, half-close, ordering, cancellation, deadlines and terminal ownership are those of
`+tcp_read_into+`, `+tcp_write_from+`, `+tcp_write_all_from+` (R-SLIB-NET-0010),
`+tcp_shutdown+` and `+tcp_close+` (R-SLIB-NET-0006). `+std.net::unix_stream+` implements
`+std.stream::Reader+`, `+std.stream::Writer+` and `+std.stream::Stream+` through them
(R-SLIB-STREAM-0002).

[[R-SLIB-NET-0016]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0016* — `+std.net::peer_credentials+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public
fields `+u32 user_id+`, `+u32 group_id+` and `+i32 process_id+`. Exact operation
`+std.net::unix_peer_credentials(const std.net::unix_stream* stream) ->
std.net::peer_credentials throws std.net::net_error+` is a bounded native query that
allocates nothing. It returns the effective user and group identifiers and the process
identifier that the target recorded for the peer when the connection was established. A
closing or closed identity reports `+closed+`; every other failure follows R-SLIB-NET-0002.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0017]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0017* — `+std.net::unix_message+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+usize count+` and `+bool truncated+`. Exact Unix-domain datagram operations are:

* `+async std.net::unix_datagram_bind(str path, bool replace,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::unix_datagram throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_datagram_connect(str path, o<std.time::instant> deadline) ->
  std.net::unix_datagram throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_send_from(const std.net::unix_datagram* socket, const u8[] source,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_receive_into(const std.net::unix_datagram* socket, u8[] target,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.net::unix_message throws std.net::net_error+`;
* `+async std.net::unix_datagram_close(std.net::unix_datagram socket,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.net::net_error+`.

`+unix_datagram_bind+` creates a socket file at the path under the file rules of
`+unix_listen+` and receives the datagrams sent to it. `+unix_datagram_connect+` creates an
unnamed socket whose datagrams go to the socket at the path; a path that names no socket
reports `+address_not_available+` and a stream socket `+unsupported+`. A send goes to the
connected peer, and on a socket without one reports `+not_connected+`. Receive fills
`+count+` and `+truncated+` as `+udp_receive_into+` does and reports no sender. Datagram
submission, size limits, zero-length sends, ordering, cancellation, deadlines and close
follow R-SLIB-NET-0008..R-SLIB-NET-0010.

[[R-SLIB-NET-0018]]
*R-SLIB-NET-0018* — The R part of `+std.net+` (R-SLIB-NET-0011) also provides
`+std.net::ip_octets(std.net::ip_address value) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`, the
octets of the address in network order: four for an IPv4 address and sixteen for an IPv6 address,
the same address that `+format_ip+` writes.

[[processes]]
== 18. Processes and asynchronous pipes

[[R-SLIB-PROC-0001]]
*R-SLIB-PROC-0001* — `+std.process::error_code+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with
exactly `+invalid_command+`, `+invalid_argument+`, `+not_found+`,
`+permission_denied+`, `+resource_exhausted+`, `+spawn_failed+`, `+not_running+`,
`+cancelled+`, `+timed_out+`, `+unsupported+` and `+other+`.
`+std.process::process_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.process::error_code code+` and `+i64 native_code+`. Exact conversion is
`+std.process::as_error(std.process::process_error value) -> std.error::error+`.

The portable classification is exhaustive:

* `+invalid_command+` means the executable spelling or command state violates a
  pre-native command invariant;
* `+invalid_argument+` means an argument or environment entry cannot be represented as
  one exact bounded native value, including an embedded native terminator;
* `+not_found+` means the executable, a required loader/interpreter dependency or the
  requested working directory does not exist;
* `+permission_denied+` means the executable or another required object exists but access
  control denies search, execution or child creation;
* `+resource_exhausted+` means R allocation/size arithmetic or a native process, handle,
  descriptor or system resource limit prevents the operation;
* `+spawn_failed+` means a recognized executable-format, loader, wrong working-directory
  object kind or pre-commit child bootstrap failure occurred after the required objects
  were found and permitted;
* `+not_running+` means an operation requiring a running child observed that the child is
  already terminal; wait still reports its `+exit_status+` rather than this code;
* `+cancelled+` means cancellation won before the applicable non-cancellable commit;
* `+timed_out+` means the deadline won before that commit or was already reached at the
  R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008 check;
* `+unsupported+` means a semantically valid process, stream or environment facility is
  not supported by the selected backend and target;
* `+other+` means an otherwise unclassified native failure status, whether its native
  value is known or unknown to the target adapter.

Command validation precedes argument/environment validation, which precedes allocation,
native conversion and submission. R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008 and R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007 then determine
deadline/cancellation precedence, subject to each process commit point. A missing
executable always selects `+not_found+`, never `+spawn_failed+` or `+other+`. A
format/loader/bootstrap failure selects `+spawn_failed+`; any native failure with no
listed semantic classification selects `+other+`, whether its status is documented or
unknown. An R allocation or size failure selects `+resource_exhausted+` with
native code zero. Every native-mapped failure preserves the immediately captured native
code; every failure without a native failure status uses zero.

[[R-SLIB-PROC-0002]]
*R-SLIB-PROC-0002* — `+std.process::command+` is a Move-only, Send builder owning an
executable path, ordered argument strings, an environment policy, a working-directory
option, a captured current-directory resolution base and standard-stream policy; it is
not Sync. `+std.process::pipe_mode+` is a Copy,
Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly `+inherit+`, `+null_device+` and `+piped+`.
`+std.process::stdio+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.process::pipe_mode input+`, `+std.process::pipe_mode output+` and
`+std.process::pipe_mode error+`.

Exact synchronous builder operations are:

* `+std.process::command_create(const std.fs::path* executable) ->
  std.process::command throws std.process::process_error+`;
* `+std.process::arg(std.process::command* command, str value) ->
  void throws std.process::process_error+`;
* `+std.process::environment(std.process::command* command, str name, str value) ->
  void throws std.process::process_error+`;
* `+std.process::remove_environment(std.process::command* command, str name) ->
  void throws std.process::process_error+`;
* `+std.process::clear_environment(std.process::command* command) -> void+`;
* `+std.process::working_directory(std.process::command* command,
  const std.fs::path* path) -> void throws std.process::process_error+`;
* `+std.process::set_stdio(std.process::command* command,
  std.process::stdio policy) -> void+`.

Create and setters own copies independent of argument borrows. Each fallible setter is
transactional and preserves command on failure. `+command_create+` first rejects an
empty executable path as `+invalid_command+`; its path type has already established NUL
absence and native representability. It then captures the canonical inherited
environment under R-SLIB-ENV-0002 and an identity for the process current directory, in
that order, and publishes the command only after both captures succeed. A malformed
inherited entry is `+invalid_argument+`. For either capture, R allocation or checked-size
failure is `+resource_exhausted+` with native code zero; native access denial and native
resource limits are `+permission_denied+` and `+resource_exhausted+`; failure to resolve
the process current directory is `+not_found+`; and any remaining native failure follows
the residual `+other+` classification of R-SLIB-PROC-0001. The immediately captured
native code is preserved. No failure creates or exposes a partial command.

`+arg+` rejects the first NUL or first value not representable as one target-native
argument as `+invalid_argument+`. `+environment+` validates in this fixed order: a
nonempty name containing neither NUL nor `+=+`, native representability of the name,
absence of NUL in the value, and native representability of the value.
`+remove_environment+` applies the same name checks. These lexical and representability
checks all precede owned-copy allocation, so allocation pressure cannot mask invalid
input. `+working_directory+` first rejects an empty path as `+invalid_command+` and only
then copies the already validated native path. An absolute working directory is used
exactly; a relative one is resolved against the current-directory identity captured by
`+command_create+`. Every such
failure leaves the prior builder byte-for-byte and logically unchanged.

Arguments are distinct native argument
values in their insertion order. `+command_create+` initializes the first native
argument, argv[0] where that convention applies, to the executable's native spelling;
each `+arg+` appends one subsequent argument. It captures an inherited environment
snapshot, selects the captured current directory as the default child working directory,
and selects inherited stdin, stdout and stderr. `+environment+` adds or replaces
one key, `+remove_environment+` masks one inherited or explicit key, and
`+clear_environment+` discards the current inherited and explicit entries and selects an
empty base; later `+environment+` calls add to that base. Environment entries are distinct by the target's documented
environment-name equality.

[[R-SLIB-PROC-0003]]
*R-SLIB-PROC-0003* — `+std.process::child+` is a Move-only, Send+Sync runtime-retained
process handle. `+std.process::spawn_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly
`+spawned(std.process::child)+` and
`+failed { std.process::process_error error; std.process::command command; }+`.
Exact operation
`+async std.process::spawn(std.process::command command,
o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.process::spawn_result+` creates the child directly
from the owned executable, argument and environment arrays. Child creation is its
non-cancellable commit point. A pre-commit failure returns the same command; successful
creation returns the sole R child owner. Spawn-time validation or native failure cannot
partially consume any executable, argument, environment, working-directory or stream
policy state inside the returned command. The child starts with no blocked signal, whatever
the signal mask of the thread that spawns it, and with the dispositions that
R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0003 states.

[[R-SLIB-PROC-0004]]
*R-SLIB-PROC-0004* — Exact pipe extraction operations are
`+std.process::take_stdin(std.process::child* child) -> o<std.io::output>+`,
`+std.process::take_stdout(std.process::child* child) -> o<std.io::input>+` and
`+std.process::take_stderr(std.process::child* child) -> o<std.io::input>+`. A piped
endpoint is returned exactly once; an inherited, null-device or already extracted endpoint is
`+o::none+`. The returned handle independently retains its native endpoint and uses the
std.io async operations and ownership outcomes. Extraction transfers that parent
endpoint out of the child view; its lifetime is thereafter independent of child
destruction. Every unextracted parent endpoint remains owned by the child view.

[[R-SLIB-PROC-0005]]
*R-SLIB-PROC-0005* — `+std.process::termination_kind+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum
with exactly `+exited+`, `+signalled+` and `+other+`.
`+std.process::exit_status+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+std.process::termination_kind kind+`, `+i32 code+` and `+bool success+`. Success is true
exactly for kind `+exited+` and target success code zero.

`+std.process::wait_result+` is a Move-only tagged type with exactly
`+exited(std.process::exit_status)+` and
`+failed { std.process::process_error error; std.process::child child; }+`.
Exact operation
`+async std.process::wait(std.process::child child,
o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> std.process::wait_result+` consumes the observation
right. Every observed native terminal status maps totalistically to
`+std.process::exit_status+`; an unknown target reason uses kind `+other+` and the
documented i32 code mapping. Reap and successful result publication form one
non-cancellable commit. Every `+failed+` outcome occurs before reap and returns the same
retryable child; a cancel or deadline observed after reap therefore returns `+exited+`.

[[R-SLIB-PROC-0006]]
*R-SLIB-PROC-0006* — Exact child control and observation operations are:

* `+std.process::id(const std.process::child* child) -> u64+`;
* `+async std.process::terminate(const std.process::child* child,
  o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> void throws std.process::process_error+`.

At successful spawn commit the runtime captures the target child identity and maps it to
u64 by R-SLIB-IDB-0010. `+id+` returns that exact value without allocation, native query
or failure. It is stable while the child owner remains available, including after native
termination and before wait consumes/reaps the owner, and does not promise process-image
or cross-execution uniqueness. Terminate requests the target's forceful process termination and succeeds when the
request is accepted; wait remains the operation that observes and reaps exit. A
successful async start independently retains the child identity. Destroying a child
closes and releases every unextracted parent pipe endpoint exactly once and does not
close an extracted independent handle. It registers one nonblocking native wait with the
runtime; if and when that child terminates, the runtime observes and reaps it exactly
once. Ordinary destruction alone does not terminate a still-running child, and no
executor worker blocks while waiting. At orderly async-main or `+std.process::exit+`
shutdown, the runtime covers every unreaped child created by `+std.process+`, whether an
R child view is still live or was already dropped. It closes and releases every
remaining unextracted parent pipe endpoint, force-terminates every still-live child,
awaits native termination, and reaps each child exactly once before module/process
completion. `+std.process::abort+` is exempt from those duties.
Native acceptance of the termination request is its non-cancellable commit point and
returns success despite a later cancel or deadline event.

[[R-SLIB-PROC-0007]]
*R-SLIB-PROC-0007* — Exact current-process terminal operations are
`+std.process::exit(i32 status) -> never+` and `+std.process::abort() -> never+`.
Exit initiates orderly executor shutdown as for async main completion, drains cancelled
runtime operations, performs required module destruction, flushes runtime-owned process
streams, and then invokes C17 `+exit+` or the target equivalent exactly once with the
mapped status. When the caller is a task, or a thread that a task joins, that task and every
task waiting on it can never resume: shutdown cancels and drains every other task and does
not wait for them, and their frames are never released. A second concurrent exit waits for
the first to end the process. Abort invokes C17 `+abort+` or the target's Core-equivalent immediate
abnormal-termination primitive. It immediately reports an
abnormal termination through the Core abort strategy and performs no subsequent R
drops. Neither operation returns.

[[R-SLIB-PROC-0008]]
*R-SLIB-PROC-0008* — Spawn passes the executable and each argument as a separately
bounded native value and passes the constructed environment directly. It performs no
command-language interpretation and never searches `+PATH+` or another executable-search
list. An absolute executable denotes that exact path. A relative executable is resolved
against the command's explicit resolved working directory, or against its captured
current-directory identity when no explicit directory was set. The argv[0] value remains
the original executable spelling stored by the builder rather than the resolved path.
Native path, argument and environment conversion is
complete before the child-creation commit point. On conversion or native-start failure,
the child does not exist and the exact staged command owner is returned. After the
transactional builder checks, only executable existence and format/architecture,
execute permission, working-directory existence/kind/access, target process or pipe
resource availability, backend support, and namespace or policy races may be deferred
to spawn. A missing executable is always `+not_found+`; it is never classified as
`+invalid_command+`, `+spawn_failed+` or `+other+`.

=== 18.1 Process signals: `+std.signal+`

[[R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0001]]
*R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0001* — `+std.signal+` is a `+hosted-native-async+` module that delivers process
signals as task completions; no R code runs in a signal handler (Core R-MEM-0008).
`+std.signal::kind+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly `+terminate+`,
`+interrupt+`, `+hangup+`, `+user1+` and `+user2+`, for SIGTERM, SIGINT, SIGHUP, SIGUSR1 and
SIGUSR2. `+std.signal::listener+` is a Move-only, Send+Sync handle.
`+std.signal::listen(std.signal::kind kind) -> std.signal::listener throws std.process::process_error+`
creates a listener that counts every delivery of the signal of kind to the process from its
return until its last retain ends. While a listener of a kind exists, that signal takes
neither its default action nor the disposition the process had before: the first listener of a
kind registers the native source of the signal and only then replaces the disposition, and the
last one to end restores it. Failure reports `+resource_exhausted+` for exhausted resources,
allocation among them, and `+other+` for every other native failure, with its native code.

[[R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0002]]
*R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0002* —
`+async std.signal::next(const std.signal::listener* listener, o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> u64 throws std.process::process_error+`
completes with the number of deliveries that the listener counted since its creation or since
the previous completed `+next+`, at least one, and restarts the count. Deliveries counted
before the call complete it at once. The waits of one listener complete in submission order,
each with the deliveries counted after the previous one completed. Deadline and cancellation
follow R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007..R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008 with `+timed_out+` and `+cancelled+`; a wait that
they end leaves its deliveries counted for the next one. A successful start retains the
listener until the terminal outcome, so a wait keeps counting after the listener handle is
dropped.

[[R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0003]]
*R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0003* —
`+std.signal::raise(std.signal::kind kind) -> void throws std.process::process_error+` sends
the signal of kind to the process itself. With a listener of kind the delivery is counted like
any other; without one the signal takes its disposition, by default ending the process. A
child that `+std.process::spawn+` starts (R-SLIB-PROC-0003) begins with the default
disposition of every signal whose disposition a listener of this process replaced.

[[c-utilities]]
== 19. Safe C17 boundary utilities

[[R-SLIB-C-0001]]
*R-SLIB-C-0001* — The C numeric suffix set is exactly the available types among
`+c_char,c_schar,c_uchar,c_short,c_ushort,c_int,c_uint,c_long,c_ulong,c_llong,c_ullong,c_bool,c_wchar,c_wint,c_int8,c_uint8,c_int16,c_uint16,c_int32,c_uint32,c_int64,c_uint64,c_intptr,c_uintptr,c_intmax,c_uintmax,c_float,c_double,c_long_double,c_size,c_ptrdiff+`.
For each destination D in that set and each R or C numeric source S,
`+std.c::checked_D(S value) -> D throws std.convert::range_error+` is the exact closed
checked-conversion family and has R-SLIB-CONV-0004 value-preservation semantics. It is
compiler-recognized metanotation, allocates nothing and performs no overload search.

[[R-SLIB-C-0002]]
*R-SLIB-C-0002* — `+std.c::c_string+` is a Move-only, Send+Sync owner of a contiguous
sequence of C char values containing exactly one trailing zero and no earlier zero.
Exact operations are:

* `+std.c::string_from_str(str source) ->
  std.c::c_string throws std.c::string_error+`;
* `+std.c::string_as_slice(const std.c::c_string* source) -> const c_char[]+`;
* `+std.c::string_as_ptr(const std.c::c_string* source) -> raw const c_char*+`;
* `+std.c::validate_utf8(const c_char[] storage) -> str throws std.c::string_error+`;
* `+std.c::copy_utf8(const c_char[] storage) ->
  std.string::string throws std.c::string_error+`;
* `+std.c::string_as_error(std.c::string_error value) -> std.error::error+`.

Successful `+string_from_str+` owns a byte-identical copy of every source byte followed
by exactly one zero. `+string_as_slice+` exposes that complete storage including the
zero. The raw pointer designates its first element
and is valid only while the source borrow and owner remain live. Validation examines at
most the supplied slice, selects bytes before the first zero, and returns a `+str+` with
the storage borrow origin. C char elements are interpreted through their corresponding
unsigned object-representation byte, independently of plain-char signedness.
`+string_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync tagged type with exactly
`+missing_nul+`, `+embedded_nul { usize index; }+`,
`+invalid_utf8 { usize index; }+` and
`+allocation_failed { std.alloc::alloc_error reason; }+`. `+string_from_str+` reports the
first source NUL as `+embedded_nul+`, reports allocation failure with its reason and
constructs no `+c_string+` on either error. It scans the already-valid UTF-8 source for
NUL before any allocation, so embedded NUL has priority over allocation failure.
`+validate_utf8+` and `+copy_utf8+` first search the complete bounded slice for its first
zero; absence reports `+missing_nul+` even if preceding bytes would not form UTF-8. They
then validate exactly the preceding bytes and report `+invalid_utf8+` at the first
invalid byte. Successful `+validate_utf8+` returns a byte-identical view of exactly those
preceding bytes; successful `+copy_utf8+` owns a byte-identical copy of them.
`+validate_utf8+` allocates nothing. `+copy_utf8+` allocates only after both
checks succeed, so its allocation failure has last priority. Bytes following the first
zero are outside the selected C string and do not affect its result.
`+string_as_error+` uses domain `+c_abi+`, code
`+0x0000 + variant declaration index+` and native code zero; an index or embedded
allocation reason remains available only in the typed error.

[[R-SLIB-C-0003]]
*R-SLIB-C-0003* — `+std.c::handle+` is a Move-only owner holding a non-null
`+raw void*+` and an exact `+raw fn(raw void*) -> void+` C destructor. It is neither Send
nor Sync. Exact unsafe adoption and safe observation/release operations are:

* `+unsafe std.c::adopt_handle(raw void* pointer,
  raw fn(raw void*) -> void destructor) -> std.c::handle+`;
* `+std.c::handle_pointer(const std.c::handle* handle) -> raw void*+`;
* `+std.c::release_handle(std.c::handle handle) -> raw void*+`.

`+handle_pointer+` returns the exact non-null pointer originally adopted, without
allocation or ownership transfer; its usable validity remains bounded by the handle
borrow and the adopted resource obligation. The adoption safety contract requires a live uniquely owned foreign resource whose
destructor may be called exactly once on the current R thread and cannot unwind or
re-enter after runtime shutdown. Destruction invokes it once. Release consumes the R
obligation and transfers the still-live foreign obligation to the caller without
calling the destructor.

[[R-SLIB-C-0004]]
*R-SLIB-C-0004* — `+std.c::thread_attachment+` is a Move-only, non-Send, non-Sync proof
that the current foreign-origin thread is attached to the R runtime generation. Exact
adapter operations are
`+unsafe std.c::attach_thread() ->
std.c::thread_attachment throws std.c::runtime_error+` and
`+std.c::detach_thread(std.c::thread_attachment attachment) -> void+`; exact conversion
is `+std.c::runtime_as_error(std.c::runtime_error value) -> std.error::error+` with domain
`+c_abi+`, code `+0x0100 + variant declaration index+` and native code zero.
`+runtime_error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly
`+runtime_stopping+`, `+resource_exhausted+` and `+floating_environment_unavailable+`.
The unsafe caller establishes R-FFI-0055 entry prerequisites. Recoverable selection is
exhaustive and ordered: a runtime already stopping is `+runtime_stopping+`; otherwise
failure to reserve attachment/runtime-generation state is `+resource_exhausted+`;
otherwise inability to capture and later restore the current C floating environment is
`+floating_environment_unavailable+`. Each failure returns no attachment, releases all
provisional state and leaves the thread's generation membership and floating environment
unchanged. These fieldless errors and `+runtime_as_error+` have native code zero.
Attachment captures the C
floating environment and runtime generation; destruction or detach restores it after
all R values created in that attachment have ended.

[[R-SLIB-C-0005]]
*R-SLIB-C-0005* — `+std.c::target_info+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with public fields
`+u32 pointer_bits+`, `+bool c_wint_available+`, `+bool c_long_double_available+` and
`+bool hosted_native_async+`. Exact manifest queries are
`+std.c::target() -> std.c::target_info+` and
`+std.c::link_available(constexpr str logical_name) -> bool+`. They allocate nothing and
return the selected translation target and resolved logical-link facts used for the
current program. A value that does not match the R-FFI logical-link-name syntax returns
false without querying the manifest; a matching value reports its resolved availability.

[[R-SLIB-C-0006]]
*R-SLIB-C-0006* — Safe C utilities validate complete ranges, encodings, representability
and ownership state before a foreign call or transfer. Raw pointers returned by these
utilities gain no dereference permission; using one remains governed by its exact FFI
safety contract. A native C result is copied into an R value before its source storage,
locale view, `+errno+` or floating environment can be changed by another standard call.

[appendix]
[[json]]
== 20. JSON

[[R-SLIB-JSON-0001]]
*R-SLIB-JSON-0001* — `+std.json::value+` and `+std.json::number+` are Move, Send+Sync owners.
The former owns a JSON tree; the latter owns a validated, exact JSON number spelling.
`+std.json::error+` is a Move checked error with `+std.json::error_code code+`,
`+usize offset+`, and an owned `+std.string::string pointer+` containing a JSON Pointer.
`+std.json::value_kind+`, `+std.json::error_code+`, `+std.json::options+`, and
`+std.json::mode+` are Copy, Send+Sync types. In-memory JSON operations do not depend
on filesystem or networking modules. Inputs are call-bounded borrows; decoded values
never borrow the input. The tree operations are:

* `+std.json::parse(const u8[] source) -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::parse_with_options(const u8[] source, std.json::options options) -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::stringify(const std.json::value* source) -> std.string::string throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::stringify_with_options(const std.json::value* source, std.json::options options) -> std.string::string throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::kind(const std.json::value* source) -> std.json::value_kind+`;
* `+std.json::len(const std.json::value* source) -> usize+`;
* `+std.json::get(const std.json::value* source, usize index) -> o<const std.json::value*>+`;
* `+std.json::find(const std.json::value* source, const u8[] key) -> o<const std.json::value*>+`;
* `+std.json::key_at(const std.json::value* source, usize index) -> str+`;
* `+std.json::boolean(const std.json::value* source) -> bool+`;
* `+std.json::text(const std.json::value* source) -> str+`;
* `+std.json::null() -> std.json::value+`;
* `+std.json::from_bool(bool source) -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::from_string(const u8[] source) -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::from_number(const std.json::number* source) -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::array() -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::object() -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::append(std.json::value* target, std.json::value source) -> void throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::insert(std.json::value* target, const u8[] key, std.json::value source) -> void throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::parse_number(const u8[] source) -> std.json::number throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::number_text(const std.json::number* source) -> str+`;
* `+std.json::number_is_zero(const std.json::number* source) -> bool+`;
* `+std.json::name_equal(const u8[] left, const u8[] right, bool ignore_case) -> bool+`;
* `+std.json::take_index(std.json::value* target, usize index) -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::take_field(std.json::value* target, const u8[] key) -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;

* `+std.json::marshal(const T* source) -> std.string::string throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::marshal_with_options(const T* source, std.json::options options) -> std.string::string throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::unmarshal(const u8[] source) -> T throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::unmarshal_with_options(const u8[] source, std.json::options options) -> T throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;

These schema operations generate concrete converters without runtime reflection. The decode
result type comes only from a complete local initializer or simple assignment. Assignment
commits after successful decoding. Automatic typed decoding consumes scanner tokens directly;
partial construction destroys only initialized components and never calls the aggregate drop
body before construction completes. All non-excluded fields are required, including optional
value types. Absent optional strings are constructed as empty owners. Name matching first
tries exact names, then allowed Unicode 17.0.0 simple-folded matches with ASCII '-' and '_'
removed. Ambiguity and repeated aliases are errors. Unknown keys are rejected when
`+reject_unknown_fields+` is true; skipped input is still strictly validated.

`+string+` quotes the numeric representation of the field itself; it does not recurse into
arrays or structures. Integer conversion uses exact ranges without a floating intermediate;
fractional or exponent spellings cannot initialize integers. Fixed arrays require exact length.
`+omitempty+` omits JSON null, empty strings, arrays and objects; zero and false remain.
`+omitzero+` examines the original value recursively, including excluded fields, before any
conversion; `+omitnone+` omits only none. A surviving field is converted once and then tested
for `+omitempty+`. Protected fields are excluded. Fields are emitted in declaration order.
The default limits are depth 256 and 64 MiB per document or issued root-array element.

Automatic schemas cover Boolean and numeric values, char, owned strings, fixed and dynamic
arrays, lists, dictionaries with owned string keys, structures, enum/error payloads, and closed
generic applications with supported components. Bytes have the same numeric-array representation
as array<u8>. An enum without payload is its variant name; payload forms use one object member:
`+"None"+`, `+{"Some":42}+`, `+{"Fields":{"x":1}}+`. Option none is null; nested options
require an explicit converter. Borrowed strings and slices can be encoded but decoding cannot
create a borrow of input. Exact JSON numbers preserve their validated spelling; mathematical zero
includes signed zero and exponents. NaN and infinities are not JSON numbers.

Defaults are evaluated once, only for missing optional or excluded fields, in declaration order
after member validation. Missing optional scalars use zero, false or U+0000; strings and dynamic
containers use their ordinary empty constructors; options use none. Fixed arrays and structures
require a valid R default; other types need an explicit default. Owners are never initialized by
zeroing their bytes. Factory errors leave the assignment target unchanged. A factory is a safe,
synchronous, zero-argument function returning exactly the field type, with only JSON and allocation
checked errors. Scalar and string literals follow ordinary contextual type rules.

Each direction may independently use a safe, synchronous associated converter in the type's module:

[source,r]
----
std.json::value User::json_marshal(const User* value)
    throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error;
User User::json_unmarshal(const std.json::value* value)
    throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error;
bool User::json_is_zero(const User* value);
----

A converter replaces automatic conversion only in its direction. Decoding materializes the converter's
subtree, and its borrow ends with the call. The zero hook has no checked errors and takes priority over
structural zero. Hooks run at most once for the relevant value; early zero/none omission does not invoke
the encoding hook. With `+string+`, a custom encoder must produce a number, while a custom decoder
receives the number parsed from the quoted input. The parameter does not recursively quote fields.
Generic hooks match their schema's parameters and normalized constraints; `+json_encode+` and
`+json_decode+` are independent capabilities, not consequences of Copy or POD.

Tree accessors return borrowed values: absent/wrong-kind get or find yields none, invalid key_at
and wrong-kind text yield an empty view, and wrong-kind boolean yields false. Inspect kind when this
distinction matters. Len returns the array/object element count, and zero for other kinds. Mutations
require unique access. Append and insert consume their source operand as ordinary Move parameters;
the target is unchanged on failure. Take removes and returns an owner. These operations preserve
storage order and cannot invalidate a live borrow. No arbitrary movement from borrowed fields is added.

`+embed+` flattens a structure without JSON converters, a dictionary with owned string keys,
or a JSON value containing an object. It creates no separate input key; child field contracts
remain in force. An expanded object has at most one dictionary/object collector. Static expanded
name conflicts are compile-time errors. Unknown keys enter that collector only outside strict
unknown-key mode. A collector with no incoming keys is constructed as an empty dictionary/object.
Output keeps declaration order with embedded children expanded in place. A collector key conflicting
with any declared name, including its effective case policy, raises key_conflict even if the declared
field is omitted. Input aliases targeting the same expanded field are duplicate keys. The shared
schema tracks every initialized component; a completed embedded object runs its ordinary drop once,
while a partial object destroys only completed children.

`+std.json::decoder<T>+` is a Move owner of scanner state and a partially built T. Its Send
and Sync properties follow T. `+std.json::feed_state+` and `+std.json::feed_result+` are Copy,
Send+Sync; the result has `+usize consumed+` and `+std.json::feed_state state+`. States are
`+need_input+`, `+value_ready+` and `+end+`.

* `+std.json::new_decoder(std.json::options options) -> std.json::decoder<T> throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::feed(std.json::decoder<T>* decoder, const u8[] fragment, bool final) -> std.json::feed_result throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::take(std.json::decoder<T>* decoder) -> T throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

New_decoder obtains T from its complete initializer or assignment target; omitting options selects
the defaults. Feed consumes each fragment only for the call and owns unfinished tokens. A ready
value must be taken before feeding again. Take initializes its result only after a complete value
has been validated. Document mode validates trailing input before value_ready; sequence and
array_elements modes stop at each value and report the consumed prefix. End means clean completion,
whereas EOF inside a value fails. Limits apply to each returned value, excluding the outer array
in array_elements mode. Failure poisons the decoder and destroys initialized components immediately;
destruction also releases a partial result or a ready value that has not been taken. Automatic
converters construct T directly, and only explicit DOM values or converter subtrees build a tree.

`+std.json::reader<S>+` owns S, buffered unread bytes and persistent scanner state. S is exactly
`+std.fs::file+`, `+std.io::input+` or `+std.net::tcp_stream+`. `+std.json::detached<S>+` owns
the returned handle and unread bytes. Both are Move, Send+Sync.

* `+std.json::new_reader(S source, std.json::options options) -> std.json::reader<S> throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::read_next(std.json::reader<S>* reader, o<std.time::instant> deadline) -> task<o<T> throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error, TransportError> throws std.async::start_error+`;
* `+std.json::detach(std.json::reader<S> reader) -> std.json::detached<S> throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::take_handle(std.json::detached<S>* detached) -> S throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.json::take_bytes(std.json::detached<S>* detached) -> bytes throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

New_reader requires `+move source+`; omitting options uses the defaults. Reservations precede
consuming the handle, so failure preserves its owner. Read_next obtains T from the complete
initializer or simple assignment of its task, or from the local initialized by direct await.
T must prove json_decode, Send and unborrowed. TransportError is std.io::io_error for file/input
and std.net::net_error for TCP. None means clean end, including an empty or whitespace-only
transport in every reader mode; EOF inside a value is a JSON error.
One read may be active per reader. A concurrent operation reports invalid_state without modifying
the active operation. Successful task start independently retains reader state. Start_error leaves
scanner, handle and unread bytes unchanged. JSON errors, allocation errors during parsing, transport
errors and cancellation after start clear the partial value and poison further JSON reads.
Cancellation acknowledges the transport before releasing its buffers. Detach requires `+move reader+`
and no active operation; it permits recovery of unread bytes after a failed parse. Its accessors
consume their respective component once; dropping the detached owner releases remaining components.

[[R-SLIB-JSON-0002]]
*R-SLIB-JSON-0002* — `+std.json::schema() -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`,
called as `+std.json::schema::<T>()+` with an explicit type argument and no value arguments,
returns a JSON Schema (draft 2020-12) of the JSON form that the automatic conversion of
R-SLIB-JSON-0001 writes and reads for T. The schema is derived from the same type model as
`+marshal+` and `+unmarshal+` when the program is translated; the call builds it as a value. In a
generic body T may depend on a type parameter that has the `+json_encode+` or `+json_decode+`
constraint; the schema is then derived for each instantiation, and an instantiation whose type
has no derived schema requires `+R-DIAG-JSON-001+`.
`+bool+` is `+{"type":"boolean"}+`; an integer type is `+{"type":"integer"}+` with the
`+minimum+` and `+maximum+` of its range; a floating type and `+std.json::number+` are
`+{"type":"number"}+`; `+char+` is a string of length 1; strings are `+{"type":"string"}+`;
`+std.json::value+` is the empty schema, which every value satisfies. Fixed arrays, dynamic
arrays, lists, slices and bytes are arrays of the schema of their element, a fixed array with
`+minItems+` and `+maxItems+` equal to its length; a dictionary with owned string keys is an
object whose `+additionalProperties+` is the schema of its values. `+o<T>+` is
`+{"anyOf":[S,{"type":"null"}]}+` for the schema S of T. A field with `+string+` is a string.

A structure is `+{"type":"object","properties":{...},"required":[...]}+`: a property per field
that is neither protected nor skipped, under its JSON name, in declaration order, with the
fields of an embedded structure in place; `+required+` lists the fields without `+optional+`,
and is left out when there are none. An embedded dictionary or JSON object makes
`+additionalProperties+` the schema of its values, or `+true+` for a JSON object. The
`+description+` of a field's `+@json+` contract (Core R-JSON-0001) becomes the
`+description+` of its property. An enum without payloads is `+{"type":"string","enum":[...]}+`
of its variant names; an enum with payloads is a `+oneOf+` of that string schema for its
fieldless variants and, for each payload variant, an object with exactly one required
property named by the variant whose schema is that of the payload. A structure or enum that
contains itself is written once: a reference to the root is `+{"$ref":"#"}+`, and another such
type is an entry of `+$defs+` named by its spelling with every character outside
`+[A-Za-z0-9._-]+` replaced by `+_+` and referred to with `+{"$ref":"#/$defs/NAME"}+`.

A type with a JSON converter of R-SLIB-JSON-0001 has no derived schema. It may define the
associated operation

[source,r]
----
std.json::value User::json_schema() throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error;
----

in its module, a safe synchronous function without parameters, like the converters. Where
such a type occurs, the schema refers to an entry of `+$defs+` named like those above, and the
call puts the value that the hook returns under that entry, once per type; for a root of such
a type the schema is a reference to its entry. A type with a converter and no hook, a nested
option, a type parameter, a dictionary with keys other than owned strings and a type without a
JSON form require `+R-DIAG-JSON-001+`. A schema that the hook returns is not checked against
the converters.

[[R-SLIB-JSON-0003]]
*R-SLIB-JSON-0003* — The R part of the module, loaded by `+import std.json;+`, provides
`+std.json::escape_html(str text) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`, the
text with `+<+`, `+>+` and `+&+` written as `+\u003c+`, `+\u003e+` and `+\u0026+` and U+2028
and U+2029 as `+\u2028+` and `+\u2029+`, the rest unchanged. Applied to JSON text it changes no
value, since outside strings JSON text holds none of these characters.

[[r-source-modules]]
== 21. Standard modules written in R

[[R-SLIB-RSRC-0001]]
*R-SLIB-RSRC-0001* — A standard module written in R is a module under the reserved `+std+`
root whose definition is an R source file shipped by the implementation and listed in its
library map with the least profile that provides it. The implementation translates the
module together with the importing program under Core R-MOD-0002: the program imports it
with `+import std.name;+`, its traits, types, functions and methods resolve like those of
any imported module, and its generic definitions instantiate from that source under Core
R-TYPE-0040. Such a module has no C symbol of its own: its items reach C17 only through the
translation of the program that uses them, so the module contributes no library target to
the link plan and no record to the C17 ABI inventory; the native provider that it may import
under R-SLIB-RSRC-0003 is the only C it brings. The library map names each module,
its source path and its profile; a program shall not declare a module under a reserved root
(Core R-MOD-0001), and an implementation shall not ship two sources for one module path.
A module that the implementation provides in C may have an R part listed in the library map
under the same path: `+import std.name;+` loads that part, its items resolve like those of
any imported module, and every name that the C part provides keeps its meaning.

[[R-SLIB-RSRC-0002]]
*R-SLIB-RSRC-0002* — Availability is cumulative under R-SLIB-PROFILE-0001: importing a
module written in R from a profile below the profile its library map entry names requires
`+R-DIAG-PROFILE-001+`. The modules of this section and their least profiles are
`+std.cmp+`, `+std.slice+` and `+std.text+` (`+freestanding+`), `+std.iter+`, `+std.set+`,
`+std.deque+`, `+std.heap+`, `+std.sorted+`, `+std.deflate+`, `+std.xml+`, `+std.arena+`
and the R part of `+std.alloc+` (`+allocation+`), `+std.regex+`, `+std.encoding+`, `+std.uuid+`, `+std.args+`, `+std.url+`, `+std.mime+`,
`+std.cbor+`, `+std.crypto+`, `+std.cose+`, `+std.jwt+` and the R
parts of `+std.bytes+`, `+std.string+`, `+std.hash+`, `+std.random+`, `+std.test+` and
`+std.time+` (`+hosted+`), and `+std.service+`, `+std.stream+`, `+std.bufio+`, `+std.console+`,
`+std.log+`, `+std.config+`, `+std.tls+`, `+std.http+`, `+std.dns+`, `+std.websocket+`,
`+std.jsonrpc+`, `+std.mcp+`, `+std.sqlite+`, `+std.metrics+`, `+std.trace+`, `+std.pool+`, `+std.postgres+`, `+std.oauth2+` and the R parts of `+std.net+` and `+std.fs+` (`+hosted-native-async+`). The rejection of an import below that profile
precedes every check of the module's items. In its least profile and in each higher one
every module translates: an item that needs a higher profile is declared under a module
`+@if+` on `+core::profile+` (Core R-META-0001). Every item below keeps the Core ownership, borrow and effect
rules of ordinary R code: an item that allocates declares the allocation error specified
by its contract, an item that returns a borrow borrows from the argument its signature
names, and no item panics except through Core arithmetic and bounds checks of its own
body.

[[R-SLIB-RSRC-0003]]
*R-SLIB-RSRC-0003* — A standard module written in R may import the C functions of a native
provider that the implementation ships with it through an `+extern "C"+` block under Core
R-FFI-0028..0044: the block names the provider with `+@link+` and its header with
`+@header+`, every import carries its `+@safety+` contract, and every call of an import is
in an unsafe context of the module. The implementation keeps a link manifest of its
library beside the library map, whose entries are the native providers and their
dependencies. When a program uses a module that imports a native provider, the entries of
that manifest are resolved together with those of the program's own manifest (Core
R-FFI-0030..0036); a program without a manifest of its own needs none, and a manifest of the
program that names a logical name of the library's manifest is rejected before translation.
The imports are verified against the header of the provider (Core R-FFI-0042) and bridged
like those of the program, and the provider joins the link plan of the program with the
dependencies that its entry names. The provider is no part of the public surface of the
standard library: only the items of the module that imports it are.

=== 21.1 Comparison: `+std.cmp+`

[[R-SLIB-CMP-0001]]
*R-SLIB-CMP-0001* — `+std.cmp::ordering+` is the Copy fieldless enum with the variants
`+less+`, `+equal+` and `+greater+`. `+std.cmp::Equal+` is the trait with the single
prototype `+bool eq(const Self* this, const Self* other)+`, and `+std.cmp::Ordered+` is
the trait with the single prototype
`+std.cmp::ordering cmp(const Self* this, const Self* other)+`. The module implements both
traits for `+bool+`, `+char+`, every R integer type and `+f32+`/`+f64+`: equality is value
equality; integers, characters and booleans (`+false+` before `+true+`) order by value;
floating values order by value with every NaN after every number and equal to every NaN,
so that `+cmp+` is a total order. A program implements the traits for its own types and
uses them as generic constraints. The module also implements both traits for `+str+`, by
its UTF-8 bytes, so that text orders by code point; for `+o<T>+`, none before some and
some by its value; and element by element in order, a sequence that is a prefix of a
longer one first, for tuples of two to eight elements, a fixed array `+T[N]+` and the
slices `+const T[]+` and `+T[]+`. From the allocation profile it also implements them for
`+array<T>+`, element by element, and from the hosted profile, which provides `+std.string+`,
for `+std.string::string+`, by its bytes. An
implementation over `+o<T>+`, a tuple, an array or a slice requires each element type to
implement the same trait (Core R-TYPE-0042). `+@derive(equal, ordered)+` implements the
traits for a program type from those of its fields (Core R-AGG-0012).

[[R-SLIB-CMP-0002]]
*R-SLIB-CMP-0002* — The helpers over any `+Ordered+` or `+Equal+` type are:

* `+std.cmp::min(const T* left, const T* right) -> const T*+` and
  `+std.cmp::max(const T* left, const T* right) -> const T*+`, returning the argument that
  is not greater respectively not less, the left one on equality;
* `+std.cmp::clamp(const T* value, const T* low, const T* high) -> const T*+`, returning
  `+low+` when the value is less than it, `+high+` when greater, and the value otherwise;
* `+std.cmp::is_less(const T* left, const T* right) -> bool+` and
  `+std.cmp::is_equal(const T* left, const T* right) -> bool+`.

Each result borrows from the arguments it may return.

=== 21.2 Iterators: `+std.iter+`

[[R-SLIB-ITER-0001]]
*R-SLIB-ITER-0001* — The sources are `+std.iter::slice_iter<T>+`, a `+core::Iterator+`
over a shared slice whose items are `+const T*+` in index order, created by
`+std.iter::of_slice(const T[] items) -> std.iter::slice_iter<T>+`;
`+std.iter::range_i32+`, created by `+std.iter::range(i32 low, i32 high) -> std.iter::range_i32+`
and yielding every `+i32+` from `+low+` up to but excluding `+high+`; and
`+std.iter::range_usize+`, created by
`+std.iter::range_of_usize(usize low, usize high) -> std.iter::range_usize+`. A slice
iterator borrows the slice it was created from for its whole life.

[[R-SLIB-ITER-0002]]
*R-SLIB-ITER-0002* — The adapters own their inner iterator, borrow the closure they apply
and pass every item to it by value. `+std.iter::map(I inner, const F* function) -> std.iter::map_iter<I, U, F>+`
yields `+function(item)+` for a closure constrained `+fn(I::Item) -> U+`;
`+std.iter::filter_map(I inner, const F* function) -> std.iter::filter_map_iter<I, U, F>+`
yields the payload of every `+o::some+` that a closure constrained `+fn(I::Item) -> o<U>+`
returns and skips every `+o::none+`; `+std.iter::take(I inner, usize limit) -> std.iter::take_iter<I>+`
yields at most `+limit+` items; `+std.iter::skip(I inner, usize amount) -> std.iter::skip_iter<I>+`
drops the first `+amount+` items; `+std.iter::enumerate(I inner) -> std.iter::enumerate_iter<I>+`
yields `+std.iter::indexed<T>+` values with the public fields `+usize index+` and
`+T value+`; `+std.iter::zip(A left, B right) -> std.iter::zip_iter<A, B>+` yields
`+std.iter::pair<L, R>+` values with the public fields `+L left+` and `+R right+` until
either side ends; and `+std.iter::chain(I first, I second) -> std.iter::chain_iter<I>+`
yields the items of `+first+` and then those of `+second+`. Every adapter type implements
`+core::Iterator+` with the item type stated here.

[[R-SLIB-ITER-0003]]
*R-SLIB-ITER-0003* — The consumers take the iterator by value and drive it to its end or to
the first decisive item:

* `+std.iter::count(I inner) -> usize+`;
* `+std.iter::fold(I inner, A initial, const F* function) -> A+` for a closure constrained
  `+fn(A, I::Item) -> A+`, applied left to right;
* `+std.iter::any(I inner, const F* predicate) -> bool+` and
  `+std.iter::all(I inner, const F* predicate) -> bool+` for `+fn(I::Item) -> bool+`, ending
  at the first item that decides the result;
* `+std.iter::position(I inner, const F* predicate) -> o<usize>+`, the zero-based index of
  the first item the predicate accepts;
* `+std.iter::find_map(I inner, const F* function) -> o<U>+` for `+fn(I::Item) -> o<U>+`,
  the first `+o::some+` payload;
* `+std.iter::last(I inner) -> o<I::Item>+` and `+std.iter::nth(I inner, usize index) -> o<I::Item>+`;
* `+std.iter::collect_array(I inner) -> array<I::Item> throws std.array::push_error<I::Item>+`
  and `+std.iter::collect_list(I inner) -> list<I::Item> throws std.list::push_error<I::Item>+`,
  appending every item in order.

[[R-SLIB-ITER-0004]]
*R-SLIB-ITER-0004* — Further sources and adapters:

* `+std.iter::reversed(const T[] items) -> std.iter::reversed_iter<T>+` yields shared borrows
  of the elements from the last to the first;
* `+std.iter::chunks(const T[] items, usize size) -> std.iter::chunks_iter<T>+` yields
  consecutive subslices of size elements, the last one possibly shorter, and
  `+std.iter::windows(const T[] items, usize size) -> std.iter::windows_iter<T>+` every
  subslice of size adjacent elements in order; a size of zero, and for windows a size
  above the length, yields nothing;
* `+std.iter::filter(I inner, const F* predicate) -> std.iter::filter_iter<I, F>+` with
  `+F: fn(const I::Item*) -> bool+` yields the items for which the predicate holds; the
  predicate borrows each item.

[[R-SLIB-ITER-0005]]
*R-SLIB-ITER-0005* — `+std.iter::min(I inner) -> o<I::Item>+` and
`+std.iter::max(I inner) -> o<I::Item>+` with
`+@generic<I: core::Iterator, I::Item: std.cmp::Ordered>+` (Core R-TYPE-0043) return the
least respectively greatest item, the first of equal ones, or `+o::none+` for no item.
`+std.iter::Summable+` is the trait with the single prototype
`+Self plus(Self this, Self other)+`; the module implements it for `+i32+`,
`+i64+`, `+isize+`, `+u32+`, `+u64+`, `+usize+`, `+f32+` and `+f64+` by Core addition.
`+std.iter::sum(I inner, I::Item start) -> I::Item+` with
`+@generic<I: core::Iterator, I::Item: std.iter::Summable>+` adds every item to start in
order; an integer overflow panics as that addition does.

=== 21.3 Containers: `+std.set+`, `+std.deque+`, `+std.heap+`, `+std.sorted+`

[[R-SLIB-SET-0001]]
*R-SLIB-SET-0001* — `+std.set::set<T>+` is a Move-only set of dictionary keys (Core
R-TYPE-0034 constraint `+key+`) stored in a `+dict<T, bool>+`, so that iteration follows
insertion order. Its methods are `+set<T>::create() -> std.set::set<T>+`;
`+insert(set<T>* this, T value) -> bool throws std.dict::insert_error<T, bool>+`, true when
the value was absent; `+contains(const set<T>* this, const T* value) -> bool+`;
`+remove(set<T>* this, const T* value) -> bool+`, true when it was present;
`+count(const set<T>* this) -> usize+`; `+is_empty(const set<T>* this) -> bool+`;
`+clear(set<T>* this) -> void+`; and `+iter(const set<T>* this) -> std.set::set_iter<T>+`.
`+std.set::set_iter<T>+` implements `+core::Iterator+` with item `+const T*+` and borrows the
set for its whole life; a structural mutation of the set while an iterator is live is
rejected by the borrow rules. T is also `+unborrowed+` (Core R-TYPE-0033): an insertion
stores the caller's value through `+this+`, which Core R-BORROW-0019 admits only for a value
without views; this holds for every container of this section.

[[R-SLIB-DEQUE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-DEQUE-0001* — `+std.deque::deque<T>+` is a Move-only double-ended queue over two
arrays. Its methods are `+deque<T>::create() -> std.deque::deque<T>+`;
`+push_back(deque<T>* this, T value) -> void throws std.array::push_error<T>+` and
`+push_front(deque<T>* this, T value) -> void throws std.array::push_error<T>+`;
`+pop_front(deque<T>* this) -> o<T> throws std.array::push_error<T>+` and
`+pop_back(deque<T>* this) -> o<T> throws std.array::push_error<T>+`, which may move
elements between the two arrays and therefore declare the growth error: they reserve room for
all the elements before moving any, and without it take the element from the other array in
place, so that a pop neither loses nor reorders an element; `+front+` and
`+back+`, each `+(const deque<T>* this) -> o<const T*>+`; `+count(const deque<T>* this) -> usize+`;
`+is_empty(const deque<T>* this) -> bool+`; and `+clear(deque<T>* this) -> void+`. Both
ends push and pop in amortized constant time. T is `+unborrowed+` (R-SLIB-SET-0001).

[[R-SLIB-HEAP-0001]]
*R-SLIB-HEAP-0001* — `+std.heap::heap<T>+` is a Move-only binary max-heap over an array of
Copy, `+unborrowed+` elements constrained `+std.cmp::Ordered+`. Its methods are
`+heap<T>::create() -> std.heap::heap<T>+`; `+push(heap<T>* this, T value) -> void throws std.array::push_error<T>+`;
`+pop(heap<T>* this) -> o<T>+`, the greatest element; `+peek(const heap<T>* this) -> o<const T*>+`;
`+count(const heap<T>* this) -> usize+`; and `+is_empty(const heap<T>* this) -> bool+`.
`+push+` and `+pop+` take logarithmic time in the element count.

[[R-SLIB-SORTED-0001]]
*R-SLIB-SORTED-0001* — `+std.sorted::set<K>+` is a Move-only set of Copy, `+unborrowed+` keys
constrained `+std.cmp::Ordered+`, kept sorted in one array. Its methods are
`+set<K>::create() -> std.sorted::set<K>+`; `+insert(set<K>* this, K key) -> bool throws std.array::push_error<K>+`,
true when the key was absent; `+contains(const set<K>* this, const K* key) -> bool+`;
`+remove(set<K>* this, const K* key) -> bool+`; `+count(const set<K>* this) -> usize+`;
`+lower_bound(const set<K>* this, const K* key) -> usize+`, the position of the first key
not less than the argument; and `+as_slice(const set<K>* this) -> const K[]+`, the sorted
keys borrowed from the set. Lookups take logarithmic time; insertion and removal are linear
in the key count.

[[R-SLIB-SORTED-0002]]
*R-SLIB-SORTED-0002* — `+std.sorted::map<K, V>+` is a Move-only map from Copy, `+unborrowed+`
keys constrained `+std.cmp::Ordered+` to Copy, `+unborrowed+` values, kept in two arrays at
matching positions.
Its methods are `+map<K, V>::create() -> std.sorted::map<K, V>+`;
`+insert(map<K, V>* this, K key, V value) -> o<V> throws std.array::push_error<K>, std.array::push_error<V>+`,
returning the replaced value of an existing key and leaving the map unchanged when it fails;
`+get(const map<K, V>* this, const K* key) -> o<const V*>+`;
`+contains(const map<K, V>* this, const K* key) -> bool+`;
`+remove(map<K, V>* this, const K* key) -> o<V>+`, the removed value;
`+count(const map<K, V>* this) -> usize+`; and `+lower_bound(const map<K, V>* this, const K* key) -> usize+`.

=== 21.4 Slices and text: `+std.slice+`, `+std.text+`

[[R-SLIB-SLICE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-SLICE-0001* — The searches over a shared slice are, with the element constraints
`+std.cmp::Equal+` for equality and `+std.cmp::Ordered+` for order:

* `+std.slice::contains(const T[] items, const T* value) -> bool+` and
  `+std.slice::index_of(const T[] items, const T* value) -> o<usize>+`;
* `+std.slice::first(const T[] items) -> o<const T*>+` and
  `+std.slice::last(const T[] items) -> o<const T*>+`;
* `+std.slice::min_of(const T[] items) -> o<const T*>+` and
  `+std.slice::max_of(const T[] items) -> o<const T*>+`, the first extreme element;
* `+std.slice::is_sorted(const T[] items) -> bool+`, ascending order;
* `+std.slice::binary_search(const T[] items, const T* value) -> o<usize>+` over a sorted
  slice.

Each borrowed result borrows from `+items+`.

[[R-SLIB-SLICE-0002]]
*R-SLIB-SLICE-0002* — The in-place rearrangements of an exclusive slice are
`+std.slice::swap(T[] items, usize left, usize right) -> void+`;
`+std.slice::reverse(T[] items) -> void+`;
`+std.slice::rotate_left(T[] items, usize count) -> void+`, which moves the first
`+count+` elements to the end, and
`+std.slice::rotate_right(T[] items, usize count) -> void+`, which moves the last
`+count+` elements to the front, both keeping the order of each part;
`+std.slice::sift_down(T[] items, usize root, usize end) -> void+`, which restores the
max-heap property below `+end+` for the element at `+root+`;
`+std.slice::sort(T[] items) -> void+`, an in-place heapsort into ascending
`+std.cmp::Ordered+` order; and
`+std.slice::sort_by(T[] items, const F* compare) -> void+` with
`+F: fn(const T*, const T*) -> std.cmp::ordering+`, the same heapsort in the order
`+compare+` defines. None recurses, allocates or preserves the order of equal elements. A
Copy element is copied; any other element is exchanged in place by Core `+core::swap+` and
shall be unborrowed, and an element type that is neither is rejected when the function is
instantiated (Core `+R-DIAG-CONST-003+`). An index outside the slice panics under Core
R-EXPR-0021, and a rotation count above the slice length panics.

[[R-SLIB-TEXT-0001]]
*R-SLIB-TEXT-0001* — The helpers over `+str+` treat their arguments as byte sequences;
every position is a byte index and every comparison is exact byte equality, so a result
respects UTF-8 boundaries exactly when the arguments do. They are
`+std.text::starts_with(str subject, str prefix) -> bool+`;
`+std.text::ends_with(str subject, str suffix) -> bool+`;
`+std.text::find(str subject, str needle) -> o<usize>+`, the byte index of the first
occurrence; `+std.text::contains(str subject, str needle) -> bool+`;
`+std.text::count_byte(str subject, u8 value) -> usize+`;
`+std.text::is_ascii(str subject) -> bool+`; `+std.text::to_ascii_lower(u8 value) -> u8+`;
and `+std.text::equal_ignore_ascii_case(str left, str right) -> bool+`, which folds only
the ASCII letters. None of them allocates or panics. `+std.text::ascii_caseless+` is a
Copy struct holding a view of a subject, and
`+std.text::ignore_ascii_case(str subject) -> std.text::ascii_caseless+` makes one; it
implements `+core::CaseMatcher+` with `+Label = str+`, so a `+switch+` or `+match+` over
it selects the label equal to the subject with only the ASCII letters folded (Core
R-STMT-0006, R-EXPR-0031).

[[R-SLIB-TEXT-0002]]
*R-SLIB-TEXT-0002* — `+std.text::is_scalar_boundary(str subject, usize index) -> bool+` is
true for the length of subject and for the index of a byte that does not continue a scalar;
`+std.text::next_scalar_boundary(str subject, usize index) -> usize+` is the first boundary
after index, or the length, and
`+std.text::previous_scalar_boundary(str subject, usize index) -> usize+` the last boundary
before index, or zero. `+std.text::scalars(str subject) -> std.text::scalar_iter+` yields
the scalars of subject in order as `+char+` (a `+core::Iterator+` with `+Item = char+`).
`+std.text::trim(str subject) -> str+`, `+std.text::trim_start(str subject) -> str+` and
`+std.text::trim_end(str subject) -> str+` remove the ASCII whitespace (space, tab, line
feed, vertical tab, form feed and carriage return) at both ends, at the start or at the end,
and borrow from subject. `+std.text::to_ascii_upper(u8 value) -> u8+` folds one ASCII
letter to upper case. None of them allocates or panics.

[[R-SLIB-TEXT-0003]]
*R-SLIB-TEXT-0003* — `+std.text::split(str subject, str separator) -> std.text::split_iter+`
yields the pieces of subject between the occurrences of separator, found from left to right
without overlap, empty pieces included; an empty separator yields the whole subject once.
`+std.text::lines(str subject) -> std.text::lines_iter+` yields the lines of subject without
their line feed and without one carriage return before it; a final line feed ends the last
line and adds no empty one. Both are `+core::Iterator+` with `+Item = str+` borrowing from
subject, and neither allocates.

[[R-SLIB-TEXT-0004]]
*R-SLIB-TEXT-0004* — From the hosted profile, which provides `+std.string+`, and each throwing
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+`: `+std.text::join(const str[] parts, str separator) -> std.string::string+` joins the parts with separator between each two;
`+std.text::replace(str subject, str pattern, str replacement) -> std.string::string+`
replaces every occurrence of pattern, found from left to right without overlap, and an
empty pattern replaces nothing; `+std.text::ascii_uppercase(str subject) -> std.string::string+` and `+std.text::ascii_lowercase(str subject) -> std.string::string+`
fold the ASCII letters and copy every other scalar.

=== 21.5 Regular expressions: `+std.regex+`

[[R-SLIB-REGEX-0001]]
*R-SLIB-REGEX-0001* — `+std.regex+` is an R-source module in the `+hosted+` profile.
`+std.regex::regex+` is a Move, Send+Sync owner of an immutable compiled program. Its
representation is protected; construction is through compilation. The program retains
neither the pattern nor searched text. `+std.regex::span+` is Copy, Send+Sync with
`+usize start; usize end;+`, a half-open byte range at UTF-8 scalar boundaries.
`+std.regex::options+` is Copy, Send+Sync with `+bool ignore_ascii_case; bool multiline;
bool dot_all; usize max_steps;+`. `+std.regex::default_options() -> std.regex::options+`
returns three false flags and `+max_steps = 10000000+`.

[[R-SLIB-REGEX-0002]]
*R-SLIB-REGEX-0002* — `+std.regex::error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync declared error with
`+std.regex::error_code code; usize offset;+`. The Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum
`+std.regex::error_code+` has exactly `+invalid_pattern+`, `+unsupported+`,
`+program_limit+`, `+step_limit+`, `+invalid_options+` and `+invalid_offset+`.
The offset is a byte position in the pattern for compile errors, in the searched text
for search errors, and zero for invalid options. `+std.regex::compile(str pattern) ->
std.regex::regex throws std.regex::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` uses default options.
`+std.regex::compile_with_options(str pattern, std.regex::options settings) ->
std.regex::regex throws std.regex::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` copies its options and
rejects zero `+max_steps+`. Failure destroys partial state and preserves all caller inputs.

[[R-SLIB-REGEX-0003]]
*R-SLIB-REGEX-0003* — Pattern and subject are valid UTF-8 `+str+` values, including
embedded U+0000. Literals match exact Unicode scalars. Syntax comprises concatenation,
alternation `+|+`, grouping `+(...)+` and `+(?:...)+`, dot, anchors `+^+` and `+$+`,
classes `+[abc]+`, scalar ranges `+[a-z]+`, negated classes `+[^abc]+`, and
`+*+`, `+++`, `+?+`, `+{m}+`, `+{m,n}+`, `+{m,}+`. Empty patterns, groups
and alternatives are allowed. A group `+(...)+` captures and `+(?:...)+` does not; capturing
groups count from 1 in the order of their opening parentheses. Search selects the earliest starting
position and the longest match at that position, independently of alternative order.
Dot excludes LF unless `+dot_all+`. Anchors denote the absolute start/end, additionally
the positions after/before LF when `+multiline+`; dollar has no implicit pre-final-LF case.
`+ignore_ascii_case+` folds only ASCII letters, including class membership.
Escapes include escaped punctuation, `+\\n+`, `+\\r+`, `+\\t+`, `+\\f+`,
`+\\v+` and `+\\xHH+` for one scalar U+0000..U+00FF.
`+\\d+` is ASCII digits; `+\\w+` is ASCII letters, digits and underscore;
`+\\s+` is U+0009..U+000D and space. Uppercase forms negate these classes.
`+\\b+` and `+\\B+` assert an ASCII word boundary or its absence.
Positive class escapes are allowed inside brackets; negated class escapes and boundary
escapes there are unsupported. A bracket dash is literal at an edge or when escaped.
Unknown alphanumeric escapes, backreferences, lookaround, named groups, inline flags,
Unicode property escapes, lazy and possessive repetitions require `+unsupported+`;
malformed supported syntax requires `+invalid_pattern+`.

[[R-SLIB-REGEX-0004]]
*R-SLIB-REGEX-0004* — Search operations borrow the compiled program and subject for the
call only and throw `+std.regex::error+` or `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`:

* `+std.regex::find(const std.regex::regex* compiled, str text) -> o<std.regex::span>+`;
* `+std.regex::find_from(const std.regex::regex* compiled, str text, usize offset) ->
  o<std.regex::span>+`;
* `+std.regex::is_match(const std.regex::regex* compiled, str text) -> bool+`;
* `+std.regex::full_match(const std.regex::regex* compiled, str text) -> bool+`;
* `+std.regex::find_all(const std.regex::regex* compiled, str text) -> array<std.regex::span>+`.

`+none+` means no match; a zero-length span is a successful empty match.
`+find_from+` rejects an offset beyond the subject or inside a UTF-8 scalar, and preserves
absolute anchor and word-boundary context. `+is_match+` tests existence of a substring;
`+full_match+` requires the complete subject. `+find_all+` returns nonoverlapping spans
in order. After an empty match the next search advances one scalar; an empty match at EOF
is returned once. Empty matches adjacent to a preceding nonempty match are included.
These calls are synchronous and may run inside an async function; they do not suspend.

[[R-SLIB-REGEX-0005]]
*R-SLIB-REGEX-0005* — `+std.regex::replace_all(const std.regex::regex* compiled, str text,
str replacement) -> std.string::string throws std.regex::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
replaces the spans of `+find_all+` with literal replacement text, without interpreting
dollar or backslash substitutions. `+std.regex::split(const std.regex::regex* compiled,
str text) -> array<std.string::string> throws std.regex::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
returns the text between those same spans, retaining leading, trailing and adjacent empty
pieces. No match yields one piece containing the complete input. Results own their storage;
failure releases all partial results and leaves the input unchanged.
`+std.regex::escape_literal(str text) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
escapes pattern metacharacters so the compiled result matches that text literally.

[[R-SLIB-REGEX-0006]]
*R-SLIB-REGEX-0006* — Compilation and execution are iterative and do not recurse or
backtrack. Compile limits are 16384 pattern bytes, 128 open groups, 255 capturing groups, 1000 for each repetition
bound, 4096 instructions and 4096 class ranges after expansion; exceeding any limit yields
`+program_limit+`. Each search call has its own `+max_steps+` budget; all searches within
one `+find_all+`, `+replace_all+` or `+split+` share one budget. Processing an NFA
work item, testing a consuming instruction or inspecting a class range consumes one step.
Exhaustion yields `+step_limit+` before that action. A single search takes
O((program instructions + class ranges) * (subject scalars + 1)) time and O(program size)
scratch space. Repeated searches may revisit suffixes; their aggregate work remains
budgeted. Result storage and literal output copying are proportional to their output size
and use checked allocation errors. Concurrent calls share only immutable program state.
