Conformance requires executable sync/async vectors, empty-match progress, UTF-8 boundaries,
all option/error kinds, deterministic differential probes and injected failure at every
allocation in compilation, search, replacement and splitting.

[[R-SLIB-REGEX-0007]]
*R-SLIB-REGEX-0007* — `+std.regex::group_count(const std.regex::regex* compiled) -> usize+`
is the number of capturing groups.
`+std.regex::captures(const std.regex::regex* compiled, str text) -> o<array<o<std.regex::span>>>+`
and
`+std.regex::captures_from(const std.regex::regex* compiled, str text, usize offset) -> o<array<o<std.regex::span>>>+`,
both throwing `+std.regex::error+` and `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`, select the match of
`+find+` or `+find_from+`; `+o::none+` means no match. Element zero of the array is that
match and element i the span of group i: among the paths through the pattern that match
exactly that span, the preferred one decides, preferring an alternative to the ones after it
and one more iteration of a repetition to stopping; a group that the path repeats reports its
last iteration, and a group that the path does not enter is `+o::none+`. The capture pass
shares the step budget of the search and uses scratch space proportional to the program size
times the group count.

[[annex-a]]

=== 21.6 Compression: `+std.deflate+`

[[R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0001* — `+std.deflate+` is an R-source module in the `+allocation+` profile
implementing DEFLATE (RFC 1951) with the zlib (RFC 1950) and gzip (RFC 1952) wrappers. The
Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enums are `+std.deflate::format+` with exactly `+rfc1951+` (raw
DEFLATE), `+rfc1950+` (zlib) and `+rfc1952+` (gzip); `+std.deflate::flush+` with exactly
`+none+`, `+sync+` and `+finish+`; and `+std.deflate::state+` with exactly `+need_input+`,
`+need_output+` and `+end+`. `+std.deflate::progress+` is Copy, Send+Sync with
`+usize consumed; usize produced; std.deflate::state state;+`: the input prefix a step
consumed, the output prefix it produced and what the stream needs next. A window size is
valid when it is a power of two from 256 through 32768; it bounds every back-reference
distance of the stream.

[[R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0002]]
*R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0002* — `+std.deflate::error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync declared error with
`+std.deflate::error_code code; usize offset;+`. The Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum
`+std.deflate::error_code+` has exactly `+corrupt_stream+`, `+checksum_mismatch+`,
`+invalid_level+`, `+invalid_window+`, `+unsupported+`, `+output_limit+`, `+finished+` and
`+poisoned+`. The offset is the number of input bytes the stream had taken when the failure
was detected; because the decoder buffers ahead it may exceed the position of the offending
element by at most eight bytes. A failing step poisons its state and later steps require
`+poisoned+`; a step after `+end+` requires `+finished+`. A preset dictionary (zlib FDICT)
and reserved gzip header flags require `+unsupported+`; a malformed header, block, code,
distance or trailer requires `+corrupt_stream+`; a trailer checksum or size that disagrees
with the produced bytes requires `+checksum_mismatch+`. Creation with a level above 9
requires `+invalid_level+` and with an invalid window `+invalid_window+`.

[[R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0003]]
*R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0003* — `+std.deflate::inflater+` is a Move, Send+Sync owner of decoding
state; its representation is protected. Its associated function `+create(std.deflate::format
format, usize window_size, usize output_limit) -> std.deflate::inflater throws
std.deflate::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` allocates the window; a zlib header declaring a window above
`+window_size+` is `+corrupt_stream+`. `+inflate(std.deflate::inflater* this, const u8[]
input, u8[] output) -> std.deflate::progress throws std.deflate::error+` consumes a prefix
of `+input+`, writes at most `+len(output)+` bytes from index zero and reports
`+need_input+` when the input was consumed and the stream is incomplete, `+need_output+`
when the output is full while input or pending bytes remain, or `+end+` when the stream
including its trailer is complete; bytes after the end are not consumed. Input may be split
at any byte boundary and the state owns unfinished bits and tokens. Producing more than
`+output_limit+` bytes over the whole stream requires `+output_limit+` before the byte is
written. `+reset(std.deflate::inflater* this) -> void+` returns to the start of a new
stream and keeps the allocation. `+consumed_bytes(const std.deflate::inflater* this) ->
usize+` and `+produced_bytes(const std.deflate::inflater* this) -> usize+` are the totals
so far. The gzip header CRC is consumed but not verified. A step allocates nothing.

[[R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0004]]
*R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0004* — `+std.deflate::deflater+` is a Move, Send+Sync owner of encoding
state; its representation is protected. Its associated function `+create(std.deflate::format
format, u8 level, usize window_size) -> std.deflate::deflater throws std.deflate::error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+` allocates a buffer of twice the window, the hash chains and the
staging area. Level 0 emits stored blocks only, levels 1 through 3 use greedy matching and
levels 4 through 9 lazy matching with increasing search effort. `+deflate(std.deflate::deflater*
this, const u8[] input, u8[] output, std.deflate::flush flush) -> std.deflate::progress
throws std.deflate::error+` consumes a prefix of `+input+` and writes a prefix of
`+output+`. With `+none+` blocks are emitted only when the block buffer is full and the
step ends in `+need_input+` or `+need_output+`. `+sync+` additionally emits every pending
symbol followed by an empty stored block, so the output ends on a byte boundary; `+finish+`
emits the final block and, for zlib and gzip, the trailer, and the step ends in `+end+` once
everything was written. Every block is written in whichever of the stored, fixed-Huffman
and dynamic-Huffman forms costs the fewest bits, and no stored block exceeds the window.
The checksum covers the consumed input. `+reset+`, `+consumed_bytes+` and
`+produced_bytes+` follow the inflater. A step allocates nothing; the module provides no
async operation, so a program composes steps with `+std.io+` itself.

[[R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0005]]
*R-SLIB-DEFLATE-0005* — `+std.deflate::inflate(const u8[] input, std.deflate::format format,
usize output_limit) -> array<u8> throws std.deflate::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
decodes one complete stream with a 32768-byte window and requires `+corrupt_stream+` when
the input ends before the stream does or continues after its end.
`+std.deflate::deflate(const u8[] input, std.deflate::format format, u8 level) -> array<u8>
throws std.deflate::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` encodes the complete input with a
32768-byte window as one finished stream. Both own their result and release partial results
on failure. Encoding and decoding are iterative and do not recurse; every table and loop is
bounded by the window, the block buffer and the fixed alphabets of RFC 1951.

=== 21.7 XML: `+std.xml+`

[[R-SLIB-XML-0001]]
*R-SLIB-XML-0001* — `+std.xml+` is an R-source module in the `+allocation+` profile: a
streaming XML 1.0 reader over caller fragments, a streaming selector over the path of open
elements and an escaping writer. It builds no tree. It accepts UTF-8 only, the five
predefined entities and character references only, and no document type declaration or
external entity. The Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enums are `+std.xml::event_kind+` with
exactly `+none+`, `+declaration+`, `+start_element+`, `+end_element+`, `+text+`, `+cdata+`,
`+comment+` and `+instruction+`, and `+std.xml::state+` with exactly `+need_input+`,
`+event_ready+` and `+end+`. `+std.xml::progress+` is Copy, Send+Sync with
`+usize consumed; std.xml::state state;+`. `+std.xml::options+` is Copy, Send+Sync with
`+usize max_depth; usize max_attributes; bool skip_whitespace;+`;
`+std.xml::default_options() -> std.xml::options+` returns 256, 256 and true.

[[R-SLIB-XML-0002]]
*R-SLIB-XML-0002* — `+std.xml::error+` is a Copy, Send+Sync declared error with
`+std.xml::error_code code; usize offset;+`. The Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum
`+std.xml::error_code+` has exactly `+malformed+`, `+unsupported+`, `+invalid_utf8+`,
`+unbalanced+`, `+depth_limit+`, `+attribute_limit+`, `+duplicate_attribute+`,
`+invalid_selector+`, `+misplaced+`, `+finished+` and `+poisoned+`. The offset is the byte
position in the whole document for the reader, in the pattern for the selector and zero or the
output length for the writer. A DOCTYPE, an entity other than `+amp+`, `+lt+`, `+gt+`,
`+quot+`, `+apos+` or a character reference, an encoding other than UTF-8 and a character
reference to a value that is no character of XML 1.0 (only tab, line feed, carriage return,
U+0020..U+D7FF, U+E000..U+FFFD and U+10000..U+10FFFF are) require `+unsupported+`; `+]]>+` in
character data requires `+malformed+`; an end tag that does not close the open element and a
document that ends inside an element require `+unbalanced+`; a failing reader step poisons the
reader and later steps require `+poisoned+`; a step after `+end+` requires `+finished+`.

[[R-SLIB-XML-0003]]
*R-SLIB-XML-0003* — `+std.xml::reader+` is a Move, Send+Sync owner of the parse state, the
open-element path and the current event; its representation is protected. Its associated
function `+create(std.xml::options settings) -> std.xml::reader throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
starts an empty document. `+feed(std.xml::reader* this, const u8[] fragment, bool final) ->
std.xml::progress throws std.xml::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` consumes a prefix of the
fragment and reports `+event_ready+` as soon as one event is complete, `+need_input+` when
the fragment was consumed without completing one, or `+end+` when `+final+` is set and the
document is complete. A fragment may end anywhere, including inside a name, a tag or an
entity; the reader owns the unfinished token. A ready event is read through the accessors
and discarded by the next feed; the caller continues with the unconsumed suffix. The end of
an empty-element tag is reported as an `+end_element+` event by the next feed without
consuming input. Text is reported when the following tag begins or at the final fragment,
with entities decoded; whitespace-only text is dropped when `+skip_whitespace+` holds.
Attribute values normalize tab, LF and CR to spaces. `+reset(std.xml::reader* this) -> void+`
starts a new document keeping the allocations; `+depth(const std.xml::reader* this) -> usize+`
is the number of open elements after the event and `+offset(const std.xml::reader* this) ->
usize+` the number of consumed bytes. More open elements than `+max_depth+` require
`+depth_limit+` and more attributes than `+max_attributes+` require `+attribute_limit+`.

[[R-SLIB-XML-0004]]
*R-SLIB-XML-0004* — The accessors borrow from the reader for the life of the event:
`+kind(const std.xml::reader* this) -> std.xml::event_kind+`;
`+name(const std.xml::reader* this) -> str+`, the qualified element name, the instruction
target, and empty otherwise; `+prefix(const std.xml::reader* this) -> str+` and
`+local_name(const std.xml::reader* this) -> str+`, the parts of that name around its first
colon; `+namespace(const std.xml::reader* this) -> str+`, the URI bound to the element's
prefix (the default namespace for an unprefixed name) by the innermost `+xmlns+`
declaration still open, or empty; `+text(const std.xml::reader* this) -> str+`, the decoded
character data, the CDATA content, the comment or the instruction data;
`+attribute_count(const std.xml::reader* this) -> usize+`,
`+attribute_name(const std.xml::reader* this, usize index) -> str+`,
`+attribute_value(const std.xml::reader* this, usize index) -> str+` in document order;
`+attribute(const std.xml::reader* this, str name) -> o<str>+` by qualified name; and
`+path_name(const std.xml::reader* this, usize depth) -> str+`, the qualified name of the
open element at that depth, zero being the root. Every returned string is valid UTF-8 because
the reader validated it while storing it.

[[R-SLIB-XML-0005]]
*R-SLIB-XML-0005* — `+std.xml::selector+` is a Move, Send+Sync compiled path pattern over the
open elements, the streaming subset of a location path: `+["/" | "//"] step { ("/" | "//") step }+`
with `+step = ("*" | name) { "[" "@" name ["=" quoted] "]" }+`. A leading `+/+` anchors the
first step at the root, otherwise the first step matches at any depth; `+/+` between steps
selects a child and `+//+` a descendant; `+*+` matches any name; a predicate requires the
attribute, or the attribute with exactly that value, and is permitted on the last step only.
Its associated function `+compile(str pattern) -> std.xml::selector throws std.xml::error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+` rejects any other pattern with `+invalid_selector+` at the offending
offset. `+matches(const std.xml::selector* this, const std.xml::reader* document) -> bool+`
is true exactly when the current event is a `+start_element+` whose path of open elements,
ending at that element, matches the pattern; it never allocates and inspects at most 512
open elements.

[[R-SLIB-XML-0006]]
*R-SLIB-XML-0006* — `+std.xml::writer+` is a Move, Send+Sync owner of the produced bytes and
the open elements; its representation is protected. Its associated function
`+create(bool pretty) -> std.xml::writer+` allocates nothing. `+declaration(std.xml::writer*
this) -> void throws std.xml::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` writes the XML 1.0 UTF-8
declaration and requires `+misplaced+` unless the output is empty; `+start(std.xml::writer*
this, str name) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` opens an element;
`+attribute(std.xml::writer* this, str name, str value) -> void throws std.xml::error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+` requires `+misplaced+` unless it directly follows the start or a
previous attribute; `+text(std.xml::writer* this, str value) -> void throws
std.alloc::alloc_error+` escapes `+&+`, `+<+` and `+>+`, attribute values additionally
`+"+`; `+cdata(std.xml::writer* this, str value) -> void throws std.xml::error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+`, `+comment(std.xml::writer* this, str value) -> void throws
std.xml::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` and `+instruction(std.xml::writer* this, str target,
str data) -> void throws std.xml::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` require `+malformed+` when
the content contains its own terminator; `+end(std.xml::writer* this) -> void throws
std.xml::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` closes the innermost element as an empty-element
tag when nothing was written inside and requires `+misplaced+` without an open element;
`+finish(std.xml::writer this) -> array<u8> throws std.xml::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
consumes the writer and requires `+unbalanced+` while elements are open;
`+reset(std.xml::writer* this) -> void+`, `+depth(const std.xml::writer* this) -> usize+`
and `+as_slice(const std.xml::writer* this) -> const u8[]+` observe the state. Names are
written as given. The pretty form places every element, comment and instruction on its own
line indented by two spaces per depth, keeps text inline and ends the document with LF.

=== 21.8 Services: `+std.service+`

[[R-SLIB-SERVICE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-SERVICE-0001* — `+std.service+` is an R-source module in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile that runs a service: one accept loop and one handler
task per connection in a bounded task group (Core R-STMT-0017). `+std.service::options+`
is Copy, Send+Sync with
`+u32 capacity; std.time::duration timeout; std.service::overflow overflow; o<std.time::duration> idle_timeout; bool stop_on_signals;+`: capacity is
the number of handler tasks that may run at once, timeout bounds each connection from its
accept and overflow chooses what happens to a connection that arrives while capacity
handler tasks run. Its fields declare the initializers of Core R-INIT-0004: an initialization
that omits a field takes a capacity of 64, a timeout of
`+std.time::duration_from_seconds(30i64)+`, `+std.service::overflow::wait+`, no idle timeout
and no stop on signals, which apply to serve_all (R-SLIB-SERVICE-0007), so
`+std.service::options {}+` is a complete setting. The constant `+std.service::max_capacity+` is
1024. The Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enums `+std.service::overflow+` and `+std.service::stop+` have exactly
`+reject+` and `+wait+`, and `+drain+` and `+cancel+`.

[[R-SLIB-SERVICE-0002]]
*R-SLIB-SERVICE-0002* — `+std.service::report+` is Copy, Send+Sync with
`+u64 accepted; u64 rejected; u64 completed; u64 failed; u64 cancelled; o<std.error::error> last_failure;+`.
accepted counts the connections given to a handler task and rejected those closed without
one. Every accepted connection is counted once more: as completed when its handler
returned, as failed when it threw and as cancelled when it ended otherwise, which a stop
request or a panic under unwind causes. last_failure is the portable error
(`+std.error::from_fault+`, R-SLIB-ERR-0004) of the failure counted last, or `+o::none+`.

[[R-SLIB-SERVICE-0003]]
*R-SLIB-SERVICE-0003* —
`+async std.service::serve_with(std.net::tcp_listener listener, std.service::options settings, std.sync::receiver<std.service::stop> stop, arc S state, H handler) -> std.service::report throws std.async::start_error, std.net::net_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
with
`+@generic<S: send & sync & unborrowed, H: copy & async fn once(arc S, std.net::tcp_connection) -> void throws(std.error::fault)>+`
owns the listener, the stop receiver and one reference to the state. A capacity above
`+max_capacity+`, or a capacity of zero with `+overflow::wait+`, throws
`+std.async::start_error::scope_full+` before any accept. Until a stop value arrives or
every stop sender is gone, which counts as `+drain+`, serve_with accepts connections in
order; a connection that its peer aborted or reset before the accept completed is skipped.
While fewer than capacity accepted handler tasks run, a copy of the handler runs with a
clone of the state (`+std.arc::clone+`) and the connection in a new task inside a deadline
block (Core R-STMT-0019) whose value is the accept instant plus timeout, so its operations
report `+timed_out+` after that instant; otherwise, with `+overflow::reject+` and whenever
that task cannot start, the connection is closed and rejected. With `+overflow::wait+`,
while capacity handler tasks run, serve_with accepts nothing until one of them has ended
and its slot in the handler group is free (Core R-STMT-0018) or a stop request arrives;
connections meanwhile stay with the listener. A stop request ends accepting: `+drain+`
waits until every handler task has ended or timeout has passed since the request,
`+cancel+` does not wait, and the handler tasks still running are then cancelled.
serve_with returns the report once every handler task is terminal, and the listener is
closed when serve_with ends. Any other accept failure is thrown after the handler tasks
have been cancelled and have ended, and cancellation of the serving task cancels the
accept and every handler task and waits for them (Core R-STMT-0017). The operations of
serve_with follow the deadline of its task, and a handler task takes the earlier of that
deadline and its own.
`+async std.service::serve(std.net::tcp_listener listener, std.service::options settings, std.sync::receiver<std.service::stop> stop, H handler) -> std.service::report throws std.async::start_error, std.net::net_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
with
`+@generic<H: copy & async fn once(std.net::tcp_connection) -> void throws(std.error::fault)>+`
is serve_with for a handler that receives no state.

[[R-SLIB-SERVICE-0004]]
*R-SLIB-SERVICE-0004* — `+std.service::listener+` is a Move enum with exactly the variants
`+tcp(std.net::tcp_listener)+`, `+unix(std.net::unix_listener)+` and
`+tls { std.net::tcp_listener socket; arc std.tls::config settings; }+`: a TCP listener, a
Unix-domain stream listener (R-SLIB-NET-0015) and a TCP listener whose connections each begin
with the server handshake of std.tls (R-SLIB-TLS-0004) under the configuration. The constant
`+std.service::max_listeners+` is 16.

[[R-SLIB-SERVICE-0005]]
*R-SLIB-SERVICE-0005* — `+std.service::connection+` is a Move struct with no public field: one
connection of serve_all over the transport of its listener. It implements
`+std.stream::Reader+`, `+std.stream::Writer+` and `+std.stream::Stream+` (R-SLIB-STREAM-0001)
by the operations of that transport; with an idle timeout, each operation runs inside a
deadline block (Core R-STMT-0019) whose value is its start plus the timeout, so an operation
that waits longer reports `+timed_out+`.
`+std.service::connection::source(const std.service::connection* this) -> u32+` is the index of
its listener in the array that serve_all received.

[[R-SLIB-SERVICE-0006]]
*R-SLIB-SERVICE-0006* — `+std.service::health+` is a Move, Send+Sync struct with no public
field: the state of a service that serve_all keeps current and any task or thread reads.
`+std.service::health::create() -> std.service::health throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` makes one
that does not serve, and
`+std.service::health::share(const std.service::health* this) -> std.service::health+` another
handle of the same state.
`+std.service::health::serving(const std.service::health* this) -> bool+` is true from the
moment serve_all accepts connections, its signals already listened for, until a stop
request or a failed listener ends accepting;
`+std.service::health::active(const std.service::health* this) -> u32+` is the number of handler
tasks running as of the last round of serve_all and
`+std.service::health::accepted(const std.service::health* this) -> u64+` the number of
connections that it gave to a handler task.

[[R-SLIB-SERVICE-0007]]
*R-SLIB-SERVICE-0007* —
`+async std.service::serve_all(array<std.service::listener> listeners, std.service::options settings, std.sync::receiver<std.service::stop> stop, std.service::health status, arc S state, H handler) -> std.service::report throws std.async::start_error, std.net::net_error, std.process::process_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
with
`+@generic<S: send & sync & unborrowed, H: copy & async fn once(arc S, std.service::connection) -> void throws(std.error::fault)>+`
is serve_with of R-SLIB-SERVICE-0003 over every listener at once: one accepting task per
listener hands its connections to the loop in the order it accepts them, and each handler
task receives a `+std.service::connection+` with the idle timeout of the settings; the handshake
of a TLS listener runs in that task before the handler, inside its connection deadline, and a
handshake that fails fails the task. More than `+max_listeners+` listeners throw
`+std.async::start_error::scope_full+` before any accept, like the capacities of
R-SLIB-SERVICE-0003. With stop_on_signals, serve_all listens for SIGTERM and SIGINT
(R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0001) before its health reports serving, and either counts as a `+drain+`
request; a signal that cannot be listened for throws its `+std.process::process_error+` before
any accept. A listener whose accept fails with another error than
an aborted or reset connection ends accepting as a stop request does; its handler tasks end
as for `+drain+`, and serve_all then throws that failure instead of returning the report. The
health is serving while connections are accepted and follows the number of running handler
tasks and accepted connections; every listener is closed when serve_all ends.

=== 21.9 Streams, buffered I/O and the console: `+std.stream+`, `+std.bufio+`, `+std.console+`

[[R-SLIB-STREAM-0001]]
*R-SLIB-STREAM-0001* — `+std.stream+` is an R-source module in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile whose traits describe byte streams. Every method of them is
`+@scoped async+` like the scoped operations of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012, takes its receiver as
`+const Self*+`, throws `+std.error::fault+` (R-SLIB-ERR-0004) and runs under the deadline
of the calling task (Core R-STMT-0019). `+std.stream::Reader+`, declared
`+trait Reader : send & sync+`, has
`+read_into(const Self* this, u8[] target) -> usize+`, which places at most
`+len(target)+` bytes at the start of target and returns their count; zero means the end of
the stream or an empty target. `+std.stream::Writer+`, declared
`+trait Writer : send & sync+`, has
`+write_from(const Self* this, const u8[] source) -> usize+`, which writes a nonempty prefix
of a nonempty source and returns its length, and zero for an empty source;
`+write_all_from(const Self* this, const u8[] source) -> void+`, which writes every byte;
`+flush(const Self* this) -> void+`, which hands the written bytes to their destination;
and `+shutdown(const Self* this) -> void+`, which ends the write direction, so that the
peer reads the end of the stream. The default of write_all_from repeats write_from on the
rest of the source and throws `+std.io::io_error+` with `+broken_pipe+` and native code zero
when a write accepts no byte of a nonempty rest; the defaults of flush and shutdown complete
without an effect. `+std.stream::Stream+`, declared `+trait Stream : Reader & Writer+`, is
both directions of one connection. An interface over these traits (Core R-TYPE-0051), such
as `+const dyn(std.stream::Reader)*+` or `+own dyn(std.stream::Stream)*+`, chooses the
implementation when the program runs.

[[R-SLIB-STREAM-0002]]
*R-SLIB-STREAM-0002* — The module implements Reader for `+std.io::input+`, Writer for
`+std.io::output+`, and Reader, Writer and Stream for `+std.fs::file+`,
`+std.net::tcp_stream+`, `+std.net::tcp_connection+` and `+std.net::unix_stream+`. Each
read_into, write_from and write_all_from is the scoped operation of that name of
R-SLIB-IO-0008, R-SLIB-FS-0013 or R-SLIB-NET-0010, for a connection that of its stream and
for a Unix-domain stream its `+unix_+` form of R-SLIB-NET-0015, with its counts, ordering and
end-of-stream rules; the `+std.io::io_error+` or `+std.net::net_error+` of the operation is
the thrown error. flush is `+std.io::flush+` for an output and `+std.fs::flush+` for a file;
a TCP or Unix-domain stream keeps no buffer of its own, so its flush completes without a
native operation. shutdown is `+std.net::tcp_shutdown+` for a TCP stream and a connection and
`+std.net::unix_shutdown+` for a Unix-domain stream, with
`+std.net::shutdown_direction::write+`; an output and a file end their write direction only
when their handle is closed, so their shutdown flushes. On these standard types the receiver form of
Core R-FUNC-0014 keeps naming their registered standard methods of the same names (Core
R-NAME-0010); the implementations are reached through a generic constraint or an interface.

[[R-SLIB-STREAM-0003]]
*R-SLIB-STREAM-0003* — `+std.stream::duplex<R, W>+` with
`+@generic<R: std.stream::Reader, W: std.stream::Writer>+` is a struct with the public
fields `+R reader+` and `+W writer+`: one stream whose reads are those of reader and whose
writes, flush and shutdown are those of writer, such as the output and input pipes of a
child process (R-SLIB-PROC-0004) or standard input and output. The module implements Reader,
Writer and Stream for it.

[[R-SLIB-STREAM-0004]]
*R-SLIB-STREAM-0004* — The module implements Reader for `+own dyn(std.stream::Reader)*+`,
Writer for `+own dyn(std.stream::Writer)*+`, and Reader, Writer and Stream for
`+own dyn(std.stream::Stream)*+` (Core R-TYPE-0055): each method calls the method of the
member the owner holds, so a stream chosen when the program runs serves wherever a Reader,
Writer or Stream is expected, such as the source of `+std.bufio::reader+`.

[[R-SLIB-BUFIO-0001]]
*R-SLIB-BUFIO-0001* — `+std.bufio+` is an R-source module in the `+hosted-native-async+`
profile. `+std.bufio::reader<R>+` with `+@generic<R: std.stream::Reader>+` is a Move struct
with the public field `+R source+`, the stream it reads, and protected state: a buffer of a
fixed capacity and the bytes read from source and not yet handed out.
`+reader<R>::create(R source, usize capacity) -> std.bufio::reader<R> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
allocates the buffer, and
`+buffered(const reader<R>* this) -> usize+` is the number
of those bytes. A read of source first moves them to the start of the buffer and reads at
most the free space after them. The operations of R-SLIB-BUFIO-0002 are `+@scoped async+`,
take `+std.bufio::reader<R>* this+` and throw `+std.error::fault+`, including every failure
of source.

[[R-SLIB-BUFIO-0002]]
*R-SLIB-BUFIO-0002* — The reading operations of `+std.bufio::reader<R>+` are:

* `+read_line(std.string::string* line) -> bool+`, which clears line and stores the next
  line without its line feed and without one carriage return before it; a last line without
  a line feed counts, and false means that source has ended with no byte left. A line that
  is not valid UTF-8 is consumed and throws `+std.string::string_error+`;
* `+read_until(u8 delimiter, bytes* out) -> usize+`, which appends to out the bytes up to
  and including the next delimiter and returns their number; the last bytes without a
  delimiter count, and zero means that source has ended;
* `+read_exact(u8[] target) -> bool+`, which fills target and returns true; false means
  that source ended before the first byte, and an end after it throws
  `+std.bits::read_error+` with `+unexpected_end+` and the number of bytes placed as its
  byte index;
* `+read_into(u8[] target) -> usize+`, which places at most `+len(target)+` bytes, those
  already buffered first, and otherwise reads source once, directly into target when target
  is at least the capacity; zero means the end of source or an empty target.

A line or delimited sequence, its terminator included, shall fit in the capacity: when the
buffer is full of bytes without a terminator, read_line and read_until throw
`+std.io::io_error+` with `+resource_exhausted+` and native code zero and leave those bytes
buffered.

[[R-SLIB-BUFIO-0003]]
*R-SLIB-BUFIO-0003* — `+std.bufio::writer<W>+` with `+@generic<W: std.stream::Writer>+` is
a Move struct with the public field `+W sink+` and a protected buffer of a fixed capacity.
`+writer<W>::create(W sink, usize capacity) -> std.bufio::writer<W> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
allocates the buffer, and
`+buffered(const writer<W>* this) -> usize+` is the number
of bytes written to the writer and not yet handed to sink. Its `+@scoped async+`
operations, which take `+std.bufio::writer<W>* this+` and throw `+std.error::fault+`, are
`+write(const u8[] data) -> void+`, which buffers data after handing the buffered bytes to
sink with write_all_from when data does not fit in the free space, and writes data of at
least the capacity to sink directly; `+write_str(str text) -> void+`, the write of the UTF-8
bytes of text; and `+flush() -> void+`, which hands the buffered bytes to sink and then
flushes sink. Bytes still buffered when a writer is destroyed are discarded without a write.

[[R-SLIB-BUFIO-0004]]
*R-SLIB-BUFIO-0004* —
`+std.bufio::reader<R>::into_source(std.bufio::reader<R> this, bytes* unread) -> R throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
ends the reader: it appends the bytes read from source and not yet handed out to unread, in order,
and returns source, which a protocol that takes over a connection after a buffered exchange reads
on from there.

[[R-SLIB-CONSOLE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-CONSOLE-0001* — `+std.console+` is an R-source module in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile.
`+async std.console::print(std.string::string text) -> void throws std.error::fault+` and
`+async std.console::eprint(std.string::string text) -> void throws std.error::fault+` write
text to standard output and to standard error with one `+std.io::write_all+`
(R-SLIB-IO-0004) through a new console view (R-SLIB-IO-0002);
`+async std.console::println(std.string::string text) -> void throws std.error::fault+` and
`+async std.console::eprintln(std.string::string text) -> void throws std.error::fault+`
first append a line feed. A failed write throws its `+std.io::io_error+`.

[[R-SLIB-CONSOLE-0002]]
*R-SLIB-CONSOLE-0002* —
`+async std.console::read_line() -> o<std.string::string> throws std.error::fault+`
returns the next line of standard input without its line feed and without one carriage
return before it, or `+o::none+` when the input has ended with no byte left; a last line
without a line feed counts. It reads one byte at a time and never a byte after the line
feed, so other readers of standard input continue after the line. The constant
`+std.console::max_line+` is 65536: a line of more bytes throws `+std.io::io_error+` with
`+resource_exhausted+` and native code zero, and a line that is not valid UTF-8 throws
`+std.string::string_error+`.

=== 21.10 Encodings: `+std.encoding+`

[[R-SLIB-ENCODING-0001]]
*R-SLIB-ENCODING-0001* — `+std.encoding+` is an R-source module in the `+hosted+`
profile. `+std.encoding::encode_base64(const u8[] data) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
writes the base64 of RFC 4648 section 4 with padding, and
`+std.encoding::encode_base64_url(const u8[] data) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
the base64url of section 5 without padding.

[[R-SLIB-ENCODING-0002]]
*R-SLIB-ENCODING-0002* —
`+std.encoding::decode_base64(str text) -> bytes throws std.convert::parse_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
and
`+std.encoding::decode_base64_url(str text) -> bytes throws std.convert::parse_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
accept canonical text only: the standard alphabet in groups of four symbols with padding,
the URL alphabet with or without it. The checks run in this order: the first byte outside
the alphabet, a `+=+` before the padding included, reports `+invalid_digit+` at its index;
an incomplete last group reports `+trailing_character+` at its first byte; unused bits that
are not zero report `+invalid_digit+` at the last symbol.

[[R-SLIB-ENCODING-0003]]
*R-SLIB-ENCODING-0003* —
`+std.encoding::encode_hex(const u8[] data) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
writes two lowercase hexadecimal digits per byte;
`+std.encoding::decode_hex(str text) -> bytes throws std.convert::parse_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
reads digits of either case. The first byte that is not a digit reports `+invalid_digit+`
at its index, and otherwise an odd length `+trailing_character+` at the last digit.

[[R-SLIB-ENCODING-0004]]
*R-SLIB-ENCODING-0004* —
`+std.encoding::percent_encode(str text) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
keeps the unreserved bytes of RFC 3986 (letters, digits, `+-+`, `+.+`, `+_+` and `+~+`) and
writes every other byte as `+%+` and two uppercase hexadecimal digits;
`+std.encoding::percent_decode(str text) -> bytes throws std.convert::parse_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
turns `+%+` and two digits of either case into that byte and keeps every other byte; a `+%+`
without two digits reports `+invalid_digit+` at its index, the first such one.

=== 21.11 Identifiers: `+std.uuid+`

[[R-SLIB-UUID-0001]]
*R-SLIB-UUID-0001* — `+std.uuid+` is an R-source module in the `+hosted+` profile for the
identifiers of RFC 9562. `+std.uuid::uuid+` is a Copy, Send+Sync struct with the public field
`+u8[16] bytes+` in the byte order of the RFC. It derives `+equal+`, `+ordered+` and `+key+`
(Core R-AGG-0012), so identifiers compare by their bytes, those of version 7 in the order of
their time, and serve as dictionary keys; `+core::Format+` writes the 36 lowercase characters
`+xxxxxxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx+` (Core R-TYPE-0046).
`+std.uuid::nil() -> std.uuid::uuid+` has every bit zero, `+std.uuid::max() ->
std.uuid::uuid+` every bit one, and `+std.uuid::version(const std.uuid::uuid* value) -> u8+`
is the upper four bits of byte 6.

[[R-SLIB-UUID-0002]]
*R-SLIB-UUID-0002* — `+std.uuid::from_random_v4(u8[16] random) -> std.uuid::uuid+` keeps the
random bytes except the version 4 in the upper four bits of byte 6 and the variant `+10+` in
the upper two bits of byte 8, and `+std.uuid::v4() -> std.uuid::uuid+` does so with bytes of
`+std.random::fill+`. `+std.uuid::from_time_v7(u64 unix_milliseconds, u8[10] random) ->
std.uuid::uuid+` writes the lower 48 bits of the time big-endian into bytes 0..5, the version
7 and the lower four bits of random[0] into byte 6, random[1] into byte 7, the variant and the
lower six bits of random[2] into byte 8 and random[3..10] into bytes 9..15, and
`+std.uuid::v7() -> std.uuid::uuid throws std.time::time_error+` does so with the time of
`+std.time::system_now+` and bytes of `+std.random::fill+`; a time before 1970 or beyond
the 48-bit range reports `+overflow+`. Two identifiers of one millisecond compare in random
order.

[[R-SLIB-UUID-0003]]
*R-SLIB-UUID-0003* — `+std.uuid::parse(str text) -> std.uuid::uuid throws
std.convert::parse_error+` reads the 36-character form with hyphens at indices 8, 13, 18 and
23 and hexadecimal digits of either case elsewhere. An empty text reports `+empty+` at index
zero; the first byte that is not the hyphen or digit its index requires reports
`+invalid_digit+` there; a shorter text then reports `+invalid_digit+` at its length and a
longer one `+trailing_character+` at index 36.

=== 21.12 Logging: `+std.log+`

[[R-SLIB-LOG-0001]]
*R-SLIB-LOG-0001* — `+std.log+` is an R-source module in the `+hosted-native-async+` profile
for structured records. The Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enums `+std.log::level+` and
`+std.log::format+` have exactly `+trace+`, `+debug+`, `+info+`, `+warn+` and `+error+`, in
rising severity, and `+text+` and `+json+`. `+std.log::fields+` is a Move list of named text,
number, flag and secret fields in the order they were added:

* `+std.log::fields::create() -> std.log::fields+`;
* `+std.log::fields::text(std.log::fields* this, str name, str value) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::fields::number(std.log::fields* this, str name, i64 value) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::fields::flag(std.log::fields* this, str name, bool value) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::fields::secret(std.log::fields* this, str name) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::fields::count(const std.log::fields* this) -> usize+`.

A field is written with its name, in which every byte other than an ASCII letter, digit, `+_+`,
`+.+` or `+-+` becomes `+_+`, at most the first 64 bytes are kept and an empty name becomes
`+_+`, and a name equal to the name of a member that every record of the logger carries
(R-SLIB-LOG-0004) gets a leading `+_+` where it is written, so that no name breaks a record. A
secret field keeps no value.

[[R-SLIB-LOG-0002]]
*R-SLIB-LOG-0002* — `+std.log::logger+` is a Move, Send+Sync handle that formats records on
the calling task and hands each line to the bounded queue of its writer without waiting:

* `+std.log::logger::share(const std.log::logger* this) -> std.log::logger throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::logger::mask(std.log::logger* this, str name) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::logger::enabled(const std.log::logger* this, std.log::level value) -> bool+`;
* `+std.log::logger::dropped(const std.log::logger* this) -> u64+`;
* `+std.log::logger::log(const std.log::logger* this, std.log::level value, str message, const std.log::fields* extra) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+std.log::logger::trace+`, `+debug+`, `+info+`, `+warn+` and `+error+`, each
  `+(const std.log::logger* this, str message) -> void throws std.error::fault+`.

A record of a level below the threshold of the logger is not formatted. Otherwise, with the
layout `+std.log::layout::standard()+` of R-SLIB-LOG-0004, the line holds the time of
`+std.time::system_now+` in RFC 3339 with three fractional digits, the level, the identifier
of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0018 of the calling task, the message, the bound fields of the logger and the
fields in order.
With `+format::text+` it is `+time=... level=... task=... message=...+` followed by a space and
`+name=value+` for each field; a text value that is empty or holds a space, `+"+`, `+=+`, a
backslash or a control character is written in quotes with `+\"+`, `+\\+`, `+\n+`, `+\t+`,
`+\r+`, `+\b+`, `+\f+` and `+\u00XX+` escapes with lowercase hexadecimal digits, and numbers and flags as they
are. With `+format::json+` it is one JSON object with the members `+time+`, `+level+`,
`+task+`, `+message+` and the fields, the task and number and flag fields unquoted. Each line
ends with a line feed. A secret field, and a field whose name or written name equals ignoring
ASCII case `+password+`, `+secret+`, `+token+`, `+authorization+` or a name given to `+mask+`,
is written as `+***+`. When the queue is full or its writer has ended, the record is dropped and
counted: `+dropped+` returns the records dropped by every logger of the queue. `+share+`
returns another logger of the same queue with the threshold, format, masked names, layout and
bound fields of the original, for another task, and `+mask+` rewrites its name as a field name.

[[R-SLIB-LOG-0003]]
*R-SLIB-LOG-0003* — `+std.log::writer+` is the single Move, Send+Sync consumer of one queue:

* `+std.log::writer::create(usize capacity) -> std.log::writer throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::writer::logger(const std.log::writer* this, std.log::level threshold, std.log::format style) -> std.log::logger throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+async std.log::writer::to_stderr(std.log::writer this) -> u64 throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.log::writer::to_file(std.log::writer this, const std.fs::path* path) -> u64 throws std.error::fault+`.

The queue holds `+capacity+` lines, and one line when `+capacity+` is zero; the count of
dropped records that its loggers share is held by an `+arc+`, whose allocation failure is the
allocation panic of Core R-OBJ-0006. `+to_stderr+` writes each line to standard error and
`+to_file+` appends it to the file at the path, created when absent, each line as one write in
queue order, until every logger of the queue has been dropped; a last line
`+message="records dropped" dropped=N+` follows when records were dropped. Both return the
number of queued lines they wrote. Both take the writer, so the loggers of a program come from
it before it starts writing, and each task that logs owns a logger made by `+writer::logger+`
or `+share+`.

[[R-SLIB-LOG-0004]]
*R-SLIB-LOG-0004* — The Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum `+std.log::order+` has exactly
`+time_first+` and `+level_first+`. `+std.log::layout+` is a Move struct with the public fields
`+std.string::string time_name+`, `+std.string::string level_name+`,
`+std.string::string message_name+`, `+o<std.string::string> task_name+`,
`+o<std.string::string> caller_name+`, `+u32 time_digits+`, `+bool trim_time+`,
`+bool omit_empty_message+` and `+std.log::order sequence+`:

* `+std.log::layout::standard() -> std.log::layout throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::logger::set_layout(std.log::logger* this, std.log::layout value) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::logger::with(const std.log::logger* this, const std.log::fields* extra) -> std.log::logger throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::logger::log_at(const std.log::logger* this, std.log::level value, str message, const std.log::fields* extra, str caller) -> void throws std.error::fault+`.

`+standard+` is the layout of R-SLIB-LOG-0002, with which every logger of `+writer::logger+`
starts: `+time+` with three fractional digits, `+level+`, `+task+` and `+message+`, time
first, every message written and no place of the call. `+set_layout+` makes the logger write its records with the layout. The members carry
its names, rewritten as field names (R-SLIB-LOG-0001); the task is left out when
`+task_name+` is none; the time, always in UTC, has `+time_digits+` fractional digits, nine
when more are asked, truncated, and with `+trim_time+` its trailing zeros are left out, with
the dot when every digit is zero; with `+omit_empty_message+` a record whose message is empty
has no message member. `+log_at+` logs as `+log+` does with the place of
the call, written as the member named `+caller_name+` when the layout has one and caller is not
empty; `+log+` and the methods of each level write no place. A program passes
`+core::location()+` (Core R-REFL-0004) as caller. With `+time_first+` the time, level, task,
place and message come before the bound fields and the fields of the record; with
`+level_first+` the level comes first, then the bound fields, the fields of the record, the
time, the task, the place and the message.
`+with+` returns another logger of the same queue, as `+share+` does, whose records carry the
bound fields of this logger and then the fields of extra before the fields of each record;
this logger is unchanged.

[[R-SLIB-LOG-0005]]
*R-SLIB-LOG-0005* — `+std.log::panic_record+` is a Move struct with the public fields
`+std.string::string category+`, `+std.string::string text+` and `+std.string::string place+`,
and `+std.log::panic_reports+` a Move, Send+Sync listener of the reports of panics that nothing
observes (Core R-ERR-0009), reached through the native provider `+std.log.native+`:

* `+std.log::panic_reports::listen(usize capacity) -> std.log::panic_reports throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.log::panic_reports::take(const std.log::panic_reports* this) -> o<std.log::panic_record> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+@scoped async std.log::panic_reports::next(const std.log::panic_reports* this) -> std.log::panic_record throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+std.log::panic_reports::dropped(const std.log::panic_reports* this) -> u64+`.

From `+listen+` until the listener is dropped, a report delivered to the runtime panic hook
(Core R-ERR-0009), which would write it as a line on standard error, is queued instead, up to capacity reports and at least one; a report that finds the
queue full is written as its line and counted by `+dropped+`, and dropping the listener writes
the reports it still queues as their lines. Only the listener made last
receives reports; an earlier one takes none and counts none, and dropping it changes nothing.
`+take+` returns the oldest queued report: the category of the panic, its text, cut at the last
whole scalar, and its place as the line writes it, `+module M bytes [S,E)+`. `+next+` returns it
too, and while none is queued it looks again after a pause that starts at one millisecond and
doubles up to 128 milliseconds; a cancelled `+next+` leaves the queue unchanged. A program
writes the records to its log, as no R code runs where the runtime delivers a report.

=== 21.13 Command-line arguments: `+std.args+`

[[R-SLIB-ARGS-0001]]
*R-SLIB-ARGS-0001* — `+std.args+` is an R-source module in the `+hosted+` profile that
reads the arguments of a program against their declarations. `+std.args::parser+` is a
Move struct:

* `+std.args::parser::create(str program, str summary) -> std.args::parser throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.args::parser::flag(std.args::parser* this, str name, str short_name, str help) -> void throws std.args::args_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.args::parser::option(std.args::parser* this, str name, str short_name, str value_name, str help) -> void throws std.args::args_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.args::parser::positional(std.args::parser* this, str name, str help, bool required) -> void throws std.args::args_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.args::parser::remaining(std.args::parser* this, str name, str help) -> void throws std.args::args_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

A name is 1..64 lowercase ASCII letters, digits, `+-+`, `+_+` or `+.+` starting with a letter,
and a short name is empty or one ASCII letter; `+help+` and `+h+` are taken by the help. A name
or short name that breaks these rules, or a required positional argument declared after an
optional one, reports `+invalid_name+`, and a name or short name already declared
`+duplicate_name+`, with the index of the declaration. `+remaining+` collects every positional
argument after the declared ones; a second one, or one with an invalid name, reports
`+invalid_name+` with the number of declarations. `+std.args::args_error+` is an error of the
module with the fields `+std.args::error_code code+` and `+usize index+`; the enum has exactly
`+invalid_name+`, `+duplicate_name+`, `+unknown_option+`, `+missing_value+`,
`+unexpected_value+`, `+missing_argument+`, `+extra_argument+` and `+unknown_name+`.

[[R-SLIB-ARGS-0002]]
*R-SLIB-ARGS-0002* — `+std.args::parser::parse(const std.args::parser* this, const str[] arguments) -> std.args::matches throws std.args::args_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
reads the arguments after the program name in order. `+--+` ends the options. `+-h+` and
`+--help+` ask for help. `+--name+` gives a flag; `+--name=value+` and `+--name value+` give an
option, and a flag with `+=+` reports `+unexpected_value+`. `+-abc+` gives the flags of the
letters in turn; an option among them takes the rest of the argument, or the next argument
when nothing is left. Another argument is the next positional argument, or one of the
remaining ones; beyond them it reports `+extra_argument+`. An unknown name or letter reports
`+unknown_option+` and an option without a value `+missing_value+`, each with the index of
the argument. Unless help was asked for, a required positional argument that is missing
reports `+missing_argument+` with the number of arguments. The last value of an option
counts.

[[R-SLIB-ARGS-0003]]
*R-SLIB-ARGS-0003* — `+std.args::matches+` is a Move struct:

* `+std.args::matches::help_requested(const std.args::matches* this) -> bool+`;
* `+std.args::matches::has(const std.args::matches* this, str name) -> bool throws std.args::args_error+`;
* `+std.args::matches::value(const std.args::matches* this, str name) -> o<std.string::string> throws std.args::args_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.args::matches::remaining(const std.args::matches* this) -> array<std.string::string> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+has+` tells whether a declared flag, option or positional argument was given and `+value+`
returns a copy of the value of an option or positional argument that was given, or none, which
is also the value of a flag; an undeclared name reports `+unknown_name+` with index zero.
`+remaining+` returns copies of the remaining positional arguments in order.
`+std.args::parser::help(const std.args::parser* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
returns `+PROGRAM - SUMMARY+`, the usage line and one line per declaration and for the help,
each of two spaces, the forms of the declaration padded with spaces to 24 bytes, a space and
its help.

=== 21.14 Configuration: `+std.config+`

[[R-SLIB-CONFIG-0001]]
*R-SLIB-CONFIG-0001* — `+std.config+` is an R-source module in the `+hosted-native-async+`
profile for the settings of a program, declared with a default and replaced by later layers.
`+std.config::source+` is a Copy, Send+Sync fieldless enum with exactly `+default_value+`,
`+file+`, `+environment+` and `+arguments+`. `+std.config::config+` is a Move struct:

* `+std.config::config::create() -> std.config::config+`;
* `+std.config::config::define(std.config::config* this, str key, str default_value, str help) -> void throws std.config::config_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.config::config::set(std.config::config* this, str key, str value, std.config::source origin) -> void throws std.config::config_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

A key is 1..64 lowercase ASCII letters, digits and `+_+` in segments separated by `+.+`,
otherwise `+invalid_key+`; a second declaration of a key reports `+duplicate_key+`, both with
the index of the declaration. `+set+` replaces the value and source of a declared key, and an
undeclared key reports `+unknown_key+` with index zero. `+std.config::config_error+` is an
error of the module with the fields `+std.config::error_code code+` and `+usize index+`; the
enum has exactly `+invalid_key+`, `+duplicate_key+`, `+unknown_key+`, `+invalid_value+`,
`+invalid_file+` and `+missing_value+`, formatted by its variant names.

[[R-SLIB-CONFIG-0002]]
*R-SLIB-CONFIG-0002* — Each layer replaces the value and source of the declared keys it
names:

* `+@scoped async std.config::config::load_file(std.config::config* this, const std.fs::path* path) -> void throws std.config::config_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+std.config::config::load_environment(std.config::config* this, str prefix) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+std.config::config::load_arguments(std.config::config* this, const std.args::matches* matches) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+load_file+` reads the file with a limit of 1 MiB (R-SLIB-FS-0009) and parses one JSON object;
a document that is not one reports `+invalid_file+` with the byte offset of `+std.json::error+`
or zero. Its members name keys, a member object prefixing its members with its key and `+.+`;
strings and numbers give their text, `+true+` and `+false+` their spelling, and null leaves the
key unchanged. A member that names no declared key reports `+unknown_key+`, and an array
`+invalid_value+`; a file that fails changes no key. `+load_environment+` reads, for each
declared key, the variable made of the prefix, `+_+` and the key in upper case with `+.+` as
`+_+`, such as `+SERVICE_LOG_LEVEL+` for `+log.level+`. `+load_arguments+` takes, for each
declared key that names a flag, option or positional argument that was given, its value, or
`+true+` for a flag. The usual order is defaults, file, environment, arguments, so that each
later layer wins.

[[R-SLIB-CONFIG-0003]]
*R-SLIB-CONFIG-0003* — The queries of a declared key are:

* `+std.config::config::get(const std.config::config* this, str key) -> str throws std.config::config_error+`;
* `+std.config::config::source_of(const std.config::config* this, str key) -> std.config::source throws std.config::config_error+`;
* `+std.config::config::get_i64(const std.config::config* this, str key) -> i64 throws std.config::config_error+`;
* `+std.config::config::get_u64(const std.config::config* this, str key) -> u64 throws std.config::config_error+`;
* `+std.config::config::get_bool(const std.config::config* this, str key) -> bool throws std.config::config_error+`;
* `+std.config::config::describe(const std.config::config* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+get+` borrows the current text from the configuration. An undeclared key reports
`+unknown_key+` with index zero. `+get_i64+` and `+get_u64+` read a decimal integer and
`+get_bool+` exactly `+true+` or `+false+`; any other text reports `+invalid_value+` with the
index of the declaration. `+describe+` returns one line `+key=value (source)+` per key in
declaration order; the value of a key that contains `+password+`, `+secret+` or `+token+` is
written as `+***+`.

[[R-SLIB-CONFIG-0004]]
*R-SLIB-CONFIG-0004* — A struct of settings is read in one call by:

* `+std.config::from_environment<T: json_decode>(str prefix) -> T throws std.config::field_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.config::config::decode<T: json_decode>(const std.config::config* this) -> T throws std.config::field_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+std.config::field_error+` is an error of the module with the fields
`+std.config::error_code code+` and `+std.string::string name+`, the environment variable or
key of the setting. Both read the properties of the JSON Schema of T (R-SLIB-JSON-0002), the
fields under their JSON names: `+from_environment+` the environment variable of each, the
prefix followed by the name in upper case with every byte other than an ASCII letter or digit
written as `+_+`, and `+decode+` the current value of the declared key of that name. A text
converts by the schema of its field: as it is for a string, as a decimal integer within the
`+minimum+` and `+maximum+` for an integer, as a JSON number for a number, as `+true+`, `+1+`,
`+yes+`, `+on+`, `+false+`, `+0+`, `+no+` or `+off+` in any case for a boolean, as a variant name
for an enum without payloads, as items separated by commas for an array of such scalars, and as
JSON text otherwise; a text that does not convert is `+invalid_value+` with the name. An absent
setting of an `+o<T>+` field is none; an absent setting of a required field is `+missing_value+`
with the name, and of an optional field leaves its default. The value is then read with
`+std.json::unmarshal+`, whose rules decide the defaults.

=== 21.15 Tests: `+std.test+`

[[R-SLIB-TEST-0001]]
*R-SLIB-TEST-0001* — `+std.test+` is a `+hosted+` module for the test functions of Core
R-FUNC-0025. Its R part (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001) holds the assertions and the account of a test run,
and its C part the allocation failures of R-SLIB-TEST-0003. `+std.test::failure+` is an error
of the module with the field `+std.string::string message+`. The assertions are:

* `+std.test::check(bool condition, str message) -> void throws std.test::failure, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::fail(str message) -> void throws std.test::failure, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::equal(T actual, T expected) -> void throws std.test::failure, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::not_equal(T actual, T unexpected) -> void throws std.test::failure, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::equal_text(str actual, str expected) -> void throws std.test::failure, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::contains(str text, str part) -> void throws std.test::failure, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+check+` throws a failure with the message when the condition is false, and `+fail+` always
does. `+equal+` and `+not_equal+` are generic over one type `+T+` that implements
`+std.cmp::Equal+` and `+core::Format+`: `+equal+` fails with `+expected E, got A+` unless
`+actual.eq(&expected)+`, and `+not_equal+` with `+expected a value other than U+` when
`+actual.eq(&unexpected)+`, each value written by its formatting. `+equal_text+` compares the
bytes of two texts and fails with `+expected "E", got "A"+`, and `+contains+` fails with
`+expected "T" to contain "P"+` unless the part occurs in the text. An assertion that holds
allocates nothing.

[[R-SLIB-TEST-0002]]
*R-SLIB-TEST-0002* — `+std.test::runner+` is the Move account that the test entry of Core
R-FUNC-0025 keeps of a test run:

* `+std.test::runner::create() -> std.test::runner+`;
* `+std.test::runner::start(std.test::runner* this, str name) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::runner::fail(std.test::runner* this, const std.test::failure* reason) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::runner::fault(std.test::runner* this, std.error::fault error) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::runner::unexpected(std.test::runner* this, E error) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::runner::expected(std.test::runner* this, E error) -> void+`;
* `+std.test::runner::missing(std.test::runner* this, str expected) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::runner::inject(std.test::runner* this, u64 attempt, u64 attempts) -> void+`;
* `+std.test::runner::passing(const std.test::runner* this) -> bool+`;
* `+std.test::runner::finish(std.test::runner* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::runner::summary(const std.test::runner* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.test::runner::status(const std.test::runner* this) -> i32+`.

`+start+` begins a test and returns `+test NAME ... +`. The first problem of a test counts:
`+fail+` records the message of the failure, `+fault+` `+error N+` with the portable name N of
the standard error, `+unexpected+`, generic over the error type `+E+`, `+error T+` with the
type name T of `+core::type_name::<E>()+`, and `+missing+` `+expected the error E+`;
`+expected+`, generic in the same way, records nothing. After `+inject+` with a nonzero attempt
N, a problem that is recorded ends with `+ (allocation N failing)+`. `+passing+` tells whether
the test has no problem. `+finish+` counts the test and returns `+ok+`, `+ok (N allocation
failures)+` when `+inject+` gave it N attempts, or `+FAILED: PROBLEM+`, with a line feed;
`+summary+` returns `+N tests: P passed, F failed+` with a line feed, and `+status+` returns
zero when no test failed and one otherwise.

[[R-SLIB-TEST-0003]]
*R-SLIB-TEST-0003* — The allocation failures of tests act on the hosted allocator of the
program:

* `+std.test::allocation_attempts() -> u64+`;
* `+std.test::fail_allocation_at(u64 attempt) -> void+`.

`+fail_allocation_at+` restarts the count of allocation attempts of the hosted allocator; the
attempt with the given number after the restart fails as if no memory were left, every other
attempt proceeds, and zero makes none fail. `+allocation_attempts+` returns the attempts since
the last restart. The count covers every allocation of the program, those of other threads
included. Both operations allocate nothing and cannot fail.

=== 21.16 Transport security: `+std.tls+`

[[R-SLIB-TLS-0001]]
*R-SLIB-TLS-0001* — `+std.tls+` is a standard module written in R in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile that secures a byte stream with TLS 1.3 or TLS 1.2 (RFC 8446,
RFC 5246). Its engine is the native provider `+std.tls.native+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0003) over
Mbed TLS 3.6, which exchanges records through memory; the R part moves those records over any
`+std.stream::Stream+`. Certificates are X.509 v3 in PEM or DER; the provider draws its
randomness from the operating system. The provider's objects are internally synchronized:
the owners of this module are Send+Sync.

[[R-SLIB-TLS-0002]]
*R-SLIB-TLS-0002* — `+std.tls::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly
`+invalid_certificate+`, `+invalid_key+`, `+untrusted_certificate+`, `+name_mismatch+`,
`+expired_certificate+`, `+handshake_failed+`, `+peer_alert+`, `+protocol_error+`,
`+invalid_argument+`, `+frozen+`, `+closed+` and `+missing_authorities+`, formatted by its
variant names (`+@derive(format)+`). `+std.tls::tls_error+` is an error of the module with the fields
`+std.tls::error_code code+` and `+i64 native_code+`, the error of Mbed TLS or zero.
`+std.tls::version+` is the Copy fieldless enum with `+tls12+` and `+tls13+`, formatted by its
variant names. A failure of memory in the provider is `+std.alloc::alloc_error::out_of_memory+`.

[[R-SLIB-TLS-0003]]
*R-SLIB-TLS-0003* — `+std.tls::config+` is the Move settings of one side, shared by the
sessions made from it:

* `+std.tls::client_config() -> std.tls::config throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.tls::server_config() -> std.tls::config throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.tls::config::add_authority(std.tls::config* this, const u8[] certificates) -> void throws std.tls::tls_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.tls::config::set_identity(std.tls::config* this, const u8[] chain, const std.secret::buffer* key) -> void throws std.tls::tls_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.tls::config::add_protocol(std.tls::config* this, str name) -> void throws std.tls::tls_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.tls::config::set_verification(std.tls::config* this, bool required) -> void throws std.tls::tls_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+add_authority+` trusts the certificates of a PEM text or of one DER certificate and fails
with `+invalid_certificate+` for anything else. `+set_identity+` sets the certificate chain
that this side presents and its private key; a key that does not parse, or does not match the
chain, is `+invalid_key+`. `+add_protocol+` offers an ALPN name of 1 to 255 bytes without a
zero byte, at most eight in the order of the calls; any other name is `+invalid_argument+`.
A client verifies the chain of its server against its authorities by default, and
`+set_verification(false)+` skips that verification; a server does not ask for client
certificates. The first session made from a configuration freezes it: a later change fails
with `+frozen+`. A server configuration needs an identity before its first session, and a
client configuration that verifies needs a server name. The configuration lives until its
last session ends.

[[R-SLIB-TLS-0004]]
*R-SLIB-TLS-0004* — `+std.tls::stream<S: std.stream::Stream>+` is a Move session over the
transport S that it owns:

* `+@scoped async std.tls::connect<S: std.stream::Stream & unborrowed>(S transport, const std.tls::config* settings, str server_name) -> std.tls::stream<S> throws std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.tls::accept<S: std.stream::Stream & unborrowed>(S transport, const std.tls::config* settings) -> std.tls::stream<S> throws std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+std.tls::stream<S>::protocol(const std.tls::stream<S>* this) -> str+`;
* `+std.tls::stream<S>::version(const std.tls::stream<S>* this) -> std.tls::version+`.

`+connect+` starts a client session that sends `+server_name+` by SNI and, when it verifies,
requires the certificate of the server to name it: a DNS name matches a subject alternative
name or the common name, and an IP address a subject alternative IP address. `+accept+`
starts a server session. Both complete the handshake before they return, sending an alert
of their own failure to the peer before they report it: a chain that no authority signs is
`+untrusted_certificate+`, a certificate outside its validity `+expired_certificate+`, a name
that does not match `+name_mismatch+`, an alert of the peer `+peer_alert+`, records that do
not parse `+protocol_error+`, the end of the transport and any other failure
`+handshake_failed+`; a failure of the transport is its fault. `+protocol+` returns the ALPN
name that the handshake selected, empty when none was, as a view of the stream that allocates
nothing: the name stays valid and unchanged while the stream is borrowed (Core R-UNSAFE-0008).
`+version+` returns the negotiated version.

[[R-SLIB-TLS-0005]]
*R-SLIB-TLS-0005* — `+std.tls::stream<S>+` implements `+std.stream::Reader+`,
`+std.stream::Writer+` and `+std.stream::Stream+` (R-SLIB-STREAM-0001). `+read_into+`
returns application data, reading the transport as the session needs records; it returns
zero after the close_notify of the peer, and an end of the transport without close_notify is
`+std.io::io_error+` with `+broken_pipe+`, since the data may be truncated. `+write_from+`
encrypts the whole source and writes its records to the transport before it returns the
length of the source. `+flush+` writes pending records and flushes the transport, and
`+shutdown+` sends close_notify and then shuts down the transport. One task may read while
another writes; the session orders their records.

[[R-SLIB-TLS-0006]]
*R-SLIB-TLS-0006* — After its handshake a session reports a failure of memory as
`+std.alloc::alloc_error::out_of_memory+` and every other failure of the engine as
`+std.io::io_error+` with `+other+` and the error of Mbed TLS as its native code. A failed session keeps its failure: every later operation reports it again. A session
releases its engine state when it is dropped, whether or not it was shut down.

[[R-SLIB-TLS-0007]]
*R-SLIB-TLS-0007* — `+async std.tls::system_client_config() -> std.tls::config throws
std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+` returns a client configuration of R-SLIB-TLS-0003 that
trusts the certificate authorities of the system. When the environment variable
`+SSL_CERT_FILE+` is set and not empty, the authorities are the certificates of the PEM file it
names, and a failure to read that file is reported. Otherwise they are those of the first file
of the system that can be read among `+/etc/ssl/cert.pem+`, the bundle of macOS, and
`+/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt+`, `+/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt+`,
`+/etc/pki/ca-trust/extracted/pem/tls-ca-bundle.pem+`, `+/etc/ssl/ca-bundle.pem+` and
`+/etc/pki/tls/cacert.pem+`, the bundles of common Linux distributions. Each certificate of the
bundle is trusted on its own and a certificate that does not parse is skipped. A bundle
without any certificate that parses, and a system without such a file, are
`+missing_authorities+`. The configuration may still be changed before its first session, so
a program may add authorities of its own.

=== 21.17 URLs: `+std.url+`

[[R-SLIB-URL-0001]]
*R-SLIB-URL-0001* — `+std.url+` is a `+hosted+` module written in R for the URIs of RFC 3986.
`+std.url::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+empty+`, `+missing_scheme+`,
`+invalid_scheme+`, `+invalid_character+`, `+invalid_percent_encoding+`, `+invalid_host+`,
`+invalid_port+` and `+invalid_utf8+`, formatted by its variant names, and
`+std.url::url_error+` is an error of the module with the fields `+std.url::error_code code+`
and `+usize offset+`, the byte of the text where the failure was found.

[[R-SLIB-URL-0002]]
*R-SLIB-URL-0002* — `+std.url::url+` is a Move absolute URL in its parts:

* `+std.url::parse(str text) -> std.url::url throws std.url::url_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.url::url::scheme(const std.url::url* this) -> str+`, `+host+`, `+path+` and
  `+has_authority(const std.url::url* this) -> bool+`, `+is_ipv6(const std.url::url* this) -> bool+`;
* `+std.url::url::userinfo+`, `+query+` and `+fragment(const std.url::url* this) -> o<str>+`;
* `+std.url::url::port(const std.url::url* this) -> o<u16>+` and `+effective_port(const std.url::url* this) -> o<u16>+`;
* `+std.url::default_port(str scheme) -> o<u16>+`;
* `+std.url::url::target+`, `+authority_text+` and `+text(const std.url::url* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+parse+` accepts a scheme followed by `+:+`, then `+//+` and an authority when present, a
path, a query after `+?+` and a fragment after `+#+`, each holding only the characters that its
production of RFC 3986 section 3 allows and valid percent-encoded triplets, which are kept as
written. The scheme and a registered name or IPv4 host are kept in lower case; an IPv6 host is
written in brackets and kept without them; a port is at most 65535, and an empty port is none.
An empty text is `+empty+`, a text whose part before its first `+:+` is not a scheme
`+invalid_scheme+`, and a text without a scheme `+missing_scheme+`. `+default_port+` is 80 for `+http+` and `+ws+` and
443 for `+https+` and `+wss+`, compared without ASCII case, and none otherwise;
`+effective_port+` is the explicit port or that default. `+target+` is the path, `+/+` when it is
empty, and the query after `+?+`; `+authority_text+` is the host, in brackets for IPv6, and the
explicit port after `+:+`; `+text+` recomposes the URL as RFC 3986 section 5.3 describes, and
`+std.url::url+` implements `+core::Format+` with that text.

[[R-SLIB-URL-0003]]
*R-SLIB-URL-0003* — `+std.url::url::resolve(const std.url::url* this, str reference) ->
std.url::url throws std.url::url_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` resolves a URI reference
against the URL as its base by the strict algorithm of RFC 3986 section 5.2.2, with the dot
segments removed as section 5.2.4 describes; a relative path whose first segment holds `+:+` is
`+missing_scheme+`. `+std.url::url::normalized(const std.url::url* this) -> std.url::url throws
std.alloc::alloc_error+` returns the syntax-based normal form of section 6.2.2: percent-encoded
triplets in upper-case hexadecimal, the triplets of unreserved characters decoded and the dot
segments removed; for `+http+`, `+https+`, `+ws+` and `+wss+` it also drops the default port
and writes an empty path with an authority as `+/+` (section 6.2.3).

[[R-SLIB-URL-0004]]
*R-SLIB-URL-0004* — `+std.url::percent_encode(str text) -> std.string::string throws
std.alloc::alloc_error+` writes every byte except the unreserved characters of RFC 3986 as a
triplet in upper-case hexadecimal. `+std.url::percent_decode(str text) -> std.string::string
throws std.url::url_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` decodes the triplets and keeps every other
byte; an incomplete or non-hexadecimal triplet is `+invalid_percent_encoding+` and decoded bytes
that are not UTF-8 are `+invalid_utf8+`.

[[R-SLIB-URL-0005]]
*R-SLIB-URL-0005* — `+std.url::query_pair+` is the Move pair of fields
`+std.string::string name+` and `+std.string::string value+`.
`+std.url::parse_query(str query) -> array<std.url::query_pair> throws std.url::url_error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+` reads the application/x-www-form-urlencoded form: pieces separated by
`+&+`, empty pieces skipped, each split at its first `+=+` (a piece without one has an empty
value), `+++` read as a space and the triplets decoded as `+percent_decode+` does.
`+std.url::append_query(std.string::string* query, str name, str value) -> void throws
std.alloc::alloc_error+` appends `+name=value+` in that form, after `+&+` when the query is not
empty: unreserved characters are kept, a space is written as `+++` and every other byte as a
triplet.

=== 21.18 Media types: `+std.mime+`

[[R-SLIB-MIME-0001]]
*R-SLIB-MIME-0001* — `+std.mime+` is a `+hosted+` module written in R for the media types of
RFC 9110 section 8.3.1. `+std.mime::mime_error+` is an error of the module with the field
`+usize offset+`, and `+std.mime::parameter+` the Move pair of fields
`+std.string::string name+` and `+std.string::string value+`. `+std.mime::media_type+` is a Move
media type:

* `+std.mime::parse(str text) -> std.mime::media_type throws std.mime::mime_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mime::media_type::kind+` and `+subtype(const std.mime::media_type* this) -> str+`;
* `+std.mime::media_type::essence(const std.mime::media_type* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mime::media_type::parameter(const std.mime::media_type* this, str name) -> o<str>+`;
* `+std.mime::media_type::parameter_count(const std.mime::media_type* this) -> usize+`;
* `+std.mime::is_token(str text) -> bool+` and `+std.mime::is_token_byte(u8 value) -> bool+`.

`+parse+` accepts white space, a type token, `+/+`, a subtype token and parameters, each after
`+;+` and optional white space, as a token name, `+=+` and a token or a quoted string whose
backslash escapes the next byte; a final `+;+` is allowed. Type, subtype and parameter names
are kept in lower case, and values without their quotes and escapes; anything else is an error
at the offset of the first byte that does not fit. `+essence+` is `+type/subtype+`, and
`+parameter+` the value of the first parameter with the name compared without ASCII case.
`+std.mime::media_type+` implements `+core::Format+` as the type, the subtype and
`+; name=value+` for each parameter, the value quoted with escapes when it is not a token.
`+is_token+` tells whether a text is a nonempty token of RFC 9110 section 5.6.2.

[[R-SLIB-MIME-0002]]
*R-SLIB-MIME-0002* — `+std.mime::from_extension(str extension) -> o<str>+` is the media type
of a file name extension given without its dot and compared without ASCII case: `+html+`,
`+htm+`, `+css+`, `+js+`, `+mjs+`, `+txt+`, `+csv+` and `+md+` are text types with
`+charset=utf-8+`, and `+json+`, `+xml+`, `+svg+`, `+png+`, `+jpg+`, `+jpeg+`, `+gif+`,
`+webp+`, `+ico+`, `+wasm+`, `+pdf+`, `+zip+`, `+gz+`, `+tar+`, `+mp3+`, `+mp4+`, `+webm+`,
`+woff+`, `+woff2+`, `+otf+` and `+ttf+` have their registered types; any other is none.
`+std.mime::for_path(str path) -> str+` is that type for the extension after the last `+.+` of
the last segment of a path, and `+application/octet-stream+` when there is none or it is
unknown.

=== 21.19 HTTP/1.1: `+std.http+`

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0001]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0001* — `+std.http+` is a `+hosted-native-async+` module written in R for
HTTP/1.1 (RFC 9110 and RFC 9112) over any `+std.stream::Stream+`. `+std.http::error_code+` is a
Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+invalid_request_line+`, `+invalid_status_line+`,
`+invalid_header+`, `+head_too_large+`, `+too_many_headers+`, `+body_too_large+`,
`+invalid_length+`, `+invalid_chunk+`, `+unsupported_method+`, `+unsupported_version+`,
`+unsupported_encoding+`, `+invalid_encoding+`, `+unexpected_end+`, `+invalid_url+`,
`+unsupported_scheme+`, `+too_many_redirects+` and `+timed_out+`, formatted by its variant names,
and `+std.http::http_error+` an error of the module with the field `+std.http::error_code code+`.
`+std.http::method+` is the Copy fieldless enum with `+get+`, `+head+`, `+post+`, `+put+`,
`+delete+`, `+patch+`, `+options+`, `+trace+` and `+connect+`;
`+std.http::method_name(std.http::method value) -> str+` is its upper-case name and
`+std.http::parse_method(str name) -> o<std.http::method>+` the method of a name, compared
exactly.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0002]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0002* — `+std.http::header+` is the Move pair of fields `+std.string::string
name+` and `+std.string::string value+`, and `+std.http::headers+` the Move header section of a
message, its fields in the order they were added:

* `+std.http::headers::create() -> std.http::headers+`;
* `+std.http::headers::add(std.http::headers* this, str name, str value) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::headers::set(std.http::headers* this, str name, str value) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::headers::remove(std.http::headers* this, str name) -> usize throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::headers::get(const std.http::headers* this, str name) -> o<str>+`;
* `+std.http::headers::contains(const std.http::headers* this, str name) -> bool+`;
* `+std.http::headers::has_token(const std.http::headers* this, str name, str token) -> bool+`;
* `+std.http::headers::count(const std.http::headers* this) -> usize+`, `+name_at+` and `+value_at(const std.http::headers* this, usize index) -> str+`.

`+add+` appends a field whose name is a token and whose value, kept without the white space
around it, holds no control character but a tab; any other is `+invalid_header+`. `+remove+`
removes the fields with the name, compared without ASCII case, and returns their count, and
`+set+` replaces them by one field. `+get+` is the value of the first field with the name.
`+has_token+` tells whether a field with the name lists the token among its comma-separated
elements, compared without ASCII case, as `+Connection+` and `+Transfer-Encoding+` do.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0003]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0003* — `+std.http::request+` is a Move request with the fields
`+std.http::method method+`, `+std.string::string target+`, `+u8 minor_version+` of HTTP/1,
`+std.http::headers headers+` and `+bytes body+`, and the path parameters of R-SLIB-HTTP-0006;
`+std.http::response+` is a Move response with the fields `+u16 status+`,
`+std.http::headers headers+` and `+bytes body+`:

* `+std.http::request::create(std.http::method value, str target) -> std.http::request throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::request::path(const std.http::request* this) -> str+` and `+query(const std.http::request* this) -> o<str>+`;
* `+std.http::response::create(u16 status) -> std.http::response+`;
* `+std.http::response::text(u16 status, str body) -> std.http::response throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` and `+json+`;
* `+std.http::reason(u16 status) -> str+`.

A created request has HTTP/1.1, no fields and an empty body. `+path+` is the target before its
first `+?+` and `+query+` the rest after it. `+text+` and `+json+` answer with the body and the
`+Content-Type+` `+text/plain; charset=utf-8+` or `+application/json+`. `+reason+` is the
reason phrase of RFC 9110 section 15 for the status codes it lists and empty for others.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0004]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0004* — `+std.http::limits+` bounds the reading of a message with the fields
`+usize max_head = 16384+`, `+u32 max_headers = 100+`, `+usize max_body = 8388608+` and
`+std.time::duration request_timeout+` of ten seconds.
`+std.http::body_state+` is the Copy framing of a body being read, and
`+std.http::body_state::done(const std.http::body_state* this) -> bool+` tells whether it ended.
For `+T: std.stream::Stream+`:
