* `+@scoped async std.http::read_request_head(std.bufio::reader<T>* input, const std.http::limits* bounds) -> o<std.http::request> throws std.http::http_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+std.http::request::framing(const std.http::request* this) -> std.http::body_state throws std.http::http_error+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::read_body(std.bufio::reader<T>* input, std.http::body_state* state, u8[] target) -> usize throws std.http::http_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::read_whole_body(std.bufio::reader<T>* input, std.http::body_state* state, usize limit) -> bytes throws std.http::http_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::read_request(std.bufio::reader<T>* input, const std.http::limits* bounds) -> o<std.http::request> throws std.http::http_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::read_response(std.bufio::reader<T>* input, std.http::method sent, const std.http::limits* bounds) -> std.http::response throws std.http::http_error, std.error::fault+`.

Lines end with a line feed, optionally after a carriage return, and empty lines before a
request line are skipped. `+read_request_head+` returns none when the stream ends before the
first byte of a request; a request line is a known method, one space, a target of visible
ASCII, one space and `+HTTP/1.0+` or `+HTTP/1.1+`. A field line is a token, `+:+` and a value;
white space before the colon and an obsolete folded line are `+invalid_header+`. A head beyond
`+max_head+` bytes is `+head_too_large+` and beyond `+max_headers+` fields
`+too_many_headers+`; an unknown method is `+unsupported_method+` and another HTTP version
`+unsupported_version+`. The framing of a request is chunked when `+Transfer-Encoding+` is
`+chunked+`, otherwise its `+Content-Length+`, otherwise no body; another transfer coding is
`+unsupported_encoding+`, and both fields, a length that is not decimal or several lengths that
differ are `+invalid_length+`. `+read_body+` places the next bytes of the body and returns zero
at its end: a chunked body skips chunk extensions and its trailer section, and a malformed
chunk is `+invalid_chunk+`; an end of the stream inside a body of known length or of chunks is
`+unexpected_end+`. `+read_whole_body+` reads the rest of a body under the limit, beyond which it
is `+body_too_large+`, and `+read_request+` reads a head and its whole body under
`+max_body+`. `+read_response+` skips interim 1xx responses; a response to `+HEAD+` and a 204 or
304 response have no body, and a response without `+Transfer-Encoding+` and
`+Content-Length+` ends with the connection.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0005]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0005* — For `+T: std.stream::Stream+`:

* `+@scoped async std.http::write_request(const T* output, const std.http::request* message, str host) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::write_response(const T* output, const std.http::response* message, bool head, bool close) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::write_chunked_head(const T* output, const std.http::response* message, bool close) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::write_chunk(const T* output, const u8[] data) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::finish_chunks(const T* output) -> void throws std.error::fault+`.

The writers set the framing themselves and skip `+Content-Length+` and `+Transfer-Encoding+`
fields of the header section. `+write_request+` writes the request line with HTTP/1.1, `+Host+`
with the given host unless the section has one, the fields, and a `+Content-Length+` for a
nonempty body or a `+POST+`, `+PUT+` or `+PATCH+`. `+write_response+` writes the status line,
the fields, `+Connection: close+` when close is true, and `+Content-Length+` for every final
status but 204 and 304, then the body unless head is true. `+write_chunked_head+` writes a
status line, the fields, `+Connection: close+` when close is true and
`+Transfer-Encoding: chunked+`, `+write_chunk+` one nonempty
chunk and `+finish_chunks+` the last chunk with an empty trailer section.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0006]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0006* — For shared state `+S: send & sync & unborrowed+`,
`+std.http::router<S>+` is the Move set of routes and hooks of a server:

* `+std.http::router<S>::create() -> std.http::router<S>+`;
* `+std.http::router<S>::add(std.http::router<S>* this, std.http::method value, str pattern, async fn(arc S, std.http::request) -> std.http::response throws(std.error::fault) handler) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::router<S>::before(std.http::router<S>* this, fn(const S*, const std.http::request*) -> o<std.http::response> throws(std.alloc::alloc_error) hook) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::router<S>::after(std.http::router<S>* this, fn(std.http::method, str, std.http::response*) -> void throws(std.alloc::alloc_error) hook) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::request::param(const std.http::request* this, str name) -> o<str>+`.

`+add_stream+` of R-SLIB-HTTP-0009 adds a route whose handler streams its response, and
`+add_upgrade+` of R-SLIB-HTTP-0011 a route whose handler takes over the connection.

A pattern starts with `+/+`, otherwise `+add+` throws `+invalid_url+`; its segments are literal,
`+{name}+` for one nonempty segment captured under the name and percent-decoded, or a final `+*+`
that captures the rest of the path under `+*+`. A request is answered by the first before hook,
in the order of the calls, that returns a response; otherwise by the handler of the first
route, in the order of the calls of `+add+`, `+add_stream+` and `+add_upgrade+`, whose pattern matches the path of the request and whose
method is the method of the request, where `+HEAD+` also matches a `+GET+` route; with the
parameters that route captured, which `+param+` returns. When patterns match but no method
does, the answer is 405 with `+Allow+` listing their methods, when no pattern matches it is
404, and a handler that throws is answered with 500. Every after hook then sees the method,
the target and the response.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0007]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0007* — For `+S: send & sync & unborrowed+`:

* `+async std.http::serve(std.net::tcp_listener listener, std.service::options settings, std.sync::receiver<std.service::stop> stop, arc S state, std.http::router<S> routes, std.http::limits bounds) -> std.service::report throws std.async::start_error, std.net::net_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+async std.http::serve_tls(std.net::tcp_listener listener, std.service::options settings, std.sync::receiver<std.service::stop> stop, arc S state, std.http::router<S> routes, std.http::limits bounds, arc std.tls::config tls) -> std.service::report throws std.async::start_error, std.net::net_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

Both serve the connections of the listener with `+std.service::serve_with+`
(R-SLIB-SERVICE-0003); `+serve_tls+` first completes a TLS server handshake on each connection
and drops a connection whose handshake fails. A connection answers its requests in order, each
read whole within `+request_timeout+`: an HTTP/1.1 request keeps it open unless it lists `+close+` in
`+Connection+`, and an HTTP/1.0 request only when it lists `+keep-alive+`. A request that cannot
be read is answered with 400, 413 for `+body_too_large+`, 431 for `+head_too_large+` and
`+too_many_headers+`, 501 for `+unsupported_method+` and `+unsupported_encoding+`, or 505 for
`+unsupported_version+`, and closes the connection; a connection that sends no request in time
is closed without an answer. Each response carries `+Date+` unless it has one and a response to
`+HEAD+` no body. Before it closes a connection the server ends its write direction and reads
what the client still sends, at most 64 KiB during at most two seconds, so that the client
reads the response before the connection closes.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0008]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0008* — `+std.http::client_options+` holds `+u32 max_redirects = 5+`,
`+u32 max_idle = 4+`, `+std.http::limits bounds+` and `+bool accept_gzip = true+`, and
`+std.http::client+` is a Move client with a pool of kept-alive connections:

* `+std.http::client::create(std.http::client_options settings) -> std.http::client+`;
* `+std.http::client::with_tls(std.http::client_options settings, arc std.tls::config tls) -> std.http::client+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::client::send(std.http::client* this, std.http::request message, str address) -> std.http::response throws std.http::http_error, std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::client::get(std.http::client* this, str address) -> std.http::response throws std.http::http_error, std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+std.http::client::idle_count(const std.http::client* this) -> usize+`.

`+send+` sends the request to the absolute URL: a text that is no URL is `+invalid_url+`, and a
scheme other than `+http+` and `+https+`, or `+https+` for a client without a TLS configuration,
is `+unsupported_scheme+`. The target of the request becomes that of the URL, `+Host+` its
authority and, when `+accept_gzip+` is true and the request has none,
`+Accept-Encoding: gzip+` is added. The request goes on an idle connection to the same scheme,
host and port, or on a new one that connects to the host by name and, for `+https+`, completes a
TLS client handshake that verifies the host (R-SLIB-TLS-0004); a failure on an idle connection
before its response is retried once on a new connection. After the response a connection that
may carry another request returns to the pool while it holds fewer than `+max_idle+` idle
connections. A response 301, 302, 303, 307 or 308 with `+Location+` is followed, the location
resolved against the URL, up to `+max_redirects+` times, beyond which the result is
`+too_many_redirects+`; 303, and 301 or 302 after `+POST+`, continue with `+GET+` and no body.
The final response with `+Content-Encoding: gzip+` is decoded under `+max_body+`
(`+body_too_large+` beyond it, `+invalid_encoding+` for a corrupt body) and loses its
`+Content-Encoding+` and `+Content-Length+` fields. `+get+` sends `+GET+`, and `+idle_count+`
is the number of idle connections.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0009]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0009* — `+std.http::body_writer+` is the Move writer through which a streaming
handler sends its response:

* `+std.http::router<S>::add_stream(std.http::router<S>* this, std.http::method value, str pattern, async fn(arc S, std.http::request, std.http::body_writer) -> void throws(std.error::fault) streamer) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::body_writer::start(std.http::body_writer* this, std.http::response first) -> bool throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::body_writer::send(std.http::body_writer* this, bytes chunk) -> bool throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::body_writer::send_text(std.http::body_writer* this, str text) -> bool throws std.error::fault+`.

The server runs a streaming handler as a task of a group of the connection, next to the writer
of its response, so the handler ends before the connection serves another request. `+start+`
sends the status and fields of the response, whose body is ignored; only the first start
counts. `+send+` sends one chunk, after a head of status 200 when none was sent; both wait
while the connection has four parts it has not written yet. The head passes the after hooks
and is written with chunked framing when it arrives, each chunk as it arrives, and the body
ends when the handler drops its writer; a response to `+HEAD+` writes the head only. `+start+`
and `+send+` return false once the connection is gone, as when the client closed it. While the
handler runs, the server also watches the connection: when the client ends it, the handler is
cancelled (Core R-STMT-0018) and the connection closes; a byte that arrives meanwhile, such as a
pipelined request, closes the connection after the response. A handler
that ends without a head is answered with 500; a handler that throws after its head leaves the
body without its last chunk and closes the connection, so the client sees an incomplete body.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0010]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0010* — The event stream format of the HTML standard (`+text/event-stream+`):

* `+std.http::sse_event(str event, str id, str data) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::sse_message+`, the Move message with the fields `+std.string::string event+`, `+id+` and `+data+`;
* `+std.http::sse_parser::create() -> std.http::sse_parser+`;
* `+std.http::sse_parser::feed(std.http::sse_parser* this, const u8[] input) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::sse_parser::next(std.http::sse_parser* this) -> o<std.http::sse_message> throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+sse_event+` writes an `+event:+` line and an `+id:+` line when they are not empty, each cut at
its first line end, a `+data:+` line for each line of the data, split at LF, CRLF and CR, and an
empty line. An `+std.http::sse_parser+` takes the bytes of a stream as they arrive: `+next+`
returns the next message whose empty line has arrived and none until more bytes arrive. A line
ends with LF, CRLF or CR; a line that starts with `+:+` is a comment; a field is the text before
the first `+:+` and its value the rest without one leading space; `+data+` appends its value and
LF to the data, `+event+` sets the event type, `+id+` without a zero byte sets the last event id,
and other fields are ignored. An empty line with data yields a message with the event type,
`+message+` when none was set, the last event id and the data without its last LF, and resets the
data and the event type; an empty line without data resets the event type. Bytes that are not
UTF-8 are `+invalid_encoding+`.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0011]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0011* — Switching protocols (RFC 9110 section 7.8). `+std.http::upgraded+` is the
Move pair of fields `+own dyn(std.stream::Stream)* transport+` and `+bytes buffered+`: a connection
that has switched to another protocol and the bytes already read from it after the head of the
exchange. `+std.http::upgrade+` is the Move connection of a request to an upgrade route before any
response, and `+std.http::handshake+` the Move pair of fields `+std.http::response answer+` and
`+o<std.http::upgraded> connection+`:

* `+std.http::router<S>::add_upgrade(std.http::router<S>* this, std.http::method value, str pattern, async fn(arc S, std.http::request, std.http::upgrade) -> void throws(std.error::fault) upgrader) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+async std.http::upgrade::accept(std.http::upgrade this, std.http::headers fields) -> std.http::upgraded throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+async std.http::upgrade::refuse(std.http::upgrade this, std.http::response answer) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::client::upgrade(std.http::client* this, std.http::request message, str address) -> std.http::handshake throws std.http::http_error, std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+`.

A request whose route was added with `+add_upgrade+` hands its connection to the handler of the
route, and the connection then serves no other request; the before hooks still see the request
first. `+accept+` writes a 101 (Switching Protocols) response with the fields and `+Date+` and no
body, and returns the connection with the bytes that the server had read after the request.
`+refuse+` writes the response with `+Date+` and `+Connection: close+` and closes the connection as
R-SLIB-HTTP-0007 does; a handler that ends without either, or throws, ends the connection.
`+client::upgrade+` sends the request to the absolute `+http+` or `+https+` URL as `+send+` does
(R-SLIB-HTTP-0008), with its target and `+Host+` from the URL, on a new connection outside the pool,
and does not follow redirects. A 101 response ends the exchange with its head and hands over the
connection in `+connection+`; any other response is read whole and closes the connection.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0012]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0012* — `+std.http::streamed+` is a Move response whose body is read as it
arrives, on a connection of its own: the public field `+std.http::response head+` holds the
status and header fields, and its body is empty.

* `+@scoped async std.http::client::open(const std.http::client* this, std.http::request message, str address) -> std.http::streamed throws std.http::http_error, std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::streamed::next(std.http::streamed* this) -> o<bytes> throws std.http::http_error, std.error::fault+`.

`+open+` sends the request like `+send+` of R-SLIB-HTTP-0008 to the absolute `+http+` or
`+https+` URL, with the same failures for a text that is no URL and for another scheme, but on a
new connection outside the pool; it reads the head of the response within the limits of the
client and returns. Redirects are not followed and no content coding is asked for. `+next+`
returns the next piece of the body under its framing, at most 16 KiB, as it arrives, and none
at its end; a body that ends before its framing does is `+unexpected_end+`. Dropping the
response closes its connection, which ends an endless body such as an event stream.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0013]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0013* — A router answers two endpoints itself, before every before hook and route:

* `+std.http::router<S>::health(std.http::router<S>* this, str path, std.service::health status) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
answers GET and HEAD of path with 200 and the text `+ok+` and a line feed while the health is
serving (R-SLIB-SERVICE-0006), and with 503 and `+stopping+` once it no longer is;
* `+std.http::router<S>::metrics(std.http::router<S>* this, str path, std.metrics::registry registry) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
answers GET and HEAD of path with 200, the media type `+std.metrics::content_type+` and the
registry rendered by R-SLIB-METRICS-0004.

A path that does not start with `+/+` is `+invalid_url+`, and a second call replaces the endpoint
of the first. The path is compared with the path of the request target, without its query.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0014]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0014* —
`+async std.http::serve_all(array<std.service::listener> listeners, std.service::options settings, std.sync::receiver<std.service::stop> stop, std.service::health status, arc S state, std.http::router<S> routes, std.http::limits bounds) -> std.service::report throws std.async::start_error, std.net::net_error, std.process::process_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
with `+@generic<S: send & sync & unborrowed>+` serves HTTP/1.1 as serve of R-SLIB-HTTP-0007 on
every connection of std.service::serve_all (R-SLIB-SERVICE-0007): TCP, Unix-domain sockets and
HTTPS over the TLS listeners, under the idle timeout, the signals and the health of that service.
A connection whose read waits past the idle timeout ends like one that its client closed.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0015]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0015* — For shared state `+S: send & sync & unborrowed+` and a request context
`+C: send & sync & unborrowed+`, `+std.http::app<S, C>+` is the Move set of routes and middleware
of a server whose requests carry a context, and `+std.http::flow<C>+` is one request on its way
through it, a Move value with the fields `+std.http::request request+`, `+C context+`,
`+std.http::notes notes+`, `+std.string::string route+`, `+std.http::response response+` and
`+bool answered+`.
`+std.http::notes+` is a Move, Send+Sync handle of named texts of one request:

* `+std.http::app<S, C>::create(fn(const S*, const std.http::request*) -> C context) -> std.http::app<S, C>+`;
* `+std.http::app<S, C>::route(std.http::app<S, C>* this, std.http::method value, str pattern, async fn(arc S, std.http::flow<C>) -> std.http::flow<C> throws(std.error::fault) handler) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::app<S, C>::before(std.http::app<S, C>* this, str prefix, async fn(arc S, std.http::flow<C>) -> std.http::flow<C> throws(std.error::fault) hook) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::app<S, C>::after(std.http::app<S, C>* this, str prefix, async fn(arc S, std.http::flow<C>) -> std.http::flow<C> throws(std.error::fault) hook) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::app<S, C>::around(std.http::app<S, C>* this, str prefix, async fn(arc S, std.http::flow<C>) -> std.http::flow<C> throws(std.error::fault) before, async fn(arc S, std.http::flow<C>) -> std.http::flow<C> throws(std.error::fault) after) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::flow<C>::with(std.http::flow<C> this, std.http::response value) -> std.http::flow<C>+`;
* `+std.http::notes::set(const std.http::notes* this, str name, str value) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::notes::get(const std.http::notes* this, str name) -> o<std.string::string> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::notes::count(const std.http::notes* this) -> usize+`;
* `+std.http::notes::name_at(const std.http::notes* this, usize index) -> o<std.string::string> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::notes::value_at(const std.http::notes* this, usize index) -> o<std.string::string> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.http::app<S, C>::on_panic(std.http::app<S, C>* this, fn(const S*, std.http::method, str, const std.http::notes*, const std.thread::panic_report*) -> void hook) -> void+`;
* `+@scoped async std.http::app<S, C>::dispatch(const std.http::app<S, C>* this, arc S state, std.http::request incoming) -> std.http::response throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+async std.http::serve_app(array<std.service::listener> listeners, std.service::options settings, std.sync::receiver<std.service::stop> stop, std.service::health status, arc S state, std.http::app<S, C> routes, std.http::limits bounds) -> std.service::report throws std.async::start_error, std.net::net_error, std.process::process_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

A pattern or prefix starts with `+/+`, otherwise the call throws `+invalid_url+`. Each request
gets the context that `+context+` makes from the state and the request, in a flow with empty
notes whose response has status 200, no field and no body, and is not answered. `+set+` gives a
name a text, replacing the one it had; `+get+` returns a copy of the text of a name;
`+count+`, `+name_at+` and `+value_at+` give the names and texts in the order the names were
first set, none past the last. A middleware applies to a request
whose path equals its prefix or continues it after a `+/+`; the prefix `+/+` applies to every
request. The before hooks of the middleware that apply run in the order of the calls, each with
the flow that the previous one returned; a hook may change the request and the context, and it
answers the request by returning a flow made by `+with+`, which sets the response and
`+answered+`: the remaining before hooks and the route are then skipped. Otherwise the route of
R-SLIB-HTTP-0018 receives the flow, with the path parameters it captured and its pattern in
`+route+`, which is empty when no route receives the flow. Then the after hooks of
every middleware that applied and whose before hook, if it has one, ran see the flow in the
reverse order of the calls. The response of the last flow answers the request. A hook or handler
that throws is answered with 500 without a body, and no further hook runs. `+dispatch+` answers a
request as the server would, without a connection: it answers a preflight request by
R-SLIB-HTTP-0017 and otherwise runs the hooks and the route as a task of its own that it awaits
with `+std.async::join+` (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0020). A panic of that task is answered with 500 and the
text of `+reason+`; its report goes to the hook of the last `+on_panic+`, with the state, the method,
the target and the notes of the request, which outlive the flow, or without a hook to the standard error as one line `+R panic: category
in METHOD target+`, followed by `+: text+` when the report has text; the unwind drops the context
and every value of the flow, and the connection and the server go on. The fields of
R-SLIB-HTTP-0017 are then added. `+serve_app+` serves the connections of every listener as
`+serve_all+` of R-SLIB-HTTP-0014 does, answering each request with `+dispatch+` and adding
`+Date+`; an application has neither streaming nor upgrade routes.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0016]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0016* — Cookies (RFC 6265): `+std.http::cookie+` is a Move value with the fields
`+std.string::string name+`, `+std.string::string value+`, `+o<std.string::string> path+`,
`+o<std.string::string> domain+`, `+o<i64> max_age+`, `+bool secure+`, `+bool http_only+` and
`+o<std.string::string> same_site+`, whose defaults are none and false:

* `+std.http::request::cookie(const std.http::request* this, str name) -> o<str>+`;
* `+std.http::response::set_cookie(std.http::response* this, const std.http::cookie* value) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+cookie+` returns the value of the first `+name=value+` pair with the name in the `+Cookie+`
fields of the request, whose pairs are separated by `+;+` and whose names and values are trimmed
of spaces, without the double quotes around the value; none when no pair has the name.
`+set_cookie+` adds a `+Set-Cookie+` field `+name=value+` followed by `+; Path=+`, `+; Domain=+`,
`+; Max-Age=+`, `+; Secure+`, `+; HttpOnly+` and `+; SameSite=+` for the attributes that are
present; a name that is not a token, or a field value with a control character, throws
`+invalid_header+`.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0017]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0017* — Cross-origin resource sharing of an application: `+std.http::cors_policy+` is
a Move value with the fields `+array<std.string::string> origins+`, `+o<std.string::string>
methods+`, `+o<std.string::string> headers+`, `+bool credentials+` and `+u32 max_age+`, whose
defaults are an empty list, none, none, false and 600:

* `+std.http::app<S, C>::cors(std.http::app<S, C>* this, std.http::cors_policy policy) -> void+`.

An origin is allowed when the list is empty or holds it exactly. With a policy, an `+OPTIONS+`
request with `+Origin+` and `+Access-Control-Request-Method+` is a preflight request, answered
before every hook: with 403 for an origin that is not allowed, otherwise with 204,
`+Access-Control-Allow-Methods+` (the methods of the policy, or else the requested method),
`+Access-Control-Allow-Headers+` (the headers of the policy, or else the requested
`+Access-Control-Request-Headers+` when there are any) and `+Access-Control-Max-Age+`. Every
response to a request whose `+Origin+` is allowed, the answer of a preflight request included,
carries `+Access-Control-Allow-Origin+`: `+*+` when the list is empty and credentials are not
allowed, otherwise the origin together with `+Vary: Origin+`; with credentials also
`+Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true+`. A later call replaces the policy.

[[R-SLIB-HTTP-0018]]
*R-SLIB-HTTP-0018* — The routes of an application:

* `+std.http::app<S, C>::redirect_trailing_slash(std.http::app<S, C>* this, bool enabled) -> void+`;
* `+std.http::app<S, C>::method_not_allowed(std.http::app<S, C>* this, bool enabled) -> void+`.

A route matches a request as in R-SLIB-HTTP-0006, where `+HEAD+` also matches `+GET+` routes.
Among the routes whose pattern and method match, the most specific answers, whatever the order of
the calls: comparing the patterns segment by segment, at the first segment where they differ a
literal segment is more specific than `+{name}+`, which is more specific than `+*+`; of equally
specific routes the first added answers. When no route matches: with redirects enabled, a path
that a route of the method matches with its final `+/+` removed, or with one added, is answered
with 301 for `+GET+` and `+HEAD+` or 308 for other methods and a `+Location+` naming that path with
the query of the request; otherwise, when routes of other methods match the path, with 405 and
`+Allow+` listing their methods once each, unless 405 is disabled; otherwise with 404. A created
application does not redirect and answers 405.

=== 21.20 Name resolution: `+std.dns+`

[[R-SLIB-DNS-0001]]
*R-SLIB-DNS-0001* — `+std.dns+` asks name servers over UDP and TCP (RFC 1035) for the records of a
name. `+std.dns::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+invalid_name+`,
`+name_error+`, `+server_failure+`, `+refused+`, `+format_error+`, `+timed_out+`,
`+malformed_response+` and `+no_servers+`, and `+std.dns::dns_error+` the error with the field
`+std.dns::error_code code+`.

[[R-SLIB-DNS-0002]]
*R-SLIB-DNS-0002* — `+std.dns::record_type+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+a+`, `+ns+`,
`+cname+`, `+ptr+`, `+mx+`, `+txt+`, `+aaaa+` and `+srv+`, and
`+std.dns::type_code(std.dns::record_type kind) -> u16+` its TYPE value: 1, 2, 5, 12, 15, 16, 28
and 33. The lookups take the TYPE as a `+u16+`, so every other type can be asked for too.

[[R-SLIB-DNS-0003]]
*R-SLIB-DNS-0003* — The wire form. A name is written as labels of 1 to 63 bytes separated by dots
with an optional final dot, at most 255 bytes on the wire; `+""+` and `+"."+` are the root, and any
other name is `+invalid_name+`. `+std.dns::record+` is the Move struct with the fields
`+std.string::string name+`, `+u16 kind+`, `+u32 ttl+`, `+bytes data+` (the data of the record,
names in it uncompressed), `+std.string::string target+` (the name that ends the data of an NS,
CNAME, PTR, MX or SRV record, without its final dot), and `+u16 priority+`, `+weight+` and `+port+`
(of SRV; `+priority+` also holds the preference of MX). `+std.dns::question+` is the Move struct
with the fields `+u16 id+`, `+std.string::string name+`, `+u16 kind+` and `+bool recursion+`:

* `+std.dns::record::srv(str name, u32 ttl, u16 priority, u16 weight, u16 port, str target) -> std.dns::record throws std.dns::dns_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.dns::record::ptr(str name, u32 ttl, str target) -> std.dns::record throws std.dns::dns_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.dns::record::txt(str name, u32 ttl, str text) -> std.dns::record throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.dns::record::address(str name, u32 ttl, std.net::ip_address value) -> std.dns::record throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.dns::encode_query(u16 id, str name, u16 kind) -> bytes throws std.dns::dns_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.dns::parse_query(const u8[] message) -> std.dns::question throws std.dns::dns_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.dns::encode_answer(const std.dns::question* asked, const array<std.dns::record>* answers, u8 rcode, bool truncated) -> bytes throws std.dns::dns_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.dns::parse_response(const u8[] message, u16 id, str name, u16 kind) -> array<std.dns::record> throws std.dns::dns_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

The constructors build records of class IN; `+txt+` splits the text into character strings of at
most 255 bytes, and `+address+` makes an A or AAAA record. `+encode_query+` writes a query with the
id, recursion desired, one question of class IN and an EDNS0 OPT record (RFC 6891) that accepts
answers of 1232 bytes. `+parse_query+` reads the question of a query, which shall have QR clear,
opcode 0 and one question of class IN, otherwise `+format_error+`. `+encode_answer+` writes an
authoritative response to the question with the records, the response code and the TC flag, names
uncompressed. `+parse_response+` returns the records of type kind and class IN of the answer
section, in order, following compressed names backwards at most 128 times; a message that is not
the answer to the query with that id and question (the name compared without ASCII case and a final
dot) or that is cut is `+malformed_response+`, and the response codes 1, 2, 3 and 5 are
`+format_error+`, `+server_failure+`, `+name_error+` and `+refused+`, other nonzero codes
`+server_failure+`.

[[R-SLIB-DNS-0004]]
*R-SLIB-DNS-0004* — `+std.dns::resolver+` is a Move resolver: the name servers it asks in order,
the time it waits for each answer, 2 seconds unless set, and how many times it asks the whole
list, 2 unless set:

* `+std.dns::resolver::with_servers(array<std.net::socket_address> servers) -> std.dns::resolver+`;
* `+std.dns::resolver::set_timeout(std.dns::resolver* this, std.time::duration timeout) -> void+`;
* `+std.dns::resolver::set_attempts(std.dns::resolver* this, u32 attempts) -> void+`;
* `+std.dns::parse_resolv_conf(str text) -> array<std.net::socket_address> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+async std.dns::resolver::system() -> std.dns::resolver throws std.dns::dns_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.dns::resolver::lookup(const std.dns::resolver* this, str name, u16 kind) -> array<std.dns::record> throws std.dns::dns_error, std.error::fault+`.

`+set_attempts+` takes zero as one. `+parse_resolv_conf+` returns the addresses of the
`+nameserver+` lines of a resolv.conf text with port 53, a scope suffix after `+%+` dropped, and
skips lines that are not addresses; `+system+` reads `+/etc/resolv.conf+`, and a text without
servers or that is not UTF-8 is `+no_servers+`. `+lookup+` asks with a random id: in each attempt
it asks every server in order over UDP from an unspecified local port and takes the first datagram
from that server with that id, within the timeout; an answer with the TC flag is asked again over
TCP with its two-byte length. A name error ends the lookup; a format error, server failure, refusal,
malformed answer, network failure or timeout moves on to the next server, and when every attempt
has failed the lookup throws the last of them, `+timed_out+` for a timeout. A resolver without
servers is `+no_servers+`, an invalid name `+invalid_name+`, and the deadline of an enclosing
deadline block bounds the whole lookup; cancellation is thrown as `+std.net::net_error+`.

[[R-SLIB-DNS-0005]]
*R-SLIB-DNS-0005* — `+std.dns::srv_record+` is the Move struct with the fields `+u16 priority+`,
`+u16 weight+`, `+u16 port+` and `+std.string::string target+`, and `+std.dns::txt_record+` the
Move struct with the field `+array<bytes> strings+`:

* `+@scoped async std.dns::resolver::lookup_srv(const std.dns::resolver* this, str name) -> array<std.dns::srv_record> throws std.dns::dns_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.dns::resolver::lookup_txt(const std.dns::resolver* this, str name) -> array<std.dns::txt_record> throws std.dns::dns_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+std.dns::txt_record::text(const std.dns::txt_record* this) -> std.string::string throws std.dns::dns_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+lookup_srv+` returns the SRV records (RFC 2782) of a name such as `+_service._tcp.example.org+`
in the order of priority, then of weight from the largest, then of target; a single record with
the root as target means that the service is not offered and yields none. `+lookup_txt+` returns
the character strings of each TXT record in the order of the answer, and `+text+` joins them as
UTF-8 text, `+malformed_response+` when they are not.

[[R-SLIB-DNS-0006]]
*R-SLIB-DNS-0006* — `+std.dns::reverse_name(std.net::ip_address address) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
is the name of the reverse lookup of an address: its octets in reverse order under
`+in-addr.arpa+`, or its nibbles in reverse order as lowercase hexadecimal digits under
`+ip6.arpa+` (RFC 3596), and
`+@scoped async std.dns::resolver::reverse(const std.dns::resolver* this, std.net::ip_address address) -> array<std.string::string> throws std.dns::dns_error, std.error::fault+`
returns the names of the PTR records of that name.

=== 21.21 WebSocket: `+std.websocket+`

[[R-SLIB-WS-0001]]
*R-SLIB-WS-0001* — `+std.websocket+` implements the WebSocket protocol (RFC 6455) over a
connection that `+std.http+` has switched (R-SLIB-HTTP-0011). `+std.websocket::error_code+` is a
Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+handshake_failed+`, `+protocol_error+`, `+invalid_data+`,
`+message_too_large+` and `+closed+`, and `+std.websocket::websocket_error+` the error with the
field `+std.websocket::error_code code+`.

[[R-SLIB-WS-0002]]
*R-SLIB-WS-0002* — The opening handshake:

* `+std.websocket::accept_key(str key) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+@scoped async std.websocket::connect(std.http::client* web, str address, str protocol) -> std.websocket::websocket throws std.websocket::websocket_error, std.http::http_error, std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.websocket::accept(std.http::request incoming, std.http::upgrade connection, str protocol) -> std.websocket::websocket throws std.websocket::websocket_error, std.error::fault+`.

`+accept_key+` is the base64 of the SHA-1 of the key followed by
`+258EAFA5-E914-47DA-95CA-C5AB0DC85B11+`. `+connect+` takes a `+ws+` or `+wss+` URL, or an `+http+`
or `+https+` one, and sends a GET with `+client::upgrade+`: `+Upgrade: websocket+`,
`+Connection: Upgrade+`, the base64 of 16 random bytes as `+Sec-WebSocket-Key+`,
`+Sec-WebSocket-Version: 13+` and, unless protocol is empty, `+Sec-WebSocket-Protocol+`. The answer
shall be 101 with `+websocket+` in `+Upgrade+`, `+upgrade+` in `+Connection+`, the accept value
of the key and exactly the subprotocol asked for, or none when none was asked; otherwise the
result is `+handshake_failed+`. `+accept+` answers the request of an upgrade route: a GET with
those fields, version 13 and a key that is the base64 of 16 bytes, which offers the subprotocol in
its `+Sec-WebSocket-Protocol+` list unless protocol is empty. The answer is 101 with
`+Upgrade: websocket+`, `+Connection: Upgrade+`, the accept value and the subprotocol; a request
that does not qualify is refused with 400, or with 426 when only the version differs, both with
`+Sec-WebSocket-Version: 13+`, and the result is `+handshake_failed+`.

[[R-SLIB-WS-0003]]
*R-SLIB-WS-0003* — `+std.websocket::message_kind+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+text+`,
`+binary+` and `+close+`, and `+std.websocket::message+` the Move struct with the fields
`+std.websocket::message_kind kind+`, `+bytes data+` and `+u16 code+`: the data of a text or binary
message, or of a close the status code of the peer, 1005 when it sent none, and its reason as
data. `+std.websocket::websocket+` is a Move, Send+Sync end of a connection that owns its transport
and has one lock for receiving and one for sending, so that one task may receive while others
send:

* `+std.websocket::message::text(const std.websocket::message* this) -> str+`;
* `+std.websocket::websocket::create(std.http::upgraded connection, bool client) -> std.websocket::websocket throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.websocket::websocket::set_max_message(std.websocket::websocket* this, usize limit) -> void+`.

`+text+` is the data as text, `+""+` when it is not UTF-8. `+create+` makes the end of a connection
whose handshake is done, the buffered bytes its first bytes; a client end masks the frames it sends
and a server end does not. The largest message accepted is 1 MiB unless set.

[[R-SLIB-WS-0004]]
*R-SLIB-WS-0004* —
`+std.websocket::encode_frame(u8 opcode, bool final, const u8[] payload, bool masked) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
writes one frame of RFC 6455 section 5.2 with the low four bits of the opcode, the FIN bit and the
payload length in 7, 16 or 64 bits; a masked frame carries a masking key of four random bytes and
the payload masked with it.

[[R-SLIB-WS-0005]]
*R-SLIB-WS-0005* — Messages:

* `+@scoped async std.websocket::websocket::send_text(const std.websocket::websocket* this, str text) -> void throws std.websocket::websocket_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.websocket::websocket::send_binary(const std.websocket::websocket* this, const u8[] data) -> void throws std.websocket::websocket_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.websocket::websocket::ping(const std.websocket::websocket* this, const u8[] data) -> void throws std.websocket::websocket_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.websocket::websocket::close(const std.websocket::websocket* this, u16 code, str reason) -> void throws std.websocket::websocket_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.websocket::websocket::receive(const std.websocket::websocket* this) -> o<std.websocket::message> throws std.websocket::websocket_error, std.error::fault+`.

Each send writes one final frame under the send lock; nothing is sent after a close frame, and a
send then is `+closed+`. `+ping+` takes at most 125 bytes and `+close+` a reason of at most 123
bytes, otherwise `+protocol_error+`; `+close+` starts the closing handshake with the status code
and the reason. `+receive+` returns the next text or binary message, whose fragments it joins, or
the close of the peer, which it answers with a close carrying the same status code unless this end
closed first, and then ends the write direction of the connection; after the close it returns
none. It answers each ping with a pong carrying its data and skips pongs. A frame with a reserved
bit or opcode, a masked frame received by a client or an unmasked one received by a server, a
fragmented or longer control frame, a continuation without a start or a start within a message,
and a close with a length of one byte or a status code that shall not be sent are
`+protocol_error+`; text or a close reason that is not UTF-8 is `+invalid_data+`, and a message
above the limit `+message_too_large+`. Each of those failures is answered with a close of status 1002, 1007
or 1009 and thrown, and receive then returns none; a connection that ends without a close is
`+closed+`.

=== 21.22 JSON-RPC 2.0: `+std.jsonrpc+`

[[R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0001]]
*R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0001* — `+std.jsonrpc+` reads and writes the messages of JSON-RPC 2.0 as
`+std.json::value+` trees. The constants `+std.jsonrpc::parse_error+`,
`+std.jsonrpc::invalid_request+`, `+std.jsonrpc::method_not_found+`,
`+std.jsonrpc::invalid_params+` and `+std.jsonrpc::internal_error+` are the `+i64+` codes
-32700, -32600, -32601, -32602 and -32603 of that specification. `+std.jsonrpc::rpc_error+` is
the error of a text that is no message, with the fields `+i64 code+`,
`+std.string::string message+` and `+o<std.jsonrpc::request_id> id+`, the id of the message
when it could be read.

[[R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0002]]
*R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0002* — `+std.jsonrpc::request_id+` is an enum with `+@derive(equal)+` (Core
R-AGG-0012) whose variants `+number(std.string::string)+` and `+text(std.string::string)+`
hold a numeric id in its JSON spelling, so that it is echoed exactly, and a string id.
`+std.jsonrpc::request+` is the Move struct with the fields `+std.jsonrpc::request_id id+`,
`+std.string::string method+` and `+o<std.json::value> params+`;
`+std.jsonrpc::notification+` has `+method+` and `+params+` and gets no response;
`+std.jsonrpc::result_response+` has `+id+` and `+std.json::value result+`;
`+std.jsonrpc::error_response+` has `+o<std.jsonrpc::request_id> id+`, none when the request
could not be read, `+i64 code+`, `+std.string::string message+` and
`+o<std.json::value> data+`. `+std.jsonrpc::message+` is the enum of one message with the
variants `+request+`, `+notification+`, `+result+` and `+failure+` of those types.

* `+std.jsonrpc::request_id::from_integer(i64 value) -> std.jsonrpc::request_id throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jsonrpc::request_id::from_text(str value) -> std.jsonrpc::request_id throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jsonrpc::request_id::spelling(const std.jsonrpc::request_id* this) -> str+`;
* `+std.jsonrpc::request_id::is_text(const std.jsonrpc::request_id* this) -> bool+`;
* `+std.jsonrpc::request_id::copy(const std.jsonrpc::request_id* this) -> std.jsonrpc::request_id throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jsonrpc::request_id::to_json(const std.jsonrpc::request_id* this) -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jsonrpc::request_id::from_json(const std.json::value* value) -> o<std.jsonrpc::request_id> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jsonrpc::error_response::of(const std.jsonrpc::rpc_error* problem) -> std.jsonrpc::error_response throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+spelling+` is the digits of a number or the text of a string, `+from_json+` the id of a JSON
number or string and none for any other kind, and `+of+` the response that answers a failure
with its code, message and id, without data.

[[R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0003]]
*R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0003* —

* `+std.jsonrpc::parse(const u8[] text) -> std.jsonrpc::message throws std.jsonrpc::rpc_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jsonrpc::encode(const std.jsonrpc::message* value) -> std.string::string throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+parse+` reads one message. A text that is no JSON is `+parse_error+` without id. A value that
is no message is `+invalid_request+`, with the id when the value has a valid one: a value that
is no object, such as the array of a batch; a `+jsonrpc+` member other than the string
`+"2.0"+`; an id other than a number or a string, including null; a `+method+` that is no
string; `+params+` that are neither an object nor an array; a response with both or neither of
`+result+` and `+error+`, or without id for a result; and an `+error+` that is no object with
an integer `+code+` and a string `+message+`. An object with a `+method+` is a request when it
has an id and a notification otherwise. `+encode+` writes `+"jsonrpc":"2.0"+` and the members
of the message on one line, the id of a numeric id in its spelling.

[[R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0004]]
*R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0004* — For `+R: std.stream::Reader+` and `+W: std.stream::Writer+`:

* `+@scoped async std.jsonrpc::read_line(std.bufio::reader<R>* input) -> o<bytes> throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.jsonrpc::write_message(const W* output, const std.jsonrpc::message* value) -> void throws std.json::error, std.error::fault+`.

These frame one message per line, as the stdio transport of MCP does. `+read_line+` returns the
next line without its line feed and a carriage return before it, skips empty lines, and returns
none at the end of the input; a line shall fit in the capacity of the reader
(R-SLIB-BUFIO-0002). `+write_message+` writes the encoding of the message and a line feed and
flushes the writer.

=== 21.23 Model Context Protocol: `+std.mcp+`

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0001]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0001* — `+std.mcp+` implements servers and clients of the Model Context Protocol,
revision 2026-07-28, over `+std.jsonrpc+` (R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0001..0004) with the stdio and the
Streamable HTTP transports. That revision is stateless: each request carries its protocol
version, the capabilities of the client and its identity in `+_meta+`, so there is no
initialization, no session and no event stream opened by GET. The module speaks this revision
and no other. `+std.mcp::protocol_version+` is the `+constexpr str+` `+"2026-07-28"+`, and the
`+i64+` constants `+std.mcp::header_mismatch+`, `+std.mcp::missing_required_client_capability+`
and `+std.mcp::unsupported_protocol_version+` are the codes -32020, -32021 and -32022 that the
protocol adds to those of JSON-RPC. `+std.mcp::mcp_error+` is the error of this module, with the
fields `+i64 code+` and `+std.string::string message+`: an error response of the peer with its
code and message, or a failure that the client meets itself (R-SLIB-MCP-0017).

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0002]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0002* — `+std.mcp::implementation+` names a server or a client: the fields
`+std.string::string name+`, `+title+`, `+version+` and `+description+`, where a JSON form
leaves out an empty title and description.

* `+std.mcp::implementation::create(str name, str version) -> std.mcp::implementation throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

The types of this module that appear in messages carry `+@json+` contracts (Core R-JSON-0001)
with the member names of the protocol, such as `+mimeType+` for a field `+mime_type+`, and
leave out optional members that are empty or none.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0003]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0003* — `+std.mcp::tool+` is a tool that a server offers, with the fields
`+std.string::string name+`, `+title+` and `+description+`, `+std.json::value input_schema+`
(`+inputSchema+`, a JSON Schema whose root describes an object),
`+o<std.json::value> output_schema+` (`+outputSchema+`) and
`+o<std.mcp::tool_annotations> annotations+`. `+std.mcp::tool_annotations+` holds the hints of a
tool for a client: `+title+` and the `+o<bool>+` fields `+read_only+`, `+destructive+`,
`+idempotent+` and `+open_world+` (`+readOnlyHint+`, `+destructiveHint+`, `+idempotentHint+` and
`+openWorldHint+`); none leaves a hint at the default of the protocol.

* `+std.mcp::tool::create(str name, str description, std.json::value input_schema) -> std.mcp::tool throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::no_arguments() -> std.json::value throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+no_arguments+` is the input schema of a tool without arguments,
`+{"type":"object","additionalProperties":false}+`. The schemas of typed arguments and results
come from `+std.json::schema::<T>()+` (R-SLIB-JSON-0002).

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0004]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0004* — `+std.mcp::contents+` is the content of a resource at a URI, with the
fields `+std.string::string uri+`, `+mime_type+` and `+text+`, `+bytes blob+` and
`+bool binary+`; its JSON form has `+uri+`, `+mimeType+` when not empty, and `+text+`, or
`+blob+` with the base64 of the bytes when binary is true. `+std.mcp::media+` is the data of
an image or audio block, with the fields `+bytes data+` and `+std.string::string mime_type+`.
`+std.mcp::content+` is one block of content, an enum with the variants
`+text(std.string::string)+`, `+image(std.mcp::media)+`, `+audio(std.mcp::media)+`,
`+link(std.mcp::resource)+` and `+embedded(std.mcp::contents)+`, written with the
`+type+` member `+"text"+`, `+"image"+`, `+"audio"+`, `+"resource_link"+` and `+"resource"+`;
an image and an audio block carry `+data+` in base64 and `+mimeType+`, a link the members of
the resource and an embedded block its contents under `+resource+`.

* `+std.mcp::contents::of_text(str uri, str mime_type, str text) -> std.mcp::contents throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::contents::of_blob(str uri, str mime_type, bytes data) -> std.mcp::contents throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::content::of_text(str text) -> std.mcp::content throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+std.mcp::content+` defines `+json_marshal+`, `+json_unmarshal+` and `+json_schema+`
(R-SLIB-JSON-0001..0002): a block of an unknown type, a missing member of its type or data that
is no base64 is `+std.json::error+`, and the schema of a block is the `+oneOf+` of its five
kinds.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0005]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0005* — `+std.mcp::resource+` is a resource that a server offers, with the fields
`+std.string::string uri+`, `+name+`, `+title+`, `+description+` and `+mime_type+` and
`+o<u64> size+`. `+std.mcp::resource_template+` names a family of resources by a URI template
of RFC 6570 with simple variables, with the fields `+std.string::string uri_template+`
(`+uriTemplate+`), `+name+`, `+title+`, `+description+` and `+mime_type+`: in the template,
`+{name}+` matches a nonempty part of a URI without `+/+` and `+{+name}+` a nonempty rest that
may contain `+/+`, and other text matches itself. `+std.mcp::read+` is a read of a resource as
its handler receives it, with the public fields `+std.string::string uri+`,
`+std.mcp::input input+` and `+std.mcp::progress progress+`. `+std.mcp::read_outcome+` is the
outcome of a read: `+complete(array<std.mcp::contents>)+`,
`+needs_input(std.mcp::input_required)+` or `+not_found+`.

* `+std.mcp::resource::create(str uri, str name, str mime_type) -> std.mcp::resource throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::resource_template::create(str uri_template, str name, str mime_type) -> std.mcp::resource_template throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::read::variable(const std.mcp::read* this, str name) -> o<str>+`.

`+variable+` is the value of a variable of the template that matched the URI, percent-decoded,
and none for a variable that it does not have.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0006]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0006* — `+std.mcp::tool_result+` is the result of a tool call, with the fields
`+array<std.mcp::content> content+`, `+o<std.json::value> structured+`
(`+structuredContent+`, which conforms to the output schema of the tool) and
`+bool is_error+` (`+isError+`, a failure of the tool that the model reads).
`+std.mcp::tool_outcome+` is `+complete(std.mcp::tool_result)+` or
`+needs_input(std.mcp::input_required)+`. `+std.mcp::call+` is a call as its handler receives
it, with the fields `+std.string::string name+`, `+std.json::value arguments+` (an object),
`+std.mcp::input input+` and `+std.mcp::progress progress+`.

* `+std.mcp::tool_result::of_text(str text) -> std.mcp::tool_result throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::tool_result::of_error(str text) -> std.mcp::tool_result throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::tool_result::of_structured(const T* value) -> std.mcp::tool_result throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::call::arguments_text(const std.mcp::call* this) -> std.string::string throws std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+of_text+` is a result of one text block and `+of_error+` the same with `+is_error+`.
`+of_structured+`, for `+T: json_encode+`, is the value that `+std.json::marshal+` writes as
the structured result and its JSON text as a text block, for clients that read only content.
`+arguments_text+` is the JSON text of the arguments, which `+std.json::unmarshal+` reads into
the type whose schema the tool declares.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0007]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0007* — A handler that needs the user asks through its client with a Multi
Round-Trip Request: it returns `+needs_input+`, the request ends with an
`+InputRequiredResult+`, and the client sends the same request again with the answers.
`+std.mcp::elicitation+` is one request for input, `+form(std.mcp::form_request)+` or
`+url(std.mcp::url_request)+`: `+std.mcp::form_request+` has `+std.string::string message+`
and `+std.json::value schema+`, a flat schema of an object whose properties are strings,
numbers, Booleans or enums; `+std.mcp::url_request+` has `+message+` and `+url+`, a page that
the user opens. `+std.mcp::answer_action+` is `+accept+`, `+decline+` or `+cancel+`, and
`+std.mcp::answer+` the answer of the client to one request, with the fields
`+std.mcp::answer_action action+` and `+o<std.json::value> content+`, the content of an
accepted form. `+std.mcp::input_required+` holds the requests of one round, each under a key
that the server chooses, and the public field `+o<std.string::string> state+`, an opaque text
that the client echoes. `+std.mcp::input+` is what a request carries for these rounds: the
answers under their keys, the public field `+state+` echoed from the previous round, and the
public `+bool+` fields `+form+` and `+url+`, the modes of elicitation that the client declared
on this request.

* `+std.mcp::answer::field(const std.mcp::answer* this, str name) -> o<const std.json::value*>+`;
* `+std.mcp::input_required::create() -> std.mcp::input_required+`;
* `+std.mcp::input_required::ask_form(std.mcp::input_required* this, str key, str message, std.json::value schema) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::input_required::ask_url(std.mcp::input_required* this, str key, str message, str url) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::input_required::set_state(std.mcp::input_required* this, str state) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::input_required::count(const std.mcp::input_required* this) -> usize+`;
* `+std.mcp::input_required::key_at(const std.mcp::input_required* this, usize index) -> str+`;
* `+std.mcp::input_required::request_at(const std.mcp::input_required* this, usize index) -> const std.mcp::elicitation*+`;
* `+std.mcp::input::get(const std.mcp::input* this, str key) -> o<const std.mcp::answer*>+`;
* `+std.mcp::input::state_text(const std.mcp::input* this) -> str+`.

`+field+` is a member of the content of an answer, `+get+` the answer under a key and
`+state_text+` the echoed state, empty when there is none. The server answers
`+needs_input+` with `+{"resultType":"input_required"}+`, the requests under
`+inputRequests+`, each an `+elicitation/create+` of mode `+form+` with `+requestedSchema+` or
of mode `+url+`, and `+requestState+`; a result without requests and without state is
-32603. When the client did not declare a mode that a request needs, the response is instead
-32021 with the missing modes under `+requiredCapabilities+` of its data. A retry carries
`+inputResponses+`, an object of answers with `+action+` and, for an accepted form,
`+content+`, and `+requestState+`; entries that are no answers are ignored, and an unknown
action or content that is no object is -32602.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0008]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0008* — `+std.mcp::prompt+` is a prompt that a server offers, with the fields
`+std.string::string name+`, `+title+` and `+description+` and
`+array<std.mcp::prompt_argument> arguments+`; `+std.mcp::prompt_argument+` has `+name+`,
`+title+`, `+description+` and `+bool required+`. `+std.mcp::role+` is `+user+` or
`+assistant+`, `+std.mcp::prompt_message+` has `+std.mcp::role role+` and
`+std.mcp::content content+`, and `+std.mcp::prompt_result+` has `+description+` and
`+array<std.mcp::prompt_message> messages+`. `+std.mcp::prompt_outcome+` is
`+complete(std.mcp::prompt_result)+` or `+needs_input(std.mcp::input_required)+`, and
`+std.mcp::prompt_call+` a request as its handler receives it, with the public fields
`+std.string::string name+`, `+input+` and `+progress+`.

* `+std.mcp::prompt::create(str name, str description) -> std.mcp::prompt throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::prompt::argument(std.mcp::prompt* this, str name, str description, bool required) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::prompt_result::create(str description) -> std.mcp::prompt_result throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::prompt_result::say(std.mcp::prompt_result* this, std.mcp::role speaker, str text) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::prompt_call::argument(const std.mcp::prompt_call* this, str name) -> o<str>+`.

`+say+` adds a message of one text block, and `+argument+` of a call is the value that the
client gave for an argument, none when it gave none.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0009]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0009* — `+std.mcp::completion+` is the completion of an argument, with the fields
`+array<std.string::string> values+`, `+o<u64> total+` and `+bool has_more+` (`+hasMore+`).
`+std.mcp::completion_request+` asks to complete an argument of a prompt, when its field
`+bool prompt+` is true and `+reference+` is the name of the prompt, or a variable of a
resource template, when `+reference+` is the template; `+argument+` names the argument and
`+value+` is what was typed.

* `+std.mcp::completion::create() -> std.mcp::completion+`;
* `+std.mcp::completion_request::context(const std.mcp::completion_request* this, str name) -> o<str>+`.

`+context+` is the value of another argument that the client already knows. The server sends
at most 100 values, and `+hasMore+` when it cut the list.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0010]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0010* — `+std.mcp::notifier+` is the Move, Send+Sync handle through which a
program announces the changes that the subscriptions of clients receive
(R-SLIB-MCP-0015): its handles share one broadcast of capacity 64 (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0016).

* `+std.mcp::notifier::create() -> std.mcp::notifier throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::notifier::share(const std.mcp::notifier* this) -> std.mcp::notifier+`;
* `+std.mcp::notifier::tools_changed(const std.mcp::notifier* this) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::notifier::prompts_changed(const std.mcp::notifier* this) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::notifier::resources_changed(const std.mcp::notifier* this) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::notifier::resource_updated(const std.mcp::notifier* this, str uri) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::notifier(const std.mcp::server<S>* this) -> std.mcp::notifier+`.

`+share+` is another handle of the same notifier. The announcements do not wait for
subscribers; one that no subscription is open for is lost. `+server::notifier+` is a handle of
the notifier of the server.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0011]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0011* — `+std.mcp::options+` holds the settings of a server: `+u64 ttl_ms = 300000+`
and `+bool private_cache = false+`, the freshness of discovery, lists and reads in milliseconds
and whether they are private to one user; `+usize page_size = 100+`, the length of a page of a
list; `+bool list_changed = false+` and `+bool subscribe = false+`, whether subscriptions may
ask for list changes and for updates of resources; `+usize max_message = 4194304+`, the
longest message; and `+u64 task_ttl_ms = 3600000+` and `+u64 task_poll_ms = 1000+`, the time a
task is kept and the interval at which its client polls it (R-SLIB-MCP-0022). For shared state
`+S: send & sync & unborrowed+`, `+std.mcp::server<S>+` is a Move server: its identity,
instructions and settings, the public field `+arc S state+`, the tools, resources, resource
templates and prompts it offers with their handlers, its notifier and its tasks.

* `+std.mcp::server<S>::create(std.mcp::implementation info, arc S state, std.mcp::notifier changes, std.mcp::options settings) -> std.mcp::server<S>+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::set_instructions(std.mcp::server<S>* this, str text) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::add_tool(std.mcp::server<S>* this, std.mcp::tool definition, async fn(arc S, std.mcp::call) -> std.mcp::tool_outcome throws(std.error::fault) handler) -> void throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::add_resource(std.mcp::server<S>* this, std.mcp::resource definition, async fn(arc S, std.mcp::read) -> std.mcp::read_outcome throws(std.error::fault) handler) -> void throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::add_template(std.mcp::server<S>* this, std.mcp::resource_template definition, async fn(arc S, std.mcp::read) -> std.mcp::read_outcome throws(std.error::fault) handler) -> void throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::add_prompt(std.mcp::server<S>* this, std.mcp::prompt definition, async fn(arc S, std.mcp::prompt_call) -> std.mcp::prompt_outcome throws(std.error::fault) handler) -> void throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::list_resources_with(std.mcp::server<S>* this, async fn(arc S) -> array<std.mcp::resource> throws(std.error::fault) lister) -> void+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::complete_with(std.mcp::server<S>* this, async fn(arc S, std.mcp::completion_request) -> std.mcp::completion throws(std.error::fault) completer) -> void+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::allow_origin(std.mcp::server<S>* this, str origin) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+set_instructions+` sets the guidance for the model that discovery returns. A tool or a
prompt shall have a name of 1 to 128 ASCII letters, digits, `+_+`, `+-+` and `+.+` that the
server does not have yet, and a tool an input schema whose root has `+"type":"object"+`; a
resource shall have a nonempty URI that the server does not have yet; a template shall be
nonempty, with variable names of ASCII letters, digits and `+_+` and no two variables next to
each other. Otherwise the addition throws `+std.mcp::mcp_error+` with -32602.
`+list_resources_with+` adds to the list of resources those that the lister returns, after the
added ones, such as the members of a collection that a template reads; `+complete_with+` sets
the completer of the arguments of prompts and the variables of templates. `+allow_origin+`
admits browser requests from an origin over HTTP (R-SLIB-MCP-0016).

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0012]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0012* — `+std.mcp::progress+` is the progress of a request whose client asked for
it with a `+progressToken+` in `+_meta+`.

* `+std.mcp::progress::wanted(const std.mcp::progress* this) -> bool+`;
* `+std.mcp::progress::report(std.mcp::progress* this, f64 value, o<f64> total, str message) -> bool throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+wanted+` is whether the client asked. `+report+` sends `+notifications/progress+` with the
token, the value, the total when there is one and the message when it is not empty, before the
response of the request; a report without a token, one whose value does not exceed that of the
previous report, and one that finds the messages of the request full are dropped, and false
means that nothing was sent. In the handler of a task (R-SLIB-MCP-0022) `+wanted+` is true and
`+report+` sets the status message of the task instead.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0013]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0013* — Both transports check each request the same way. Its `+params+` shall have
`+_meta+` with `+io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion+`, a string, and
`+io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities+`, an object, otherwise -32602; another version is
-32022 with the data `+{"supported":["2026-07-28"],"requested":V}+`; a method that the server
does not offer is -32601, and a `+io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel+` that is not one of the
levels of RFC 5424 that MCP names is -32602. The server offers `+server/discover+` and
`+subscriptions/listen+` always, the methods of tools when it has tools, of resources when it
has resources, templates or a lister, of prompts when it has prompts,
`+completion/complete+` when it has a completer, and those of tasks when it has a task tool
(R-SLIB-MCP-0022); `+initialize+`, `+ping+` and every other method are unknown. A form of
elicitation is declared by `+"elicitation":{"form":{}}+`, or by `+"elicitation":{}+`, a URL by
`+"elicitation":{"url":{}}+`, and the Tasks extension by
`+"extensions":{"io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks":{}}+` (R-SLIB-MCP-0021).

Each result has `+"resultType":"complete"+`, `+"input_required"+` (R-SLIB-MCP-0007) or
`+"task"+` (R-SLIB-MCP-0022), and
`+io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo+` in its `+_meta+`. `+server/discover+` returns
`+supportedVersions+` with the one version, the capabilities of the server (`+tools+`,
`+resources+` and `+prompts+` for its features, with `+listChanged+` and `+subscribe+` as its
options allow, `+completions+`, and `+extensions+` with the Tasks extension when it has a task
tool), its instructions when there are some, and `+ttlMs+` and
`+cacheScope+` (`+"public"+` or `+"private"+`). The lists return one page of `+page_size+`
entries from the index that the decimal cursor names, or from the start without cursor, with
`+nextCursor+` when more follow and the cache hints; a cursor that is no such index is -32602.
`+tools/call+` of a tool that the server does not have is -32602 with the message
`+Unknown tool: NAME+`, and arguments that are no object -32602; `+resources/read+` of a URI
that no added resource and no template matches is -32602 with the message
`+Resource not found+` and the data `+{"uri":URI}+`, as is `+not_found+` of a handler, and a
read returns its contents with the cache hints; `+prompts/get+` of an unknown prompt is
-32602 with `+Unknown prompt: NAME+`, arguments that are no object of strings -32602 and a
required argument that is missing -32602 with `+Missing required argument: NAME+`;
`+completion/complete+` needs a `+ref+` of type `+ref/prompt+` with a name or
`+ref/resource+` with a URI, and an `+argument+` with a name and a value, of a prompt or
template that has it. A handler that throws is answered with -32603.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0014]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0014* — For `+S: send & sync & unborrowed+`, `+R: std.stream::Reader & unborrowed+`
and `+W: std.stream::Writer & unborrowed+`:

* `+async std.mcp::serve_streams(arc std.mcp::server<S> host, R input, W output) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+async std.mcp::serve_stdio(arc std.mcp::server<S> host) -> void throws std.error::fault+`.

`+serve_streams+` serves the stdio transport over a reader and a writer: one JSON-RPC message
per line (R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0004); a line longer than `+max_message+` bytes is the failure of
R-SLIB-BUFIO-0002, which ends the serving. It runs the requests concurrently, at most 64 at
once, and writes each response and notification as one line from one writing task. A line
that is no message is answered with the error of R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0003, and a response, which a
server of this revision never asks for, with -32600; `+notifications/cancelled+` ends the
requests of its `+requestId+`, which then get no response, and other notifications are
ignored. When the input ends, every open subscription ends gracefully (R-SLIB-MCP-0015) and
every other request finishes, and the function returns. `+serve_stdio+` serves standard input
and output.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0015]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0015* — `+subscriptions/listen+` opens a subscription: `+params+` shall have a
`+notifications+` object, otherwise -32602. The server grants `+toolsListChanged+`,
`+promptsListChanged+` and `+resourcesListChanged+` that the request asks for when
`+list_changed+` is true and it has that feature, and the URIs of `+resourceSubscriptions+` when
`+subscribe+` is true and it has resources, and it first sends
`+notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged+` with the granted `+notifications+`. Then each
announcement of its notifier that the subscription covers becomes
`+notifications/tools/list_changed+`, `+notifications/prompts/list_changed+`,
`+notifications/resources/list_changed+` or `+notifications/resources/updated+` with the
`+uri+`, where a subscription to a URI also covers the URIs below it, and the tasks of
R-SLIB-MCP-0023 add their own. Each notification carries
`+io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId+`, the id of the request, in its `+_meta+`. A
subscription that falls behind the notifier sends every notification it may send once, so a
client refreshes what it holds. The subscription ends when the client cancels it, when its
stream is gone or when the notifier is closed; over stdio a subscription that the server ends
at the end of its input gets a result, `+"resultType":"complete"+` with the subscription id,
followed by `+notifications/cancelled+` for its request.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0016]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0016* — For `+S: send & sync & unborrowed+`:

* `+std.mcp::route(std.http::router<std.mcp::server<S>>* routes, str path) -> void throws std.http::http_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+route+` adds the Streamable HTTP endpoint at path to a router whose state is the server, as
a streaming route of `+POST+` (R-SLIB-HTTP-0009), and the routes of R-SLIB-MCP-0020; the router
answers `+GET+`, `+DELETE+` and other methods on the path with 405 and `+Allow: POST+`. A POST
carries one message and is checked in this order: an `+Origin+` other than the authority of
the request, a local host (`+localhost+`, `+127.0.0.1+` or `+::1+`) and the allowed origins is
403; a `+Content-Type+` other than `+application/json+` is 415; an `+Accept+` that admits
neither JSON nor an event stream is 406; the authorization of R-SLIB-MCP-0020; a body that is
no message is 400 with its JSON-RPC error; a notification is 202 without body, and a response
400 with -32600. A request shall have `+MCP-Protocol-Version+`, `+Mcp-Method+` equal to its
method and, for `+tools/call+`, `+resources/read+` and `+prompts/get+`, `+Mcp-Name+` equal to
the name or URI of its params, and for `+tasks/get+`, `+tasks/update+` and `+tasks/cancel+`
equal to its `+taskId+`, otherwise it is 400 with -32020. The checks of R-SLIB-MCP-0013
follow, where a header version that differs from the version in `+_meta+` is -32020 before the
version itself is checked; such a refusal is 400, or 404 for -32601. When the input schema of
the tool marks a string, integer or Boolean property of the arguments with an
`+x-mcp-header+` name, the request shall have `+Mcp-Param-NAME+` equal to that argument, a
number by its value, after decoding the Base64 form `+=?base64?...?=+` of UTF-8 text, otherwise
400 with -32020. A request is answered with its JSON response of status 200, errors of its
method included, or with a `+text/event-stream+` of its notifications that ends with the
response when a notification comes first, as for progress and subscriptions; a subscription
keeps its stream alive with a comment after 15 seconds without an event. A client that closes
the stream cancels its request (R-SLIB-HTTP-0009).

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0017]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0017* — `+std.mcp::client+` is a Move client of one server over the stdio framing
or over Streamable HTTP. Its requests carry the protocol version, the capabilities of the client
and its identity in `+_meta+`, may run concurrently, and throw an error response as
`+std.mcp::mcp_error+` with its code and message. The `+i64+` constants
`+std.mcp::request_timeout+`, `+std.mcp::connection_closed+` and `+std.mcp::protocol_violation+`
(1, 2 and 3) are the codes of the failures that the client meets itself: a request that took
longer than its timeout, a connection that ended before the response, and a server that broke
the protocol. `+std.mcp::client_options+` holds `+bool form = true+` and `+bool url = false+`,
the modes of elicitation that the client declares on the requests that may ask for input;
`+u32 rounds = 10+`, the most rounds of one request; `+std.time::duration timeout+`, 60
seconds unless set; `+usize max_message = 4194304+`; and `+bool tasks = false+`, whether the
client declares the Tasks extension (R-SLIB-MCP-0024). For `+S: send & sync & unborrowed+`,
`+std.mcp::elicitor<S>+` answers the requests for input of a server, with the fields
`+arc S state+` and `+async fn(arc S, std.mcp::elicitation) -> std.mcp::answer throws(std.error::fault) handler+`.
`+std.mcp::discovery+` is what discovery returns, with the fields
`+array<std.string::string> versions+`, `+std.json::value capabilities+`,
`+std.string::string instructions+`, `+std.mcp::implementation server+`, `+u64 ttl_ms+` and
`+bool private_cache+`.

* `+std.mcp::client::over_streams(own dyn(std.stream::Reader)* input, own dyn(std.stream::Writer)* output, std.mcp::implementation info, std.mcp::client_options settings) -> std.mcp::client throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::client::over_http(str endpoint, std.mcp::implementation info, std.mcp::client_options settings, o<arc std.tls::config> tls) -> std.mcp::client throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::discover(const std.mcp::client* this) -> std.mcp::discovery throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::list_tools(const std.mcp::client* this) -> array<std.mcp::tool> throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::list_resources(const std.mcp::client* this) -> array<std.mcp::resource> throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::list_templates(const std.mcp::client* this) -> array<std.mcp::resource_template> throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::list_prompts(const std.mcp::client* this) -> array<std.mcp::prompt> throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::call_tool(const std.mcp::client* this, str name, std.json::value arguments) -> std.mcp::tool_result throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::call_tool_with(const std.mcp::client* this, str name, std.json::value arguments, const std.mcp::elicitor<S>* answers) -> std.mcp::tool_result throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::read_resource(const std.mcp::client* this, str uri) -> array<std.mcp::contents> throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::read_resource_with(const std.mcp::client* this, str uri, const std.mcp::elicitor<S>* answers) -> array<std.mcp::contents> throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::get_prompt(const std.mcp::client* this, str name, std.json::value arguments) -> std.mcp::prompt_result throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::get_prompt_with(const std.mcp::client* this, str name, std.json::value arguments, const std.mcp::elicitor<S>* answers) -> std.mcp::prompt_result throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::complete(const std.mcp::client* this, bool prompt, str reference, str argument, str value) -> std.mcp::completion throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`.

`+over_streams+` makes a client over the input and output of a server, whose messages `+run+`
reads (R-SLIB-MCP-0019); `+over_http+` a client of the endpoint at an `+http+` or `+https+` URL,
where `+https+` needs a TLS configuration that verifies the server. Over HTTP a request is a
POST with `+MCP-Protocol-Version+`, `+Mcp-Method+`, `+Mcp-Name+` and the `+Mcp-Param+` fields
of the `+x-mcp-header+` properties of the tool, and its answer is read as JSON or as an event
stream; a status without a JSON-RPC response is `+protocol_violation+`. A stream client that
waits longer than the timeout sends `+notifications/cancelled+` and throws `+request_timeout+`.
A server that does not speak 2026-07-28 answers `+discover+` and the other requests with
-32022, which the client throws like any error response. The lists return
every entry over all pages; a client over HTTP leaves out a tool whose `+x-mcp-header+`
annotations are invalid. The forms with an elicitor declare the modes of the options and repeat
a request whose result needs input, answering each of its requests through the handler, up to
`+rounds+` times; a request of a mode that the client did not declare, or more rounds, are
`+protocol_violation+`. The forms without an elicitor declare no mode, so a server that needs
input answers -32021. `+call_tool+` and `+call_tool_with+` follow a task that the server makes
to its end (R-SLIB-MCP-0024). `+complete+` completes an argument of a prompt, when prompt is
true, or a variable of a template.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0018]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0018* — `+std.mcp::interests+` is what a subscription asks for, with the fields
`+bool tools+`, `+bool prompts+` and `+bool resources+` for the changes of the lists,
`+array<std.string::string> uris+` for the updates of resources and
`+array<std.string::string> tasks+` for the states of tasks (R-SLIB-MCP-0023).
`+std.mcp::change+` is one change that a subscription reports: `+tools+`, `+prompts+`,
`+resources+`, `+updated(std.string::string)+` with the URI of the resource or
`+state(std.mcp::task_state)+` with the state of a task. `+std.mcp::subscription+` is an
open subscription, with the public field `+std.mcp::interests granted+`, what the server
honours.

* `+std.mcp::interests::create() -> std.mcp::interests+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::listen(const std.mcp::client* this, const std.mcp::interests* wanted) -> std.mcp::subscription throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::subscription::next(std.mcp::subscription* this) -> o<std.mcp::change> throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::unlisten(const std.mcp::client* this, std.mcp::subscription watched) -> void throws std.error::fault+`.

`+listen+` sends `+subscriptions/listen+` and returns when the acknowledgement arrives, within
the timeout. `+next+` waits for the next change and returns none when the subscription has
ended, because the server ended it or its connection ended; messages that are no changes are
skipped. `+unlisten+` ends the subscription: over a stream the client sends
`+notifications/cancelled+`, over HTTP it closes the event stream.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0019]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0019* — `+std.mcp::launched+` is a server started as a child process with its client,
