with the fields `+std.mcp::client link+` and `+o<std.process::child> child+`.

* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::run(const std.mcp::client* this) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::close(const std.mcp::client* this) -> void throws std.error::fault+`;
* `+async std.mcp::launch(std.process::command command, std.mcp::implementation info, std.mcp::client_options settings) -> std.mcp::launched throws std.error::fault+`.

`+run+`, which a task of the program runs next to the requests of a stream client, reads the
messages of the server and hands each to the request or subscription it belongs to until the
server ends its output; lines that are no messages are skipped, and every request still waiting
then fails with `+connection_closed+`. A client over HTTP returns at once. `+close+` shuts down
and drops the writer to the server, which a server over stdio takes as the end of its input;
`+run+` returns when the server has ended its output. `+launch+` starts the command with piped
standard input and output and inherited standard error and returns the client over those pipes;
the program takes the child out of the result with `+core::replace+` and waits for it after
`+close+`.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0020]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0020* — A server over HTTP may be an OAuth 2.1 resource server.
`+std.mcp::protection+` is what it announces as its Protected Resource Metadata (RFC 9728),
with the fields `+std.string::string resource+`, its canonical URI,
`+array<std.string::string> authorization_servers+` and `+array<std.string::string> scopes+`.
`+std.mcp::access+` is the decision about a bearer token: `+allowed+`, `+invalid+` (not
valid for this server) or `+insufficient+` (valid without the scope that the method needs).
The `+i64+` constants `+std.mcp::unauthorized+` and `+std.mcp::forbidden+` (4 and 5) are the
failures of a client whose token the server refused with 401 or 403.

* `+std.mcp::protection::create(str resource, str authorization_server) -> std.mcp::protection throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::protection::scope(std.mcp::protection* this, str name) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.mcp::server<S>::protect(std.mcp::server<S>* this, std.mcp::protection settings, fn(const S*, str, str) -> std.mcp::access throws(std.alloc::alloc_error) check) -> void+`;
* `+std.mcp::client::set_token(std.mcp::client* this, str token) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

After `+protect+`, each POST shall carry `+Authorization: Bearer TOKEN+`; the check receives the
state, the token and the method of the message, or an empty method for the check before the
body is read, and shall verify that the token was issued for the resource. A POST without a token or with an invalid
one is answered with 401, one whose token lacks a scope with 403, both with
`+WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="URL", scope="..."+` and, after a check,
`+error="invalid_token"+` or `+error="insufficient_scope"+`, where the metadata URL is the
resource with `+/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource+` inserted before its path. `+route+`
answers `+GET+` on `+/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource+` and on that path followed by the
path of the endpoint with the metadata as JSON (`+resource+`, `+authorization_servers+`,
`+scopes_supported+` when there are scopes, and `+bearer_methods_supported+` `+["header"]+`),
and with 404 for an unprotected server. `+set_token+` sets the
token that a client over HTTP sends with every request. The authorization flow of the client
(PKCE, RFC 8707) is not part of this revision.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0021]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0021* — A server implements the Tasks extension of MCP, revision 2026-07-28, for the
calls of its task tools. `+std.mcp::tasks_extension+` is the `+constexpr str+`
`+"io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks"+`, the name under which a client declares the extension in
`+extensions+` of its capabilities on each request and a server with a task tool offers it in
the capabilities of discovery, each with an empty object. `+std.mcp::task_status+` is the Copy
fieldless enum of the status of a task, `+working+`, `+input_required+`, `+completed+`,
`+failed+` or `+cancelled+`, where the last three are final. For
`+S: send & sync & unborrowed+`:

* `+std.mcp::server<S>::run_as_task(std.mcp::server<S>* this, str tool, bool required) -> void throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+async std.mcp::run_tasks(arc std.mcp::server<S> host, std.sync::receiver<std.service::stop> stop) -> void throws std.error::fault+`.

`+run_as_task+` makes an added tool a task tool, otherwise it throws -32602. A call of a task
tool from a client that declares the extension becomes a task while a runner of the server
runs; a call from another client, or without a runner, runs as the call of any tool, except
that a tool made with required true answers a client that does not declare the extension with
-32021 and the data `+{"requiredCapabilities":{"extensions":{"io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks":{}}}}+`.
`+run_tasks+` is the runner of the tasks of a server, which a task of the program runs next to
its transports. It runs at most 64 tasks at once until a stop arrives (R-SLIB-SERVICE-0001) or
the channel of the stops is closed, which counts as `+drain+`: with `+drain+` the tasks whose
handlers run finish and those that wait for input are cancelled, with `+cancel+` every task is
cancelled, and it returns when the tasks have ended. A server has one runner; `+run_tasks+`
returns at once for a server without task tools and for any later runner.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0022]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0022* — A task of a server has an id, a version 4 UUID (R-SLIB-UUID-0002), a status,
a status message, the time of its creation and of its last change in RFC 3339 with milliseconds
in UTC, and it is kept for `+task_ttl_ms+` from its creation; a task past that time is removed,
and its handler, when it runs, is cancelled. The call that makes a task is answered at once with
`+"resultType":"task"+` and the task: `+taskId+`, `+status+` `+working+`, `+createdAt+`,
`+lastUpdatedAt+`, `+ttlMs+` and `+pollIntervalMs+`, which is `+task_poll_ms+`. The handler of
the tool then runs in rounds as the runner admits the task. `+complete+` completes the task with
the result of the tool, as `+tools/call+` gives it with `+"resultType":"complete"+`;
`+needs_input+` moves it to `+input_required+` with input requests whose
keys are those of the handler prefixed by the number of the round and a full stop, so that no
key returns, unless the client declared no mode of the requests, which fails the task with the
-32021 error of R-SLIB-MCP-0007; a handler that throws fails the task with -32603. When every
request of a round has its answer, the task works again and the handler runs with the answers
under its own keys and the state of the round (R-SLIB-MCP-0007). In the handler of a task,
`+progress::report+` sets the status message of the working task to its message, or to the
value and the total without one (R-SLIB-MCP-0012).

The server offers `+tasks/get+`, `+tasks/update+` and `+tasks/cancel+` to the clients that
declare the extension, and answers other clients with the -32021 of R-SLIB-MCP-0021. Each takes
the string `+taskId+`, otherwise -32602, and answers a task that does not exist or was removed
with -32602 and the message `+Failed to retrieve task: Task not found+`. `+tasks/get+` returns
the task with `+"resultType":"complete"+`: the fields above, `+statusMessage+` when there is
one, `+inputRequests+` with the requests that wait for an answer while the task waits for input,
`+result+` when it completed and `+error+`, the JSON-RPC error object, when it failed.
`+tasks/update+` takes `+inputResponses+`, an object of answers (R-SLIB-MCP-0007) under the
keys of `+inputRequests+`, otherwise -32602; answers under other keys are ignored, as is an
update of a task that does not wait for input. `+tasks/cancel+` cancels a task that has not
ended: one that waits for input at once, and a working one when its handler, which is
cancelled, has stopped. Both return an empty result with `+"resultType":"complete"+`.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0023]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0023* — The server announces each change of the state of a task through its notifier
(R-SLIB-MCP-0010). A `+subscriptions/listen+` whose `+notifications+` has `+taskIds+` from a
client that does not declare the extension is answered with the -32021 of R-SLIB-MCP-0021;
otherwise the server grants the ids of tasks that it has, lists them as `+taskIds+` in the
acknowledgement when there are some, and after each change of a granted task sends
`+notifications/tasks+` with the state of the task as `+tasks/get+` returns it and the id of the
subscription in `+_meta+`. A subscription that falls behind sends the state of each granted task
(R-SLIB-MCP-0015). A client asks for the states of tasks with the ids in `+interests.tasks+`
(R-SLIB-MCP-0018), which needs `+client_options.tasks+`; the granted interests list the ids that
the server granted, and `+subscription::next+` returns each notification as
`+change::state+` with the state of the task.

[[R-SLIB-MCP-0024]]
*R-SLIB-MCP-0024* — A client with `+client_options.tasks+` true declares the Tasks extension on
each request. `+std.mcp::task_cancelled+` is the `+i64+` constant 6, the failure of a task that
a client followed and that was cancelled. `+std.mcp::task_state+` is a task as a client sees it,
with the fields `+std.string::string id+`, `+std.mcp::task_status status+`,
`+std.string::string message+`, `+u64 poll_ms+` (1000 when the server suggests no interval),
`+array<std.string::string> keys+` and `+array<std.mcp::elicitation> requests+` of the input
requests that wait for an answer, `+o<std.mcp::tool_result> result+` and
`+o<std.mcp::mcp_error> error+`; an input request of no known kind is left out.
`+std.mcp::tool_start+` is what a call started: `+finished(std.mcp::tool_result)+` or
`+running(std.mcp::task_state)+`. For `+S: send & sync & unborrowed+`:

* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::start_tool(const std.mcp::client* this, str name, std.json::value arguments, const std.mcp::elicitor<S>* answers) -> std.mcp::tool_start throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::get_task(const std.mcp::client* this, str id) -> std.mcp::task_state throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::answer_task(const std.mcp::client* this, str id, str key, const std.mcp::answer* given) -> void throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::cancel_task(const std.mcp::client* this, str id) -> void throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`;
* `+@scoped async std.mcp::client::finish_task(const std.mcp::client* this, str id, const std.mcp::elicitor<S>* answers) -> std.mcp::tool_result throws std.mcp::mcp_error, std.error::fault+`.

`+call_tool+`, `+call_tool_with+` and `+finish_task+` follow a task to its end: the client polls
it with `+tasks/get+` at the interval that the server suggests, held between 10 milliseconds and
a minute, answers each input request once with `+tasks/update+` through the elicitor, and
returns the result of the task; a failed task throws its error and a cancelled one
`+task_cancelled+`. `+start_tool+` sends `+tools/call+` with the modes of the options, answers
the rounds of a tool that runs at once through the elicitor (R-SLIB-MCP-0017) and returns its
result, or returns the first state of a task. `+get_task+` returns the state of a task,
`+answer_task+` sends the answer to the input request under a key with `+tasks/update+`, and
`+cancel_task+` asks the server to cancel a task with `+tasks/cancel+`. Over HTTP these requests
carry the id of the task as `+Mcp-Name+` (R-SLIB-MCP-0016).


=== 21.24 CBOR: `+std.cbor+`

[[R-SLIB-CBOR-0001]]
*R-SLIB-CBOR-0001* — `+std.cbor+` is a standard module written in R in the `+hosted+` profile
that encodes and decodes the Concise Binary Object Representation of RFC 8949.
`+std.cbor::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+truncated+`, `+malformed+`,
`+invalid_utf8+`, `+duplicate_key+`, `+too_deep+`, `+trailing_data+` and
`+not_deterministic+`, formatted by its variant names. `+std.cbor::cbor_error+` is an error of
the module with the fields `+std.cbor::error_code code+` and `+usize offset+`: the offset of
the item in the input where decoding found the failure, or the length of the output where
encoding did. Arrays, maps and tags nest at most 1024 levels; deeper nesting is `+too_deep+`.
The operations keep nested items on stacks in allocated memory, so nesting costs no call
stack.

[[R-SLIB-CBOR-0002]]
*R-SLIB-CBOR-0002* — `+std.cbor::value+` is the Move tagged enum of one data item of the CBOR
data model, with exactly the variants `+unsigned(u64)+` (major type 0), `+negative(u64)+`
(major type 1, holding n for the integer -1 - n), `+bytes(bytes)+`,
`+text(std.string::string)+`, `+sequence(array<std.cbor::value>)+` (an array),
`+map(array<std.cbor::entry>)+` (the entries in order), `+tagged+` with the fields
`+u64 tag+` and `+own std.cbor::value* content+`, `+simple(u8)+`, `+boolean(bool)+`,
`+null_value+`, `+undefined+` and `+float(f64)+`. `+std.cbor::entry+` is the Move struct
with the fields `+std.cbor::value key+` and `+std.cbor::value item+`. The constructors are:

* `+std.cbor::value::integer(i64 number) -> std.cbor::value+`;
* `+std.cbor::value::of_bytes(const u8[] data) -> std.cbor::value throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::value::of_text(str data) -> std.cbor::value throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::value::tag_of(u64 tag, std.cbor::value content) -> std.cbor::value throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+integer+` makes `+unsigned+` for a value of zero or more and `+negative+` otherwise; the
others copy their data or box the content. A simple value is 0 to 19 or 32 to 255; 20 to 23
are the values `+boolean+`, `+null_value+` and `+undefined+` represent, and 24 to 31 have no
encoding.

[[R-SLIB-CBOR-0003]]
*R-SLIB-CBOR-0003* — `+std.cbor::encoder+` is a Move writer that appends items to owned bytes
in preferred serialization (RFC 8949 section 4.1): every head in its shortest form and every
float in the shortest of binary16, binary32 and binary64 that holds its value exactly, NaN as
`+0xf97e00+`. Its operations are:

* `+std.cbor::encoder::create() -> std.cbor::encoder+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::unsigned(std.cbor::encoder* this, u64 number) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::negative(std.cbor::encoder* this, u64 number) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::integer(std.cbor::encoder* this, i64 number) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::bytes(std.cbor::encoder* this, const u8[] data) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::text(std.cbor::encoder* this, str data) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::begin_array(std.cbor::encoder* this, u64 count) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::begin_map(std.cbor::encoder* this, u64 count) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::tag(std.cbor::encoder* this, u64 number) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::simple(std.cbor::encoder* this, u8 number) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cbor::cbor_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::boolean(std.cbor::encoder* this, bool truth) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::null_value(std.cbor::encoder* this) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::undefined(std.cbor::encoder* this) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::float(std.cbor::encoder* this, f64 number) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::encoded(std.cbor::encoder* this, const u8[] item) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::value(std.cbor::encoder* this, const std.cbor::value* item) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cbor::cbor_error+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::length(const std.cbor::encoder* this) -> usize+`;
* `+std.cbor::encoder::finish(std.cbor::encoder* this) -> bytes+`.

`+begin_array+` and `+begin_map+` write the head of a definite-length array of count items or
map of count entries, which the caller writes next, a map as key and value in turn.
`+simple+` of 24 to 31 is `+malformed+`. `+encoded+` appends bytes that are already one encoded
item, `+value+` appends the deterministic encoding of R-SLIB-CBOR-0004, and `+finish+` returns
the bytes written and leaves the encoder empty.

[[R-SLIB-CBOR-0004]]
*R-SLIB-CBOR-0004* — `+std.cbor::encode(const std.cbor::value* item) -> bytes throws
std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cbor::cbor_error+` is the core deterministic encoding of RFC 8949
section 4.2.1: preferred serialization, definite lengths only, and the entries of every map
in the bytewise lexicographic order of their encoded keys. Two keys with equal encodings are
`+duplicate_key+`.

[[R-SLIB-CBOR-0005]]
*R-SLIB-CBOR-0005* — `+std.cbor::decode(const u8[] input) -> std.cbor::value throws
std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cbor::cbor_error+` reads the single data item that is the whole
input. It accepts every well-formed encoding: heads of any length, indefinite-length strings,
whose definite chunks of the same major type it joins, and indefinite-length arrays and maps.
An input that ends inside an item is `+truncated+`; reserved additional information, a break
outside an indefinite-length item, a chunk of another type and a two-byte simple value below
32 are `+malformed+`; a text string or chunk that is not UTF-8 is `+invalid_utf8+`; a map with
two keys of equal deterministic encoding is `+duplicate_key+`; bytes after the item are
`+trailing_data+`. Tags keep their number and content without interpretation.
`+std.cbor::decode_deterministic(const u8[] input) -> std.cbor::value throws
std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cbor::cbor_error+` additionally requires the input to be the
deterministic encoding of its value, and reports any other form as `+not_deterministic+`.

[[R-SLIB-CBOR-0006]]
*R-SLIB-CBOR-0006* — `+std.cbor::diagnostic(const std.cbor::value* item) ->
std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` is the diagnostic notation of RFC 8949
section 8: integers in decimal, byte strings as `+h'...'+` in lowercase hexadecimal, text
strings quoted with `+\"+`, `+\\+` and `+\u00XX+` escapes for the other control characters,
arrays as `+[a, b]+`, maps as `+{k: v}+`, tags as `+N(content)+`, `+simple(N)+`, `+true+`,
`+false+`, `+null+`, `+undefined+`, and floats in the shortest decimal form that reads back
exactly, with `+.0+` for an integral value, `+NaN+`, `+Infinity+` and `+-Infinity+`.

=== 21.25 Cryptography: `+std.crypto+`

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0001]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0001* — `+std.crypto+` is a standard module written in R in the `+hosted+`
profile over the native provider `+std.crypto.native+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0003), which calls
libsodium and the PSA Crypto interface of Mbed TLS 3.6. The provider reads and writes the
buffers it is given only and keeps no key of a caller between calls; a secret key of this
module lives in a `+std.secret::buffer+` (R-SLIB-SECRET-0001).
`+std.crypto::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+invalid_length+`,
`+authentication_failed+`, `+weak_key+`, `+limits+`, `+resource_exhausted+`,
`+unavailable+`, `+invalid_key+`, `+invalid_padding+` and `+unsupported+`, formatted by its
variant names, and `+std.crypto::crypto_error+` is an error of the module with the field
`+std.crypto::error_code code+`. Every operation reports `+unavailable+` when the library it
uses cannot start, a key, nonce or length outside the sizes stated below as
`+invalid_length+`, and memory that the provider cannot obtain as `+resource_exhausted+`.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0002]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0002* — `+std.crypto::random(usize length) -> bytes throws
std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+` returns length bytes from the random
source of the system.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0003]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0003* — `+std.crypto::aead+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly
`+chacha20_poly1305+`, the AEAD of RFC 8439 with a 12-byte nonce, and `+xchacha20_poly1305+`,
its variant with a 24-byte nonce; both take a 32-byte key and add a 16-byte tag. Its methods
`+key_length+`, `+nonce_length+` and `+tag_length+` take the enum by value and return those
sizes. The operations are:

* `+std.crypto::seal(std.crypto::aead algorithm, const u8[] key, const u8[] nonce, const u8[] aad, const u8[] plaintext) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::open(std.crypto::aead algorithm, const u8[] key, const u8[] nonce, const u8[] aad, const u8[] ciphertext) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`.

`+seal+` returns the ciphertext followed by the tag, which also authenticates aad. `+open+`
returns the plaintext only when ciphertext, tag, key, nonce and aad authenticate and reports
`+authentication_failed+` otherwise, including for a ciphertext shorter than the tag. A
nonce shall not be used twice with one key.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0004]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0004* — `+std.crypto::signing_key+` is the Move Ed25519 key pair of RFC 8032,
with a protected 64-byte secret part and the public field `+u8[32] public_key+`:

* `+std.crypto::signing_key::from_seed(const u8[] seed) -> std.crypto::signing_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::signing_key::generate() -> std.crypto::signing_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::signing_key::sign(const std.crypto::signing_key* this, const u8[] message) -> u8[64] throws std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::verify(const u8[] public_key, const u8[] message, const u8[] signature) -> bool throws std.crypto::crypto_error+`.

`+from_seed+` derives the key pair of a 32-byte seed as RFC 8032 section 5.1.5 specifies;
`+generate+` uses a random seed. `+sign+` returns the deterministic detached signature, and
`+verify+` is true exactly when signature is a valid signature of message by public_key; a
public key or signature of another length is not.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0005]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0005* — `+std.crypto::exchange_key+` is the Move X25519 key pair of RFC 7748,
with a protected 32-byte secret part and the public field `+u8[32] public_key+`:

* `+std.crypto::exchange_key::from_secret(const u8[] secret) -> std.crypto::exchange_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::exchange_key::generate() -> std.crypto::exchange_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::exchange_key::shared(const std.crypto::exchange_key* this, const u8[] peer) -> std.secret::buffer throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`.

`+shared+` returns the 32-byte shared secret with the holder of the peer public key; a peer
key whose result is all zero bytes, as for a point of small order, is `+weak_key+`. The
shared secret is input to a key derivation, not a key.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0006]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0006* — HKDF of RFC 5869 is
`+std.crypto::hkdf_sha256(const u8[] salt, const u8[] key_material, const u8[] info, usize length) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`
and `+std.crypto::hkdf_sha512+` with the same parameters: extract with salt, an empty salt
standing for zero bytes of the hash length, then expand with info to length bytes, which
shall be 1 to 255 hash lengths.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0007]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0007* — `+std.crypto::password_limits+` is the Copy struct of the work of
Argon2id with the fields `+u64 operations+` (passes over memory) and `+usize memory+` (bytes);
`+std.crypto::password_limits::interactive() -> std.crypto::password_limits+` returns 2 passes
over 64 MiB. The operations are:

* `+std.crypto::argon2id(const u8[] password, const u8[] salt, std.crypto::password_limits limits, usize length) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::password_hash(const u8[] password, std.crypto::password_limits limits) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::password_verify(str hash, const u8[] password) -> bool throws std.crypto::crypto_error+`.

`+argon2id+` derives length bytes, at least 16, from password and a 16-byte salt with
Argon2id version 1.3 of RFC 9106 in one lane. `+password_hash+` returns the encoded hash
`+$argon2id$v=19$m=...,t=...,p=1$salt$hash+` with a random salt, and `+password_verify+` is
true exactly when password matches such a hash. Limits outside the ranges of libsodium are
`+limits+`, and memory that cannot be allocated is `+resource_exhausted+`.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0008]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0008* — BLAKE2b of RFC 7693 is
`+std.crypto::blake2b(const u8[] data, const u8[] key, usize length) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`,
the length-byte digest of data under key, with a digest of 16 to 64 bytes and a key of up to
64 bytes, an empty key for a plain hash. `+std.crypto::blake2b_state+` is the Copy state of
the same function over data given in pieces:

* `+std.crypto::blake2b_state::create(const u8[] key, usize length) -> std.crypto::blake2b_state throws std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::blake2b_state::update(std.crypto::blake2b_state* this, const u8[] data) -> void+`;
* `+std.crypto::blake2b_state::finish(std.crypto::blake2b_state* this) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+finish+` returns the digest of the pieces given since `+create+` and erases the state.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0009]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0009* — `+std.crypto::curve+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+p256+`,
the curve secp256r1 with SHA-256, and `+p384+`, the curve secp384r1 with SHA-384 (RFC 5480).
Its methods `+scalar_length+`, `+point_length+` and `+signature_length+` take the enum by
value and return 32, 65 and 64 bytes for `+p256+` and 48, 97 and 96 bytes for `+p384+`.
`+std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key+` is the Move ECDSA public key of a curve, whose point is the
uncompressed SEC1 encoding 0x04 || X || Y, and `+std.crypto::ecdsa_key+` is the Move ECDSA
key pair, whose protected private scalar lives in a secret buffer:

* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key::from_point(std.crypto::curve which, const u8[] point) -> std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key::curve(const std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key* this) -> std.crypto::curve+`;
* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key::point(const std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key* this) -> const u8[]+`;
* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key::verify(const std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key* this, const u8[] message, const u8[] signature) -> bool throws std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_key::generate(std.crypto::curve which) -> std.crypto::ecdsa_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_key::from_scalar(std.crypto::curve which, const u8[] scalar) -> std.crypto::ecdsa_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_key::curve(const std.crypto::ecdsa_key* this) -> std.crypto::curve+`;
* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_key::public_key(const std.crypto::ecdsa_key* this) -> std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::ecdsa_key::sign(const std.crypto::ecdsa_key* this, const u8[] message) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`.

`+from_point+` accepts an uncompressed point on the curve only and reports any other point
as `+invalid_key+`. `+from_scalar+` takes the big-endian private scalar of the scalar length,
or shorter with its leading zero bytes left out, and reports zero and a scalar not below the
order of the curve as `+invalid_key+`. `+sign+` hashes message with the hash of the curve and
returns the deterministic signature of RFC 6979 as r and s of the scalar length each in
big-endian order, the form of JWS and COSE. `+verify+` is true exactly when signature is an
ECDSA signature of message by the key, deterministic or not; a signature of another length
is not.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0010]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0010* — `+std.crypto::rsa_scheme+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly
`+pkcs1_sha256+`, `+pkcs1_sha384+`, `+pkcs1_sha512+`, `+pss_sha256+`, `+pss_sha384+` and
`+pss_sha512+`: the RSA signatures of RFC 8017, RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 and RSASSA-PSS with MGF1 of
the same hash and a salt of the hash length, over SHA-256, SHA-384 and SHA-512.
`+std.crypto::rsa_public_key+` is the Move RSA public key and `+std.crypto::rsa_key+` the
Move RSA private key, whose protected PKCS#1 RSAPrivateKey lives in a secret buffer; the
modulus of either has 2048 to 4096 bits:

* `+std.crypto::rsa_public_key::from_components(const u8[] modulus, const u8[] exponent) -> std.crypto::rsa_public_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::rsa_public_key::modulus(const std.crypto::rsa_public_key* this) -> const u8[]+`;
* `+std.crypto::rsa_public_key::exponent(const std.crypto::rsa_public_key* this) -> const u8[]+`;
* `+std.crypto::rsa_public_key::bits(const std.crypto::rsa_public_key* this) -> usize+`;
* `+std.crypto::rsa_public_key::verify(const std.crypto::rsa_public_key* this, std.crypto::rsa_scheme scheme, const u8[] message, const u8[] signature) -> bool throws std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::rsa_key::generate(usize bits) -> std.crypto::rsa_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::rsa_key::bits(const std.crypto::rsa_key* this) -> usize+`;
* `+std.crypto::rsa_key::public_key(const std.crypto::rsa_key* this) -> std.crypto::rsa_public_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::rsa_key::sign(const std.crypto::rsa_key* this, std.crypto::rsa_scheme scheme, const u8[] message) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`.

`+from_components+` takes the modulus and the public exponent as big-endian unsigned numbers,
ignores their leading zero bytes and reports a key the provider refuses or a modulus outside
2048 to 4096 bits as `+invalid_key+`; `+modulus+` and `+exponent+` return them without leading
zero bytes. `+generate+` makes a key with the exponent 65537 and a modulus of bits bits, which
shall be 2048 to 4096 and a multiple of 8. `+sign+` returns a signature of the modulus size;
PKCS#1 v1.5 signatures are deterministic, PSS signatures use a random salt. `+verify+` is true
exactly when signature is a signature of message under scheme; a signature of another length
than the modulus is not.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0011]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0011* — AES in CBC mode with the padding of PKCS#7 (RFC 5652 section 6.3) is:

* `+std.crypto::cbc_encrypt(const u8[] key, const u8[] iv, const u8[] plaintext) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::cbc_decrypt(const u8[] key, const u8[] iv, const u8[] ciphertext) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`.

The key has 16, 24 or 32 bytes and selects AES-128, AES-192 or AES-256; the IV has 16 bytes.
`+cbc_encrypt+` pads the plaintext with 1 to 16 bytes to whole blocks and returns the
ciphertext. `+cbc_decrypt+` reports a ciphertext whose length is not a positive multiple of
16 as `+invalid_length+` and a padding that does not check as `+invalid_padding+`, and
otherwise returns the plaintext without the padding. CBC does not authenticate: it serves
protocols that require it, and a protocol that may choose uses an AEAD of R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0003.

[[R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0012]]
*R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0012* — `+std.crypto::private_key+` is the Move enum of a private key with
exactly the variants `+ecdsa(std.crypto::ecdsa_key)+`, `+rsa(std.crypto::rsa_key)+`,
`+ed25519(std.crypto::signing_key)+` and `+x25519(std.crypto::exchange_key)+`, and
`+std.crypto::public_key+` the Move enum of a public key with exactly the variants
`+ecdsa(std.crypto::ecdsa_public_key)+`, `+rsa(std.crypto::rsa_public_key)+`,
`+ed25519(u8[32])+` and `+x25519(u8[32])+`:

* `+std.crypto::private_key::from_der(const u8[] der) -> std.crypto::private_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::private_key::from_pem(const u8[] text) -> std.crypto::private_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::private_key::to_der(const std.crypto::private_key* this) -> std.secret::buffer throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::private_key::to_pem(const std.crypto::private_key* this) -> std.secret::buffer throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::private_key::public_key(const std.crypto::private_key* this) -> std.crypto::public_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::public_key::from_der(const u8[] der) -> std.crypto::public_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::public_key::from_pem(const u8[] text) -> std.crypto::public_key throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::public_key::to_der(const std.crypto::public_key* this) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.crypto::public_key::to_pem(const std.crypto::public_key* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+private_key::from_der+` reads the DER of a PKCS#8 PrivateKeyInfo without encryption (RFC
5958) of an id-ecPublicKey key on a curve of R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0009, an rsaEncryption key, an
Ed25519 or an X25519 key (RFC 8410), a SEC1 ECPrivateKey (RFC 5915) that names its curve, or
a two-prime PKCS#1 RSAPrivateKey, told apart by their structure. `+public_key::from_der+`
reads the DER of a SubjectPublicKeyInfo (RFC 5280, RFC 5480, RFC 8410) of the same kinds with
an uncompressed EC point, or of a PKCS#1 RSAPublicKey. `+from_pem+` reads the first block of
a PEM text (RFC 7468) labelled `+PRIVATE KEY+`, `+EC PRIVATE KEY+` or `+RSA PRIVATE KEY+`,
respectively `+PUBLIC KEY+` or `+RSA PUBLIC KEY+`, and skips blocks with other labels. An
encrypted private key, another algorithm or curve and a compressed point are
`+unsupported+`; any other input that is not such a key, including a text without such a
block, is `+invalid_key+`, and keys outside R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0009 and R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0010 are
refused as there. `+to_der+` returns the DER of the PKCS#8 PrivateKeyInfo of version 0,
whose ECPrivateKey holds the public point and not the curve, respectively of the
SubjectPublicKeyInfo; `+to_pem+` returns that DER as a PEM block labelled `+PRIVATE KEY+`,
respectively `+PUBLIC KEY+`, with 64 base64 characters per line and a line feed after each
line. A private key and its PEM text are returned in secret buffers.

=== 21.26 CBOR Object Signing and Encryption: `+std.cose+`

[[R-SLIB-COSE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-COSE-0001* — `+std.cose+` is a standard module written in R in the `+hosted+`
profile that makes and reads the single-recipient messages of RFC 9052 over `+std.cbor+`,
`+std.crypto+` and `+std.hash+`: COSE_Sign1 with EdDSA over Ed25519 (algorithm -8),
COSE_Mac0 with HMAC 256/64, 256/256, 384/384 and 512/512 (algorithms 4 to 7) and
COSE_Encrypt0 with ChaCha20/Poly1305 (algorithm 24) of RFC 9053. `+std.cose::error_code+` is
a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+malformed+`, `+wrong_type+`, `+unsupported_algorithm+`,
`+unsupported_header+`, `+missing_header+`, `+invalid_key+` and `+verification_failed+`,
formatted by its variant names, and `+std.cose::cose_error+` is an error of the module with
the field `+std.cose::error_code code+`. The i64 constants `+ALGORITHM+` (1), `+CRITICAL+` (2),
`+CONTENT_TYPE+` (3), `+KEY_ID+` (4), `+IV+` (5) and `+PARTIAL_IV+` (6) name header labels,
`+EDDSA+`, `+HMAC_256_64+`, `+HMAC_256+`, `+HMAC_384+`, `+HMAC_512+` and
`+CHACHA20_POLY1305+` the algorithms, and the u64 constants `+ENCRYPT0+` (16), `+MAC0+` (17)
and `+SIGN1+` (18) the tags.

[[R-SLIB-COSE-0002]]
*R-SLIB-COSE-0002* — `+std.cose::headers+` is the Move set of header parameters of a message
in two buckets, one that the signature, tag or ciphertext covers and one that it
does not:

* `+std.cose::headers::create() -> std.cose::headers+`;
* `+std.cose::headers::protect(std.cose::headers* this, i64 label, std.cbor::value item) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cose::headers::expose(std.cose::headers* this, i64 label, std.cbor::value item) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.cose::headers::find(const std.cose::headers* this, i64 label) -> o<const std.cbor::value*>+`;
* `+std.cose::headers::algorithm(const std.cose::headers* this) -> o<i64>+`.

`+protect+` adds a parameter to the covered bucket and `+expose+` to the other; `+find+`
returns the value of a label from the covered bucket first, and `+algorithm+` the integer of
label 1. A message writes its covered bucket as the deterministic encoding of its map, an
empty bucket as a zero-length byte string.

[[R-SLIB-COSE-0003]]
*R-SLIB-COSE-0003* — `+std.cose::message+` is the Move received message with the public fields
`+u64 tag+`, `+bytes protected_part+` (the covered bucket as received), `+std.cose::headers
fields+`, `+o<bytes> content+` (the payload or ciphertext, none when detached) and
`+bytes last+` (the signature or tag). `+std.cose::decode(const u8[] data, u64 expected_tag)
-> std.cose::message throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cose::cose_error+` reads a message of
the type that expected_tag names, tagged with it or untagged; another tag is `+wrong_type+`,
and anything that is not CBOR of the structure of RFC 9052 is `+malformed+`. A covered bucket
encoded as an empty map counts as the empty bucket (RFC 9052 section 3). The verification
operations below decode in the same way and then check a `+CRITICAL+` parameter, which shall
name only labels 1 to 6 (`+unsupported_header+` otherwise), and the algorithm, which shall
be present (`+missing_header+`) and offered (`+unsupported_algorithm+`).

[[R-SLIB-COSE-0004]]
*R-SLIB-COSE-0004* — COSE_Sign1 (tag 18):

* `+std.cose::sign1(std.cose::headers fields, const u8[] payload, const std.crypto::signing_key* key, const u8[] external_aad) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error, std.cose::cose_error+`;
* `+std.cose::verify_sign1(const u8[] data, const u8[] public_key, const u8[] external_aad) -> std.cose::message throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error, std.cose::cose_error+`.

`+sign1+` adds EdDSA to the covered bucket when the headers name no algorithm and signs the
`+Sig_structure+` `+["Signature1", protected, external_aad, payload]+`. `+verify_sign1+`
returns the message after its signature by the 32-byte public key verifies over the
received covered bucket, and reports `+verification_failed+` otherwise; a public key of
another length is `+invalid_key+`.

[[R-SLIB-COSE-0005]]
*R-SLIB-COSE-0005* — COSE_Mac0 (tag 17):

* `+std.cose::mac0(std.cose::headers fields, const u8[] payload, const u8[] key, const u8[] external_aad) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cose::cose_error+`;
* `+std.cose::verify_mac0(const u8[] data, const u8[] key, const u8[] external_aad) -> std.cose::message throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cose::cose_error+`.

The algorithm named by the headers selects HMAC with SHA-256, the first 8 bytes of it, or
HMAC with SHA-384 or SHA-512 (R-SLIB-BYTES-0011) over the `+MAC_structure+`
`+["MAC0", protected, external_aad, payload]+`. `+verify_mac0+` compares the tag in constant
time (R-SLIB-SECRET-0001) and reports `+verification_failed+` when it differs.

[[R-SLIB-COSE-0006]]
*R-SLIB-COSE-0006* — COSE_Encrypt0 (tag 16):

* `+std.cose::encrypt0(std.cose::headers fields, const u8[] plaintext, const u8[] key, const u8[] iv, const u8[] external_aad) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.crypto::crypto_error, std.cose::cose_error+`;
* `+std.cose::decrypt0(const u8[] data, const u8[] key, const u8[] external_aad) -> bytes throws std.alloc::alloc_error, std.cose::cose_error+`.

`+encrypt0+` adds ChaCha20/Poly1305 to the covered bucket when the headers name no algorithm
and the 12-byte iv to the other bucket as label 5, and seals the plaintext under the 32-byte
key with the `+Enc_structure+` `+["Encrypt0", protected, external_aad]+` as additional data.
`+decrypt0+` returns the plaintext when the message authenticates and reports
`+verification_failed+` otherwise; a missing iv is `+missing_header+`, and a key or iv of
another length `+invalid_key+`. An iv shall not be used twice with one key.

=== 21.27 SQLite: `+std.sqlite+`

[[R-SLIB-SQLITE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-SQLITE-0001* — `+std.sqlite+` is a standard module written in R in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile over the native provider `+std.sqlite.native+`
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0003), which calls an SQLite library of version 3.37 or later, named
`+sqlite3+` in the link manifest of the library; whether the implementation builds that library
or takes it from the system, its version and its compile-time options are implementation-defined
(R-SLIB-IDB-0017). The provider opens every connection in the serialized threading mode with
extended result codes and holds the mutex of the connection for the whole of each of its calls,
so that a failure and its message stay together. Every operation that reaches a database is a call of the blocking call pool
(R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017) that the operation starts and returns as a task: before it returns, the
operation copies its text and keeps the connection or the statement through a new strong
reference, so the task borrows nothing from its caller (Core R-BORROW-0024) and is awaited
with the call form of await. The immediate effects of such an operation are
`+std.async::start_error+` and `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`; its completion errors are
`+std.sqlite::sqlite_error+` and `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`, which also reports a failure of
memory inside SQLite. Cancellation follows R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017: a call that a pool thread has
taken runs to its end.

[[R-SLIB-SQLITE-0002]]
*R-SLIB-SQLITE-0002* — `+std.sqlite::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly
`+sql+`, `+busy+`, `+locked+`, `+read_only+`, `+cannot_open+`, `+corrupt+`, `+full+`, `+io+`,
`+constraint+`, `+too_big+`, `+mismatch+`, `+range+`, `+misuse+`, `+interrupted+`, `+other+`,
`+invalid_text+`, `+no_statement+`, `+several_statements+`, `+parameter_count+`,
`+column_range+`, `+type_mismatch+` and `+null_value+`, formatted by its variant names.
`+std.sqlite::sqlite_error+` is an error of the module with the fields
`+std.sqlite::error_code code+`, `+i32 native_code+` and `+std.string::string message+`. A
failure of SQLite carries its extended result code as `+native_code+` and its message, of at
most 512 bytes cut at a character boundary, as `+message+`; its code follows the primary
result code (the low byte): 1 is `+sql+` (a syntax error, an unknown table or column, or
another error of SQL), 5 `+busy+`, 6 `+locked+`, 8 `+read_only+`, 9 `+interrupted+`, 10
`+io+`, 11 and 26 `+corrupt+`, 13 `+full+`, 14 `+cannot_open+`, 18 `+too_big+`, 19
`+constraint+`, 20 `+mismatch+`, 21 `+misuse+`, 25 `+range+` and every other `+other+`. A
refusal of the module carries `+native_code+` 0 and a message of the module: `+invalid_text+`
for a zero byte in a path or in SQL text and for a TEXT value that is not UTF-8,
`+no_statement+`, `+several_statements+` and `+parameter_count+` under R-SLIB-SQLITE-0007,
and `+column_range+`, `+type_mismatch+` and `+null_value+` under R-SLIB-SQLITE-0004.

[[R-SLIB-SQLITE-0003]]
*R-SLIB-SQLITE-0003* — `+std.sqlite::value+` is a Move enum with exactly the variants
`+null_value+`, `+integer(i64)+`, `+real(f64)+`, `+text(std.string::string)+` and
`+blob(bytes)+`, one for each storage class of SQLite. `+std.sqlite::value::of_text(str text)
-> std.sqlite::value throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` and
`+std.sqlite::value::of_blob(const u8[] data) -> std.sqlite::value throws
std.alloc::alloc_error+` copy their argument, and
`+std.sqlite::value::of_bool(bool flag) -> std.sqlite::value+` is the integer 1 or 0. A
value is bound as its storage class: text with its length, so that a zero character inside it
stays; an empty text or blob stays empty and is not NULL; a NaN real is stored as NULL by
SQLite. A column of a row is read as the value of its storage class.

[[R-SLIB-SQLITE-0004]]
*R-SLIB-SQLITE-0004* — `+std.sqlite::row+` is a Move struct with the public field
`+array<std.sqlite::value> values+`, the values of one row in the order of the columns. Its
typed reads take a column index:

* `+std.sqlite::row::count(const std.sqlite::row* this) -> usize+` is the number of columns;
* `+std.sqlite::row::at(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> const std.sqlite::value*
throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` borrows a value and refuses an
index past the row with `+column_range+`;
* `+std.sqlite::row::is_null(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> bool throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.sqlite::row::integer(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> i64 throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` reads an INTEGER;
* `+std.sqlite::row::real(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> f64 throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` reads a REAL, or an INTEGER converted to
the nearest real;
* `+std.sqlite::row::boolean(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> bool throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` reads the INTEGER 0 or 1;
* `+std.sqlite::row::text(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> str throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` and
`+std.sqlite::row::blob(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> const u8[] throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` borrow a TEXT or a BLOB of the row;
* `+std.sqlite::row::optional_integer(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> o<i64>
throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`,
`+std.sqlite::row::optional_real(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> o<f64> throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`,
`+std.sqlite::row::optional_text(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> o<str> throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` and
`+std.sqlite::row::optional_blob(const std.sqlite::row* this, usize column) -> o<const u8[]>
throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` read NULL as none and any other
value as the read of the same name.

A typed read of NULL other than an optional one is refused with `+null_value+`, and of another
storage class with `+type_mismatch+`.

[[R-SLIB-SQLITE-0005]]
*R-SLIB-SQLITE-0005* — `+std.sqlite::rows+` is a Move struct with the public fields
`+array<std.string::string> columns+`, the names of the result columns, and
`+array<std.sqlite::row> items+`, every row of a query in the order SQLite returned them.
`+std.sqlite::rows::column(const std.sqlite::rows* this, str name) -> o<usize>+` is the index
of the first column whose name equals name byte for byte. `+std.sqlite::execution+` is a Copy
struct with the public fields `+u64 changes+`, the number of rows that the most recent
INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE completed on the connection changed, and `+i64 last_row_id+`, the
row id of the most recent successful insertion of the connection, both as the statement ended.

[[R-SLIB-SQLITE-0006]]
*R-SLIB-SQLITE-0006* — `+std.sqlite::options+` is a Copy struct with the public fields
`+bool read_only = false+`, `+bool create = true+`, `+bool wal = false+`,
`+bool foreign_keys = true+` and `+u32 busy_timeout_ms = 5000u32+`.
`+std.sqlite::open(str path, std.sqlite::options settings) -> task<std.sqlite::database throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error> throws std.async::start_error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+` opens the database file at path, or a private in-memory database
for `+":memory:"+`. A read-only connection never writes the file; otherwise create creates a
missing file. A statement waits up to busy_timeout_ms milliseconds for a lock that another
connection holds and then fails with `+busy+`. foreign_keys enforces foreign keys on the
connection. wal switches a read-write connection to write-ahead logging, which an in-memory
database does not use; a file that cannot use it is refused with `+other+`. Every connection
keeps the write-ahead log, emptied, and its index file after the last connection to the file
closes, so that a read-only connection can open the file later. `+std.sqlite::database+` is a
Move struct with no public field. Dropping it releases its reference to the connection, which
closes where the last reference to it, a database or a statement, is dropped;
`+std.sqlite::database::close(std.sqlite::database this) -> task<void> throws
std.async::start_error+` releases it on the blocking call pool.

[[R-SLIB-SQLITE-0007]]
*R-SLIB-SQLITE-0007* — `+std.sqlite::statement+` is a Move struct with no public field: a
statement prepared on a connection, which it keeps open.
`+std.sqlite::database::prepare(const std.sqlite::database* this, str sql) ->
task<std.sqlite::statement throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error> throws
std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` prepares the one statement of sql: a text
with no statement is refused with `+no_statement+`, one with a second statement after the
first with `+several_statements+`; comments and white space may follow the statement.
`+std.sqlite::statement::query(const std.sqlite::statement* this, array<std.sqlite::value>
parameters) -> task<std.sqlite::rows throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error>
throws std.async::start_error+` and
`+std.sqlite::statement::execute(const std.sqlite::statement* this, array<std.sqlite::value>
parameters) -> task<std.sqlite::execution throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error,
std.alloc::alloc_error> throws std.async::start_error+` run the statement: they bind the
values to the parameters 1 to N in order, where N is the largest parameter index, and refuse
another number of values with `+parameter_count+`; then they step the statement to its end.
query keeps every row, execute steps through the rows without reading them and returns what
the statement changed. A run resets the statement before it and after it, also after a
failure, so that the statement holds no lock between runs. Runs of one statement take turns:
a run started while another run of the same statement is under way waits for it to end.
`+std.sqlite::database::query(const std.sqlite::database* this, str sql,
array<std.sqlite::value> parameters) -> task<std.sqlite::rows throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error,
std.alloc::alloc_error> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` and
`+std.sqlite::database::execute(const std.sqlite::database* this, str sql,
array<std.sqlite::value> parameters) -> task<std.sqlite::execution throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error> throws std.async::start_error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+` prepare, run and finalize one statement in one call.
`+std.sqlite::database::execute_script(const std.sqlite::database* this, str sql) ->
task<void throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error> throws
std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` runs every statement of sql in order without
parameters and stops at the first failure, which leaves the statements before it applied.

[[R-SLIB-SQLITE-0008]]
*R-SLIB-SQLITE-0008* — `+std.sqlite::begin_mode+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly
`+deferred+`, `+immediate+` and `+exclusive+`.
`+std.sqlite::database::begin(const std.sqlite::database* this, std.sqlite::begin_mode mode) ->
task<void throws std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error> throws
std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+` runs `+BEGIN DEFERRED+`, `+BEGIN IMMEDIATE+`
or `+BEGIN EXCLUSIVE+`,
`+std.sqlite::database::commit(const std.sqlite::database* this) -> task<void throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error> throws std.async::start_error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+` runs `+COMMIT+` and
`+std.sqlite::database::rollback(const std.sqlite::database* this) -> task<void throws
std.sqlite::sqlite_error, std.alloc::alloc_error> throws std.async::start_error,
std.alloc::alloc_error+` runs `+ROLLBACK+`, as execute_script runs them.
`+std.sqlite::database::in_transaction(const std.sqlite::database* this) -> bool+` is true
inside a transaction of the connection and waits for nothing. Every statement prepared on a
database runs on its connection and so inside its transaction. A failed statement inside a
transaction undoes only itself unless SQLite rolls the transaction back, which
in_transaction then reports.

=== 21.28 Metrics: `+std.metrics+`

[[R-SLIB-METRICS-0001]]
*R-SLIB-METRICS-0001* — `+std.metrics+` is a standard module written in R in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile that keeps counters, gauges and histograms in a registry and
renders them in the text exposition format 0.0.4 of Prometheus. A metric is updated through a
handle from any task or thread: counters and gauges with atomic operations, a histogram under
a lock of its own; the registry takes its lock only to register and to render.
`+std.metrics::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+invalid_name+`,
`+invalid_label+`, `+conflict+` and `+invalid_buckets+`, formatted by its variant names, and
`+std.metrics::metrics_error+` is an error of the module with the field
`+std.metrics::error_code code+`.

[[R-SLIB-METRICS-0002]]
*R-SLIB-METRICS-0002* — `+std.metrics::label+` is a Move struct with the public fields
`+std.string::string name+` and `+std.string::string value+`;
`+std.metrics::label::of(str name, str value) -> std.metrics::label throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
copies both. `+std.metrics::registry+` is a Move, Send+Sync struct with no public field that
holds its metrics in the order of their registration;
`+std.metrics::registry::create() -> std.metrics::registry throws std.alloc::alloc_error+` makes an
empty one and
`+std.metrics::registry::share(const std.metrics::registry* this) -> std.metrics::registry+`
another handle of the same registry.

[[R-SLIB-METRICS-0003]]
*R-SLIB-METRICS-0003* — Registration makes a metric of a family, the metrics of one name:

* `+std.metrics::registry::counter(const std.metrics::registry* this, str name, str help, array<std.metrics::label> labels) -> std.metrics::counter throws std.metrics::metrics_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.metrics::registry::gauge(const std.metrics::registry* this, str name, str help, array<std.metrics::label> labels) -> std.metrics::gauge throws std.metrics::metrics_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.metrics::registry::histogram(const std.metrics::registry* this, str name, str help, array<std.metrics::label> labels, array<f64> bounds) -> std.metrics::histogram throws std.metrics::metrics_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

A name shall match `+[a-zA-Z_:][a-zA-Z0-9_:]*+`, otherwise `+invalid_name+`; a label name shall
match `+[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*+`, shall not begin with two underscores, shall not repeat and,
on a histogram, shall not be `+le+`, otherwise `+invalid_label+`. A metric whose family already
has another kind or another help, or a metric with the labels of another of its family, is
`+conflict+`. The bounds of a histogram shall be finite and strictly increasing, otherwise
`+invalid_buckets+`; `+std.metrics::seconds_buckets() -> array<f64> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
gives 0.005, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2.5, 5 and 10.
The handles `+std.metrics::counter+`, `+std.metrics::gauge+` and `+std.metrics::histogram+` are
Move, Send+Sync structs with no public field, each with share that gives another handle of
its metric:

* `+std.metrics::counter::increment(const std.metrics::counter* this) -> void+`,
`+std.metrics::counter::add(const std.metrics::counter* this, u64 amount) -> void+` and
`+std.metrics::counter::value(const std.metrics::counter* this) -> u64+`, adding with the
wrapping of u64;
* `+std.metrics::gauge::set(const std.metrics::gauge* this, i64 value) -> void+`,
`+std.metrics::gauge::add(const std.metrics::gauge* this, i64 amount) -> void+` and
`+std.metrics::gauge::value(const std.metrics::gauge* this) -> i64+`, an integer gauge;
* `+std.metrics::histogram::observe(const std.metrics::histogram* this, f64 value) -> void+`
counts value in the first bucket whose bound is not below it, or in the bucket of +Inf, and
adds it to the sum;
`+std.metrics::histogram::count(const std.metrics::histogram* this) -> u64 throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
and
`+std.metrics::histogram::sum(const std.metrics::histogram* this) -> f64 throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
read them.

[[R-SLIB-METRICS-0004]]
*R-SLIB-METRICS-0004* —
`+std.metrics::registry::render(const std.metrics::registry* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
writes every family in the order of its first registration: a line `+# HELP+` with its name and
help, in which a backslash and a line feed are escaped as `+\\+` and `+\n+`, a line `+# TYPE+`
with its name and `+counter+`, `+gauge+` or `+histogram+`, and then a sample line of each of its
metrics in the order of their registration: the name, the labels in braces as `+name="value"+`
pairs joined by commas, whose values escape a backslash, a quote and a line feed, and the value.
A histogram writes a line `+_bucket+` for each bound with the label `+le+`, one for `++Inf+`, each
with the count of the observations not above it, and the lines `+_sum+` and `+_count+`. A number
is written in its shortest form, in plain decimal notation when its decimal exponent is between
-5 and 20, and as `+NaN+`, `++Inf+` or `+-Inf+`. Every line ends with a line feed. The constant
`+std.metrics::content_type+` is `+text/plain; version=0.0.4; charset=utf-8+`.

=== 21.29 Tracing: `+std.trace+`

[[R-SLIB-TRACE-0001]]
*R-SLIB-TRACE-0001* — `+std.trace+` is a standard module written in R in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile that records spans of work: each has a name, the task that
started it (`+std.async::task_id+`), its start and duration on the monotonic clock, an optional
parent and attributes. A tracer keeps the finished spans of a program up to its capacity and is
shared by its handles from any task or thread. `+std.trace::attribute+` is a Move struct with
the public fields `+std.string::string key+` and `+std.string::string value+`.

[[R-SLIB-TRACE-0002]]
*R-SLIB-TRACE-0002* — `+std.trace::tracer+` is a Move, Send+Sync struct with no public field:

* `+std.trace::tracer::create(usize capacity) -> std.trace::tracer throws std.time::time_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
makes a tracer whose clock starts now, and
`+std.trace::tracer::share(const std.trace::tracer* this) -> std.trace::tracer+` another handle;
* `+std.trace::tracer::start(const std.trace::tracer* this, str name) -> std.trace::span throws std.time::time_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
starts a span without a parent; span identifiers count from 1 in the order of their start;
* `+std.trace::tracer::drain(const std.trace::tracer* this) -> array<std.trace::record>+` takes
the kept records, oldest first;
* `+std.trace::tracer::dropped(const std.trace::tracer* this) -> u64+` is the number of records
that the tracer dropped: at capacity a finished span drops the oldest record.

`+std.trace::span+` is a Move struct with no public field, a span in progress:
`+std.trace::span::child(const std.trace::span* this, str name) -> std.trace::span throws std.time::time_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
starts a span whose parent it is,
`+std.trace::span::id(const std.trace::span* this) -> u64+` is its identifier,
`+std.trace::span::attribute(std.trace::span* this, str key, str value) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
adds an attribute and
`+std.trace::span::finish(std.trace::span this) -> std.trace::record throws std.time::time_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
ends it now, keeps a copy of its record in the tracer and returns it. A span dropped without
finish is not recorded.

[[R-SLIB-TRACE-0003]]
*R-SLIB-TRACE-0003* — `+std.trace::record+` is a Move struct with the public fields
`+u64 id+`, `+o<u64> parent+`, `+u64 task_id+`, `+std.string::string name+`,
`+std.time::duration start+`, measured from the creation of its tracer,
`+std.time::duration duration+` and `+array<std.trace::attribute> attributes+`.
`+std.trace::record::json(const std.trace::record* this) -> std.string::string throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
writes it as one JSON object with the members `+id+`, `+parent+` (null without one), `+task+`,
`+name+`, `+start_us+` and `+duration_us+` in whole microseconds, and `+attributes+` with one
string member per attribute in order; a string escapes a quote, a backslash and every control
character.

=== 21.30 Arenas of bytes: `+std.arena+`

[[R-SLIB-ARENA-0001]]
*R-SLIB-ARENA-0001* — `+std.arena+` is a standard module written in R in the `+allocation+`
profile: an arena of bytes for parsers. It copies bytes into blocks of a fixed size, allocating a
block only when the current one is full, and names stored bytes by a piece that reads them back
later as a view of the arena, so that a parser stores the parts of its messages first and reads
them afterwards. `+std.arena::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly
`+limit_reached+` and `+invalid_piece+`, and `+std.arena::arena_error+` is an error of the
module with the field `+std.arena::error_code code+`.

[[R-SLIB-ARENA-0002]]
*R-SLIB-ARENA-0002* — `+std.arena::piece+` is a Copy struct with the public fields
`+usize block+`, `+usize start+` and `+usize length+`: the place of stored bytes.
`+std.arena::arena+` is a Move struct with no public field;
`+std.arena::arena::create(usize block_size, usize limit) -> std.arena::arena+` makes an empty
arena whose blocks hold block_size bytes (one when block_size is zero) and whose blocks together
hold at most limit bytes. Creating it allocates nothing.

[[R-SLIB-ARENA-0003]]
*R-SLIB-ARENA-0003* —
`+std.arena::arena::store(std.arena::arena* this, const u8[] data) -> std.arena::piece throws std.arena::arena_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
copies data after the bytes of the last block when they fit there, and otherwise into a new
block that holds the larger of block_size and the length of data; a new block that would take
the blocks past the limit is refused with `+limit_reached+` and leaves the arena unchanged.
`+std.arena::arena::store_text(std.arena::arena* this, str text) -> std.arena::piece throws std.arena::arena_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`
stores the bytes of text.

[[R-SLIB-ARENA-0004]]
*R-SLIB-ARENA-0004* —
`+std.arena::arena::bytes(const std.arena::arena* this, std.arena::piece where) -> const u8[] throws std.arena::arena_error+`
borrows the bytes that a piece names from the arena, and
`+std.arena::arena::text(const std.arena::arena* this, std.arena::piece where) -> str throws std.arena::arena_error+`
borrows them as text; a piece outside the stored bytes, or text that is not UTF-8, is
`+invalid_piece+`. The views borrow the arena, which can store more only once they have ended.

[[R-SLIB-ARENA-0005]]
*R-SLIB-ARENA-0005* — `+std.arena::arena::used(const std.arena::arena* this) -> usize+` is the
number of stored bytes and
`+std.arena::arena::reserved(const std.arena::arena* this) -> usize+` the number of bytes of its
blocks. `+std.arena::arena::reset(std.arena::arena* this) -> void+` frees every block; a piece
stored before names no stored bytes afterwards, or bytes stored since.

=== 21.31 Pools of objects: `+std.pool+`

[[R-SLIB-POOL-0001]]
*R-SLIB-POOL-0001* — `+std.pool+` is a standard module written in R in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile: a pool of a fixed number of objects made up front. A lease
lends a free object out and puts it back when it is dropped, so the objects are reused without
allocating. A pool and its leases may be used from any task or thread.

[[R-SLIB-POOL-0002]]
*R-SLIB-POOL-0002* — `+std.pool::pool<T>+` with `+@generic<T: send & unborrowed>+` is a Move,
Send+Sync struct with no public field.
`+std.pool::pool<T>::create(array<T> objects) -> std.pool::pool<T> throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`
makes a pool of the objects, which are then its capacity; every allocation of the pool happens
here. `+std.pool::pool<T>::capacity(const std.pool::pool<T>* this) -> usize+` is that number,
`+std.pool::pool<T>::available(const std.pool::pool<T>* this) -> usize+` the number of objects
not lent out and `+std.pool::pool<T>::share(const std.pool::pool<T>* this) -> std.pool::pool<T>+`
another handle of the same pool.

[[R-SLIB-POOL-0003]]
*R-SLIB-POOL-0003* — `+std.pool::lease<T>+` is a Move struct with no public field that holds a
lent object; dropping it puts the object back with the state the lease left in it.
`+std.pool::pool<T>::acquire(const std.pool::pool<T>* this) -> o<std.pool::lease<T>>+` lends a
free object out, or returns none when every object is lent out; it allocates nothing.
`+std.pool::lease<T>::get(const std.pool::lease<T>* this) -> const T*+` and
`+std.pool::lease<T>::get_mut(std.pool::lease<T>* this) -> T*+` borrow the object from the lease.

=== 21.32 PostgreSQL: `+std.postgres+`

[[R-SLIB-PG-0001]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0001* — `+std.postgres+` is a standard module written in R in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile: a client of PostgreSQL over the frontend/backend protocol 3.0,
built on `+std.net+` and `+std.tls+` without a native provider. A connection talks to the server
over TCP, over TLS on TCP after the SSLRequest of the protocol, or over a Unix-domain socket.
Every operation that talks to the server takes the session of its connection for its whole
exchange and returns its task: the operation copies its text and keeps the connection through a
new strong reference before it returns, so the task borrows nothing from its caller (Core
R-BORROW-0024), and the operations of one connection, from any number of tasks, take turns in
the order in which they take the session. The immediate effects of such an operation are
`+std.async::start_error+` and `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`; its completion errors are
`+std.postgres::pg_error+` and `+std.error::fault+`. A failure of the transport is the fault of
the transport and ends the session, after which every operation of the connection fails with
`+closed+`. The server versions and the parameters of a session are implementation-defined
(R-SLIB-IDB-0020).

[[R-SLIB-PG-0002]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0002* — `+std.postgres::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly
`+server+`, `+connection+`, `+protocol+`, `+authentication+`, `+unsupported+`, `+closed+`,
`+invalid_value+`, `+column_range+`, `+null_value+`, `+missing_column+` and
`+migration_mismatch+`, and `+std.postgres::pg_error+` is an error with the public fields `+std.postgres::error_code code+`, `+std.string::string sqlstate+`,
`+std.string::string message+`, `+std.string::string detail+` and `+std.string::string hint+`.
An ErrorResponse of the server is `+server+` with the SQLSTATE, message, detail and hint that it
carries, which are empty when absent; `+connection+` is a server that cannot be reached or
refuses TLS, `+protocol+` a message of the server that does not follow the protocol, which ends
the session, `+authentication+` a SCRAM exchange that does not verify, `+unsupported+` an
authentication method or mechanism that this client lacks, `+closed+` an operation of a
connection whose session ended, `+invalid_value+`, `+column_range+` and `+null_value+` the
refusals of R-SLIB-PG-0003, R-SLIB-PG-0004 and R-SLIB-PG-0013 to R-SLIB-PG-0015,
`+missing_column+` a column that R-SLIB-PG-0015 looks for and does not find, and
`+migration_mismatch+` the refusal of R-SLIB-PG-0016; both name the column or the version in
detail.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0003]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0003* — `+std.postgres::value+` is a Move tagged enum with exactly
`+null_value+`, `+boolean(bool)+`, `+integer(i64)+`, `+real(f64)+`,
`+text(std.string::string)+` and `+binary(bytes)+`, with the constructors

* `+std.postgres::value::of_text(str text) -> std.postgres::value throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::value::of_bytes(const u8[] data) -> std.postgres::value throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

A parameter is sent in the text format with no type, so the server gives it the type that the
statement needs: `+null_value+` as NULL, a truth value as `+t+` or `+f+`, an integer in decimal,
a real as its shortest decimal form or `+NaN+`, `+Infinity+` and `+-Infinity+`, text as its
bytes and binary as the text form of bytea, `+\x+` and two hexadecimal digits per byte. A text
holding a zero byte is refused with `+invalid_value+`.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0004]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0004* — `+std.postgres::column+` is a Move struct with the public fields
`+std.string::string name+` and `+u32 type_oid+`, `+std.postgres::row+` a Move struct with the
public field `+array<std.postgres::value> values+`, and `+std.postgres::rows+` a Move struct with
the public fields `+array<std.postgres::column> columns+`, `+array<std.postgres::row> items+` and
`+u64 affected+`, the number at the end of the command tag of the statement: the rows that it
changed or returned, zero when the tag has none. Columns arrive in the text format and become
values by the type of their column: bool is a truth value, int2, int4, int8 and oid an integer,
float4 and float8 a real, bytea binary, NULL `+null_value+` and every other type text, such as
numeric, a date or JSON in their text forms. The operations of a row are

* `+std.postgres::row::count(const std.postgres::row* this) -> usize+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::at(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> const std.postgres::value* throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::is_null(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> bool throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::integer(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> i64 throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::real(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> f64 throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::boolean(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> bool throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::text(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> str throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::bytes(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> const u8[] throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

An index past the last column is `+column_range+`, a NULL read as another value `+null_value+`
and a value of another kind `+invalid_value+`; real also reads an integer. text and bytes return
views of the row.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0005]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0005* — `+std.postgres::options+` is a Move struct with the public fields, and their
defaults, `+std.string::string host+` (127.0.0.1), `+u16 port+` (5432),
`+std.string::string socket+` (empty), `+std.string::string user+` (postgres),
`+std.string::string password+`, `+std.string::string database+` and
`+std.string::string application_name+` (empty). A nonempty socket names the socket file of the
server, such as `+/tmp/.s.PGSQL.5432+`, and host and port are not used; an empty database
connects to the database of the user's name. `+std.postgres::connection+` is a Move, Send+Sync
handle of a session.

* `+std.postgres::options::from_environment() -> std.postgres::options throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connect(std.postgres::options settings) -> task<std.postgres::connection throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connect_tls(std.postgres::options settings, arc std.tls::config tls) -> task<std.postgres::connection throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::share(const std.postgres::connection* this) -> std.postgres::connection+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::close(std.postgres::connection this) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error+`.

from_environment reads the variables of libpq: PGHOST, a directory of the socket when it starts
with `+/+`, PGPORT, PGUSER, PGPASSWORD, PGDATABASE and PGAPPNAME; a variable that is not set
keeps its default, and a PGPORT that is no port is `+invalid_value+`. connect opens the
transport, sends the startup message with the user, the database, the application name, the
client encoding UTF8 and the DateStyle `+ISO, MDY+`, and answers the authentication the server asks for: none, the cleartext
password, md5, or SASL with SCRAM-SHA-256 (RFC 5802, RFC 7677) without channel binding, whose
server signature it verifies; the password is used as its UTF-8 bytes, without the SASLprep
normalization of RFC 4013. A server that refuses the user is a `+server+` error, such as
SQLSTATE 28P01 for a wrong password. connect_tls asks for TLS first and connects with tls to the
host name, which the certificate of the server shall name; a server that refuses TLS, a failed
handshake and a socket path are `+connection+`. share returns another handle of the same
connection. close ends the session with Terminate; the other handles then fail with `+closed+`.
The server ends the session as soon as it reads Terminate and may close the transport first, so
close reports no failure of the transport. A connection whose last handle is dropped closes its
transport without Terminate.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0006]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0006* — The statements of a connection:

* `+std.postgres::connection::query(const std.postgres::connection* this, str sql, array<std.postgres::value> parameters) -> task<std.postgres::rows throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::execute(const std.postgres::connection* this, str sql, array<std.postgres::value> parameters) -> task<u64 throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::execute_script(const std.postgres::connection* this, str sql) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

query runs one statement with the extended protocol, with the values of its parameters `+$1+`
to `+$N+`, and keeps every row of its result; execute does the same and returns the affected
number. execute_script runs the statements of sql in order with the simple query protocol,
without parameters, and stops at the first failure, which leaves the statements before it
applied outside a transaction. An error of the server is thrown once the server is ready for the
next statement, so the connection stays usable; notifications that arrive during an exchange are
kept for wait_notification (R-SLIB-PG-0009). A COPY statement run by these operations moves no
data: COPY FROM STDIN fails with the error of the server, SQLSTATE 57014, and the data of COPY TO
STDOUT is discarded; copy_in and copy_out of R-SLIB-PG-0010 move data. An ErrorResponse that the
server sends outside an exchange, such as when it shuts down, is thrown by the operation that
reads it and ends the session.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0007]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0007* — `+std.postgres::statement+` is a Move struct with no public field: a
statement prepared on the server under a name of its connection.

* `+std.postgres::connection::prepare(const std.postgres::connection* this, str sql) -> task<std.postgres::statement throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::statement::query(const std.postgres::statement* this, array<std.postgres::value> parameters) -> task<std.postgres::rows throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::statement::execute(const std.postgres::statement* this, array<std.postgres::value> parameters) -> task<u64 throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::statement::close(std.postgres::statement this) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error+`.

A statement runs like query and execute of R-SLIB-PG-0006 without being parsed again. close
closes it on the server; a statement dropped without close stays prepared until its connection
ends.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0008]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0008* — `+std.postgres::transaction_status+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly
`+idle+`, `+in_transaction+` and `+failed+`.

* `+std.postgres::connection::begin(const std.postgres::connection* this) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::commit(const std.postgres::connection* this) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::rollback(const std.postgres::connection* this) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::transaction_status(const std.postgres::connection* this) -> std.postgres::transaction_status+`.

begin, commit and rollback run BEGIN, COMMIT and ROLLBACK. transaction_status returns the status
that the server reported at the end of the last exchange of the connection, also of one that
failed: `+failed+` after an error inside a transaction, until rollback ends it.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0009]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0009* — `+std.postgres::notification+` is a Move struct with the public fields
`+u32 process_id+`, `+std.string::string channel+` and `+std.string::string payload+`.

* `+std.postgres::connection::listen(const std.postgres::connection* this, str channel) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::unlisten(const std.postgres::connection* this, str channel) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::notify(const std.postgres::connection* this, str channel, str payload) -> task<u64 throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::wait_notification(const std.postgres::connection* this) -> task<std.postgres::notification throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error+`.

listen and unlisten run LISTEN and UNLISTEN with the channel as a quoted identifier, and notify
runs `+pg_notify+`. wait_notification returns the oldest notification that the connection has
received and not returned, and otherwise waits for the next one; the deadline of an enclosing
deadline block bounds the wait (Core R-STMT-0019).

[[R-SLIB-PG-0010]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0010* — COPY of a connection:

* `+std.postgres::connection::copy_in(const std.postgres::connection* this, str sql, bytes data) -> task<u64 throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::connection::copy_out(const std.postgres::connection* this, str sql) -> task<bytes throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

copy_in runs a `+COPY ... FROM STDIN+` statement, sends data in the format that it names and
returns the number of rows copied; copy_out runs a `+COPY ... TO STDOUT+` statement and returns
its data. A statement of the other direction is `+protocol+`, and an error of the server, such as
a malformed row, is thrown once the server is ready again.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0011]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0011* — `+std.postgres::canceller+` is a Move struct with no public field that holds
the address of a server and the key of one of its sessions.
