* `+std.postgres::connection::canceller(const std.postgres::connection* this) -> std.postgres::canceller throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::cancel(std.postgres::canceller target) -> task<void throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error+`.

cancel sends the cancel request of the protocol over a connection of its own, without TLS, and
does not wait for its effect: the statement that the session runs then fails with SQLSTATE
57014, and a request that arrives between statements does nothing. A failure to open that
connection or to send the request is reported; the server then closes the connection, which is
no failure.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0012]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0012* —
`+std.postgres::connect_pool(std.postgres::options settings, usize size) -> task<std.pool::pool<std.postgres::connection> throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error+`
connects size connections with the settings, one after another, and returns them as a pool of
R-SLIB-POOL-0001, whose leases lend them to tasks.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0013]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0013* — Typed reads of a column:

* `+std.postgres::row::time(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> std.time::system_time throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::date(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> std.time::utc_datetime throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::uuid(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> std.uuid::uuid throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::json(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> std.json::value throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::row::text_array(const std.postgres::row* this, usize index) -> array<o<std.string::string>> throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

Each reads the text of the column as text of R-SLIB-PG-0004 does, with its refusals. time reads
the ISO form of a timestamptz, `+YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS+` with up to nine fractional digits and the
offset `+±HH+`, `+±HH:MM+` or `+±HH:MM:SS+` that the server writes in the time zone of the
session, and returns its instant; a timestamp without an offset is read as UTC. date reads the
ISO form `+YYYY-MM-DD+` of a date as midnight of that day. uuid reads the text form of a uuid,
json the text of a json or jsonb column as a document of R-SLIB-JSON-0001, and text_array a
one-dimensional array in the text form of the server: an element in double quotes loses its
quotes and the backslashes that escape its characters, an unquoted NULL in any case is none,
the bytes of every other element are kept as UTF-8 text, and a leading `+[lower:upper]=+` is
skipped. A text that is not of its form, such as `+infinity+`, a date BC or after the year 9999, a
DateStyle other than ISO or an element that is not UTF-8, is `+invalid_value+`, and an array of
more than one dimension `+unsupported+`.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0014]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0014* — Typed parameters:

* `+std.postgres::value::of_time(std.time::system_time at) -> std.postgres::value throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::value::of_uuid(const std.uuid::uuid* id) -> std.postgres::value throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::value::of_json(const std.json::value* document) -> std.postgres::value throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::value::of_array(const array<std.postgres::value>* items) -> std.postgres::value throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

Each returns text that the server converts to the type of its place, as for every parameter of
R-SLIB-PG-0003. of_time writes the instant in RFC 3339 in UTC with six fractional digits, the
precision of the server, and refuses an instant outside the years 0 to 9999 with
`+invalid_value+`; of_uuid writes the hyphenated lower-case form, and of_json the document as
`+std.json::stringify+` does, a document it cannot write being `+invalid_value+`. of_array
writes a one-dimensional array literal: the items in order between braces, separated by commas,
`+null_value+` as NULL and every other item as its parameter text in double quotes, with double
quotes and backslashes escaped by a backslash.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0015]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0015* — Rows as structs and structs as parameters:

* `+std.postgres::rows::column_index(const std.postgres::rows* this, str name) -> usize throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::rows::decode<T: json_decode>(const std.postgres::rows* this, usize at) -> T throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::rows::decode_all<T: json_decode>(const std.postgres::rows* this) -> array<T> throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::insert_statement<T: json_encode>(str table) -> std.string::string throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::update_statement<T: json_encode>(str table, str key) -> std.string::string throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.postgres::parameters_of<T: json_encode>(const T* item) -> array<std.postgres::value> throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

column_index returns the index of the first column of the name, and `+missing_column+` with the
name when there is none. decode reads the row at `+at+`, an index past the last row being
`+column_range+`, and decode_all every row in order, through the JSON Schema of T
(R-SLIB-JSON-0002): each property takes the first column of its JSON name. A missing column of a
required field that is not `+o<T>+` is `+missing_column+`, and a NULL in it `+null_value+`,
both with the name; otherwise a missing column, or a NULL of a field that is not `+o<T>+`, leaves
the field out, and a NULL of an `+o<T>+` field is none. A value converts by its column and the
schema of its field: a truth value is a boolean; an integer is a number, or its decimal text for
a string field; a real is a number, a real that is not finite being `+invalid_value+`; binary is
Base64 for a string field and otherwise an array of byte numbers; a timestamp or timestamptz is
its instant as read by time of R-SLIB-PG-0013, written in RFC 3339 with six fractional digits
for a string field, as whole seconds since the epoch for an integer field and as seconds with
their fraction for a number field; a json or jsonb column is its document unless the field is a
string; an array of bool, bytea, int2, int4, int8, float4, float8, numeric, text, varchar, date,
timestamp, timestamptz, uuid, json or jsonb for an array field is its elements as text_array
reads them, each converted as text, and NULL as null; every other text is a JSON number for an
integer or number field, a boolean for a boolean field when it is `+t+`, `+true+`, `+f+` or
`+false+`, and a string otherwise. The document is then read with `+std.json::unmarshal+`, whose
rules decide the defaults; a document that does not read as T is `+invalid_value+`, and a T
without a schema `+unsupported+`.

insert_statement returns `+INSERT INTO table ("f1", ..., "fn") VALUES ($1, ..., $n)+` and
update_statement `+UPDATE table SET "f" = $i, ... WHERE "key" = $k+` for the JSON names of the
properties of the schema of T in their order, each name a quoted identifier with its double
quotes doubled, the parameter of the i-th property numbered i and the key left out of the SET
list; table is written as it is given, and a key that names no property is `+missing_column+`
with the key. parameters_of writes item with `+std.json::marshal+` and returns one parameter per
property in that order: an absent value or null is `+null_value+`, a boolean is a truth value,
a string or a number is its text, an array of an array field is the literal of of_array of its
elements converted the same way, and every other value is its JSON text.

[[R-SLIB-PG-0016]]
*R-SLIB-PG-0016* — `+std.postgres::migration+` is a Move struct with the public fields
`+u64 version+`, `+std.string::string name+` and `+std.string::string sql+`.

* `+std.postgres::migrate(const std.postgres::connection* conn, array<std.postgres::migration> steps) -> task<u64 throws std.postgres::pg_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error+`.

migrate applies the steps that the database has not applied and returns their number. It takes
the session-level advisory lock 125806466509938 of the server, so that migrations of several
processes run one after another, creates the table `+r_schema_migrations+` with the columns
version (bigint, the primary key), name, checksum (text) and applied_at (timestamptz) when it is
absent, and reads the applied versions. Versions that do not ascend strictly in steps, an
applied version that steps lack, an applied version whose sql has another checksum, the
lower-case hexadecimal SHA-256 of its bytes, and a step not applied whose version is below the
highest applied one are `+migration_mismatch+` with `+version N+` in detail, before any step
runs. Each pending step then runs in ascending order in a transaction of its own: BEGIN, its sql
as a script of R-SLIB-PG-0006, the row of its version, name and checksum, and COMMIT; an error
of the server rolls the step back and is thrown, the steps before it staying applied. The lock
is released after the last step and after every `+pg_error+`; a fault ends the session and with
it the lock. The sql of a step therefore cannot end the transaction itself or run a statement
that a transaction block refuses.

=== 21.33 JSON Web Tokens: `+std.jwt+`

[[R-SLIB-JWT-0001]]
*R-SLIB-JWT-0001* — `+std.jwt+` is a standard module written in R in the `+hosted+` profile
that makes and verifies JSON Web Signatures in the compact serialization (RFC 7515) whose
payload is a JSON Web Token (RFC 7519), over `+std.crypto+`, `+std.hash+` and `+std.json+`, and
reads and writes JSON Web Keys (RFC 7517, RFC 7518). `+std.jwt::error_code+` is a Copy
fieldless enum with exactly `+malformed+`, `+unsupported_algorithm+`, `+key_mismatch+`,
`+no_key+`, `+invalid_signature+`, `+expired+`, `+not_yet_valid+`, `+invalid_issuer+`,
`+invalid_audience+`, `+missing_claim+`, `+invalid_claim+` and `+invalid_key+`, formatted by its
variant names, and `+std.jwt::jwt_error+` is an error of the module with the field
`+std.jwt::error_code code+`. Signers, verifiers and key sets are Send and Sync.

[[R-SLIB-JWT-0002]]
*R-SLIB-JWT-0002* — `+std.jwt::algorithm+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+hs256+`,
`+hs384+`, `+hs512+`, `+rs256+`, `+rs384+`, `+rs512+`, `+ps256+`, `+ps384+`, `+ps512+`,
`+es256+`, `+es384+` and `+eddsa+`: HMAC with SHA-2, RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 and RSASSA-PSS of
R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0010, ECDSA on P-256 and P-384 of R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0009 (RFC 7518 section 3) and
Ed25519 (RFC 8037). Its methods are
`+std.jwt::algorithm::name(std.jwt::algorithm this) -> str+`, the `+alg+` name such as
`+ES256+` or `+EdDSA+`, and
`+std.jwt::algorithm::parse(str name) -> std.jwt::algorithm throws std.jwt::jwt_error+`, which
reports `+none+` and every other name as `+unsupported_algorithm+`.

[[R-SLIB-JWT-0003]]
*R-SLIB-JWT-0003* — `+std.jwt::signer+` is the Move key that signs tokens with one algorithm
and names a key identifier in them:

* `+std.jwt::signer::hmac(std.jwt::algorithm alg, const u8[] secret) -> std.jwt::signer throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::signer::with_private_key(std.jwt::algorithm alg, std.crypto::private_key key) -> std.jwt::signer throws std.jwt::jwt_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::signer::set_key_id(std.jwt::signer* this, str kid) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+hmac+` takes an HS algorithm and a secret of at least the hash length (RFC 7518 section
3.2); `+with_private_key+` takes an RSA key for RS and PS algorithms, an ECDSA key of the
curve of ES256 or ES384, or an Ed25519 key for EdDSA. Any other algorithm, key or shorter
secret is `+key_mismatch+`. An empty key identifier names none.

[[R-SLIB-JWT-0004]]
*R-SLIB-JWT-0004* — `+std.jwt::verifier+` is the Move key that verifies tokens, limited to
one algorithm or, read from a JWK without `+alg+`, to the algorithms its key fits, and
`+std.jwt::key_set+` is the Move set of verifiers that a verification chooses from:

* `+std.jwt::verifier::hmac(std.jwt::algorithm alg, const u8[] secret) -> std.jwt::verifier throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::verifier::with_public_key(std.jwt::algorithm alg, std.crypto::public_key key) -> std.jwt::verifier throws std.jwt::jwt_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::verifier::set_key_id(std.jwt::verifier* this, str kid) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::key_set::create() -> std.jwt::key_set+`;
* `+std.jwt::key_set::add(std.jwt::key_set* this, std.jwt::verifier key) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::key_set::size(const std.jwt::key_set* this) -> usize+`.

The keys a verifier accepts are those of R-SLIB-JWT-0003, public instead of private.

[[R-SLIB-JWT-0005]]
*R-SLIB-JWT-0005* — Tokens are made and their header is read by:

* `+std.jwt::sign(const std.jwt::signer* key, const std.json::value* claims) -> std.string::string throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::sign_claims<T: json_encode>(const std.jwt::signer* key, const T* claims) -> std.string::string throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::header(str token) -> std.json::value throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+sign+` writes the claims, which shall be a JSON object, and `+sign_claims+` writes what
`+std.json::marshal+` writes for the claims, which shall be an object; anything else is
`+malformed+`. The protected header is `+{"alg":A,"typ":"JWT"}+`, with `+"kid"+` last when the
signer names one; header and payload are base64url without padding, and the signature is the
HMAC, the RSA signature, r and s of R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0009 or the Ed25519 signature of their
joined text. `+header+` returns the header of a token, which shall be a JSON object, without
verifying the token; a text that is not three base64url parts joined by two dots is
`+malformed+`.

[[R-SLIB-JWT-0006]]
*R-SLIB-JWT-0006* — `+std.jwt::validation+` is the Move struct of the checks of the registered
claims with the fields `+std.string::string issuer+`, `+array<std.string::string> audiences+`,
`+u64 leeway+` (seconds, 60 by default) and `+bool require_expiration+` (true by default):

* `+std.jwt::validation::create() -> std.jwt::validation+`;
* `+std.jwt::validation::set_issuer(std.jwt::validation* this, str issuer) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::validation::add_audience(std.jwt::validation* this, str audience) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::verify(const std.jwt::key_set* keys, str token, const std.jwt::validation* rules, std.time::system_time now) -> std.json::value throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::verify_claims<T: json_decode>(const std.jwt::key_set* keys, str token, const std.jwt::validation* rules, std.time::system_time now) -> T throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.json::error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

Verification reads the header: an `+alg+` of R-SLIB-JWT-0002 is required, and a `+crit+`
member is `+unsupported_algorithm+`. The candidates are the keys of the set whose algorithm
admits `+alg+`, whose key fits it and, when the header names a `+kid+`, whose key identifier is
that one; no candidate is `+no_key+`, and candidates of which none verifies the signature are
`+invalid_signature+`. The payload shall be a JSON object. With `+now+` in whole seconds and the
leeway: an `+exp+` with now ≥ exp + leeway is `+expired+`; an absent `+exp+` is
`+missing_claim+` when the expiration is required; an `+nbf+` with now + leeway < nbf is
`+not_yet_valid+`; a NumericDate that is not a number is `+invalid_claim+`; with an issuer set,
an `+iss+` that is not that string is `+invalid_issuer+`; with audiences, an `+aud+` string or
array of strings that names none of them is `+invalid_audience+`. `+verify+` returns the
claims; `+verify_claims+` reads them as a T with `+std.json::unmarshal+`.

[[R-SLIB-JWT-0007]]
*R-SLIB-JWT-0007* — JSON Web Keys are read and written by:

* `+std.jwt::key_set::from_jwks(const u8[] text) -> std.jwt::key_set throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.jwt::jwk(const std.crypto::public_key* key, std.jwt::algorithm alg, str kid) -> std.json::value throws std.jwt::jwt_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+from_jwks+` reads a JWK Set `+{"keys": [...]}+` or one JWK: `+RSA+` keys with `+n+` and `+e+`,
`+EC+` keys of the curves `+P-256+` and `+P-384+` with `+x+` and `+y+`, `+OKP+` keys of the curve
`+Ed25519+` with `+x+`, and `+oct+` keys with `+k+`, in base64url, with their `+kid+` and `+alg+`.
A key of another type or curve, a key whose `+use+` is not `+sig+` and a key with an `+alg+`
outside R-SLIB-JWT-0002 are skipped; a key of a known type without a valid key is
`+invalid_key+`. `+jwk+` returns the public JWK of a key that fits the algorithm with `+kty+`,
`+crv+` for EC and OKP keys, the key members, `+alg+`, `+use+` `+sig+` and `+kid+` when it is not
empty; a key that does not fit is `+key_mismatch+`.

=== 21.34 OAuth 2.0 clients: `+std.oauth2+`

[[R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0001]]
*R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0001* — `+std.oauth2+` is a standard module written in R in the
`+hosted-native-async+` profile for programs that call APIs protected by OAuth 2.0 (RFC 6749):
requests to a token endpoint over `+std.http+`, the JWT bearer grant of RFC 7523 for service
accounts over `+std.jwt+`, and a cache of the access token that tasks share.
`+std.oauth2::error_code+` is a Copy fieldless enum with exactly `+invalid_request+`,
`+invalid_response+`, `+endpoint_error+` and `+invalid_key_file+`, formatted by its variant
names, and `+std.oauth2::oauth2_error+` is an error of the module with the fields
`+std.oauth2::error_code code+`, `+u16 status+`, the status of the endpoint's answer or zero,
and `+std.string::string error+` and `+std.string::string description+`, the `+error+` and
`+error_description+` of an error answer (RFC 6749 section 5.2) or empty.

[[R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0002]]
*R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0002* — `+std.oauth2::token+` is the Move struct of an access token with the
fields `+std.string::string access_token+`, `+std.string::string token_type+`,
`+o<std.time::system_time> expires_at+`, `+std.string::string refresh_token+` and
`+std.string::string scope+`, the last two empty when the endpoint gave none.

[[R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0003]]
*R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0003* — `+std.oauth2::code_grant+` is the Move struct of an authorization code
with the fields `+std.string::string code+`, `+std.string::string redirect_uri+` and
`+std.string::string verifier+` (RFC 7636), empty strings for none. `+std.oauth2::grant+` is the
Move enum with exactly `+client_credentials+`, `+refresh_token(std.string::string)+`,
`+authorization_code(std.oauth2::code_grant)+` and `+jwt_bearer(std.string::string)+`, the
assertion. `+std.oauth2::token_request+` is the Move struct of a request with the fields
`+std.string::string endpoint+`, `+std.string::string client_id+`,
`+std.secret::buffer client_secret+`, `+std.string::string scope+` and
`+std.oauth2::grant kind+`:

* `+std.oauth2::token_request::create(str endpoint, std.oauth2::grant kind) -> std.oauth2::token_request throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.oauth2::token_request::set_client(std.oauth2::token_request* this, str client_id, const u8[] secret) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.oauth2::token_request::set_scope(std.oauth2::token_request* this, str scope) -> void throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

A client identifier with a secret authenticates with HTTP Basic over the form-encoded
identifier and secret (RFC 6749 section 2.3.1); an identifier without a secret is sent as
`+client_id+` in the body, as a public client does; an empty identifier sends neither. An empty
scope is left out.

[[R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0004]]
*R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0004* —
`+@scoped async std.oauth2::request_token(std.http::client* http, const std.oauth2::token_request* request, std.time::system_time now) -> std.oauth2::token throws std.oauth2::oauth2_error, std.http::http_error, std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault+`
sends the request with POST and an `+application/x-www-form-urlencoded+` body that holds
`+grant_type+` and the members of its grant (RFC 6749 sections 4.1.3, 4.4.2 and 6, RFC 7523
section 2.1) and reads the answer. A 200 answer that is a JSON object with string
`+access_token+` and `+token_type+` gives the token, whose `+expires_at+` is `+now+` plus
`+expires_in+` when that is a whole number; another 200 answer is `+invalid_response+`. Any other
status is `+endpoint_error+` with the `+error+` and `+error_description+` of a JSON error answer,
and `+invalid_response+` with the status for anything else. A secret that is not UTF-8 is
`+invalid_request+`.

[[R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0005]]
*R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0005* — `+std.oauth2::service_account+` is the Move service account of a key
file, with the fields `+std.string::string client_email+` and `+std.string::string token_uri+`
and a protected RS256 signer of R-SLIB-JWT-0003 that names the key identifier of the file:

* `+std.oauth2::service_account::from_json(const u8[] text) -> std.oauth2::service_account throws std.oauth2::oauth2_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.oauth2::service_account::assertion(const std.oauth2::service_account* this, str scope, str subject, std.time::system_time now) -> std.string::string throws std.oauth2::oauth2_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.oauth2::service_account::request_for(const std.oauth2::service_account* this, str scope, str subject, std.time::system_time now) -> std.oauth2::token_request throws std.oauth2::oauth2_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+from_json+` reads the JSON key file of Google Cloud: `+type+` `+service_account+` when
present, `+client_email+`, `+private_key+` with an RSA key in PEM (R-SLIB-CRYPTO-0012),
`+private_key_id+`, and `+token_uri+`, which defaults to `+https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token+`;
any other file is `+invalid_key_file+`. `+assertion+` signs a JWT with the claims `+iss+` the
account, `+scope+` when not empty, `+aud+` the token endpoint, `+sub+` when not empty,
`+iat+` `+now+` and `+exp+` one hour later (RFC 7523 section 3). `+request_for+` returns the
request of the grant `+jwt_bearer+` with such an assertion to the token endpoint.

[[R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0006]]
*R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0006* — `+std.oauth2::token_source+` is the Move, Send and Sync handle of an
access token that the tasks of a program share; it owns an HTTP client and the source of its
tokens:

* `+std.oauth2::token_source::from_request(std.http::client http, std.oauth2::token_request request) -> std.oauth2::token_source throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.oauth2::token_source::from_service_account(std.http::client http, std.oauth2::service_account account, str scope, str subject) -> std.oauth2::token_source throws std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.oauth2::token_source::access_token(const std.oauth2::token_source* this) -> task<std.string::string throws std.oauth2::oauth2_error, std.http::http_error, std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`;
* `+std.oauth2::token_source::renew(const std.oauth2::token_source* this) -> task<std.string::string throws std.oauth2::oauth2_error, std.http::http_error, std.tls::tls_error, std.error::fault> throws std.async::start_error, std.alloc::alloc_error+`.

`+access_token+` returns the cached token while its expiry is more than 60 seconds away or it
has none, and otherwise gets a new one; `+renew+` always gets a new one, as after an API
refused the cached token. Requests of one source take turns, so tasks that need a token
together send one request. A source of a request sends it again, with the refresh token that
the endpoint returned last in place of its grant, so a code is exchanged once and a rotated
refresh token is followed; a source of a service account signs a new assertion for its scope
and subject each time.

== Annex A — Normative public API inventory

[[R-SLIB-API-A001]]
*R-SLIB-API-A001* — Each row below incorporates every exact operation and public type
named by its first-column scope. Cited rules identify those items; an explicit qualifier
such as “synchronous items”, “public types” or “async operations only” restricts the row
to that stated subset of the cited rules. Those signatures and closed outcome variants
are the public API items for that row. The remaining columns apply to every incorporated
item unless its cited rule states a narrower property. “Transactional” means externally
observable target state and caller-owned inputs are unchanged on the indicated failure.

=== A.1 Synchronous and ownership APIs

[cols="2,3,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Module and exact items |Ownership and capabilities |Allocation and recoverable failure |Panic and thread behavior |C17/native basis

|`+core+`: R-LIB-0001, R-LIB-0011, R-LIB-0020 (`+core::hash+` and
`+core::key_equal+`), R-LIB-0024 (static reflection) and Core R-ARRAY-0007,
R-UNSAFE-0007..0008, R-REFL-0001..0004
|Copy scalars/slices; atomic objects follow Core Send/Sync
|No implicit allocation; checked outcomes fixed by R-LIB-0001
|Core arithmetic, bounds and exact unsafe contracts; atomics are thread-safe
|C17 arithmetic, `<stdint.h>`, `<stdatomic.h>` or conforming lock fallback, plus bounded
RFC 3629 byte validation, raw-pointer/owner transfer and volatile/intrinsic lowering
without allocation

|`+std.alloc+`: R-LIB-0004, R-SLIB-ALLOC-0001..0004
|Move owner or `+array<u8>+`; checked `+new_error<T>+` returns the staged T
|Allocates on nonzero request; exact `+alloc_error+`/`+new_error<T>+`
|No library panic; allocator synchronization supports every declared capability
|C allocator or target allocator with C-compatible alignment

|`+std.arc+`, `+std.rc+`: R-LIB-0012
|Exact owner/weak/raw-token families; `+into_raw+` is safe and `+from_raw+` is unsafe; structural capabilities
|Owner construction may allocate through language `+new+`; listed observations do not
|Reference overflow and unsafe-token contracts follow Core
|C17 atomics for arc; ordinary counts for thread-local rc; exact unsafe raw-token contract

|`+std.thread+`: R-LIB-0005, R-LIB-0010, R-LIB-0013, R-LIB-0017
|Move join handles; thread identity independently retained
|Spawn returns `+thread_error+`; failed creation preserves staged named operands; observation allocates only where stated
|Join/park/sleep may block; panic captured by declared strategy
|C threads or a target thread runtime

|`+std.sync+` synchronous items: R-LIB-0006, R-LIB-0014..R-LIB-0017
|Move resources and guards; structural table in R-LIB-0017
|Constructors and closed outcomes exactly as cited
|Blocking operations establish Core synchronization edges; poison is advisory
|C atomics plus target synchronization primitives

|`+std.array+`, `+std.list+`, `+std.dict+`: R-LIB-0018..R-LIB-0023
|Move containers; element/key/value capabilities are structural
|Fallible growth returns staged values and preserves target
|Bounds use o; borrow conflicts are compile-time; destruction panic follows Core
|C allocation and byte movement behind typed R drop/borrow rules

|`+std.error+`: R-SLIB-ERR-0001..0004
|Copy common error/domain and Copy typed conversion sources; diagnostic returns Move string
|Conversions and name do not allocate; diagnostic returns `+alloc_error+`
|No panic; Send+Sync
|Stable 0x0000/0x0100 family bands; captured native status is preserved exactly; typed payload stays in its source

|`+std.bytes+`: R-SLIB-BYTES-0001..0004, R-SLIB-BYTES-0009
|Call-bounded slices and Move `+bytes+`/`+array<u8>+` owners; no retained state
|Owned construction/append may allocate transactionally; search and comparison do not allocate
|No panic; capability follows slice origins
|C allocation/movement and bounded byte operations

|`+std.hash+`: R-SLIB-BYTES-0005..0008, R-SLIB-BYTES-0010..0011
|Non-owning `+const u8[]+` input and Copy digest values; Copy states of the R part retain no input
|No allocation, copying or moving of source data
|No panic; reentrant and thread-safe; HMAC erases its padded keys
|Independent bounded unsigned loops implementing CRC-32/ISO-HDLC, RFC 1321 MD5 and FIPS PUB 180-4; the R part is translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.utf8+`: R-SLIB-UTF8-0001..0002
|One canonical `+const u8[]+` parameter; successful validated `+str+` preserves the original source origin
|No allocation; contextual byte views copy no bytes
|No panic; no hidden retain; source freezing follows the returned borrow
|RFC 3629 validation over an explicit pointer-and-length view

|`+std.bits+`: R-SLIB-BITS-0001..0004
|Copy reader and errors; input slice is borrowed only for one call
|No allocation; failed read preserves all reader fields
|No panic; reentrant; exact invalid-width-before-end priority
|Bounded C17 unsigned shifts and masks; the 64-bit case never shifts by 64

|`+std.secret+`: R-SLIB-SECRET-0001..0004
|Move `+buffer+` owner, Send and Sync; views are call-bounded and carry the buffer origin
|`+with_length+` may allocate transactionally; adoption, views, erasure and comparison do not allocate
|No panic; drop erases the whole allocation through volatile stores before release
|C allocation/movement, volatile byte stores and a branch-free accumulated comparison

|`+std.random+`: R-SLIB-RANDOM-0001..0002
|Call-bounded mutable byte view for `+fill+`; Copy generator values
|No allocation; `+fill+` cannot fail and does not wait for entropy
|Only Core arithmetic panics for a zero bound or an empty range; Send+Sync
|The operating-system generator (`+arc4random_buf+` on Darwin); the R part is translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.test+`: R-SLIB-TEST-0001..0003
|Move failure and runner; values that assertions take by value; call-bounded texts
|A failing assertion or a runner message allocates its text and reports `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`; the allocation operations cannot fail
|No panic of its own; the allocation count is shared by all threads
|The hosted allocator of the runtime; the R part is translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.string+`: R-SLIB-STRING-0001..0004
|Move string, Send+Sync; returned borrows carry string origin
|Construction/growth is fallible and transactional
|No library panic; structural mutation follows borrow checking
|C allocation/movement plus RFC 3629 validator

|`+std.convert+`: R-SLIB-CONV-0001..0004
|Copy values and Copy errors
|No allocation; exact parse/range errors
|No panic; locale-independent and thread-safe
|`+strto*+` under isolated C locale or equivalent deterministic parser

|`+std.format+`: R-SLIB-FMT-0001..0003
|Move builder/string; returned str borrows builder
|Append may allocate and is transactional
|No library panic; builder is Send+Sync
|Bounded `+snprintf+`-class conversion or equivalent typed formatter

|`+std.math+`: R-SLIB-MATH-0001..0006
|Copy scalar/complex/result values
|No allocation; fallible scalar/complex and magnitude conditions return `+math_error+`
|No panic; per-call fenv/errno normalization is thread-safe
|C17 `<math.h>`/`<complex.h>` value and branch semantics or equivalent intrinsics

|`+std.time+` synchronous items: R-SLIB-TIME-0001..0004, R-SLIB-TIME-0006..0008
|Copy duration/instant/system/UTC values; the text forms read a borrowed `+str+` and return
an owned `+std.string::string+`
|No allocation except the written text; typed time errors and `+std.convert::parse_error+`
with the index of a byte
|No panic; clock calls are thread-safe
|C17 `<time.h>` and bounded native monotonic clock query; the R part is translated with the
program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.time+` zones and patterns: R-SLIB-TIME-0010..0014
|Move `+zone+` that owns its transitions, types and rule; Copy `+local_time+`; names and
abbreviations are views of the zone
|Zone errors are `+std.time::zone_error+`, field and range errors `+std.time::time_error+`; a zone
file that cannot be read is a fault of `+std.fs+`
|Zones are Send+Sync; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program; `+load_zone+` and `+local_zone+` read TZif files through
`+std.fs+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|Language type constructor `+task<T throws E...>+`, all `+std.async+` public types, synchronous
controls `+cancel+`/`+detach+` and `+task_id+`:
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0001..0010, R-SLIB-ASYNC-0018
|Move task observation right; Copy start error; control operations consume task
|No allocation or recoverable failure at the control call
|Cancel/drop request cancellation; detach preserves execution; unobserved panic is handled once
|Executor-owned frame and target cancellation request

|Synchronous `+std.async+` lock, semaphore, notify and broadcast operations:
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0013..0016
|Move-only handles, guards, permits and subscriptions own references to one shared state
|Constructors, `+subscribe+` and `+publish+` throw `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`
|Count overflow and a zero broadcast capacity panic; every operation is thread-safe
|Runtime allocator and one native mutex per shared state

|All `+std.io+` public types and synchronous `+as_error+`/`+stdin+`/`+stdout+`/`+stderr+`
items: R-SLIB-IO-0001..0008
|Copy error and Move stream/outcome types; each acquired console handle is an independent view
|No allocation or hidden acquisition failure at these calls
|No panic; stream handles are Send+Sync
|Captured native status and runtime-owned process console views

|`+std.env+`: R-SLIB-ENV-0001..0003
|Returned strings/containers own snapshots
|Snapshot/set may allocate; mutation is transactional
|No panic; standard calls are serialized
|`+argc/argv+`, `+getenv+` and target environment adapter

|All `+std.fs+` public types and synchronous path/error items: R-SLIB-FS-0001..0016
|Move path/handles/iterators/outcomes; Copy metadata, errors and option enums/struct
|Conversion/clone may allocate and returns exact error
|No panic; path values are Send+Sync
|Target native path encoding adapter

|`+std.net+` public types, address items, synchronous address observations, socket options
and peer credentials: R-SLIB-NET-0001..0010, R-SLIB-NET-0012..0017
|Copy address/error/config/option/datagram/credential values and Move handle/outcome types
|Parsing, options and credentials do not allocate; formatting may allocate
|No panic; handles are Send+Sync; observations, option changes and credential queries are
bounded
|C byte arithmetic, native socket address conversion, socket options and peer credentials

|`+std.process+` public types and synchronous builder/extraction/`+id+`/host-terminal
items: R-SLIB-PROC-0001..0008
|Move command/child/pipe handles; builder is Send, not Sync
|Fallible setters are transactional; extraction and `+id+` do not allocate
|No panic; extracted pipes are Send+Sync
|Owned native argv/environment and pipe handles; C17 `+exit+`/`+abort+` or target
equivalents after their exact R cleanup boundary

|`+std.c+`: R-SLIB-C-0001..0006
|Exact Copy conversions, Move C string/handle/attachment
|String construction may allocate; checked outcomes retain ownership
|Safe items do not panic; unsafe items require stated FFI contract
|Direct C17 ABI representation and selected link manifest

|`+std.cmp+`, `+std.slice+`, `+std.text+`: R-SLIB-CMP-0001..0002, R-SLIB-SLICE-0001..0002,
R-SLIB-TEXT-0001..0004
|Copy `+ordering+`; traits over `+Self+`; results borrow from their arguments
|No allocation; no checked errors
|Only Core arithmetic and bounds panics of the item bodies
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol

|`+std.regex+`: R-SLIB-REGEX-0001..0007
|Immutable Move program; Copy byte spans, options and errors; owned output strings
|Checked compile/search limits and allocation failure; inputs unchanged
|Send+Sync; iterative, budgeted matching without backtracking
|R source translated with the program; no additional C ABI or native dependency

|`+std.iter+`, `+std.set+`, `+std.deque+`, `+std.heap+`, `+std.sorted+`: R-SLIB-ITER-0001..0005,
R-SLIB-SET-0001, R-SLIB-DEQUE-0001, R-SLIB-HEAP-0001, R-SLIB-SORTED-0001..0002
|Move-only iterators and containers; adapters borrow their closure; iterators borrow their source
|Growth through `+std.array+`, `+std.list+` and `+std.dict+` with their exact checked errors
|Only Core arithmetic and bounds panics of the item bodies
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol

|`+std.encoding+`: R-SLIB-ENCODING-0001..0004
|Call-bounded inputs; owned output strings and byte arrays
|Output allocation reports `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`; malformed input reports
`+std.convert::parse_error+` with its byte index; inputs unchanged
|Only Core arithmetic and bounds panics of the item bodies
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol

|`+std.uuid+`: R-SLIB-UUID-0001..0003
|Copy identifiers; call-bounded text input
|No allocation except formatting; `+v7+` reports `+std.time::time_error+`, `+parse+`
`+std.convert::parse_error+` with its byte index
|Only Core arithmetic and bounds panics of the item bodies
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol

|`+std.args+`: R-SLIB-ARGS-0001..0003
|Move parser and matches own copies of names and values; arguments are call-bounded
|Declarations and parses report `+std.args::args_error+` with an index and
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+`
|Only Core arithmetic and bounds panics of the item bodies
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol
|===

=== A.2 Asynchronous APIs

[cols="2,3,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Module and exact items |Start/owned result |Cancellation and deadline |Panic/capabilities |Native mapping

|Async declaration calls, await and runtime semantics only: R-SLIB-ASYNC-0002..0005,
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0007..0010
|Every async call returns `+task<T throws E...>+` or immediately throws `+start_error+`;
await consumes the observation right and returns T or throws one stored E
|Cancel/drop interaction and deadlines select one terminal result
|Task is Move, Send iff T and every E are Send, never Sync; observed panic re-raised
|Target executor and native completion queue

|`+std.time+`: R-SLIB-TIME-0005, R-SLIB-TIME-0009
|Logical `+void throws time_error+`; a Copy `+interval+` borrowed exclusively by
`+@scoped+` `+tick+`
|Cancel is `+cancelled+` and leaves an interval unchanged; monotonic target determines success
|Task capabilities follow logical result
|Native monotonic timer

|`+std.io+` async operations only: R-SLIB-IO-0003..0008
|Every owning read/write variant returns its owned array/arc; scoped forms loan the
caller's view until acknowledgement; close consumes handle
|Exact deadline; partial progress preserved; close reaches terminal release
|Handles Send+Sync; task follows result
|Native console/pipe readiness or completion

|`+std.fs+` async open/data/metadata/iteration/mutation operations only:
R-SLIB-FS-0004..0016
|Handles retained at start; buffers/iterators returned by closed outcomes; scoped forms
loan the caller's view until acknowledgement
|Exact deadline/cancel; atomic publication has one commit point
|Path/handles Send+Sync; tasks structural
|Native async file and directory-capability operations

|`+std.net+` async DNS/TCP/UDP/Unix-domain operations only:
R-SLIB-NET-0003..0010, R-SLIB-NET-0014..0015, R-SLIB-NET-0017
|DNS owns copied host; a Unix-domain path is converted at start; stream/datagram buffers
returned or loaned by scoped forms until acknowledgement; close consumes handle
|Exact deadline/cancel and positive stream progress
|All handles Send+Sync; per-direction submission ordering
|Native resolver and socket readiness/completion

|`+std.net+` R part: R-SLIB-NET-0011, R-SLIB-NET-0018
|Owned candidate array; the scoped name form borrows host until its await completes
|Deadline of the enclosing deadline block; the last failure is reported, cancellation and
deadline end the attempts
|Task follows tcp_connect; attempts run one at a time in order
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001) over resolve and tcp_connect

|`+std.process+` async operations only: R-SLIB-PROC-0003,
R-SLIB-PROC-0005..0006, R-SLIB-PROC-0008
|Spawn returns child or command; wait returns status or child
|Exact deadline/cancel; child creation is spawn commit point
|Child Send+Sync; pipes use std.io
|Native process creation, wait and termination services

|`+std.signal+`: R-SLIB-SIGNAL-0001..0003
|Move listener handle retained by each wait; Copy signal kind
|Listen and raise are bounded native calls; next has an exact deadline, and cancellation leaves
the deliveries counted
|Handles Send+Sync; no R code runs in a signal handler
|Dispatch signal sources; ignore disposition while a listener exists; POSIX_SPAWN_SETSIGDEF for
spawned children

|`+std.sync+` asynchronous receive and reserve: R-LIB-0016
|Task retains the channel; owned `+o<T>+` or `+reserve_result<T>+` result
|Cancellation leaves the value queued and the slot free; no deadline parameter
|Task Send iff T is Send; reserve on a rendezvous channel panics
|Waiters of one channel served in start order

|`+std.async+` asynchronous `+lock+`, `+read+`, `+write+`, `+acquire+`, `+notified+` and
`+broadcast_receive+`: R-SLIB-ASYNC-0013..0016
|Task retains the shared state; owned guard, permit, void or
`+broadcast_result<T>+` result
|Cancellation removes a waiter and loses no lock, permit, notification or value; no deadline
parameter
|Task Send iff its result is Send; a broadcast clone that fails to allocate
panics
|Waiters of one resource served in start order

|`+std.async::blocking+`: R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017
|Task owns the staged arguments and the entry's R or E
|Cancellation removes a call no pool thread took; a taken call is acknowledged after return
|Task Send iff R and every E are Send; arguments follow Core R-MEM-0013
|Blocking call pool of Core R-TERM-0015; calls taken in submission order

|`+std.async::join+`: R-SLIB-ASYNC-0020
|Consumes the observation right of the task; owned `+std.thread::join_result<T>+` result
|Cancellation and deadline of the awaiting task as for await; the report text is the only
allocation
|Result Send iff T is Send; the panic of the task ends at the join
|Task outcome of Core R-FUNC-0012; the report is taken from the panic state of the thread

|`+std.service+`: R-SLIB-SERVICE-0001..0003
|Owns listener, stop receiver and shared state reference; Copy report
|Stop request, connection deadline and task cancellation; handler tasks end before return
|Handler tasks supervised by one group; Copy, Send+Sync options and report
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol

|`+std.stream+`, `+std.bufio+`, `+std.console+`: R-SLIB-STREAM-0001..0004,
R-SLIB-BUFIO-0001..0004, R-SLIB-CONSOLE-0001..0002
|Readers and writers own their source and sink; views are task-group loans
(R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012); console text is owned by the call
|Deadline blocks and task cancellation reach the scoped operations; failures are thrown as
`+std.error::fault+`
|Members of the traits are Send+Sync; buffers of fixed capacity; only Core arithmetic and
bounds panics
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol

|`+std.url+`, `+std.mime+`: R-SLIB-URL-0001..0005, R-SLIB-MIME-0001..0002
|Move URLs, media types and owned strings; results of accessors borrow the value
|Parsing and decoding errors carry the byte offset; allocation failures are
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+`
|Only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol

|`+std.http+`: R-SLIB-HTTP-0001..0018
|Move messages, header sections, routers and clients; a router is shared by its server through
`+arc+`; call-bounded views of streams and bodies
|Protocol failures are `+std.http::http_error+`; a server answers them and never throws them;
stream failures are faults
|Messages, routers and clients are Send+Sync; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over `+std.stream+`, `+std.service+` and `+std.tls+`
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.dns+`: R-SLIB-DNS-0001..0006
|Move records, questions and resolvers; lookups own their results; call-bounded views of names
and messages
|Protocol and lookup failures are `+std.dns::dns_error+`, cancellation `+std.net::net_error+` and
failures of memory `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`
|Resolvers are Send+Sync; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over `+std.net+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.websocket+`: R-SLIB-WS-0001..0005
|Move ends of connections that own their transport; messages own their data; call-bounded views
of text and data
|Handshake and protocol failures are `+std.websocket::websocket_error+`, a protocol failure
answered with a close; stream failures are faults
|Ends are Send+Sync with one lock for receiving and one for sending; only Core arithmetic and
bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over `+std.http+` and `+std.stream+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.jsonrpc+`: R-SLIB-JSONRPC-0001..0004
|Move messages that own their JSON values; ids copied explicitly; call-bounded views of text
|A text that is no message is `+std.jsonrpc::rpc_error+` with its JSON-RPC code; stream
failures are faults; allocation failures are `+std.alloc::alloc_error+`
|Messages are Send+Sync; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over `+std.json+`, `+std.stream+` and `+std.bufio+`
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.mcp+`: R-SLIB-MCP-0001..0024
|Move servers, clients, definitions and results; a server is shared through `+arc+` and its
handlers share its state `+arc S+`; subscriptions and launched children are owned by the program
|Protocol failures are `+std.mcp::mcp_error+` with the codes of JSON-RPC, MCP and the client;
a server answers them and never throws them; stream failures are faults
|Servers, clients and notifiers are Send+Sync; requests of one client and of one server run
concurrently; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over `+std.jsonrpc+`, `+std.http+`, `+std.process+` and
`+std.tls+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.tls+`: R-SLIB-TLS-0001..0007
|Move configuration and session; a session owns its transport and a configuration lives
until its last session; call-bounded certificate views and a borrowed secret key
|Configuration errors and handshake rejections are `+std.tls::tls_error+`, failures of memory
`+std.alloc::alloc_error+` and other session failures after the handshake `+std.io::io_error+`;
a system bundle that cannot be read is a fault of `+std.fs+`
|Configurations and sessions are Send+Sync; one reader and one writer at a time; only Core
arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over the native provider `+std.tls.native+` and
Mbed TLS 3.6 (R-SLIB-RSRC-0003)

|`+std.fs+` mappings, the R part of `+std.fs+`: R-SLIB-FS-0017
|Move mapping that owns its pages through `+arc+`; views of the range are anchored to the
mapping and a sync call keeps the pages
|Mapping failures are `+std.fs::fs_error+`, path conversion `+std.fs::path_error+`; the
contract of the unsafe `+map_file+` keeps the range in the file and unchanged by others
|Mappings are Send+Sync; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over the native provider `+std.fs.native+`
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0003)

|`+std.postgres+`: R-SLIB-PG-0001..0016
|Move options, connections, statements, rows and notifications; a connection is shared through
`+arc+` by its handles, every operation keeps it through a new reference; row views borrow the row
|Server errors are `+std.postgres::pg_error+` with SQLSTATE, message, detail and hint; failures of
the transport are faults and end the session
|Connections are Send+Sync and their operations take turns; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over `+std.net+`, `+std.tls+` and `+std.pool+`
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.jwt+`: R-SLIB-JWT-0001..0007
|Move signers, verifiers, key sets and validations; a token is a string; keys are moved into
signers and verifiers
|Rejected tokens and keys are `+std.jwt::jwt_error+`; typed claims report `+std.json::error+`
|Signers, verifiers and key sets are Send+Sync; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over `+std.crypto+`, `+std.hash+`, `+std.encoding+` and
`+std.json+` (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.oauth2+`: R-SLIB-OAUTH2-0001..0006
|Move tokens, grants, requests and service accounts; a token source owns its HTTP client and
shares its token through `+arc+`; `+request_token+` borrows the client for its call
|Refusals of the endpoint and unreadable answers or key files are `+std.oauth2::oauth2_error+`;
transport failures are `+std.http+`, `+std.tls+` errors and faults
|Token sources are Send+Sync and their requests take turns; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program over `+std.http+`, `+std.jwt+` and `+std.crypto+`
(R-SLIB-RSRC-0001)

|`+std.log+`, `+std.config+`: R-SLIB-LOG-0001..0005, R-SLIB-CONFIG-0001..0004
|Loggers own a sender of one bounded queue; the writer owns its receiver; the
configuration owns its values and borrows them to `+get+`
|Logging never waits: a full queue drops and counts the record; the writer ends when every
logger is gone; module errors carry an index
|Loggers and writers are Send+Sync; only Core arithmetic and bounds panics
|R source translated with the program (R-SLIB-RSRC-0001); no C symbol
|===

[[R-SLIB-API-A002]]
*R-SLIB-API-A002* — Every operation incorporated by an A.2 row is an async operation and
uses the exact logical signature written in its cited rule and the universal start
signature of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0002. Every supporting/input/error/handle/result type for those
operations is incorporated by the corresponding A.1 public-types row; a type may be
described there while its ownership-bearing use is governed by A.2. Types do not
themselves start tasks. All deadline-bearing
operations accept only the final `+o<std.time::instant>+` argument shown. All buffers
used after successful start are owned by a task result or a transferred arc, or are
loaned by the caller's task group to a scoped operation (R-SLIB-ASYNC-0012); any other
call-bounded slice is fully copied before start commit when a cited signature accepts
one for preparation. Post-start implementation allocation or size failure while
materializing a data-dependent output follows the exact R-SLIB-GEN-0005
`+resource_exhausted+` mapping when the module declares that code.

[[R-SLIB-API-A003]]
*R-SLIB-API-A003* — No inventory property licenses a hidden recoverable failure. An item
whose exact signature contains no failure alternative shall fulfill its stated result
without allocation/native acquisition failure at that call, as required by R-LIB-0007.
The implementation shall reject a profile it cannot provide with those guarantees.

[appendix]
[[annex-b]]
== Annex B — Normative C17 and native mapping

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B001]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B001* — Translation of an R application, including generated declarations
for the standard library, shall remain strictly conforming C17 subject to the Core target
manifest. Target-specific asynchronous adapter sources form a separately verified
runtime boundary. They shall not cause target headers, extensions, calling conventions
or native handles to appear in generated application C interfaces.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B002]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B002* — The following mapping is normative. “Equivalent” permits a compiler
intrinsic, runtime routine or target API only when it produces the same R observable
result, ownership, error and synchronization behavior.

[cols="2,3,4",options="header"]
|===
|R facility |C17 or native basis |Required normalization

|Primitive limits and fixed arithmetic
|`+<stdint.h>+`, `+<limits.h>+`, `+<float.h>+`, C operators or equivalent intrinsics
|Manifest widths; R overflow, conversion and NaN rules before C UB

|Core unsafe intrinsics
|C pointer/volatile operations, ownership runtime hooks and compiler assumptions
|Exact Core R-UNSAFE-0007..0008 type and safety contracts; no raw operation acquires
safe provenance implicitly

|Atomics and `+arc+`
|`+<stdatomic.h>+` or runtime locks when not lock-free
|Exact R memory order; reference overflow before count mutation

|Allocation and owners
|`+malloc+`, `+calloc+`, `+realloc+`, `+free+`, `+aligned_alloc+` or target allocator
|Checked size/alignment; construction and exactly-once drop separated from raw storage

|Canonical read-only byte view
|Generated C pointer-and-length slice descriptor
|No owner decay, allocation or byte copy; length and original provenance are preserved

|Bytes and digests
|`+memmove+`, `+memset+`, `+memcmp+`, `+memchr+` and bounded unsigned C17 loops
|Complete slice/range checks before memory calls; canonical compare result -1/0/1;
exact CRC-32/ISO-HDLC, RFC 1321 MD5 and FIPS PUB 180-4 output

|UTF-8 validation
|Bounded unsigned-byte traversal over explicit pointer and length
|RFC 3629 first-error semantics and original-source borrow origin; no sentinel dependence

|LSB-first bit reading
|Bounded unsigned shifts, masks and byte loads
|Transactional end/width failure, exact reader state and no shift count equal to operand width

|Secret byte erasure and comparison
|Volatile `+unsigned char+` stores and bounded unsigned loops
|Erasure of length and unused capacity before release; length-only early exit and
byte-independent accumulation for equality

|Hashing and key equality
|Documented target hash primitive or bounded byte/value mixing
|R-SLIB-IDB-0014 canonical input, repeatability and equal-implies-equal-hash contract;
dictionary seed mixed only after `+core::hash+`

|Owned UTF-8 strings
|C allocation and bounded byte movement
|RFC 3629 validation, scalar boundaries and explicit length; no sentinel dependence

|Integer/floating parse
|`+strtoimax+`, `+strtoumax+`, `+strtof+`, `+strtod+`, `+strtold+` or equivalent
|Bounded owned input, whole-input consumption, isolated C locale, errno/fenv capture

|Typed formatting
|`+snprintf+` family or equivalent integer/float converter
|Pre-sized bounded destination, isolated locale, canonical shortest/tie selection, no
public variadic call

|Scalar and complex math
|`+<math.h>+`, `+<complex.h>+` or equivalent intrinsics
|Canonical fenv, errno/exception capture, typed R outcome, prior environment restored

|System time and UTC
|`+timespec_get+`, `+gmtime+`-class conversion or bounded target equivalents
|Range checks, owned result, UTC Gregorian fields, serialized global-state fallback

|Monotonic instant and timers
|Target monotonic clock and native timer completion
|Monotonic comparison, target resolution, one cancel/deadline/completion outcome

|Arguments and environment
|Host `+argc/argv+`, `+getenv+` plus target mutation adapter
|UTF-8 owned snapshots, bounded conversion and runtime serialization

|C strings and checked ABI conversions
|C scalar types, terminated char arrays and target manifest
|Range/NUL/UTF-8 validation and exact raw-pointer lifetime contract

|C handle ownership and foreign-thread attachment
|Runtime record retaining the adopted non-null pointer and destructor; thread-local
attachment registry plus bounded C floating-environment capture/restore
|Exactly-once destructor dispatch after the last owner and generation/thread-affine
attach/detach with transactional failure

|Thread and synchronization modules
|C11/C17 threading where conforming, otherwise target runtime primitives
|Core happens-before edges, poison state, deterministic guard/resource destruction

|Console and process pipes
|Target-native readiness/completion backend
|Owned buffers, partial progress, EOF, ordering, cancel and deadline

|Filesystem
|Bounded file payload adapter for payload transfer; closed adapter lane for metadata,
namespace, cursor and durability
|Native error captured immediately; capability-relative resolution, acknowledged
cancellation and atomic publication

|DNS, TCP and UDP
|Target-native resolver and socket readiness/completion
|Owned hostname/buffers, network-order address conversion, directional ordering

|Child processes
|Target-native process creation, pipe, wait and termination APIs
|Separate argv values, owned environment, creation commit point and exactly-once reap

|Process signals
|Target-native signal event sources and signal dispositions
|No R code in a signal handler; deliveries counted per listener; the disposition is replaced
only after the source is registered and restored after the last listener

|Current-process termination
|C17 `+exit+` and `+abort+` or target equivalents
|Orderly R executor drain/module destruction/runtime-stream flush precedes host exit;
abort is immediate and performs no later R drops

|Standard modules written in R
|The Core C17 translation of the importing program
|No library symbol, target or ABI record; the generated code of the program contains the
instantiated definitions
|===

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B003]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B003* — Before invoking a C operation with a size represented by `+size_t+`,
the adapter proves the R `+usize+` range, multiplication/addition and object-size bounds.
Before converting a returned pointer difference, byte count, time field, status or error
code, it proves representability in the exact R destination. A native value outside the
portable result becomes the specified `+overflow+`, `+resource_exhausted+` or `+other+`
error and never reaches a narrowing C conversion. Native child terminal status is the
explicit exception: the implementation applies the total documented i32 mapping of
R-SLIB-IDB-0010 and returns `+exit_status+` rather than a post-reap error.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B004]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B004* — The native async backend represents a pending operation by a runtime
record owning every handle retain, buffer owner and completion callback. Publishing that
record is part of start commit. The backend releases it after one terminal completion
has been enqueued and observed or detached cleanup has run. Cancellation retains the
record until the target acknowledges completion or cancellation. No ownership-bearing
result is enqueued for R observation until the adapter has that acknowledgement and has
ceased every native access to the returned state, or until an independent bounce buffer
has been reconciled into that state and itself is no longer native-accessible.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B005]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B005* — Executor workers may execute only bounded submission/completion calls
and ready R computation. An implementation claiming hosted-native-async shall demonstrate
that file payload, console, DNS, socket and child waiting consumes no worker thread
blocked in a C I/O call. A bounded native call that reports immediate readiness,
completion or error satisfies this rule. Only the separate filesystem adapter lane, for
the closed operation families of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0011, the file payload adapter, for the
regular-file payload transfers of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0019, and the blocking call pool, for the
entries of R-SLIB-ASYNC-0017, may block.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B006]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B006* — A C facility exposing shared locale, environment, error-text,
calendar or floating-environment state is wrapped by a reentrant per-thread variant, an
owned precomputed table, or runtime serialization. The wrapper snapshots every returned
value before releasing protection. Calls from foreign C code outside the R runtime
remain governed by their external safety contract.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B007]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B007* — Capability-relative filesystem mapping holds an open native root
identity during every component operation and requests no-follow or equivalent verified
resolution. When a target operation returns a new native identity, the adapter verifies
that it remains under the retained root before publication. Verification failure closes
the identity and reports a portable error without exposing it to safe R.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B008]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B008* — All C and native compilation units supplied by the implementation
shall compile under the documented toolchain with every warning treated as an error.
The conformance record shall distinguish validation of generated strict C17 from
validation of target-specific runtime adapters.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B009]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B009* — A filesystem adapter-lane request is one runtime record with a
linearizable `+queued+`, `+entered+` or `+terminal+` state. Exactly one of a lane thread,
cancellation or deadline removes it from `+queued+`. The lane thread changes
`+queued+` to `+entered+` before invoking an admitted native entry point. Cancellation
or deadline observed in `+entered+` records the candidate terminal event without
releasing state or preempting the call. On return, the adapter captures native status,
applies the operation's external commit rule, publishes exactly one outcome and releases
the record exactly once. Every target mapping shall prove that an admitted call cannot
invoke arbitrary callbacks or execute R code on the lane thread.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B010]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B010* — A standard module written in R (section 21) maps to C17 only through
the Core translation of the program that imports it: its functions, methods and instantiated
generic definitions are emitted as ordinary R functions of that program, its aggregates as
ordinary R aggregates, and it introduces no library C symbol, no link-plan library target
and no C17 ABI record. Two programs that import the same module receive independent
translations of it; the module has no shared state.

[[R-SLIB-MAP-B011]]
*R-SLIB-MAP-B011* — A static reflection intrinsic (R-LIB-0024) maps to C17 without any
library symbol: a reflection constant is a translation-time literal (an integer, an enum
constant, a fixed-array initializer or a program string of Core R-TYPE-0028), and a
selection is a call of one translation-unit-local static function per selection and
enumeration that holds a `+switch+` over the enumeration value or the active tag, or a
search of a `+static const+` table of program strings by a shared lookup helper.

[appendix]
[[annex-c]]
== Annex C — Implementation-defined library behavior

The following list is complete for implementation-defined choices introduced by this
document. Core-language implementation-defined choices remain in the Core catalogue.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0001]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0001* — The implementation shall document allocator identity, maximum
supported allocation/alignment, zero-size policy and whether an allocation failure
originated in C or a target allocator. These choices do not change `+alloc_error+`.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0002]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0002* — It shall document the accuracy guarantee for each transcendental
math operation and C ABI floating type, including whether the implementation is
correctly rounded or its maximum error in ulps. R error classification and fenv
restoration remain fixed.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0003]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0003* — It shall document system-time epoch/range, monotonic clock
resolution/range, representable instant horizon and timestamp resolution.
`+system_time+` uses Unix seconds at the R interface regardless of native
epoch.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0004]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0004* — It shall document the hosted-native-async backend name/version,
executor worker policy, timer source and native cancellation mechanism for every
mandatory operation family. The manifest capability values shall agree with this record.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0005]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0005* — It shall document native path encoding, case and normalization
behavior, maximum component/path sizes, reserved component rules, recognized root forms,
and the handling that guarantees R-SLIB-FS-0012 on the selected filesystem target.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0006]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0006* — It shall document the durability reached by successful
`+std.fs::flush+`, by successful `+std.fs::sync+` at each `+std.fs::sync_level+` and by each pre-publication and post-publication durability action of
atomic whole-file publication, including destination-parent metadata durability and the
treatment of a post-commit durability failure. It shall also document the
filesystem/volume conditions under which atomic no-replace is supported. The
`+committed+` variant certifies complete atomic namespace publication; it is not changed
by the result of a later metadata-durability action.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0007]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0007* — It shall document directory enumeration order, treatment of entries
created or removed after iterator creation, and which native kinds map to `+other+`.
Each concrete iterator still emits a native entry at most once.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0008]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0008* — It shall document DNS resolver address ordering, search-domain and
hosts-database policy, cache participation and internationalized-name preprocessing.
The R input accepted directly by resolve remains the ASCII presentation fixed by
R-SLIB-NET-0003.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0009]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0009* — It shall document environment-name equality, initial argument
spelling, the representability mapping from native startup argv to validated UTF-8,
whether a host argument zero is present and the synthesized UTF-8 argument-zero spelling
used when it is absent, without promising executable identity,
native conversion of UTF-8 argument/environment values, and behavior of
concurrent foreign environment access outside standard R serialization. It shall also
document the native environment enumeration order and duplicate-name policy used to
construct one canonical all-or-error snapshot shared by `+get+`, `+variables+` and
inherited process environments.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0010]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0010* — It shall document mapping between R i32 process statuses and host
statuses, signal/exception values placed in `+exit_status.code+`, distinct nonzero host
statuses for the pre-main `+argument_encoding_failure+` and `+allocation_failure+`
categories, the nonzero async-root start-failure status, and the native forceful-
termination mechanism. It shall also document the injective mapping from native child
identities to the u64 returned by `+std.process::id+` among concurrently live or
reap-pending standard children, when an identity may be reused after final reap, and any
target namespace or truncation normalization. The value remains stable for the owner
lifetime fixed by R-SLIB-PROC-0006.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0011]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0011* — It shall document the origin and numeric encoding stored in each
`+native_code+`, diagnostic-text language/source, mappings to each `+other+` variant and
any additional native text used by `+diagnostic+`. The domain membership, 0x0000/0x0100
code bands, declared variant name returned by `+name+`, typed-detail discard and exact
native-code preservation rules are fixed and are not implementation-defined.
For every native adapter and documented target version, the implementation shall publish
an exhaustive table mapping every native failure status or status class that can arise to
its portable code, including context rules for aliased native values. Such a table records
conformance to the semantic classifications in this document and does not make them
implementation-defined. A documented status may map explicitly to `+other+` only when
none of the module's narrower semantic classifications applies; a wildcard unknown-
status row also maps to `+other+`. Each table shall test the narrower classifications
before either residual case.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0012]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0012* — It shall document the default positive TCP listen backlog selected
by zero, socket option mapping, IPv6 dual-stack policy, the maximum UDP payload accepted
for send, the maximum native datagram size observable on receive, whether those limits
differ, UDP truncation detection and which target conditions report `+unsupported+`.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0013]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0013* — It shall document the scheduling policy for ready tasks, executor
thread count and runtime panic-handler destination. Scheduling may choose among ready
tasks; per-resource submission ordering and synchronization edges remain fixed.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0014]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0014* — It shall document the algorithm and version used by
`+core::hash+` and the exact canonical input mapping for every predefined Boolean,
character, R/C numeric, fieldless-enum, string and safe/raw-pointer key family of
R-LIB-0020. Repeated hashing of one unchanged value under one selected target and program
execution shall not vary by call, thread or dictionary. Values equal under
`+core::key_equal+` shall have equal core hashes; unequal values may collide. The mapping
shall not read padding or an indeterminate representation. It shall canonicalize both
signed zeros and every NaN to their respective equality classes, hash strings from their
exact bounded UTF-8 bytes, and hash pointers from preserved allocation identity and
offset rather than unrelated object bytes. The implementation shall state whether any
non-pointer predefined hash is stable across executions; cross-execution stability is
not otherwise required. Per-dictionary seed mixing occurs only after this core hash and
remains separate from it.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0015]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0015* — It shall document the exact capacity rounding and growth policy for
`+std.array::create+`, `+with_capacity+`, `+filled+`, `+reserve+` and `+push+`, including the
geometric-growth rule, allocator/alignment rounding and the maximum selectable capacity
for each target type and address space. Every value observed through
`+std.array::capacity+` shall follow that published policy while satisfying the lower
bounds and amortized-growth contract of R-LIB-0019.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0016]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0016* — A target using the filesystem adapter lane shall document its four
thread identities as a class disjoint from executor workers; the exact admitted native
entry points and flags; queue bounds and admission policy; cancellation and deadline
linearization before entry and after entry; the external commit point of each mutating or
durability operation; and the tracing mechanism used to prove the closed admission list.
The record shall state explicitly that payload I/O, console, DNS, sockets, child waiting,
callbacks and R continuations are excluded from the lane. A target using the file payload
adapter shall document its bound of concurrently entered transfers, its admission policy,
its exact native entry points and the linearization of cancellation and deadline before
admission, before entry and after entry.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0017]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0017* — It shall document the SQLite library that `+std.sqlite+` calls
(R-SLIB-SQLITE-0001): whether it is built with the implementation or provided by the system, its
version, and its compile-time options, which fix the SQL dialect that programs see, among them
the treatment of double-quoted string literals, loadable extensions, the mathematical and
full-text functions and the default threading mode.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0018]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0018* — It shall document the advisory locks of `+std.fs::try_lock+`,
`+std.fs::lock+` and `+std.fs::unlock+` (R-SLIB-FS-0016): the native lock that implements them,
the file systems on which they are unsupported or not shared with other hosts, and the intervals
at which `+std.fs::lock+` attempts again.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0019]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0019* — It shall document the mappings of `+std.fs::map_file+` (R-SLIB-FS-0017):
the native mapping that implements them, the page size that aligns the mapped pages, how the
program ends when it touches a page past the end of a truncated file, and the file systems on
which a mapping is refused or its writes are not shared with other hosts.

[[R-SLIB-IDB-0020]]
*R-SLIB-IDB-0020* — It shall document the PostgreSQL servers that `+std.postgres+`
(R-SLIB-PG-0001) supports: the versions it is tested with, the parameters it sets at the start
of a session, the authentication methods it answers, and how it treats a server that reports
another client encoding or integer dates.

[appendix]
[[annex-d]]
== Annex D — Conformance requirements

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D001]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D001* — A library conformance suite shall compile and type-check one use of
every exact public signature in Annex A and exercise every closed outcome variant. For
each by-value Move input, it shall verify consumption on commit, preservation on start
failure, recovery through every non-committing failure payload that permits retry, and
exactly-once destruction on success, error, cancellation, timeout and panic.
Common-error tests shall exercise every exact typed conversion and every exact
`+as_error+` operation, verifying the assigned domain, 0x0000/0x0100 family, declaration-order
variant offset, exact preservation of `+native_code+` when present and zero otherwise.
For every indexed source and every embedded `+std.alloc::alloc_error reason+`, they shall
verify that Copy conversion leaves the typed source unchanged and that two values of one
outer variant differing only in that typed detail produce the same common error. They
shall verify `+name+` for every assigned code and `+"unknown"+` for an unassigned code,
and exercise successful and allocation-failing `+diagnostic+` paths.
Thread-spawn tests shall inject every creation failure before commit, verify that each
named Move source remains initialized and unchanged, and verify consumption before entry
observation after successful commit. Positive cases shall stage a named source only as a
complete direct `+move place+` argument. Negative cases shall require
`+R-DIAG-MOVE-003+` for a named move nested in an aggregate, constructor or other
argument expression and shall reject every access overlapping an active staged
reservation; a separately constructed named payload passed by direct move shall succeed.
Under unwind, a later-argument panic shall release every reservation and destroy each
initialized temporary exactly once without creating a thread. An isolated abort-strategy
case shall instead confirm the R-ERR-0005 absence of additional R drops.
For every R-LIB-0001 integer suffix, core arithmetic tests shall verify both public
bounds and each checked, wrapping and saturating operation at the two bounds, zero, one,
and overflowing positive and negative mathematical results where the type admits them.
Raw shared-owner tests shall accept `+into_raw+` in safe code, require an unsafe context
for `+from_raw+`, and exercise the exact one-token restoration contract through verified
fixtures. Thread-creation fixtures shall exercise `+permission_denied+`,
`+resource_exhausted+` and `+unavailable+`, including an overlapping native description
that proves their stated priority; each failure shall preserve every staged argument.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D002]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D002* — Allocation tests shall inject size overflow and each
`+alloc_error+`, verify zero-size guarantees, validate alignment and prove allocator
balance. Combined faults shall prove size before alignment before allocator refusal.
Array tests shall compare every capacity after create, requested capacity, reserve and
repeated push against R-SLIB-IDB-0015. Dictionary tests shall prove `+contains+` exact
lookup equality and shall repeat `+core::hash+` across calls, threads and dictionaries
for Boolean/numeric/enum/string/pointer keys, including signed-zero and NaN classes;
equal keys shall hash equally and results shall match R-SLIB-IDB-0014 independently of
the later dictionary seed. Async allocation tests shall distinguish pre-commit frame/submission/copy
failure from post-start data-dependent-output `+resource_exhausted+` and verify closed
outcome cleanup for filesystem, DNS and an analogous operation. Byte tests shall cover
empty and boundary ranges, distinct-slice copy,
overlapping `+copy_within+` in both directions and transactional range rejection.
`+copy_within+` cases shall independently overflow destination addition, overflow source
addition, exceed destination bounds and exceed source bounds, including combined inputs
that prove destination-before-source priority in each phase.
They shall verify that `+fill+` changes every element, `+equal+` is exact length-and-byte
equality, and `+find+`/`+find_slice+` choose the lowest of multiple matches or none.
They shall test matching/nonmatching and empty prefix/suffix for `+starts_with+` and
`+ends_with+`. They shall prove that `+bytes+` construction and all array observations
use the exact same canonical owner as `+array<u8>+`; exercise zero and nonzero capacity;
and inject each append size/allocation failure to verify unchanged length, capacity,
allocation identity and contents. Little-endian append tests shall cover zero, each
maximum value and a mixed-byte value at every width. `+std.utf8::validate+` shall match
`+core::validate_utf8+` in success view, inferred origin and first-error index, while
`+std.utf8::is_valid+` shall select the corresponding Boolean without allocation. Each
shall accept direct `+const u8[]+`, `+u8[]+`, fixed `+u8[N]+`, `+array<u8>+`, `+bytes+`,
`+str+` and `+constexpr str+` arguments through the single signature, without byte copy,
and a returned validated view shall freeze the original source rather than a temporary
descriptor. CRC tests
shall include empty input, `+"123456789"+`, embedded zero bytes and incremental reference
vectors. MD5 tests shall include the RFC 1321 empty, `+"abc"+`, `+"123456789"+` and
multi-block vectors; SHA tests shall include the FIPS empty, `+"abc"+` and multi-block
vectors for each algorithm. Every case shall compare every digest byte in standard
display order. Profile checks shall reject a slice/object limit that violates the
MD5/SHA-1/SHA-256 message-length bound
of R-SLIB-BYTES-0007.
Bit-reader tests shall cover every width 0..64, every initial bit offset, reads crossing
one and several byte boundaries, buffered-only reads, repeated reads and byte alignment.
They shall compare each result and public reader field with an independent bit-by-bit
reference, prove invalid-width priority over end-of-input, verify the exact first-missing
byte index, preserve all fields on every error and instrument generated C to reject a
shift by 64.
String tests shall cover every UTF-8 sequence class, first-error byte, scalar-boundary
truncate, self-independent append, relocation borrow checking and allocation rollback.
They shall prove validation-before-allocation for `+from_utf8+` and `+append_utf8+`.
For `+from_bytes+`, valid input shall transfer the same allocation and exact bytes, while
invalid input shall return the identical byte owner and contents with the first error
index under injected allocation pressure and zero allocator calls. String `+clear+`
shall retain capacity and storage while producing valid empty UTF-8 without allocation.
They shall verify exact constructor, view, reserve, append and scalar-encoding bytes and
the truncate error priority and capacity preservation.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D003]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D003* — Conversion and format tests shall cover every listed R/C numeric
suffix, radices 0 and 2..36, each sign and boundary value, one-byte trailing input,
non-finite float spellings, subnormal/overflow cases and shortest round-trip output.
They shall prove that leading plus is accepted for every integer destination, while
leading minus is accepted exactly for a destination with a negative range, including
both manifest signedness choices for `+c_char+` and `+c_wchar+`. Floating formatting
shall distinguish `+-0+` from `+0+` and produce `+nan+` independently of NaN sign and
payload.
Finite formatting vectors shall cover equal-length fixed/scientific candidates, two
round-tripping decimals around one binary value, even-last-digit ties, exponent signs
and zero suppression; every implementation shall emit identical bytes.
Overlapping parser-error cases shall prove invalid-radix, empty, missing-digit,
invalid-digit, trailing-character and post-lexical range priority and exact byte index.
Checked-conversion cases shall prove nonfinite, range, fractional and inexact precedence,
