including integer-to-float and floating-narrowing exactness.
They shall run under at least two installed C locales and produce byte-identical R
results. Integer and float append failure shall preserve the exact builder prefix.
Builder `+clear+` shall retain capacity and storage while producing valid empty UTF-8
without allocation.
An invalid-radix plus allocator-failure fixture shall produce only `+invalid_radix+` and
perform no allocation or mutation; builder construction/view/append/finish bytes shall
match the exact R-SLIB-FMT-0003 postconditions.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D004]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D004* — Math tests shall exercise every scalar and complex operation with
ordinary, boundary, signed-zero, infinity and NaN inputs and each applicable one of the
four exact error codes.
Scalar cases shall include halfway-away `+round_S+`, single-NaN and signed-zero
`+min_S+`/`+max_S+`, finite-edge `+next_after_S+` to infinity, exact-subnormal success,
inexact-subnormal underflow and nonzero-to-zero underflow.
Complex cases shall cover both signed-zero sides of every branch cut, log zero, nonzero
and zero numerators divided by zero, magnitude overflow, and component overflow and
underflow.
Scalar special-case tests shall cover every R-SLIB-MATH-0003 table row, including
logarithm and gamma poles, power zero/negative-base cases and remainder divisor zero.
They shall additionally cover negative/infinite inputs to logarithm, square-root and
inverse functions, an infinite remainder dividend, `+pow_S(NaN,0)+`,
`+pow_S(1,NaN)+`, and negative infinity for both gamma families.
Split/compose tests shall cover signed zero, infinity, NaN, fixed exponents and finite
overflow/underflow boundaries.
They shall set non-canonical C rounding and exception flags before each wrapper, verify
the R result under the canonical environment, and then verify exact restoration of the
prior rounding mode and flags. Conformance evidence shall state the R-SLIB-IDB-0002
accuracy method and bound.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D005]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D005* — Time tests shall check duration normalization/overflow, monotonic
non-regression, system-time range, Gregorian leap years, rejection of second 60 and UTC
round trips for valid representable values. Timer tests use a tolerance derived from documented resolution, prove no
early success, and cover cancellation before and after native submission. Exact-error
fixtures shall produce every `+invalid_value+` and `+overflow+` condition named in
R-SLIB-TIME-0001..0003, a recognized temporary clock/timer failure as `+unavailable+`,
a winning timer cancellation as `+cancelled+`, and both a documented otherwise-
unclassified native failure and a synthetic unknown status as `+other+`; they shall
verify zero or preserved native code as specified.
They shall also overflow positive `+sleep_for+` at the monotonic horizon before timer
submission and exercise a safe `+sleep_until+` instant rejected by a known native timer
representation limit as `+unavailable+`.
Exact arithmetic fixtures shall verify from-seconds/parts components, normalized
add/subtract/multiply, monotonic and system clock observation, instant displacement and
duration difference, system-time addition, and both representable boundaries.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D006]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D006* — Environment tests shall prove ownership of argument/value snapshots,
exact valid-name equality, transactional set/remove and runtime serialization under
concurrent standard calls. They shall compare `+arguments+` byte-for-byte with the
startup snapshot supplied to `+main+`, inject its allocation/checked-size failures and prove
that it never returns `+invalid_value+` after startup. Fixtures shall cover both a
host-supplied argument zero preserved by conversion and zero supplied native arguments,
for which the nonempty snapshot contains the documented synthesized argument-zero
spelling without assuming executable identity. Hosted-launch fixtures shall
inject an unrepresentable native argument and startup-snapshot allocation failure before
static initialization and observe respectively the exact pre-main
`+argument_encoding_failure+` and `+allocation_failure+` categories and their documented
nonzero statuses. Tests shall capture and restore the test process environment.
Combined-failure fixtures shall prove the pre-main order of dynamic-provider readiness,
complete argv representability/size validation and snapshot allocation. Native
environment fixtures shall prove all-or-error UTF-8 conversion, last-entry duplicate
selection in documented enumeration order, and identical winners from `+get+`,
`+variables+` and inherited command capture. A malformed native entry shall expose no
partial snapshot. Setter fixtures shall check name before value before allocation and
prove transactional preservation.
Foreign concurrent mutation is tested only through a fixture satisfying its published
external contract.
Exact error fixtures shall map invalid names and values to `+invalid_name+` and
`+invalid_value+`; injected R allocation and checked-size failures to
`+allocation_failed+` with native code zero; native access denial and snapshot/resource
limits to `+permission_denied+` and `+resource_exhausted+` with the captured native code;
and both a documented otherwise-unclassified native failure and a synthetic unknown
status to `+other+` with that status preserved. Removing a valid unbound name shall
succeed and leave it unbound.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D007]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D007* — Hosted-native-async tests shall inject both start-error variants at
every asynchronous family and verify that no body/native operation runs, no named Move
operand changes state and every provisional retain is balanced. Successful start shall
be eager and independently runnable. Start-argument positive cases shall provisionally
stage a named source only as a complete direct `+move place+`; a separately constructed
named payload passed that way shall succeed. Negative cases shall require
`+R-DIAG-MOVE-003+` for a nested named move and shall reject any access overlapping an
active staged reservation. Under unwind, panic in a later argument shall release all
reservations and destroy initialized temporaries exactly once without publishing a task;
an isolated abort-strategy case shall confirm R-ERR-0005. Positive syntax tests shall use a named
`+await move+` only as a complete initializer or void statement. Negative tests shall
require `+R-DIAG-ASYNC-001+` or the more specific Core diagnostic for await outside an
async body, a non-name operand, missing move, non-void statement await, an escaping
borrow/slice/`+str+`, and a task stored where its Send or non-Sync capability is
insufficient.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D008]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D008* — Cancellation, ordinary task drop, detach, deadline and native
completion shall be raced at a controlled barrier for every async resource family. Each
run shall produce one terminal outcome, keep all frame/buffer/handle storage live until
native acknowledgement, make no ownership-bearing result observable while native access
to its returned state remains possible, and perform one cleanup. Under unwind, observed panic is
re-raised and unobserved panic reaches the configured handler once. Runtime tracing
shall prove that pending file, console, DNS, socket and child waits occupy no executor
worker blocked in C I/O. Every applicable family shall test invalid input plus an expired
deadline, and a specified valid no-op plus an expired deadline, to prove the
R-SLIB-ASYNC-0008 precedence. Terminal-close tests shall start with an expired deadline
and still prove handle consumption, native acknowledgement and exactly-once release
before the `+timed_out+` outcome. For every scoped operation the race shall additionally
prove that the loaned storage is neither read nor written by the backend once the outcome
is observable and that the group's barrier waits for that acknowledgement.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D009]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D009* — Root tests shall execute both accepted async-main signatures,
verify argument copying before suspension, and prove shutdown cancellation/drain before
module destruction. Injected executor and root-frame reservation
failures shall run no main instruction, report the exact start category, select the
documented nonzero status and perform no R static/module destruction.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D010]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D010* — Stream tests shall cover empty and nonempty buffers, partial read and
write, EOF distinct from zero-length read, write-all continuation, flush ordering,
broken pipe, simultaneous read/write, per-direction submission order, cancel/timeout
after positive progress, close with pending operations and return of the identical
array or arc owner in every outcome. They shall assert exact read-prefix replacement,
suffix and end preservation, byte-immutable write owners and byte-immutable shared writes.
For every nonempty stream, file and TCP buffer, successful single-read and single-write
outcomes shall report positive progress; a zero-progress readiness notification shall
remain pending or produce its exact terminal outcome.
They shall verify that zero-length stream, file and TCP writes succeed with zero progress
without native submission, including with an expired deadline and after a successful
TCP write-side shutdown; an empty TCP read after read-side shutdown shall likewise keep
the zero-count no-op outcome rather than `+end+`.
Opened-file stream fixtures shall exercise every exhaustive R-SLIB-FS-0005 mapping and
verify the preserved native code. Console and process-pipe fixtures shall exercise every
R-SLIB-IO-0001 code, including normal input end versus local closure and peer write
closure, the stated aliasing-status precedence, zero native code for pre-native,
cancellation and deadline outcomes, exact captured code for native failures, and
`+other+` for both a documented otherwise-unclassified native failure and a synthetic
unknown status.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D011]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D011* — Filesystem tests shall cover UTF-8/native path round trips, embedded
NUL, target limits, each open mode, cursor ordering, metadata absence, enumeration end,
whole-file limit and close races.
Path-construction fixtures shall combine invalid UTF-8, NUL, absolute component,
unrepresentable native unit and injected allocation failure to prove the lexical-index,
semantic, representability and allocation priority fixed by R-SLIB-FS-0001. Path-join
fixtures shall cover either empty operand and two empty operands without inserting a
separator, then prove that an empty result is rejected by an ordinary access operation.
Open fixtures shall exercise each access/create mode, creation races, truncate/append,
final-symlink follow and no-follow, and every invalid combination. Seek fixtures shall
exercise each origin with positive and negative offsets, signed/u64 boundary overflow,
beyond-end positioning without extension, a later extending write, append interaction
and non-seekable identities. They shall verify exact shared-position advancement for
successful and partial-failure read/write/write-all outcomes, no advancement for end,
empty or pre-progress outcomes, and the append-mode rule that write progress leaves the
read/shared observation position unchanged. Namespace fixtures shall distinguish recursive and
nonrecursive creation, wrong-kind removal, `+directory_not_empty+`, atomic no-replace
rename to every existing destination kind, nonempty-directory movement, descendant-cycle
rejection and unsupported cross-root atomic movement.
Capability fixtures shall race parent components,
symlinks, mounts or reparse points and renames while attempting parent/root traversal;
no successful operation may escape the retained directory. Atomic no-replace tests
shall cover absent and existing destinations, injected write/flush/publication failures,
concurrent publishers, exact `+already_exists+`, staging cleanup and unchanged existing
content. Iterator failure, cancellation and deadline tests shall retry and observe the
same logical next-entry position, including a cached native entry, and shall cover
ordinary iterator drop. Atomic outcomes shall return byte-identical data owners.
Final-entry fixtures shall prove that metadata observes a final symbolic link as a link,
file removal removes that link, directory removal reports `+not_directory+`, and rename
moves that link, without any operation opening, changing or removing the link target.
For a target using the directory-creation private-staging mapping of R-SLIB-FS-0008, fixtures
shall observe the reserved staging entry while native execution is paused and prove its
manifest mode, confinement and requested-target absence before publication. Collision
injection shall prove the exact retry bound and exhaustion outcome. Injected
pre-publication native failure, cancellation and deadline shall each perform exactly one
logical staging cleanup before terminal acknowledgement; successful atomic publication
shall perform no cleanup operation because rename consumes the staging entry, and it
shall return success despite a later cancellation or deadline.
For the private-directory whole-file mapping of R-SLIB-FS-0010, fixtures shall additionally
prove a 0700 staging directory, its single 0600 regular payload file, complete payload
transfer on the file payload adapter and pre-publication file durability. Every terminal path after
staging creation, including successful publication, shall perform exactly one cleanup of
the staging directory. Beneath publication shall resolve both rename paths from the
independently retained original root while parent-renaming races run. Cancellation,
deadline and post-commit cleanup, directory-durability or close outcomes shall not replace
`+committed+` after publication; terminal acknowledgement shall still wait for all
required cleanup and native-resource release.
File/directory drop fixtures shall prove that the view is released without cancelling a
successfully started operation and that native close waits for its independent retain;
explicit close shall instead cancel and acknowledge pending operations.
Exact-code fixtures shall distinguish `+invalid_path+`, `+invalid_relative_path+` and
`+invalid_operation+`; absence, existing destination and wrong object kind shall produce
`+not_found+`, `+already_exists+`, `+not_directory+` or `+is_directory+` as applicable.
Nonempty directory removal shall produce exactly `+directory_not_empty+`.
Separate target fixtures shall distinguish `+read_only+` from `+permission_denied+`,
`+no_space+` from `+resource_exhausted+`, and `+file_too_large+` from both. Component/link
limits shall produce `+name_too_long+` and `+too_many_links+`; a known unavailable
facility shall produce `+unsupported+`; a documented otherwise-unclassified failure and
an injected unknown native status shall each produce `+other+`. Every native fixture shall preserve its exact native
code, while each pre-native validation fixture shall report native code zero.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D012]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D012* — Network tests shall parse/format IPv4 and IPv6, resolve a controlled
DNS fixture, validate malformed v4 and v6 scope identifiers for TCP listen/connect and
UDP bind/send, and run TCP and UDP loopback. Address parsing shall cover IPv4 leading
zeroes, empty/missing/excess components, range crossing, IPv6 compression at each
position, a second `+::+`, a fifth hexadecimal digit, embedded final IPv4 and an invalid
character at competing positions, proving the exact code and byte/end index.
Formatting vectors shall prove v4 leading-zero suppression and v6 longest/leftmost
zero-run selection, noncompression of a single field and absence of dotted-v4 output.
Local/peer observation shall return the exact bound/connected endpoints, including an
assigned ephemeral port, and shall exercise closed and not-connected outcomes. DNS input
shall cover every label/total-length boundary, optional final dot and first invalid
label byte. It shall also prove that a failed all-digit-and-dot IPv4 candidate such as
`+1.2.3.999+` is `+invalid_name+` and is never submitted as a DNS name. Numeric v4/v6
family mismatches shall produce `+name_not_found+` without a DNS query, while
`+family::any+` shall retain either family. TCP cases cover refusal, accept/connect,
bidirectional progress, ordered writes, EOF, read/write/both shutdown, reset,
cancellation and timeout. UDP cases cover zero-length and maximum supported datagrams,
source address, truncation, message-too-large, cancellation and timeout. Every send and
receive outcome returns the staged owner. UDP tests shall assert receive-prefix
replacement with suffix preservation, full preservation on failure and no send-buffer
mutation. They shall also cover ordinary network-handle destruction while a successfully
started operation retains the native identity.
Half-close fixtures shall prove read shutdown produces later nonempty local `+end+`, write
shutdown produces peer EOF after prior bytes and later nonempty local `+not_connected+` writes
with unchanged owners, the opposite direction remains usable, both combines the effects,
repeated shutdown is idempotent without duplicate native publication, and empty
read/write operations preserve their declared no-op outcomes after half-close.
UDP cases shall prove that a zero-length send performs one native empty-datagram
submission rather than the stream no-op.
Exact-code fixtures shall produce `+invalid_address+` and `+invalid_name+` before native
submission; controlled definitive and retryable DNS failures shall produce
`+name_not_found+` and `+temporary_failure+`. An actively refused TCP connect shall
produce exactly `+connection_refused+`; distinct peer-reset and local-abort fixtures shall
produce `+connection_reset+` and `+connection_aborted+`. Bind/route fixtures shall
distinguish `+address_in_use+`, `+address_not_available+`, `+network_unreachable+` and
`+host_unreachable+`; live-unconnected and closed identities shall distinguish
`+not_connected+` and `+closed+`. Further fixtures shall distinguish
`+permission_denied+`, `+resource_exhausted+`, `+message_too_large+` and
`+unsupported+`; a documented otherwise-unclassified failure and an unknown native
status shall each produce `+other+`.
Every native fixture shall preserve its exact native code, while pre-native validation
shall report native code zero.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D013]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D013* — Process tests shall pass arguments containing whitespace, quotes
and command-language metacharacters to a fixture that reports its exact argv; they shall
arrive as unchanged separate arguments. Tests shall cover environment replacement,
working directory, inherited/null-device/piped streams, async pipe I/O, spawn failure returning
command, cancel before/after creation commit, wait timeout returning child, forceful
termination, exit status and child-handle drop followed by exactly-once reap with no
zombie. Builder fixtures shall reject an empty executable, embedded NUL and target-
unrepresentable argument, invalid environment name, and NUL or unrepresentable
environment value at the defining builder call, reject an empty working-directory path
as `+invalid_command+`, preserve the previous builder, and
verify the fixed name/value/allocation priority. Spawn fixtures shall prove that only
the deferred native conditions listed by R-SLIB-PROC-0008 remain and that every
pre-commit failure returns the complete command.
Command-create fixtures shall inject environment capture and current-directory capture
failure in their fixed order, verify no partial command, and check each exact code/native
code mapping. Child-identity fixtures shall verify the published u64 mapping, concurrent
uniqueness and stability through native termination until wait consumes the owner.
Resolution fixtures shall place a same-named executable on `+PATH+` and prove it is never
searched, resolve a relative executable against both the captured default directory and
an explicit relative or absolute working directory, preserve the original argv[0]
spelling, and remain unaffected by a later process-current-directory change.
Dropping a child with unextracted piped stdin shall close the parent endpoint so the
child observes EOF; dropping one whose unextracted stdout or stderr fixture fills its
pipe shall close those parent endpoints without blocking an executor worker. Extracted
handles shall remain independent. A still-running dropped child shall register a
nonblocking wait without immediate termination. Orderly async-main and
`+std.process::exit+` shutdown fixtures shall include both a dropped child and a still-
live local child view; they shall close all remaining unextracted parent endpoints,
force termination where still running, await it and reap each child exactly once before
completion, while an isolated `+std.process::abort+` case is exempt. Every
failed wait shall be pre-reap and return a retryable child.
Pre-native fixtures shall distinguish `+invalid_command+` from `+invalid_argument+` and
report native code zero. A nonexistent executable shall produce exactly `+not_found+`,
while executable access denial, native process-limit exhaustion and a recognized invalid
executable format or wrong working-directory kind shall produce `+permission_denied+`, `+resource_exhausted+` and
`+spawn_failed+`. A running-required operation on an already terminal child shall produce
`+not_running+`; a known unavailable facility shall produce `+unsupported+`; an
otherwise-unclassified documented failure or an unknown native status shall produce
`+other+`. Injected R allocation or
size failure shall produce `+resource_exhausted+` with native code zero. Every
native-mapped fixture shall preserve its exact native code.
Current-process fixtures shall prove that `+exit+` completes the required R cleanup
before exactly one mapped host exit and that isolated `+abort+` performs no subsequent R
drop or orderly cleanup.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D014]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D014* — C-boundary tests shall cover every checked numeric edge, bounded
C-string validation, first NUL and first invalid UTF-8, embedded-NUL rejection by
`+std.c::string_from_str+` with `+embedded_nul+`, balanced handle adoption/release,
attachment generation/thread affinity and target-manifest facts. A bounded slice with
invalid bytes but no zero shall produce `+missing_nul+`; adding a zero after those bytes
shall produce the first `+invalid_utf8+` index. Injected allocation failure shall be
observed only after successful terminator/UTF-8 validation, and embedded NUL in a source
`+str+` shall win over it. They shall also include an invalid logical link name for which
`+std.c::link_available+` returns `+false+` without a manifest query. Native-failure fixtures
shall prove immediate errno/status capture and stable portable mapping. Unsafe-contract
violations are reviewed through metadata and negative compilation rather than executed
as safe tests.
Successful C-string fixtures shall compare exact source/copy/view bytes and the sole
terminator. Attachment fixtures shall inject, in priority order, runtime stopping,
attachment-state exhaustion and floating-environment unavailability; each shall return
no attachment, preserve generation/fenv state and map to common native code zero.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D015]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D015* — Each claimed profile shall have a selection test. A target claiming
hosted-native-async shall exercise every mandatory native family and manifest capability;
a target missing one shall reject that profile before translation. Generated application
C shall compile as strict C17, and runtime adapter units shall compile separately, all
with warnings treated as errors. Applicable sanitizers or documented substitutes shall
cover memory, race and undefined behavior. A target using a filesystem adapter lane shall
also run the trace, queue/entered cancellation, deadline, commit-race, retention and
progress scenarios required by Core R-CONF-G012 and R-SLIB-MAP-B009. A target using the file
payload adapter shall run the admission-bound, queued and entered cancellation, deadline,
retention and progress scenarios that Core R-CONF-G012 requires of it.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D016]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D016* — The English and Russian files of one library revision shall have
identical anchor sequences, normative rule-ID multisets, headings by structural role,
tables, source/API signature blocks and EBNF blocks. Both shall render with no AsciiDoc
warning. Every Core-to-library and library-to-Core rule reference shall resolve, and the
profile/module inventory in both documents shall agree.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D017]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D017* — Networking conformance for R 0.1 consists of IP address conversion,
DNS resolution, TCP and UDP operations listed in section 17. Standard library acceptance
is determined by those exact protocol-independent byte and address contracts.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D018]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D018* — Secret buffer conformance for R 0.1 consists of the exact
`+std.secret+` items of section 7.5. Acceptance requires an executable test that observes the
erased storage after drop and after `+zeroize+`, an allocation-failure injection that leaves
no retained allocation, and a comparison test covering equal lengths with differing bytes.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D019]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D019* — Conformance of the standard modules written in R (section 21) requires,
for each module, an executable program that imports it through the library map, exercises
every public item of that module once under the profile its entry names, and observes the
stated results; an import of such a module from a lower profile shall be rejected with
`+R-DIAG-PROFILE-001+`; and the implementation inventory shall record every item of section
21 with its R source file and declared name, so that a renamed or removed declaration fails
the layout check before the program tests run.

[[R-SLIB-CONF-D020]]
*R-SLIB-CONF-D020* — Conformance of the static reflection intrinsics (R-LIB-0024)
requires an executable program that observes every form of Core R-REFL-0001..R-REFL-0004
over a fieldless enumeration with explicit discriminants, a tagged union and a struct, in
a synchronous and in an async frame, and a freestanding program that observes the
enumeration constants and selections without the runtime; a form over a type of the
wrong kind, and a field index beyond the field count, shall be rejected with the
diagnostics those Core rules name.

[appendix]
[[annex-e]]
== Annex E — Portability recommendations (non-normative)

This annex is informative.

* Use a monotonic deadline for timeout policy and system time only for timestamps.
* Retain explicit limits for whole-file reads and untrusted formatting growth.
* Prefer directory-capability operations for untrusted relative paths.
* Preserve owned buffers from error outcomes when retrying partial I/O.
* Await or explicitly cancel/detach every task so ownership intent is visible.
* Treat native error text as diagnostic data and branch only on portable code enums.
* Keep C adapters minimal and copy foreign transient storage before releasing its guard.
* Test the selected native backend on the real target; strict C17 generation alone does
  not validate cancellation, filesystem or process behavior.

_End of R Standard Library Specification 0.1 draft._
